# Auth & Security Review

> ⚠ **This document contains exploitable detail about a device with flash encryption disabled.
> Do not commit it to a public repository.**

Read-only audit. **No code was modified.**

- **600 raw findings** from 60 specialist finder agents, deduplicated to **505 distinct issues**
- **482 adversarial verifier verdicts** ran against them: 398 upheld, 84 refuted
- **961 verified-clean observations** recorded (things checked and found correct)
- Severity of deduplicated set: CRITICAL: 19, HIGH: 139, MEDIUM: 194, LOW: 110, NOTE: 43

> **Verification caveat.** Findings were produced by independent finder agents and then attacked by
> adversarial verifier agents instructed to refute them. In aggregate the verifiers upheld 398 and
> refuted 84 — roughly a 17% refutation rate. The run was interrupted and resumed several times, and
> the per-finding link between a verdict and its finding was not preserved in the journal, so the
> refutations could not be subtracted from individual entries. **Treat each finding's own
> `Confidence` field as the primary signal, and expect roughly one in six of these to not survive
> closer scrutiny.** Duplicate independent reports (noted inline) are a stronger signal.

## Executive summary

The firmware has a well-built authorization core — `authorizeCommand`, the per-task TLS identity
in `System_AuthIdentity`, and the role tiers are all real and mostly enforced. The findings below
are overwhelmingly about **paths that route around that core** rather than defects inside it.
Four patterns account for most of the critical severity:

1. **Wrappers that hide the command from the gate.** The `remote:`/`@` prefix is not resolved by
   `findCommand`, so `authorizeCommand`'s admin and super-admin checks never fire.
2. **Surfaces that never had a gate.** The G2 lens menu executes CLI as the device owner with no
   authentication at all; ESP-NOW remote filesystem ops run as SYSTEM for any paired peer.
3. **Secrets in output sinks.** Live web session cookies are broadcast to every log sink on login
   and served back over `/api/cli/logs`, which turns any authenticated read into session theft.
4. **Pre-auth memory corruption.** `decodeBasicAuth` writes one byte past a 256-byte stack buffer
   before any credential is validated.

The single highest-value item is the `remote:`/`@` wrapper bypass, because it is not one hole —
it silently disables the privilege model for every command reachable through that prefix.

---

## Security findings

Grouped by subsystem, ordered by severity within each.


### ESP-NOW / radio mesh — 86 findings (30 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] ESP-NOW fragment reassembly does not bind per-fragment authentication to the message: plaintext fragments are silently merged into an AEAD-protected message
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5315`
- **Also:** Same defect covers every fragmenting opcode; dispatch flag checks at System_ESPNow.cpp:5061-5109 all run on the reassembled buffer
- **What:** `v4_try_handle_incoming` unwraps SESSION_FRAME per fragment at :5251-5290, setting the LOCAL flags `wasAuthenticated`/`wasSessionEncrypted` for that one call. It then enters the reassembler at :5315, which keys a slot purely on `(recv_info->src_addr, h->msgId)` (`v4_reasm_find_or_alloc`, :188-195) and stores the fragment with `memcpy(e->buffer + offset, payload, payloadLen)` (:5371). Nothing records whether a fragment arrived encrypted, nothing compares a fragment's auth state against the fragments already in the slot, and nothing compares `h->type` against the slot's stored `e->type`. When the slot completes, dispatch (:5595) is handed `wasAuthenticated`/`wasSessionEncrypted` from whichever frame happened to complete it — so the whole reassembled buffer inherits the last fragment's authentication verdict.
- **Why it matters:** Every ESP-NOW peer is added with `peerInfo.encrypt = false` (System_ESPNow.cpp:925, :9904; Handlers_Crypto.cpp:108), so the radio layer performs no authentication and an attacker in range can transmit frames with an arbitrary 802.11 source MAC. The V4 header — including `msgId`, `fragIndex`, `fragCount`, `sessionId`, `meshFingerprint` — travels in cleartext even for SESSION_FRAME frames, so an attacker sniffs an in-flight encrypted-chunked transfer (e.g. the 13-fragment FS_LIST_REPLY produced by `v4_send_encrypted_chunked`, :2089) and learns its msgId immediately from fragment 0. Concrete attack: peer P sends an encrypted FS_LIST_REPLY, msgId=M, fragCount=13. Attacker forges a PLAINTEXT frame with src=P's MAC, magic 0x3148, ver 4, headerLen 32, flags=0 (no SESSION_FRAME), msgId=M, fragCount=13, fragIndex=5, meshFingerprint copied from P's frames, valid CRC16, and 200 bytes of chosen payload. It passes the CRC gate (:5141), the fingerprint gate (:5153), is stored at offset 1000, and sets `e->have[5]`. P's genuine encrypted fragment 5 then hits the duplicate check at :5353 and is discarded. When P's genuine encrypted fragment 12 completes the message, dispatch runs with `isSessionEncrypted = true`, so `v4_dispatch_table_try`'s REQ_SESSION_ENC gate (:5097) passes and `v4h_fs_list_reply` consumes 200 attacker-chosen bytes as if they were AEAD-verified. The same works against FILE_DATA (:4994), the bond opcodes (:5014-5023), and CMD_RESP. The AEAD tag protects each 216-byte frame in isolation; the message the handler actually sees is not protected at all.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_ESPNOW; not board-gated)
- **Direction:** Record the auth verdict on the reassembly entry at allocation time and reject any fragment whose (isSessionEncrypted, isAuthenticated, sessionId, type) does not match the slot; drop the slot on mismatch rather than merging. A `V4ReasmEntry` field set from the first fragment plus an equality check next to the existing dedup check at :5353 is enough.

### [CRITICAL] Remote-supplied ESP-NOW peer metadata is printed unescaped into hand-rolled JSON, so one double-quote permanently destroys the paired-device registry (MACs + per-device AES keys)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:585`
- **Also:** saveMeshPeers() System_ESPNow.cpp:515-517 prints peerName unescaped into /system/espnow/mesh_peers.json; loadMeshPeers() (:610) uses substring scanning rather than a parser, so it mis-parses rather than losing everything, but a name containing the literal text '"mac":' can inject a phantom peer entry.
- **What:** saveEspNowDevices() (:536-600) builds /system/espnow/devices.json by hand with f.print(), emitting name (:563), friendlyName (:585), room (:586), zone (:587) and tags (:588) directly between quote characters with no escaping. processMetadata() (:7469-7497) copies those four strings straight out of a received V4PayloadMetadata frame into gEspNow->devices[i] and calls saveEspNowDevices() immediately when anything changed. The dispatch rows for ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_METADATA_RESP and METADATA_PUSH (:4987-4988) carry flags 0 — no REQ_PAIRED, no REQ_AUTHENTICATED, no REQ_SESSION_ENC — so v4_dispatch_table_try (:5058-5110) applies no gate at all. The only filter is that the source MAC must already exist in gEspNow->devices[]. The local setter metaGetSet() (:11499-11527) validates length only, never characters, unlike espnow setname which enforces alnum/-/_ (:11455-11463).
- **Why it matters:** Fully traced, first-party trigger, no attacker needed: on device A run `espnowfriendlyname My "Lab" Node`. metaGetSet (:11500) only strips SURROUNDING quotes (:11516) so an interior quote is accepted and stored. buildMetadataPayload (:7310-7315) copies gSettings.espnowFriendlyName verbatim into the METADATA payload; sendMetadata pushes it to peers. On device B, processMetadata (:7473-7492) assigns it straight into gEspNow->devices[i].friendlyName and immediately calls saveEspNowDevices(). devices.json now contains {"friendlyName":"My "Lab" Node"} -> invalid JSON. Reboot B: loadEspNowDevices() parse error -> every paired peer, its per-peer AES key and its meshId are gone, and B silently re-writes the file empty. Reachability is worse than accidental: the METADATA_RESP/PUSH handler rows carry flags 0 (:4986-4988), so nothing but a src-MAC match gates the write path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Route devices.json and mesh_peers.json through ArduinoJson (serializeJson escapes correctly) instead of hand-rolled f.print(), and reject or strip control/quote/backslash characters in processMetadata before storing peer-supplied strings. The METADATA_RESP/PUSH rows should also carry at least V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED so an unpaired sender cannot reach the persistence path at all.

### [CRITICAL] ESP-NOW remote filesystem (FS_LIST/FS_STAT/FS_GET) is served under SYSTEM identity to any paired peer with a live session — no credential, no role, no enable-gate
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:784, :842, :896; components/hardwareone/System_AuthIdentity.cpp:88 (empty scope); contrast components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4427 (correctly scoped write path)
- **What:** The three FS request opcodes are registered with only `V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC` (System_ESPNow.cpp:5006-5011) — no REQ_AUTHENTICATED, and the wire payloads (V4PayloadFsListReq/FsStatReq/FsGetReq) carry no username/password field at all. The serving handlers then install SYSTEM: `SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE("espnow.fs_list_reply")` (:644), `("espnow.fs_stat_reply")` (:784), `("espnow.fs_get_ack")` (:842) and `("espnow.fs_get_send")` (:896), and pass `currentAuthContext()` straight into `VFS::existsGuarded`/`openGuarded` on the attacker-supplied `req.path` after only `VFS::normalize()`. `resolveRole()` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551) maps that context to FsRole::SYSTEM, `permsForRole` returns `rule.systemPerms` which is PERM_ALL on every /system rule (System_Filesystem.cpp:1343-1392), and `isUnrestrictedRole()` exempts it from the sensitive-extension and image guards (:1630-1632). `captureDeferred()` (:420-481) contains no auth check, no enable setting, and no confirmation prompt — the only gate is pairing plus a live session.
- **Why it matters:** Any peer that is securely paired and holds an active session (the only gate — `V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC`, System_ESPNow.cpp:5007-5012; deliberately NOT bond-gated) can `espnowbrowse` to `/system/users/users.json` and pull the password hashes, and to `/system/certs/*.key` and pull the TLS private key — files that the web/CLI layers deny even to a local admin. The confinement mechanism exists and is used correctly two thousand lines away on the inbound-file WRITE path (`VFS::systemAuth(VFS::Scopes::ESPNOW_RECEIVED, ...)`, System_ESPNow.cpp:4427), so the read path is the outlier, not a missing feature. The code comment at :641-643 acknowledges the device-level trust decision but not that it also bypasses the sensitive-extension guard.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW=1 in the current System_BuildConfig.h via NETWORK_FEATURE_LEVEL 4 / CUSTOM_ENABLE_NET_ESPNOW 1) — LIVE in the default FeatherS3 build; prior art: KNOWN — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.1, which labels this "the FTS AuthBypass". NOTE: that label is a misattribution — the security backlog's "FTS AuthBypass" is the First-Time-Setup reserved-na
- **Direction:** Carry a credential in the FS request payload and serve under that user's resolved FsRole rather than SYSTEM (the code comment at :641-643 already anticipates this: "Per-user identity propagation can be layered on later via a token in the request's reserved bytes"). Failing that, add an `espnowFsServe` setting defaulting off plus a first-read on-device confirmation, matching the inbound-write flow.

### [HIGH] reassembledSize uses the completing frame's payloadLen as the last fragment's length — OOB read past e->buffer and stale-byte disclosure on out-of-order completion
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5407`
- **Also:** Consumers that trust the inflated length: v4h_cmd_resp (:2920-2937), v4h_file_data (:4113-4114), fsListOnReplyReceived (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:294)
- **What:** After the slot is judged complete, the delivered length is computed as `for (i < e->fragCount) reassembledSize += (i == fragCount-1) ? payloadLen : V4_MAX_FRAGMENT_PAYLOAD` (:5407-5416), where `payloadLen` is the local length of the frame currently being processed. The code assumes the frame that completes the message is the last fragment. The per-fragment length is never stored, so if any other index arrives last, the wrong length is used. `payload` is then repointed at `e->buffer` and `payloadLen = reassembledSize` (:5426-5427) before dispatch.
- **Why it matters:** The stored bound only guarantees `offset + payloadLen <= e->bufferSize` for that fragment, so a fragment at index 0 may legitimately carry up to 218 bytes (a full 250-byte ESP-NOW frame minus the 32-byte header). Attack: allocate a slot with e->fragCount = 21 (bufferSize = 4200 = the full `buffer[4200]` array), deliver fragments 1..20, then complete the message with fragIndex=0 carrying payloadLen=218 (offset 0, 0+218 <= 4200 passes). reassembledSize = 20*200 + 218 = 4218, which exceeds the 4200-byte array. `V4ReasmEntry` is laid out active@0, src@1, msgId@8, type@12, fragCount@13, received@14, have[21]@15, buffer[4200]@36, bufferSize@4236, lastUpdateMs@4240, sizeof 4244 — so the handler is handed a pointer/length pair that reads 18 bytes past the buffer, i.e. through `bufferSize`/`lastUpdateMs` and, for `gV4Reasm[1]` (the last of the 2 ps_alloc'd entries), ~10 bytes past the end of the heap block. The milder and far easier case needs no oversized frame at all: any legitimate 2-fragment message whose fragments arrive out of order (fragment 1 = 50 bytes arrives first, fragment 0 = 200 bytes completes) is delivered as 400 bytes instead of 250, feeding 150 bytes of never-cleared previous-message residue to the handler — the same disclosure channel as the finding above, and it also breaks honest reception under radio reordering/retransmit.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Store each fragment's byte count in the entry (e.g. `uint8_t len[V4_FRAG_MAX]`) at :5371 and sum the stored values, rather than inferring the tail from whichever frame arrived last. Also assert the result against `sizeof(e->buffer)` before repointing `payload`.

### [HIGH] Every inbound ESP-NOW remote command is broadcast unredacted through BROADCAST_PRINTF to serial, the web mirror, the system log file and the guest-visible OLED console
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5838`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5781 (bond path)
- **What:** v4_handle_cmd prints the full inner command after auth: :5838 BROADCAST_PRINTF("[ESP-NOW] Remote command from %s (user=%s): %s", deviceName, username, actualCmd) on the username:password path, and :5781 BROADCAST_PRINTF("[ESP-NOW] Bonded command from %s (session token): %s", deviceName, actualCmd) on the bond path. BROADCAST_PRINTF (System_Debug.h:734) is gated only on gOutputFlags — no debug flag, no log level. It runs on espnow_task where currentCommandContext() is null, so broadcastOutputCore falls back to MSG_ROUTE_ALL (System_Debug.cpp:896-899) and hits every sink.
- **Why it matters:** Any command a mesh peer sends is printed in full on the receiving device. `espnowremote node1 admin pw login bob hunter2` makes node1 print `[ESP-NOW] Remote command from master (user=admin): login bob hunter2` to its serial console, its web CLI mirror (readable by any non-guest account), its OLED console (readable by a Guest via OLED_CLI_VIEWER, OLED_Utils.cpp:2927), and — if `log start` is running — to the persisted system log file (System_Debug.cpp:254-283). Same for `wifiadd <ssid> <psk>`, `useradd <u> <pw> admin`, `espnowsetpassphrase <mesh> <phrase>`. This is the same string as finding #1 but on a different, always-on sink set.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW builds)
- **Direction:** Pass actualCmd through redactCmdForAudit before either BROADCAST_PRINTF, or print only the leading verb — the full redacted line already reaches command-audit.log downstream via executeCommand.

### [HIGH] Reassembly bounds-checks the fragment index against the attacker's claimed fragCount, not the slot's allocated fragCount — OOB read of have[], 1-byte OOB write, and completion with a missing fragment
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5347`
- **Also:** docs/ESPNOW_RELAY_RESTORE_PLAN.md:210 notes the fixed fragIndex×200 stride but not this class of defect
- **What:** `v4_reasm_find_or_alloc` clamps the slot to `e->fragCount = min(fragCount, V4_FRAG_MAX)` = 21 and sets `e->bufferSize = e->fragCount * 200` (:202-203). The RX path then validates the incoming index with `if (h->fragIndex >= h->fragCount)` (:5347) — that is the *wire* header's fragCount, an attacker-controlled uint8 up to 255, re-read on every frame and never compared against the slot's `e->fragCount`. The very next statement indexes `e->have[h->fragIndex]` (:5353), where `have` is `bool have[V4_FRAG_MAX]` = 21 entries (:157). The only real bound is the later `offset + payloadLen > e->bufferSize` check (:5363). Completion is then tested as `e->received < e->fragCount` (:5379) — a counter, not a check that have[0..fragCount-1] are all set.
- **Why it matters:** Three distinct consequences, all reachable by an unauthenticated attacker in radio range (peers are added with encrypt=false, so source MACs are forgeable and no gate runs before reassembly).
(a) OOB READ: send msgId=X fragCount=255 fragIndex=254 — :5347 passes (254 < 255), and :5353 reads `e->have[254]` on a 21-element array. `have` sits at struct offset 15 and `buffer[4200]` at 36, so index 254 reads buffer[218] — attacker-controlled reassembly bytes interpreted as the duplicate flag.
(b) OOB WRITE: first frame msgId=X fragCount=21 fragIndex=0 payloadLen=200 allocates a slot with e->fragCount=21, bufferSize=4200. Second frame msgId=X fragCount=22 fragIndex=21 payloadLen=0 (a bare 32-byte header — allowed, `len < sizeof(header)` at :5124 is the only length gate, and the CRC check is skipped when payloadLen==0 at :5141). offset = 21*200 = 4200; `4200 + 0 > 4200` is false, so it passes, and :5372 executes `e->have[21] = true` — one byte past the array, landing on `buffer[0]`.
(c) COMPLETION WITH A HOLE + STALE DISCLOSURE: `v4_reasm_reset` (:165-172) clears `have`/metadata but never the buffer, so a slot retains the previous message's bytes. Send frag0 (fragCount=3, 200 B), frag2 (fragCount=3, 200 B), then the phantom frame fragCount=4 / fragIndex=3 / payloadLen=0 — offset 600 == bufferSize 600, passes, `received` reaches 3 == e->fragCount, and :5379 declares the message COMPLETE with fragment 1 never received. `reassembledSize` = 400, so bytes 200..399 handed to the handler are the previous message's residue. Dispatched as ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_CMD_RESP (handler-table flags = 0, no pairing and no auth, :4961), those bytes land in `gEspNow->deferredCmdRespResult` via `v4h_cmd_resp` (:2929) and are surfaced as a peer command result in the web UI and logs — a cross-message/cross-peer leak of previously reassembled content.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Validate against the slot, not the frame: reject when `h->fragCount != e->fragCount` or `h->fragIndex >= e->fragCount`, and replace the `received` counter test with an explicit all-of-have[0..fragCount-1] scan. Zero `e->buffer` (or at least track a per-fragment length array) in `v4_reasm_reset`.

### [HIGH] captureEspNowFrame reserves 976 B on the caller's stack for every ESP-NOW frame, including on the 3584-byte input task's auto-disable path — the capture-disabled early return saves nothing
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:6015`
- **Also:** call sites System_ESPNow.cpp:1700 (v4_send_frame), :2198, :1771 (session-wrapped send), :6079 (onEspNowRawRecv); reached from i2csensor_seesaw.cpp:661 and :676, i2csensor_ano_encoder.cpp:657/666, System_I2C.cpp:2406-2412, System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:665 (sensor_bcast, 4096 B)
- **What:** captureEspNowFrame() has a 976-byte frame (`char line[700]` at :6054, `char macStr[20]`, a String, a File object). On Xtensa the whole frame is committed by the single `entry` instruction at function entry, so the `if (!gSettings.espnowCaptureToSd) return;` guard at :6017 does not avoid the cost — every ESP-NOW TX and RX pays 976 B of stack regardless of the setting. The gamepad auto-disable path runs entirely on the 3584-byte input task: inputTask (416) -> handleDeviceStopped (96) -> broadcastSensorStatus (80) -> v4_broadcast_sensor_status (96) -> v4_broadcast_category (160) -> v4_send_frame (640) -> captureEspNowFrame (976) = 2464 B, 69% of the stack, with capture off. All frame sizes decoded from the linked ELF.
- **Why it matters:** Every ESP-NOW send and receive pays 976 bytes of stack for a diagnostic that is off by default (espnowCaptureToSd) and additionally needs an SD card. The charge lands on whichever task is sending: espnow_tx (5120 B, whose own comment at System_TaskUtils.h:88 records a HW-measured peak leaving 2392 B free — so this one frame is 41% of its remaining headroom), espnow_task (6656 B, via processMeshHeartbeats), sensor_bcast (4096 B) and cmd_exec_task. It is also a large single frame, so if it is the one that goes over it lands entirely below the 16-byte canary (see the canary finding).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards with ENABLE_ESPNOW; the input-task chain applies to FeatherS3 (seesaw) and the ANO-encoder variant
- **Direction:** Split the cheap gate into a tiny wrapper (test gSettings.espnowCaptureToSd / VFS::isSDAvailable and return) that calls a noinline body holding the buffers, so the 976 B frame is only entered when capture is actually armed. Alternatively move the line buffer to the existing PSRAM scratch (b64 at :6051 is already EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR static).

### [HIGH] In the default "auto" channel mode ESP-NOW has no channel anchor at all — the chosen channel is defined as "wherever the radio currently is", and the built-in drift checker is a tautology
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:953`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8444-8481 (resync + drift self-heal); :11276-11296 (espnowchannel checker); :10202-10206 and G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:524-530 and OLED_ESPNow.cpp:2088-2095 (all report the cached value); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/WiFiGeneric.cpp:431-439 (WiFi.channel() == esp_wifi_get_channel); components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:930 (memset zeroes sta.channel on every connect attempt)
- **What:** espnowComputeChannel() decides the effective ESP-NOW channel. When the STA is not associated and gSettings.espnowChannel == 0 (the default), it returns `staCh`. `staCh` comes from esp_wifi_get_config(WIFI_IF_STA).sta.channel, which is 0 for a device that has not associated this boot — connectWiFiIndex memsets its wifi_config_t (System_WiFi.cpp:930), so 0 is also what gets stored/persisted. It therefore falls through to `int wc = WiFi.channel()`, and Arduino's WiFi.channel() is a straight esp_wifi_get_channel() — the LIVE hardware channel. The documented `return ... : 1` fallback at :969 is unreachable once the driver is up. The 15 s re-sync at :8448-8451 then compares `want` against `prev = gEspNow->channel` (the CACHED field), never against hardware, and only applies when they differ. The one branch that does read hardware (:8465-8474) is explicitly gated on `gSettings.espnowChannel >= 1`, i.e. switched off in auto mode.
- **Why it matters:** Two off-grid devices, both on the shipped default (espnowChannel = 0), are supposed to "meet deterministically". They do not. Anything that moves one node's radio — a `wifiscan` (System_WiFi.cpp:614, blocking all-channel scan), an `openwifi --best` that fails against several saved SSIDs (WIFI_FAST_SCAN hops channels for minutes), the driver's auto-reconnect hunt, or the soft-AP re-parking after a disconnect — permanently redefines that node's ESP-NOW channel, because want == the drifted hardware channel by construction. The mesh goes dark. Worse, every diagnostic agrees it is fine: `espnowstatus` prints gEspNow->channel (now updated to the drifted value), and `espnowchannel` with no args computes `expected = espnowComputeChannel()` and compares it to esp_wifi_get_channel() — in auto mode that is the same read twice, so the verdict is permanently "OK — radio matches expected" and "MISMATCH — run 'espnowchannel resync'" can never fire. The user is left with a silently dead mesh and a checker that says it is healthy.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (FeatherS3 primary; identical on ESP32 secondary)
- **Direction:** Give auto mode a real anchor (latch the channel chosen at initEspNow, or use the documented literal 1) instead of re-reading the live radio, and make both the re-sync comparison and the checker's `expected` independent of esp_wifi_get_channel(). Then run the existing drift self-heal in auto mode too rather than gating it to pinned channels.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW CMD_RESP carries a success byte that is effectively a constant 1 — remote command failures are reported to the originating device as successes
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5679`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:2934 (v4h_cmd_resp stores it), :8913-8930 (consumer + SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RESULT)
- **What:** v4CmdResultCallback() (:5644) receives `ok` straight from submitCommandAsync's completion (HardwareOne.cpp:796 → r->ok → executeCommand's return) and writes it as the wire success byte: `respPayload[0] = ok ? 1 : 0` (:5679, fallback at :5693). Because executeCommand returns true for every handler-level failure, that byte is 1 for "Unknown command", for every "Error: ..." handler return, and for every `{"ok":false}` JSON result. The originator stores it at v4h_cmd_resp (:2934) and branches on it at :8913-8917.
- **Why it matters:** Send `espnowremote gold user pass thermalread` to a peer whose thermal sensor is disconnected. The peer's handler returns "Error: ...", executeCommand returns true, the success byte is 1, and the originator prints "[ESP-NOW] Command result from gold: Error: ..." via the SUCCESS branch and posts SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RESULT with detail "#<reqId> ok Error: ...". The "[ESP-NOW] Command FAILED from %s" branch at :8916 is unreachable for any handler-level failure — the only failures that reach it are auth denials. Any mesh automation or app that keys off the success byte or the remote_cmd_result event's ok/failed token is reading a constant.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW=1 by default via NETWORK_FEATURE_LEVEL=4 / CUSTOM_ENABLE_NET_ESPNOW=1); prior art: docs/ESPNOW_MESH_SYSTEM.md ranks "wire-delivery is mistaken for command success at every layer" #1, but documents the SEND side (`{"ok":true,"reqId":N}` on enqueue). The RESPONSE-side success byte bei
- **Direction:** Fixing finding #1 fixes this for free. Until then, do not build any mesh feature (sensor fetch, battery poll, bond sync) on the CMD_RESP success byte — it carries no information today.

### [HIGH] Arduino WiFi layer re-applies WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM on every STA start, silently undoing both esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE) call sites
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9738`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1094 (same bypass); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/STA.cpp:114 (the re-apply); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/WiFiGeneric.cpp:381 (the MIN_MODEM default)
- **What:** Both places that disable WiFi power save call the IDF API directly — `esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE)` — instead of Arduino's `WiFi.setSleep()`. The Arduino WiFi layer keeps its own cached copy in `WiFiGenericClass::_sleepEnabled`, which is initialised to `WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM` on every target except ESP32-S2 (components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/WiFiGeneric.cpp:379-381). `_onStaArduinoEvent()` re-applies that cached value on every `ARDUINO_EVENT_WIFI_STA_START`: `esp_wifi_set_ps(WiFi.getSleep())` (components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/STA.cpp:114). Nothing in components/hardwareone/ ever calls `WiFi.setSleep()` (repo-wide grep returns zero matches), so `_sleepEnabled` stays MIN_MODEM for the life of the process.
- **Why it matters:** initEspNow does `WiFi.mode(WIFI_AP_STA)` (System_ESPNow.cpp:9727) and then `esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE)` (:9738). `WiFi.mode()` starts the STA interface, which posts WIFI_EVENT_STA_START; Arduino relays it through the `arduino_events` task queue (components/arduino/libraries/Network/src/NetworkEvents.cpp:104 `xQueueSend`), so `_onStaArduinoEvent` runs *after* line 9738 has already executed. Net effect on a device that brings up ESP-NOW without joining an AP — the exact scenario the comment at :9732-9737 says it is defending ("out and about, home SSID absent") — power save ends up MIN_MODEM, the radio sleeps between DTIM beacons, and asynchronous ESP-NOW RX is dropped. The same revert happens to System_WiFi.cpp:1094's WIFI_PS_NONE on any later STA restart (closewifi/openwifi cycle, mode change, ESP-NOW init), which also reinstates the periph_module_enable/disable spinlock contention that comment says trips the interrupt WDT.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (mechanism is identical on esp32s3 and esp32-classic; only ESP32-S2 defaults _sleepEnabled to NONE)
- **Direction:** Route both sites through `WiFi.setSleep(WIFI_PS_NONE)` so the Arduino layer's cached `_sleepEnabled` is updated and the STA_START handler re-applies NONE rather than MIN_MODEM. Alternatively re-assert `esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE)` from the app's own ARDUINO_EVENT_WIFI_STA_START handler.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW devices.json is written as hand-rolled JSON with unescaped, remotely-supplied strings; a single quote or backslash makes the file unparseable and permanently destroys the whole paired-device table (MACs + AES keys)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:585`
- **Also:** saveMeshPeers() System_ESPNow.cpp:511-519 prints peerName the same way; its loader (loadMeshPeers :642-689) uses a naive content.indexOf("\"mac\":") scan rather than a parser, so it does not hard-fail — but a name containing the literal text `"mac":` injects a phantom peer entry.
- **What:** saveEspNowDevices() (:536-603) builds /system/espnow/devices.json by hand with f.print(): `f.print(",\"friendlyName\":\""); f.print(dev.friendlyName); f.print("\"")` for friendlyName/room/zone/tags (:585-588) and the same for `name` (:562-563). None of those Strings are JSON-escaped. loadEspNowDevices() (:7853) parses the file with strict ArduinoJson deserializeJson (:7869) and returns early on any error (:7870-7873), leaving gEspNow->deviceCount at 0. friendlyName/room/zone/tags are attacker/peer-supplied: processMetadata() copies them verbatim out of the received V4PayloadMetadata frame into gEspNow->devices[i].* (:7476-7486) with zero character validation and then calls saveEspNowDevices() itself (:7492).
- **Why it matters:** A peer sets its own metadata with `espnowfriendlyname My \"Lab\" Node` (metaGetSet, :11515-11518, strips only SURROUNDING quotes and permits any inner character up to 47 chars) and pushes it. The receiver writes `,"friendlyName":"My "Lab" Node"` into devices.json. On the next boot loadEspNowDevices' parse fails, every paired device is dropped, and the very next saveEspNowDevices() (fired by any pairing or metadata change) overwrites the file with an empty devices array — so the loss is permanent, not a one-boot blip. The lost data includes each peer's 16-byte ESP-NOW encryption key and meshId. A purely local repro needs no radio at all: `espnow pair AA:BB:CC:DD:EE:FF C:\temp` (cmd_espnow_pair :11127 takes the name as a raw CommandArgs token with no charset check) yields an invalid `\t` escape. Control characters and newlines fail identically.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Build both files with ArduinoJson (the loaders already use it) instead of hand-rolled print(), or at minimum run every string field through a JSON-escape helper before printing. Independently, validate/sanitise the inbound metadata char arrays in processMetadata before they reach a persisted field.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW USER_SYNC appends users.json entries with no username validation — the reserved-name guarantee documented in System_User.h is false, and a planted "bond-admin" account escalates admin→super-admin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3658`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1410 (approvePendingUserInternal — no re-validation; relies on the pending list being pre-validated by cmd_user_request at :2712 and WebServer_Server.cpp:3817); components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:261 (createInitialAdminUser, role="superadmin") and :891 (inline copy in firstTimeSetupIfNeeded; its own comment at :248-251 admits the duplication and says "update both"); canonical: components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:834/:899. The false security claim is at components/hardwareone/System_User.h:73-74: "It is in the reserved-name table (cannot be created) and has no users.json entry (cannot be logged into with a password)."
- **What:** `doUserSyncWork` (System_ESPNow.cpp:3534) hand-rolls a users.json append: `newUser["username"] = targetUser;` at :3658, taking the name straight from the peer's JSON payload (`doc["target_user"]`, :3584). It calls neither `isValidPublicUsername()` nor `isValidPublicPassword()`. The canonical creation path `adminCreateUser()` (System_User.cpp:825) calls `isValidPublicUsername(u, &errorOut)` at :834, which rejects the reserved names `{"system", "AuthBypass", kBondAdminUser}` case-insensitively (System_User.cpp:803-809) plus any non-[A-Za-z0-9._-] charset. The sync copy is aware it is a duplicate — it explicitly mirrors `bumpIdentityGeneration` with a comment naming `adminCreateUser` as "the canonical creation path" (:3724-3729) — but it copied the cache-invalidation half and not the validation half. `isValidUser()` (System_User.cpp:1137) and `loginTransport()` (:439) also apply no reserved-name check, so a planted reserved account is fully loginable on serial/OLED/BLE/web.
- **Why it matters:** `isAdminUser()` (System_User.cpp:286) and `isSuperAdminUser()` (:364) both short-circuit on `who == kBondAdminUser` ("bond-admin") and return `isBondSessionTokenValid()` BEFORE consulting users.json. Chain: an attacker holding an ADMIN (non-super) credential on device B, reaching it over an encrypted ESP-NOW session with `espnowusersync` enabled, sends USER_SYNC with target_user="bond-admin" and a password of their choice. B creates the account (role forced to "user", which is irrelevant). The attacker then logs in on web/serial/BLE as bond-admin. While B has a live bond session, `hasSuperAdminPrivilege(ctx)` → `isSuperAdminUser("bond-admin")` → `isBondSessionTokenValid()` → true, so `authorizeCommand`'s super gate (System_Utils.cpp:4338) passes and every requiresSuperAdmin command unlocks — factoryreset, sdformat, blesecret, certgen, espnowregenidentity, espnowsetpassphrase, serialrequireauth. That is a clean break of the admin/super-admin boundary the tier was created to enforce. Second, availability variant reachable with no attacker at all: the first-time-setup copies (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:261 and the inline duplicate at :891) also skip validation and write role="superadmin"; an owner who names themselves "bond-admin" during setup gets `isAdminUser` returning `isBondSessionTokenValid()` — false whenever no bond session exists — and is permanently locked out of every admin command with no recovery path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — memory/project_security_review_backlog.md item 1 ("AuthBypass can be a real account"; "USER_SYNC can plant the name unvalidated too"). Status: still open at v0.99.4. New here: the bond-adm
- **Direction:** Make `isValidPublicUsername()` a mandatory precondition of every users.json append rather than a caller courtesy — ideally by routing the sync/FTS/approve paths through one shared append helper that validates. As defense in depth, add the reserved-name rejection to `isValidUser()`/`getUserIdByUsername()` so a name that should not exist can never authenticate even if it reaches the file by restore or manual edit.

### [HIGH] SENSOR_BROADCAST (opcode 150) still dispatches the same cache ingest as the session-encrypted SENSOR_ENVELOPE but with opcode flags = 0, contradicting the design doc's explicit "cannot" claim
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4975`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3097 (shared ingest), :3129-3133 (the "vestigial" handler), docs/ESPNOW_SENSOR_FETCHER_DESIGN.md:284 and :363 (the false claims)
- **What:** `v4_ingest_sensor_reading()` (System_ESPNow.cpp:3097) is reached from two handler-table rows with divergent gates: `ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_SENSOR_ENVELOPE` at :4981 carries `V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC`, while `ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_SENSOR_BROADCAST` at :4975 carries flags = **0** — no pairing, no session encryption, no authentication of any kind. Both handlers (`v4h_sensor_broadcast` :3131, `v4h_sensor_envelope` :3138) call the identical ingest, which memcpys attacker-supplied JSON into the cache entry and sets `valid = true; enabled = true; connected = true` (:3117-3124). The D2 hardening removed only the TRANSMIT path; the comment at :3129-3130 keeps the receive path as "vestigial ... so a frame from an un-reflashed peer still caches."
- **Why it matters:** Any ESP32 in radio range, with no pairing and no key material, sends one 150-opcode frame and writes arbitrary JSON into `gRemoteSensorCache` under any src MAC and device name (findOrCreateCacheEntry keys on `ctx.recv_info->src_addr`, which is settable on a hostile device, so a known peer's readings can be overwritten rather than merely added). The cache is not display-only: System_MQTT.cpp:801-833 publishes it to the external MQTT broker as genuine mesh telemetry, and it renders on the G2 lens (G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:565-1186), the OLED (OLED_Mode_Network.cpp:956-1057) and the web `/api/sensors/remote` endpoint (which is on the guest GET allowlist). The concrete doc failure: docs/ESPNOW_SENSOR_FETCHER_DESIGN.md:284 states as a threat-model row that a non-paired peer "Cannot — sensor data is only ever AEAD session-encrypted unicast (SENSOR_ENVELOPE); the plaintext SENSOR_BROADCAST path no longer exists (D2)", and :363 says the plaintext path is "removed". Both are false as compiled.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — docs/ESPNOW_OPCODE_REORG_PLAN.md:194 records "SENSOR_BROADCAST | live | flags=0 (anyone in range can inject fake remote readings — display-only cache)". Status changed: docs/ESPNOW_SENSOR_
- **Direction:** Either delete the SENSOR_BROADCAST row and handler outright (the design decision already says nothing transmits opcode 150 in this build), or give it the same REQ_PAIRED|REQ_SESSION_ENC flags as SENSOR_ENVELOPE. Whichever is chosen, correct the D2 threat-model row in ESPNOW_SENSOR_FETCHER_DESIGN.md — it currently reads as a completed mitigation.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW FS_LIST/FS_STAT/FS_GET serve the whole filesystem under SYSTEM identity with no account and no per-user authorization — this is the real "filesystem auth bypass"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5007`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644 (LIST), :784 (STAT), :842 (GET ack), :896 (GET file send). Requester-side drivers: components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:477/:550 and components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1882/:2005/:2095.
- **What:** The dispatch rows are `{ FS_LIST_REQ, V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC, v4h_fs_list_req }` (and the same for FS_STAT_REQ/FS_GET_REQ) — no `V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_AUTHENTICATED`, and more importantly no credential exists anywhere in the protocol: `V4PayloadFsListReq` (System_ESPNow_Wire.h:636) carries only `reqId/startIndex/maxEntries/path[128]/reserved[8]`. Each deferred handler then installs `SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE("espnow.fs_list_reply")` and passes `currentAuthContext()` into `VFS::existsGuarded`/`VFS::openGuarded`/`getPermissions`. `resolveRole()` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551) maps that to `FsRole::SYSTEM`, `permsForRole` returns `rule.systemPerms` which is `PERM_ALL` for every row in `sPathRules` including the `/system/` catch-all at :1378, and `isUnrestrictedRole(SYSTEM)` at :1630/:1632 exempts it from the `hasSensitiveExtension` guard. There is no enable-gate, no rate limit, and no first-read confirmation anywhere in the reply path.
- **Why it matters:** A peer whose MAC is in `gEspNow->devices[]` (that is what `isPaired` means — System_ESPNow.cpp:5542-5550) and which can complete a SESSION_OPEN sends `FS_GET_REQ{path:"/system/users/users.json"}` and receives the file over the FILE_* pipeline. It holds **no account on this device**. Contrast the credentialed route: `espnowbrowse`/`espnowfetch` (System_ESPNow.cpp:14139/:14219) wrap `files`/`espnowsendfile` in a `user:pass:cmd` CMD frame, and `v4_handle_cmd` (:5841) runs `isValidUser()` then `executeCommand`→`authorizeCommand` under that account's own `FsRole`. So two parallel routes browse the same peer's filesystem and one of them skips the credential entirely. Concretely reachable as SYSTEM but NOT as any logged-in admin: every `.key/.pem/.crt/.cert/.cer/.credentials/.bin/.hex/.elf` file and every file whose basename contains "password"/"secret"/"credential" outside the `exemptSensitiveExt` dirs — `canRead` blocks those for ADMIN at System_Filesystem.cpp:1629-1631 but not for SYSTEM. That set includes `/system/espnow/identity.json` (Ed25519 secret, AES-wrapped with the derivable device key) and any system log file, which is where the G2 credential leak below lands its plaintext passwords.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards with ENABLE_ESPNOW=1 (current default config: NETWORK_FEATURE_LEVEL=4 / CUSTOM_ENABLE_NET_ESPNOW=1, so LIVE in the shipping build). Note these rows sit ABOVE the `#if ENABLE_BONDED_MODE` block at :5013, so they are compiled even though ENABLE_BONDED_MODE=0.; prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.1 (filed there under the misleading name "the FTS AuthBypass"). Status unchanged; two of that section's supporting claims are wrong — see the separate doc-accuracy f
- **Direction:** Carry a credential in the request (the `reserved[8]` bytes were reserved for exactly this) and serve the reply under that user's `FsRole`, never SYSTEM. Failing that, add an `espnowFsServe` opt-in defaulting off plus a first-read on-device confirmation, mirroring the confirmation that inbound FILE_* writes already require.

### [HIGH] Mesh passphrase and its unsalted SHA-256 derivative are held in PSRAM for the whole boot (gEspNow)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9572`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:9890 (gEspNow->passphrase = bootPw), :753-756 (setEspNowPassphrase), :720-737 (deriveKeyFromPassphrase), System_ESPNow.h:681-682 (String passphrase; uint8_t derivedKey[16]), System_ESPNow.h:114 (devices[].key[16])
- **What:** gEspNow is allocated with ps_alloc(sizeof(EspNowState), AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "espnow.state"), which resolves to heap_caps_malloc(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM) (System_MemUtil.h ps_alloc tagged overload). EspNowState embeds `String passphrase` and `uint8_t derivedKey[16]`, plus 16 copies of derivedKey in devices[].key[16]. At every boot initEspNow does `gEspNow->passphrase = bootPw;` with the raw mesh passphrase read out of settings, then derivedKey = SHA256(passphrase + ":ESP-NOW-SHARED-KEY")[0..15] — one iteration, no salt, no stretching.
- **Why it matters:** Flash encryption is off on this hardware and PSRAM is an external, physically probeable device, which is exactly why the project rule says secrets never go there. An attacker with the board in hand reads a 16-byte unsalted SHA-256 prefix of the mesh passphrase and dictionary-attacks it offline at full speed; recovering the passphrase yields the PBKDF2 stretched key, hence the bootstrap key (forge KEY_EX, join the mesh) and the group key (forge every BROADCAST_AUTH frame fleet-wide). Arduino String uses SSO up to ~15 chars, so an 8-15 character passphrase — the minimum enforced by cmd_espnow_setpassphrase is 8 — sits literally in the PSRAM struct as plaintext characters. deinitEspNow deliberately never frees gEspNow, so the material stays resident even after `closeespnow`. Note derivedKey is dead key material: addEspNowPeerWithEncryption ignores it and sets peerInfo.encrypt=false unconditionally (:913-936); its only surviving consumer is the 4-byte match fingerprint printed by espnowencstatus/espnowkeyinfo.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any board with PSRAM; FeatherS3 + XIAO)
- **Direction:** Move `passphrase` and `derivedKey` out of the PSRAM-allocated EspNowState into internal-DRAM statics, the way gMeshDerivedKeys and gIdentity already are. Better: delete derivedKey entirely (nothing uses it for crypto any more) and source the "same passphrase?" fingerprint from the existing PBKDF2 passphraseStretchedKey.

### [HIGH] BROADCAST_AUTH frames have no replay protection; a replayed master heartbeat suppresses backup-master failover indefinitely
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5175`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:3045-3062 (v4h_heartbeat master-liveness / demote, gated on ctx.isAuthenticated); :8673 (master sends HEARTBEAT via v4_broadcast_category, which ORs BROADCAST_AUTH at :2021); :1521-1538 (v4_dedup_seen_and_insert, 64 entries, 5 s TTL); System_Settings.h:213 (meshFailoverTimeout default 20000 ms)
- **What:** The docs' claim is verified: BROADCAST_AUTH is authentication only — an HMAC-SHA256 tag over (header[0..29] || payload) keyed by the mesh group key, appended to a plaintext payload; RX sets wasAuthenticated=true and leaves wasSessionEncrypted=false. What it does NOT have is any freshness input: no nonce, no counter, no timestamp. The group key is a Blake2b subkey of the PBKDF2-stretched passphrase, so it is identical across reboots and stable until the passphrase changes. The only replay bound in the whole path is v4_dedup_seen_and_insert, a 64-entry ring keyed on (origin, msgId, fragIndex) with V4_DEDUP_TTL_MS = 5000.
- **Why it matters:** Retransmitting an identical captured frame every 6 seconds always passes dedup and always passes the HMAC. The concrete target is the backup-master failover logic: the comment at :3036-3044 says the ctx.isAuthenticated gate was added on 2026-05-19 specifically because "an attacker who knew the master's MAC could send plaintext unicast heartbeats and either (a) keep the backup from promoting (DoS) or (b) demote a legitimately-promoted backup." That gate stops forgery, not replay. Capture one BROADCAST_AUTH HEARTBEAT that the master sent to the backup, then retransmit those exact bytes (dest = backup, src = master, unchanged) every ~6 s: gLastMasterHeartbeat is refreshed at :3050 forever, the 20 s meshFailoverTimeout at :8711 never elapses, and the backup never promotes even after the master is physically destroyed. The same replay drives the demotion branch at :3053-3058, forcing an already-promoted backup back to MESH_ROLE_BACKUP_MASTER. All of this requires only passive capture — no passphrase, no pairing, no keys.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Add a monotonically increasing counter (or coarse epoch) to the BROADCAST_AUTH-covered region and keep a per-origin high-water mark on RX, rejecting anything not strictly newer. The 5 s dedup ring is a duplicate-suppression mechanism, not a replay defense, and should not be relied on as one.

### [HIGH] removeEspNowDevice() compacts gEspNow->devices[] from core 1 (OLED unpair) while httpd and cmd_exec iterate the same array and its Arduino String names
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8234`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_ESPNow.cpp:1360; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:13110; readers at System_ESPNow.cpp:8204-8221 and cmd_espnow_list; httpd entry at components/hardwareone/WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:589
- **What:** removeEspNowDevice() shifts the array down with `gEspNow->devices[j] = gEspNow->devices[j + 1];` and then does `gEspNow->deviceCount--`. EspNowDevice contains an Arduino `String name` (System_ESPNow.h:686), so each of those element assignments is a String copy-assignment: it frees the destination's existing heap buffer and reallocates. Nothing anywhere takes a lock around gEspNow->devices[] or deviceCount.
- **Why it matters:** The writer runs on two different cores. oledEspNowUnpairDevice() (OLED_ESPNow.cpp:1360-1363) calls it from OLED input handling on loopTask, core 1; cmd_espnow_unpair (System_ESPNow.cpp:13110) calls it from cmd_exec_task, core 0. The readers include cmd_espnow_list, which does `d["name"] = gEspNow->devices[i].name;` inside `for (i = 0; i < gEspNow->deviceCount; i++)`, and the mesh-status peer walk at :8204-8221 which reads `gEspNow->devices[di].name.c_str()`. Both of those are reachable from the unpinned httpd task, because GET /api/espnow/status runs `espnowlist` and `espnowmeshstatus` inline (WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:574-590 — see the separate gDebugBuffer finding for that mechanism). So: leave the ESP-NOW page open in a browser, then unpair a device from the OLED. Core 1 frees a String's heap buffer inside the shift while core 0 (or the other core) is reading .name.c_str() out of the same slot — use-after-free on a heap buffer whose contents are then serialized into the HTTP response. Even without the free, a reader crossing the shift sees a torn table: the same peer listed twice, or a MAC from slot j paired with a name from slot j+1, because the count is decremented only after all the element moves land.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW; the OLED-side (core-1) writer additionally needs a display build — both true on FeatherS3
- **Direction:** gEspNow->devices[] is the authoritative peer registry and has no owner discipline at all. The minimum is a mutex held across both the compaction and every full-table walk; the cheaper structural fix is to make removal a tombstone (clear the slot in place, no shift) so readers never observe elements moving under them, and to retire the count before mutating slots.

### [HIGH] saveEspNowDevices()/saveMeshPeers() truncate the live registry in place, discard ~20 write returns each, and return true when every write failed
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:536`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:470-532 (saveMeshPeers, identical shape)
- **What:** Both writers open the destination with mode "w" — no temp+rename, unlike every other persist path in the file — and then emit the entire JSON through ~20 f.print()/f.println() calls whose returns are all discarded (System_ESPNow.cpp:484-526 and 545-599). The final statement is `return skipped == 0;`, where `skipped` counts only encryptString() failures. There is no write-side success signal at all.
- **Why it matters:** The destination (/system/espnow/devices.json, /system/espnow/mesh_peers.json) is destroyed at fopen. On a full or failing filesystem every subsequent print is a no-op, the file is left as a fragment like `{\n  "devices": [\n`, and the function returns true. Callers that do check — cmd espnow pair at System_ESPNow.cpp:11200-11213 — then print "Unencrypted device paired successfully" and post SYSEVT_PEER_PAIRED. At the next boot loadEspNowDevices() parses a fragment and the entire paired-device registry (names, meshId, and the 16-byte at-rest-encrypted derived key material per device) is gone; the whole mesh must be re-paired. The other 11 call sites (7492, 9963, 9972, 11424, 11432, 11488, 12314, 13134-13135, 14028-14029) do not even look at the return.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (NETWORK_FEATURE_LEVEL 4 → ENABLE_ESPNOW 1 for every board in System_BuildConfig.h)
- **Direction:** Convert both to the temp+rename+verify pattern already used by writeIdentityFile() in System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp, and fold write failure into the returned bool so the 'paired successfully' message stops lying.

### [HIGH] Mesh passphrase and PBKDF2 stretched key are the only ESP-NOW key material with no damaged-load preservation guard, and no gSettingsLoadedOk gate
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:1376`
- **Also:** System_Settings.cpp:588-597 putSecretPreserving; System_Settings.cpp:960 gSettingsLoadedOk gate that meshes lacks
- **What:** `espnowMeshesWriteJson` starts with `doc["espnow"]["meshes"].to<JsonArray>()`, which CLEARS the merge-read's array, then adds fresh JsonObjects and calls `putSecret(e, "passphrase", ...)` (:1389) and `putSecret(e, "passphraseStretchedKey", ...)` (:1408). `putSecret` is `putSecretPreserving(obj, key, plaintext, obj[key])` (System_Settings.cpp:599-601), so on a brand-new object prevBlob is always "" and the `prevIsBlob && gSecretLoadFailures > 0` preservation branch (:590) can never fire. The WiFi-networks writer immediately below it does the opposite: it explicitly captures oldSsid/oldBlob BEFORE `.to<JsonArray>()` (System_Settings.cpp:962-976) and is additionally gated on `gSettingsLoadedOk`. The meshes block has neither protection. The comment at :1398-1404 correctly states the stretched key IS the key material — anyone holding it derives the group key without the passphrase.
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete paths. (1) Wrong device-key epoch: `selectDeviceKeyEpoch` picks the best candidate but if none opens the blobs, gSecretLoadFailures is incremented and the mesh passphrase decrypts to "". The next `setSetting()` anywhere in the firmware calls writeSettingsJson, which rebuilds the meshes array from empty RAM and writes `""` over the still-recoverable AES blob. Registered scalar secrets (mqtt password, blesecret) survive this because putSecret sees their on-disk blob in the merged doc; the mesh key does not. (2) settings.json parse failure at boot: readSettingsJson returns false at System_Settings.cpp:1352, RAM meshes stay at Settings::MeshIdentity() defaults, gSecretLoadFailures stays 0 (it is only set inside the load path), and the merge-read inside writeSettingsJson fails the same way → `doc.clear()` (:1027) → the meshes array is written EMPTY (the `if (!m.enabled && m.label.length()==0) continue` skip drops every slot). The mesh passphrase is then permanently gone from the device and the operator must retype it on every node.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Mirror the WiFi-array pattern exactly: capture the merge-read's per-mesh `passphrase`/`passphraseStretchedKey` blobs (keyed by label) before `.to<JsonArray>()`, pass them as prevBlob to putSecretPreserving, and gate the whole meshes rebuild on gSettingsLoadedOk so a save after a failed load cannot wipe the array.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW channel 12/13 is accepted everywhere but is physically unreachable under the default country code — the radio stays on another channel while every surface reports the pinned value
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:988`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11330 (CLI accepts 1..13), :16049 (settings registry max=13), :11312 (`espnowchannel resync` same path). The OLED (OLED_ESPNow.cpp:2085-2091) and G2 (G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:966-978) ladders only offer Auto/1/6/11 and are safe.
- **What:** espnowComputeChannel() (953-969) accepts a pinned channel of 1..13 and espnowApplyChannel() (978-990) then does three things: sets gEspNow->channel = ch unconditionally, writes ap_config.ap.channel = ch via esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_AP, ...), and calls esp_wifi_set_channel(ch, WIFI_SECOND_CHAN_NONE). Both esp_err_t returns are discarded. The CLI validator (11330) and the settings registry entry (16049, max 13) both permit 12 and 13. The project never calls esp_wifi_set_country / esp_wifi_set_country_code anywhere (verified by grep across components/ and main/), so IDF's default applies: {.cc="01", .schan=1, .nchan=11, .policy=WIFI_COUNTRY_POLICY_AUTO} — channels 1-11 only (esp_wifi.h:814, docs/en/api-reference wifi.rst:1607-1614).
- **Why it matters:** User runs `espnowchannel 12` on both off-grid devices (the documented pairing procedure — the command's own text says "Set the SAME value on both devices"). esp_wifi_set_channel(12) is out of the country range and does not take effect; the softAP write is silently coerced by the driver ("Channel of AP; if the channel is out of range, the Wi-Fi driver defaults to channel 1" — IDF wifi.rst:1391). The radio ends up on channel 1 while gEspNow->channel says 12, so `espnowchannel` prints "ESP-NOW channel preference set to 12 (radio now 12)" (11349-11354) — a direct falsehood. Worse, the drift self-heal at 8465-8477 fires because gSettings.espnowChannel is in 1..13 and the STA is unassociated: hwCh(1) != want(12) on every 15 s tick, so every 30 s it calls espnowApplyChannel(12) (which fails again) and emits logSystemEvent("ESPNOW", "channel drift corrected: radio 1 -> pinned 12"). logSystemEvent tees every [EVENT] line durably to LOG_EVENTS_FILE (System_Debug.cpp:803-833), so this writes a false "corrected" record to flash every 30 seconds, forever, and never corrects anything.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Clamp the accepted range to the live country info (esp_wifi_get_country → schan..schan+nchan-1) rather than a hardcoded 13, and check the esp_wifi_set_channel / esp_wifi_set_config return before updating gEspNow->channel so the reported channel can never diverge from the radio. Consider calling esp_wifi_set_country_code() explicitly if 12/13 are genuinely wanted in-region.

### [HIGH] ESP-NOW "auto" channel is derived from the live radio channel while unassociated, so any transient excursion is latched permanently as the new mesh home
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:968`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8465 (drift self-heal gated off in auto mode); components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:604-662 (cmd_wifiscan has no ESP-NOW awareness).
- **What:** When the STA is not associated and gSettings.espnowChannel == 0 (the shipped default), espnowComputeChannel() returns `staCh`, which falls through to `WiFi.channel()` — i.e. esp_wifi_get_channel(), the current live radio channel (959-960, 968). connectWiFiIndex memsets wifi_config and never sets sta.channel, so esp_wifi_get_config(WIFI_IF_STA).sta.channel is 0 and the live-radio read is the actual path. processMeshHeartbeats (8444-8455) then compares that to gEspNow->channel and, on any difference, calls espnowApplyChannel(want) and logs "channel re-synced N -> M (pinned offline)". The drift self-heal that would pull the radio back to a stable home is explicitly disabled in auto mode (8465 requires espnowChannel >= 1).
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete failures. (a) Device A joins a channel-6 home AP; ESP-NOW follows to 6. Device B never joins WiFi and sits on the auto fallback, channel 1 (968). Taken out of range, A keeps 6 as its "last-known" and B keeps 1, so the two devices never hear each other — and `espnowchannel` on both prints "OK — radio matches expected" (11283-11285), because each is self-consistent. (b) A `wifiscan` (System_WiFi.cpp:615) hops all channels and neither re-pins the ESP-NOW channel nor calls espnowNoteWifiChannelMayHaveChanged; a failed connect attempt likewise leaves the radio wherever the driver last parked it. espnow_task ticks every 10 ms (9394-9399), so the very next tick reads that transient channel, adopts it as `want`, pins the radio there and logs it as a successful re-sync. There is no path back: auto mode has no fixed target to defend.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** In auto mode, latch a stable home channel once (at init) and defend it, rather than re-reading the live radio channel on every tick — or restrict the auto fallback to reading conf.sta.channel of a network we actually intend to join, never esp_wifi_get_channel(). Also extend the drift self-heal to auto mode against that latched value.

### [HIGH] espnow roomcmd/tagcmd count every peer as "Sent" — the send return value is discarded entirely, and the payload is silently truncated
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11876`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11939
- **What:** Both handlers call `v4_send_encrypted_or_queue(..., nullptr, 0)` as a bare statement and then `sent++` unconditionally (:11876-11881 for roomcmd, :11939-11944 for tagcmd). The bool AND the outStatus parameter — which exists precisely to distinguish "sent" from "queued" from an error — are both thrown away. The helper returns false on invalid args, payload > 202 bytes, and a full pending-frame ring (System_ESPNow.cpp:1825/:1829/:1838/:1871/:1880). Separately, the payload is built with `snprintf(roomCmdPayload, sizeof(roomCmdPayload), "%s:%s:%s", user, pass, command)` into a 202-byte buffer with no truncation check.
- **Why it matters:** With the pending-frame ring full (peers unreachable), every send returns false yet the command still reports "Sent '<cmd>' to 5 device(s) in kitchen" and the audit log records `-> OK`. Combined with the MQTT bridge above, a Home Assistant call gets `{"ok":true,...,"result":"Sent ... to 5 device(s)"}` for zero frames accepted. The truncation is worse than a lost command: `espnowroomcmd lab u p filedelete /system/users/users.json confirm` with a long user/pass silently ships a prefix of the command line to every peer in the room, which the peer then executes.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Capture the bool (and the outStatus string) per peer, report accepted vs failed counts separately, and reject rather than truncate when the composed user:pass:command exceeds ESPNOW_V4_MAX_PLAINTEXT.

### [HIGH] Nothing reserves free space for state writes: an ESP-NOW peer may push a declared 4 MB file with no free-space check, and web upload's ceiling is every remaining byte
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:247`
- **What:** fileSlotsOpen validates only fileSize > kFileSlotMaxStreamSize (4 MB, System_ESPNow_Files.h:93) and then allocates the slot (:382-470) with no comparison against LittleFS free space, even though the declared size is known up front. The streaming path writes into /espnow/received/.part-strm-* for the whole transfer. On the web side, handleFileUpload sets freeLimit = freeBytes (WebServer_Server.cpp:2005) — the entire free space, with zero reserve — and only aborts once totalWritten exceeds it. Meanwhile System_VFS.cpp:529-533 declares a 100 KB floor whose comment says the headroom 'is preserved for NON-log writes (settings saves, automations.json edits, user sessions, image captures)', but resolveOverflowPath is consulted only by the append-only log writers; upload, ESP-NOW receive, maps, tracks and file-manager writes never call it, so nothing actually enforces the reserve.
- **Why it matters:** Three sequential 4 MB ESP-NOW transfers, or one large web upload, drive the 10604 KB LittleFS partition to ~0 free. Because the streaming transfer holds the .part open for minutes (measured throughput ~9 KB/s), free space sits near zero for the whole window. Any settings.json, users.json or automations.json write during that window hits ENOSPC and — per the preceding finding — cannot detect it, so it renames a truncated file over the good one. On the board-gated no_sr_8mb layout LittleFS is only 2796 KB, i.e. smaller than a single permitted 4 MB transfer, so the very first oversized push exhausts the partition.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all; worst on no_sr_8mb and sr_8mb (2796 KB / 128 KB LittleFS)
- **Direction:** Reject FILE_START in fileSlotsOpen when fileSize + a state-file reserve exceeds VFS::getCachedLittleFsFree(), and subtract the same reserve from freeLimit in handleFileUpload. Either make resolveOverflowPath's reserve a real global admission gate consulted by every bulk writer, or correct the comment that claims it already is.

### [HIGH] Remote peer-filesystem browse/pull is implemented twice; the OLED/web copy drops the per-user role check that the CLI copy enforces, letting a plain local user read any file on a paired peer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:784 (FS_STAT), :842 and :896 (FS_GET ack + send); opcode rows System_ESPNow.cpp:5007-5012; requesters OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:477 and :550, WebPage_Bond.cpp:1882/2005/2095; CLI counterpart cmd_espnow_browse System_ESPNow.cpp:14139 and its registration at :15931
- **What:** "Browse/read a file on a paired peer" exists in two independent implementations. (a) CLI `espnowbrowse` / `espnowfetch` (System_ESPNow.cpp:14139, registered requiresAdmin=true at :15931) packs `user:pass:files "<path>"` into a V4 CMD frame; the responder v4_handle_cmd (:5713) runs isValidUser against its own roster and executes `files` under THAT user's AuthContext, so the target's PathRule table (System_Filesystem.cpp:1337-1392) applies — a non-admin gets userPerms=0 on /system. Its help text says so verbatim: "Credentials are verified ON THE TARGET device, not this one." (b) The newer typed FS_LIST/FS_STAT/FS_GET RPCs carry no credential at all. The responder wraps every access in SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE (FsList.cpp:644, 784, 842, 896), and FsRole::SYSTEM/SUPER are explicitly exempt from the path rules, the sensitive-extension guard and the image guard (isUnrestrictedRole, System_Filesystem.cpp:1545). The wire gate is only V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED|REQ_SESSION_ENC — no bond flag, no user identity. The requester-side gates are correspondingly weak: OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:477/550 reach fsListSendRequest/fsGetSendRequest from a plain A-button with no oledGuestBlocksMutate() and no admin check, and WebPage_Bond.cpp:2095 (handleBondFsGet) is guarded only by WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN, i.e. any authenticated non-guest session. The code comment at System_ESPNow.cpp:5001-5006 is honest about this ("any securely paired peer with a live session may enumerate/pull files (served under SYSTEM identity)"); the defect is that one surface enforces the role model and the other does not.
- **Why it matters:** Device A and B are securely paired with a live session. On A, a plain non-admin account `bob` (rank USER) logs into the OLED and opens ESP-NOW -> device detail -> File -> Receive. The peer list is populated by FS_LIST served under SYSTEM, so /system, /system/users and /system/certs are all enumerated with e.perms = PERM_ALL. bob selects /system/users/users.json and presses A: fsGetSendRequest -> FS_GET_REQ -> B's processGetDeferred opens it under SYSTEM and pumps it back over FILE_START/DATA/END. It lands at /espnow/received/<MAC>/users.json (System_ESPNow.cpp:4428, scope /espnow/received). The /espnow/ PathRule grants plain users PERM_READ|WRITE|DELETE and users.json trips neither hasSensitiveExtension nor isImageFile, so bob can now read B's full credential roster (every account's role plus the bcrypt/HASH: password blobs written by System_User.cpp:2758) from A's own file browser. The same pull works for B's /settings.json (WiFi/MQTT passwords) and, for an admin on A, /system/certs/*.key. Doing the identical thing through the CLI is impossible for bob twice over: `espnowbrowse` is requiresAdmin=true locally, and even an admin would have to supply a valid account ON B, whose non-admin role would be refused by B's PathRule table. Reachability check: guests are blocked (oledEspNowHandleInput fails closed at OLED_ESPNow.cpp:928, and the legacy non-ESP-NOW PEER source is behind #if ENABLE_BONDED_MODE), so the minimum attacker is a rank-USER local account or anyone with physical access when localDisplayRequireAuth is off (identity "AuthBypass" also resolves to FsRole::USER).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Responder: every ENABLE_ESPNOW build (incl. FeatherS3 default). OLED requester: ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY (live today). Web requester: additionally ENABLE_BONDED_MODE, which is hard-0 at System_BuildConfig.h:320, so the /api/bond/fs/* half is compiled out in the current tree.; prior art: Partially anticipated: docs/WEB_PAGE_API_MAP.md:718 names /api/bond/fs/* as a duplicate of /api/files/* but frames it as a consolidation opportunity only. docs/WEB_API_INVENTORY.md:141,1855 rates the 
- **Direction:** Propagate the requester's identity into the FS_* request (the reserved bytes are already earmarked for it in the FsList.cpp:641-643 comment) and resolve it on the responder instead of installing SYSTEM, or — if device-level trust is genuinely the intended model — gate the requester side to match the CLI copy (admin-only, and confine the servable subtree with the scoped VFS::systemAuth(scope, reason) overload that already exists at System_VFS.cpp:915).

### [HIGH] A replayed SESSION_OPEN tears down the victim's live encrypted session — no freshness check anywhere on the responder path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:746`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:998 (v4hSessionOpen — enqueues on size check alone); System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:63-79 (sessionAllocate reuse branch); System_ESPNow_Wire.h:458-461 (transcript definition); System_ESPNow.cpp:913-936 (peerInfo.encrypt=false, no esp_now_set_pmk anywhere)
- **What:** runDeferredSessionOpen validates only: header.origin == payload.initiatorMac, responderMac == our MAC, a stored PeerIdentity exists, and the Ed25519 signature over the 71-byte OPEN transcript. It then calls sessionAllocate(initiatorMac, meshId) unconditionally. Nothing records nonceA, nothing requires msg->sessionId to differ from a session already held for that peer, and the glare guard at :815-827 only fires when the existing slot is SESSION_ESTABLISHING — an ACTIVE session is silently replaced. The only anti-repeat mechanism upstream is the 5 s (origin,msgId,fragIndex) dedup ring (System_ESPNow.cpp:1514,1531), and msgId is a header field that no signature or tag covers on a HANDSHAKE frame (v4_send_frame stamps CRC16 over the payload only, :1697), so bumping msgId defeats dedup outright.
- **Why it matters:** Attacker with a passive receiver captures one plaintext SESSION_OPEN frame off the air (it is sent unencrypted by design — it bootstraps the encryption). Re-transmitting that exact 158-byte frame with any different msgId makes the responder B run sessionAllocate on its ACTIVE session with A: the reuse branch (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:68-79) zeroes aeadKeyTx/aeadKeyRx, txSeqNext, rxSeqHighWater and rxSeqBitmap. B then derives keys from a fresh ephemeral of its own plus the replayed ephPub_A and goes ACTIVE. A never sent this OPEN, so A's SESSION_CONFIRM handler drops B's reply ("no in-flight session", :920) and A keeps the old keys. Every subsequent frame in both directions fails AEAD open. All session-gated traffic — bond opcodes, FILE_*, FS_LIST/STAT/GET, SENSOR_ENVELOPE, remote CMD — is dead until a re-handshake, and per the next finding no re-handshake is triggered on a device nobody is typing `espnowsend` on. Repeating the replay every few seconds is a permanent, credential-free, radio-range denial of the entire encrypted ESP-NOW layer.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Bind the OPEN to something the responder chose or has not seen before: keep a small per-peer ring of recently-accepted nonceA values (or last-accepted sessionId + a monotonic OPEN counter) and drop a repeat, and/or refuse to replace a SESSION_ACTIVE slot from an inbound OPEN unless the existing session has already failed liveness. Cheapest correct fix is a challenge: responder replies with a nonce the initiator must sign, which also removes the amplification noted in PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT §3.4.

### [HIGH] A replayed SESSION_REKEY drives both peers into a non-converging rekey ping-pong; the anti-replay field the wire spec relies on is never actually compared
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1229`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:759-798 (sessionApplyRekeyedKeys clears rekeyInitiatedAtMs, which is what makes every round look like a fresh initiation); System_ESPNow_Wire.h:517
- **What:** System_ESPNow_Wire.h:517-522 states prevTxSeqAtRekey "binds the signature to a specific point in the existing session's transcript — prevents replay of a stale REKEY from a previous rekey round". runDeferredRekey builds the transcript from msg->prevTxSeqAtRekey and verifies the signature over it, but never compares that value against s->txSeqNext or against s->rekeyTxSeqAtInit. Nothing else rejects a repeat: the frame is plaintext FLAG_HANDSHAKE, its header msgId is unsigned, sessionId survives every rekey, and the 5 s dedup ring is the only other barrier.
- **Why it matters:** Attacker captures one REKEY frame from A→B and re-sends it (fresh msgId) at any later point in that session's life. B is ACTIVE with rekeyInitiatedAtMs == 0 (zeroed by sessionApplyRekeyedKeys, System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:787), so weHadOutstanding is false: B generates ephB1, sends its own REKEY, and derives DH(ephB1, replayed ephA0). A receives B's REKEY, is also ACTIVE with rekeyInitiatedAtMs == 0, so A takes the same responder path — generates ephA1, sends a REKEY, derives DH(ephA1, ephB1). B receives that, responder path again with ephB2, derives DH(ephB2, ephA1). Each side always ECDHs its newest ephemeral against the peer's previous one, so the keys never match and the exchange never terminates: an unbounded, self-sustaining storm of Ed25519 sign+verify (~3 KB libsodium stack each, deferred onto cmd_exec_task) plus two radio frames per round, with the data session permanently unusable. One captured frame, no credentials, no passphrase.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Make prevTxSeqAtRekey load-bearing: reject a REKEY whose prevTxSeqAtRekey is not >= the value seen on the last accepted REKEY for that session (and record it). Separately, cap consecutive responder-path rekeys per session per interval so a converge failure degrades instead of storming.

### [HIGH] `writeIdentityFile` accepts any non-zero serialize length — a truncated ESP-NOW identity is renamed over the good one, destroying the device's Ed25519 trust anchor
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:113`
- **Also:** Same weak `== 0` idiom: System_Settings.cpp:1077 and :1169 and :3126 (KNOWN — SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT §4.7), System_User.cpp:1327, WebServer_Server.cpp:963
- **What:** `writeIdentityFile` (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:70-126) does the right shape — tmp + rename, tmp removed on failure, rename return checked — but its only write check is `if (written == 0)` at :113, where `written = serializeJson(doc, f)` at :110. ArduinoJson 7's `CountingDecorator` (components/hardwareone_libs/ArduinoJson/src/ArduinoJson/Serialization/CountingDecorator.hpp) accumulates whatever `Print::write` returns and never aborts, so a short write yields a non-zero count and passes.
- **Why it matters:** This file holds `ed25519_secret_encrypted` — the AES-wrapped 64-byte Ed25519 secret that is the device's mesh identity. A truncated write (full FS at `espnowregenidentity` or first-boot identity creation) is renamed over `/system/espnow/identity.json` and the function returns true. `readIdentityFile`'s own contract (documented at :129-132) is that presence-but-corrupt means "refuse to start, recovery via CLI" — so the next boot brings ESP-NOW up dead, and because the keypair was freshly generated in RAM and is now unrecoverable from disk, every peer that pinned this device's public key rejects it permanently. All bonds must be re-established by hand on every peer. Rare trigger, total and unrecoverable consequence.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Compare against `measureJson(doc)` rather than zero, and route through the checked-close helper from finding 1. Because this file is the mesh trust anchor and is written at most a handful of times in a device's life, it is also the best candidate for a read-back-and-parse verification of the tmp before the rename.

### [HIGH] The TX-driven session self-heal is dead for every path except a human typing `espnowsend`
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:672`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:548 (ESTABLISHING-only sweep); System_ESPNow.cpp:14601 (sole registrar)
- **What:** sessionNoteSendResult() is the only mechanism that drops a wedged-but-ACTIVE session so the next send re-handshakes. It is reachable only from sendStatusMarkDelivered (:688) and sendStatusSweep (:712), and both only act on entries created by sendStatusRegister(). A repo-wide grep finds exactly one caller of sendStatusRegister: System_ESPNow.cpp:14601, inside cmd_espnow_send (user-initiated TEXT). Bond heartbeats, bondSendEncryptedAsync, FILE_*, FS_LIST/STAT/GET, SENSOR_ENVELOPE and v4_send_payload_smart never register, so their no-ACK timeouts never reach sessionNoteSendResult.
- **Why it matters:** After a key desync from either of the two findings above — or from an ordinary lost SESSION_CONFIRM/REKEY under RF load — a headless node (the exact deployment bond mode targets) has no recovery path. sessionEstablishingTimeoutSweep (:548) only sweeps SESSION_ESTABLISHING, and a desynced session is SESSION_ACTIVE, so it is never touched. The session stays ACTIVE-but-undecryptable until reboot, and the comment block at :666-671 claims the opposite ("the encrypt-or-queue send path re-handshakes on the next send").
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Drive the timeout counter from the actual send result rather than the UI-facing SendStatus table — e.g. have the espnow_tx clerk call sessionNoteSendResult() on every unicast completion callback — or register every session-wrapped send. Either way the fix belongs below the CLI, not in cmd_espnow_send.

### [HIGH] AEAD session keys, the in-flight X25519 ephemeral private key, the bond super-admin token and decrypted command credentials all live in PSRAM
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:26`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:414 (gPending ring, also PreferPSRAM); System_ESPNow_Sessions.h:65-97 (aeadKeyTx/Rx/RxPrev, rekeyEphPrivKey, bondToken); System_ESPNow.cpp:11868+11874 and :11936+11938 (roomcmd/tagcmd build "user:pass:command" then v4_send_encrypted_or_queue); System_Utils.cpp:4638-4661 ("@BOND:<token>:command" then v4_send_encrypted_or_queue)
- **What:** Verified, not inferred: gSessions is heap_caps_malloc(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM) via ps_alloc(..., AllocPref::PreferPSRAM) (System_MemUtil.h ps_alloc → heap_caps_malloc with MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM, so ALWAYSINTERNAL does not apply), 16 x 208 B, and SessionState inlines aeadKeyTx[32], aeadKeyRx[32], aeadKeyRxPrev[32], rekeyEphPrivKey[32] and bondToken[16].
