Three sites the prior docs do not list: (a) during the handshake the initiator parks its raw X25519 ephemeral PRIVATE key in s->aeadKeyRx and nonceA in s->aeadKeyTx (Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1078-1085) — a live private key in PSRAM for the whole OPEN→CONFIRM window; (b) the shared AEAD decrypt scratch is `EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR static uint8_t plainBuf[218]` (System_ESPNow.cpp:5250), so every decrypted frame — including `user:password:command` — lands in PSRAM cleartext; (c) gEspNow is ps_alloc PreferPSRAM (System_ESPNow.cpp:9572) and carries char deferredCmdPayload[256] (System_ESPNow.h:900) holding the decrypted `@BOND:<token>:cmd` or `user:pass:cmd` until cmd_exec picks it up.
- **Why it matters:** Same physical-probe threat as the finding above, but the material is directly usable rather than needing a crack: aeadKeyRx decrypts captured traffic for that session, and bondToken is the ESP-NOW remote-exec credential — v4_handle_cmd stamps kBondAdminUser (Super Admin) on any command carrying a matching token (System_ESPNow.cpp:5771-5780). The pending ring is the sharper edge on the credential side: espnowroomcmd/espnowtagcmd memcpy a literal `user:pass:command` string, and the `remote:`/`@` bond path memcpys `@BOND:<32-hex token>:command`, into PSRAM whenever no session is up yet. That window is bounded (drain or the 5 s timeout sweep, both of which sodium_memzero the slot at Sessions.cpp:506 and :529), but the session keys themselves persist for up to a full rekey interval (1 hour).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: all (any board with PSRAM; FeatherS3 quad confirmed); prior art: KNOWN — PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.1 (gSessions + gPending + bondToken); LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md §1/:132; HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md:43 marked it "🔒 Unverified". Status change: now CONFIRMED, and the 
- **Direction:** Change the ps_alloc at :26 to AllocPref::PreferInternal, or better heap_caps_calloc(..., MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL | MALLOC_CAP_8BIT) so the requirement is stated rather than inherited (the pattern already used at System_Automation.cpp:3716 and OLED_Utils.cpp:2427). 3.3 KB internal is cheap for the whole session table.

### [HIGH] Post-REKEY prev-key window accepts replayed old-key frames and one replay can wedge the session
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:759`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:329-381 (sessionUnwrapFrame prev-key fallback then replay check), :203-231 (replayCheckAndInsert), :63-79 (sessionAllocate reuse branch, same shape); System_ESPNow.cpp:8566-8596 (auto-rekey trigger)
- **What:** sessionApplyRekeyedKeys, inside one critical section, sets aeadKeyRxPrev = current RX key and prevKeysValidUntilMs = now + 5000, and in the same block resets rxSeqHighWater = 0 and rxSeqBitmap = 0. sessionUnwrapFrame tries the current key, falls back to aeadKeyRxPrev while the window is open, and only then calls replayCheckAndInsert — which, seeing rxSeqHighWater == 0, takes the "First frame on this session" branch and accepts unconditionally.
- **Why it matters:** For the 5 seconds after every rekey, any SESSION_FRAME previously captured from that peer decrypts (prev key still installed) and is accepted (window reset to zero). That is a straight replay of authenticated, confidential application traffic — a replayed FILE_DATA chunk, SUBSCRIBE_UPDATE, or sensor envelope is processed as genuine. Worse, the accepted frame seeds rxSeqHighWater with its captured frameSeq, which can be up to the pre-rekey threshold of 10000, while the new key epoch restarts at seq 1: every subsequent genuine frame then falls below the 64-frame window and is rejected as "too old", wedging the receive direction until the TX-side no-ACK self-heal tears the session down. Rekey fires automatically at 10k frames or 1 hour per session (System_ESPNow.cpp:8572-8573), so the window recurs at least hourly and an attacker simply replays continuously to catch it. The sessionAllocate reuse branch has the same shape — it resets the window but deliberately leaves aeadKeyRxPrev and prevKeysValidUntilMs untouched — so a re-handshake opens the identical hole.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §14 item 6 and System_ESPNow_Sessions.h:118-131 both note that the retained prev-RX key is a "sharp edge" on the sessionAllocate reuse path — neither states the replay-win
- **Direction:** Whenever the replay window is reset, zero aeadKeyRxPrev and prevKeysValidUntilMs in the same operation — or, if the prev-key grace period must be kept, give it its own separate high-water/bitmap so old-epoch frames are replay-checked against the old epoch's window rather than the freshly-zeroed new one.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW USER_SYNC writes the target username into users.json without isValidPublicUsername, so a remote admin can plant reserved identity names
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3658`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:786,803
- **What:** The USER_SYNC handler validates the frame well — session encryption required (:3549), espnowUserSyncEnabled required (:3557), isValidUser against the LOCAL store (:3596), isAdminUser required (:3603) — and correctly forces newUser["role"] = "user" regardless of the sender's claim (:3663, with a SECURITY comment explaining why). But newUser["username"] = targetUser at :3658 is written raw. isValidPublicUsername (System_User.cpp:786) is never called on this path, so its kReserved list ("system", "AuthBypass", kBondAdminUser at :803) and its character allowlist are both skipped.
- **Why it matters:** A peer admin can create a users.json row named `AuthBypass` (or `system`, or `bond-admin`) with a password of their choosing. The forced role="user" bounds the damage — isAdminUser finds an explicit non-admin role and returns false — but the sentinel now has credentials and is loginable on every transport, permanently corrupting the audit distinction between "a physical user with require-auth off" and "a real account". It also allows usernames containing spaces or quotes, which the JSON string-scanning parsers in isAdminUser (:305-341) and isSuperAdminUser (:389-411) are not written to survive.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: Mentioned as an aside in memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 1 ("USER_SYNC can plant the name unvalidated too"); never verified in a doc.
- **Direction:** Call isValidPublicUsername(targetUser) right after the isAdminUser check at :3603 and reply with the existing v4_send_command_response failure shape on rejection. Same one-line addition the first-time-setup finding needs.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW USER_SYNC creates accounts without `isValidPublicUsername()`, so the reserved sentinel names `system`, `AuthBypass` and `bond-admin` can be planted in users.json
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3651`
- **Also:** Canonical implementation: adminCreateUser System_User.cpp:825-936 (validators at :833 and :836). The sync copy's own comment at System_ESPNow.cpp:3726-3730 already acknowledges it is a hand-rolled duplicate that had to be patched once for the identity-generation bump.
- **What:** doUserSyncWork (System_ESPNow.cpp:3540-3731) re-implements the users.json mutation instead of calling adminCreateUser. It replicates the duplicate-name check (via getUserIdByUsername at :3608), the atomic write, the settings-file write and the identity-generation bump, but it never calls isValidPublicUsername or isValidPublicPassword — grep for either symbol in System_ESPNow.cpp returns nothing. adminCreateUser calls both at :833/:836; isValidPublicUsername (System_User.cpp:786) enforces the [A-Za-z0-9._-] charset, the 64-char cap, and case-insensitively rejects the three reserved sentinels "system", "AuthBypass" and kBondAdminUser ("bond-admin"). isValidPublicPassword enforces the 6..64 length policy. The sync path also ignores the return value of saveUserSettings at :3706, where adminCreateUser checks it and fails the whole creation.
- **Why it matters:** An admin on peer A (whose credentials on B the sender must already hold — isValidUser + isAdminUser at :3594/:3601) sends target_user="bond-admin", target_pass="x". B creates the account with role "user". Both isAdminUser (System_User.cpp:288-293) and isSuperAdminUser (:363-368) short-circuit on `who == kBondAdminUser` and return isBondSessionTokenValid() BEFORE ever reading the roster, so as soon as B has any live bond session the account authenticates normally through isValidUser and then resolves to SUPER everywhere — authorizeCommand's super gate, hasSuperAdminPrivilege, and FsRole::SUPER (unrestricted filesystem). The "bond-admin is never a real users.json account" assumption that comment :290-292 calls defense-in-depth is exactly what the missing validator breaks. Secondary effects with the same root cause: a 1-character password is accepted (policy is 6), a username with spaces or quotes is accepted and lands in the same users.json that isAdminUser/isSuperAdminUser scan with hand-rolled substring matching, and a failed saveUserSettings leaves a passwordless roster entry.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: Any build with ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: Prior art: memory file project_security_review_backlog.md item 1 ("USER_SYNC can plant the name unvalidated too"); status unchanged, re-verified at f5aea65+working tree
- **Direction:** Have doUserSyncWork call adminCreateUser(targetUser, targetPass, false, "espnow:<device>", err, "user") instead of duplicating the write, or at minimum call isValidPublicUsername/isValidPublicPassword first and honour saveUserSettings' return. The reserved-name check in particular must not be reachable-around.

### [MEDIUM] v4h_topo_peer passes the wire's non-NUL-terminated char name[32] to snprintf %s and strlen — out-of-bounds read leaked into topology output
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3460`
- **Also:** Same untermination class, lower reach: System_ESPNow.cpp:4075 (`fs->filename` char[64] via `%s`, debug-gated); System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp reply paths — `hdr.path` (:300-301), `reply.path` (:510-511), `ack.path` (:531-532) are copied without forcing a terminator, and WebPage_Bond.cpp:1910 then calls `strlcpy(peerEchoPath, sWebFsBridge.hdr.path, 128)`, which strlen()s the source past the field. That one also emits `"path":"%s"` unescaped at :1926 while the adjacent entry-name loop at :1940 is correctly bounded by `sizeof(e.name)`.
- **What:** `v4h_topo_peer` checks only `ctx.payloadLen < sizeof(V4PayloadTopoPeer)` (:3411) and then uses `tp->name` — a bare `char name[32]` at the end of the packed wire struct (System_ESPNow_Wire.h:305) — as a C string: `snprintf(peerInfoBuf, sizeof(peerInfoBuf), "  -> %s (%s)...", peerNamePtr2, ...)` at :3460, `addTopoDeviceName(tp->mac, peerNamePtr2)` at :3457 which begins `strlen(name)` (:7609), and a `%s` DEBUGF at :3420. No NUL is ever forced into the field. The sibling receive path in System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp gets this right — `captureDeferred` explicitly writes `path[sizeof(path)-1] = '\0'` after every memcpy (:443, :450, :457).
- **Why it matters:** Both TOPO_START and TOPO_PEER are registered with flags `0` in kV4HandlerTable (:4983-4984) — no pairing, no authentication, no session encryption. The only gate is `tp->reqId == gTopoRequestId`, and `requestTopologyDiscovery` sends TOPO_REQ as a plaintext frame with `flags = 0` carrying `reqId` in the clear (:8311-8316), so any device in radio range sniffs the correlation id the moment an operator runs topology discovery (and `generateMessageId()` is a plain incrementing counter anyway). The attacker then sends a forged TOPO_START followed by a TOPO_PEER whose 46-byte payload fills name[0..31] with non-zero bytes. `%s` in newlib's vfprintf without a precision specifier calls `strlen` on the source, so the read runs off the 32-byte field, through the rest of the 250-byte `InboundRxItem::data` slot in `gEspNowRxRing` (stale bytes the attacker seeded with earlier frames) and into the following ring entry until a zero byte appears. Up to ~110 bytes of that adjacent RX-ring memory are then written into `stream->accumulatedData` and printed to the operator as the peer name, and into `gTopoDeviceCache`. Both the `%s` overread and the `strlen` in addTopoDeviceName are out-of-bounds accesses on a 32-byte object.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Copy `tp->name` into a local `char[33]` with a forced terminator (or use `%.32s`) before any use, and give the FsList reply structs the same `path[last] = '\0'` treatment `captureDeferred` already applies to requests.

### [MEDIUM] Fragment reassembly table is consumable by unauthenticated frames — 2 slots, 5 s timeout, no gate runs before allocation
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5331`
- **What:** `v4_reasm_find_or_alloc` is called at :5331, before `v4_dispatch_table_try` and therefore before any of REQ_PAIRED / REQ_AUTHENTICATED / REQ_SESSION_ENC is evaluated (:5061-5109). There are `V4_REASM_MAX = 2` slots (:143) and a slot is only released by `v4_reasm_gc` after `V4_REASM_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000` (:144, :174-186) or by successful completion. When both are occupied, every further fragment is dropped at :5332-5336.
- **Why it matters:** An attacker in range needs no credentials at all: send two frames with distinct (spoofed src, msgId) pairs, `fragCount = 2`, `fragIndex = 0`, any small payload with a correct CRC16 and `meshFingerprint = 0` (which the gate at :5153 explicitly permits). Both slots are now held for 5 seconds. Repeating every ~4 s permanently prevents this device from reassembling ANY multi-fragment message — which is every bonded `FS_LIST_REPLY` (13 fragments), every CMD_RESP longer than one frame, and every `v4_send_encrypted_chunked` payload. The remote file browser and remote CLI output go dark with no indication of an attack; the only symptom is the WARN at :5333.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Consider requiring `isPaired` (or a known meshFingerprint plus a known peer identity) before a fragment may claim a reassembly slot, and/or evict the least-recently-updated slot for a paired sender when the table is full so an unpaired flood cannot starve real peers.

### [MEDIUM] Unauthenticated ESP-NOW USER_SYNC frames allocate attacker-sized PSRAM and enqueue onto the shared 6-deep cmd_exec queue before any auth check
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3740`
- **Also:** Sibling sites System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:998 (v4hSessionOpen) and :1017 (v4hSessionConfirm) — both, unlike USER_SYNC, validate `payloadLen == sizeof(struct)` first. Queue: HardwareOne.cpp:1502 `gCmdExecQ = xQueueCreate(6, ...)`. Enqueue helper: System_Utils.cpp:4925. Blocked consumers: System_Utils.cpp:4764 submitAndExecuteSync.
- **What:** `ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_USER_SYNC` is registered with flags `0` (System_ESPNow.cpp:4985), so the dispatcher requires no pairing, no authentication and no session encryption; a frame stamped `meshFingerprint = 0` also skips the mesh filter at :5155. `v4h_user_sync` then does `ps_alloc(sizeof(DeferredUserSyncWork) + ctx.payloadLen)` with **no upper bound on payloadLen**, copies the payload, and calls `submitDeferredToCmdExec`. Every credential check lives downstream in `doUserSyncWork` (:3549 session-encryption, :3557 enable flag, :3596 isValidUser, :3603 isAdminUser) — i.e. after the allocation and after the queue slot has been consumed. The rejecting branch at :3554 still transmits `v4_send_ack` before returning.
- **Why it matters:** `gCmdExecQ` is only 6 entries deep and is the *shared* serialization point for every command surface — serial, /api/cli, /api/cli/batch, MQTT, OLED, G2, voice, automations, and the BLE secure-channel handshake all funnel through it. An attacker in radio range with no credentials, no pairing and no key material can transmit USER_SYNC frames at frame rate; each one burns a queue slot and a PSRAM allocation of up to ~4.3 KB (payloadLen is post-reassembly, bounded only by V4_REASM_MAX_MSG = 4200). `submitAndExecuteSync` gives up after a 2 s enqueue timeout, so the whole command plane — including the operator's own web and serial CLI — fails while the flood runs. Each frame also forces a radio ACK back out, so the victim amplifies the attack. Nothing rate-limits per source MAC.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: Same class as docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.4 ("v4hSessionOpen does ps_alloc + enqueue before any identity check — an unauthenticated amplification vector"). This is a third, less bounded site, a
- **Direction:** Move the two cheap, purely-local gates in front of the allocation: reject in `v4h_user_sync` when `!ctx.isSessionEncrypted` or `!gSettings.espnowUserSyncEnabled`, and bound `payloadLen` to the largest legitimate sync document. A per-source-MAC token bucket in front of the flag-0 dispatch rows would cover this whole family at once.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW reassembly length is computed from the last-arriving fragment, not the last-indexed one — attacker-ordered fragments over-report size and read past the reassembly buffer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5412`
- **Also:** Consumers of the over-reported length: v4h_cmd_resp System_ESPNow.cpp:2919 (flags 0, so unauthenticated) and v4h_user_sync :3740. Buffer definition: :158 `uint8_t buffer[V4_FRAG_MAX * V4_MAX_FRAGMENT_PAYLOAD]` = 4200 B; bufferSize set at :203.
- **What:** On completion the loop assumes the final *index* is also the final *arrival*: it adds `V4_MAX_FRAGMENT_PAYLOAD` (200) for every fragment except one, and adds the local `payloadLen` — the length of whichever fragment happened to complete the set — for the last. Fragment order is entirely under the sender's control, and `payloadLen` for a plaintext frame can be up to `250 - sizeof(EspNowV4Header)` = 218, not 200. The per-fragment store is correctly bounds-checked at :5361 (`offset + payloadLen > e->bufferSize`), so nothing overflows on the way in; only the computed total is wrong.
- **Why it matters:** An attacker sends 21 fragments of a flag-0 opcode with `msgId` fixed, delivering index 19 *last* with a 218-byte payload (offset 3800 + 218 = 4018, so the store check passes). `reassembledSize` becomes 20*200 + 218 = 4218 against a 4200-byte buffer, and `payloadLen` is then set to 4218 with `payload = e->buffer`. `v4h_cmd_resp` clamps `resultLen` to 4200 and memcpys from `payload + 1`, reading 1 byte past the buffer; `v4h_user_sync` copies the full 4218 and reads 18 bytes past it into a fresh PSRAM allocation. `v4_reasm_reset` (:165) never clears `buffer`, so the trailing bytes are whatever the previous occupant of that slot left. The mirror case under-reports (last-arriving fragment shorter than the real tail), silently truncating a legitimate multi-fragment message that arrives out of order. Impact is a bounded adjacent-memory overread inside PSRAM rather than a controllable corruption — I could not build a disclosure path out of the 18 bytes — but the length is attacker-steerable and the buffer is uninitialised across reuses.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Record each fragment's own length in the reassembly entry (a `uint16_t lens[V4_FRAG_MAX]` alongside `have[]`) and sum those, or store the length of the fragment whose index equals `fragCount - 1` when that fragment arrives. Clamping the result to `e->bufferSize` before assigning `payloadLen` is a cheap belt-and-braces.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW link keys (gEspNow->derivedKey and 16 per-device keys) also live in PSRAM
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9572`
- **Also:** docs/HEAP_OFFLOAD_SWEEP_2026-07-24.md §4 excludes gOledEspNowState (2096 B) from PSRAM precisely because it holds a typed remote password — the same reasoning was never applied to gEspNow itself.
- **What:** gEspNow = ps_alloc(sizeof(EspNowState), AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "espnow.state") places the whole EspNowState in PSRAM. That struct inlines derivedKey[16] (System_ESPNow.h:682) — the passphrase-derived ESP-NOW LMK actually handed to esp_now_add_peer via addEspNowPeerWithEncryption (System_ESPNow.cpp:13994) — plus devices[16], whose EspNowDevice::key[16] (System_ESPNow.h:114, "Per-device encryption key") receives copies at :13988 and :14000.
- **Why it matters:** Same exposure class as gSessions: 17 live symmetric keys sitting in unencrypted, bus-probeable external RAM. The embedded `String passphrase` member is less exposed (only the String header is in PSRAM; its char buffer is a separate small malloc that lands in internal DRAM under ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384) — so it is specifically the raw key arrays that leak. Mitigating: derivedKey is reproducible from the passphrase, which is already stored in plaintext in settings.json on unencrypted LittleFS, so the marginal loss is smaller than for #3. It still violates the standing no-secrets-in-PSRAM rule and gives an attacker the keys without needing to parse the filesystem.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Either move the key arrays out of EspNowState into a small internal-DRAM side table keyed by device index, or make the EspNowState allocation PreferInternal. The former keeps the ~large device/unpaired arrays in PSRAM where the byte win actually is.

### [MEDIUM] `saveMeshPeers()` and `saveEspNowDevices()` write hand-rolled JSON into a truncate-in-place file with zero write-return checks, and report success based on encryption failures only
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:470`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:536 (saveEspNowDevices)
- **What:** `saveMeshPeers` (:470-531) opens `MESH_PEERS_FILE` with `"w"` and emits ~10 `file.print`/`file.println` calls, every return discarded, no `flush()`, `close()` void, then `return skipped == 0` — where `skipped` counts *encryption* failures, not I/O. `saveEspNowDevices` (:536-604) is the same shape over `ESPNOW_DEVICES_FILE`, with roughly 20 `f.print` calls per device. Neither uses a staging file.
- **Why it matters:** These are `"w"` truncate-in-place with no tmp+rename, so a short write does not just fail to update — it leaves the destination cut off mid-JSON-object, e.g. `{"mac":"AES:...` with no closing brace. The next boot's loader hits a `DeserializationError` and the whole file is discarded. For `devices.json` that means every paired peer's AES-encrypted link key (written at :578-582) is lost and all pairings must be redone by hand; for `mesh_peers.json` the entire topology is lost. Both functions returned true. Note the boot corrupt-check at System_Filesystem.cpp:172-189 does not cover either file — only settings.json, debug.json, automations.json and users.json are in `criticalFiles[]` — so there is not even a delete-and-warn.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Both should build into a String/JsonDocument and go through `writeTextAtomic` (once finding 2 is fixed) rather than streaming print calls straight into a truncated destination. As a bonus that also removes the unescaped `f.print(gEspNow->devices[i].name)` at :563 and the equivalent `peerName` write at :514, which currently let a peer-supplied name containing a quote corrupt the file by itself.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW channel 12/13 is accepted by every surface, cannot actually be applied under the default "01" country, is reported as applied, and makes the drift self-heal log a false correction forever
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:978`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11326-11332 (cmd_espnow_channel accepts 1..13); :16047 (SettingEntry max = 13); :966 and :979 (1..13 range checks); :9740-9761 (initEspNow parks the soft AP on the same value); :8465-8474 (drift self-heal); nothing anywhere calls esp_wifi_set_country / esp_wifi_set_country_code
- **What:** espnowApplyChannel() assigns `gEspNow->channel = ch` FIRST (:980), then — only when unassociated — writes ch into the soft-AP config (:984-986) and calls esp_wifi_set_channel(ch, WIFI_SECOND_CHAN_NONE) (:988). Both esp_err_t returns are discarded. The accepted range is 1..13 in the command handler, in the SettingEntry, and in the apply function. No code sets a country: the IDF default is "01" world-safe mode, {schan=1, nchan=11} (esp_wifi.h:814). Per IDF's own table for STA/AP-coexistence under WIFI_COUNTRY_POLICY_AUTO, when the station is NOT connected to an external AP the soft AP uses the configured country info — i.e. channels 1-11 only, which is precisely the unassociated branch this code runs in.
- **Why it matters:** A user sets `espnowchannel 12` (or 13) from CLI, web, OLED or the G2 page to get away from a crowded band. esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_AP) and esp_wifi_set_channel both reject the out-of-country channel; both returns are thrown away. The radio never moves. But gEspNow->channel was already set to 12 at :980, so espnowstatus, the JSON status doc (:10176), the G2 ESP-NOW page and the OLED stats row all report "Channel: 12", and cmd_espnow_channel's own success message prints "radio now 12" from the cached value. The peer never meets its partner and nothing says why. Second-order: with 12/13 pinned and the STA offline, the drift self-heal at :8465-8474 sees hwCh != want on every 15 s pass, accumulates two strikes, re-applies (no effect) and emits `logSystemEvent("ESPNOW", "channel drift corrected: radio 1 -> pinned 12")` roughly every 30 s for the life of the boot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Clamp the accepted range to 1..11 everywhere (handler, SettingEntry, applyChannel guard) unless a country is explicitly configured, check the esp_err_t from esp_wifi_set_channel/esp_wifi_set_config, and only update gEspNow->channel after a successful apply so the status surfaces cannot report a channel the radio is not on.

### [MEDIUM] initEspNow's esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE) races an unconditional Arduino re-arm of WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM, and the comment justifying it is contradicted by ESP-IDF
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9732`
- **Also:** components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/STA.cpp:112-116 (STA_START handler calls esp_wifi_set_ps(WiFi.getSleep())); components/arduino/libraries/WiFi/src/WiFiGeneric.cpp:379-381 (_sleepEnabled defaults to WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM on non-S2); components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1094 (the only other PS_NONE site, on successful connect); sdkconfig:1871 and sdkconfig.esp32:1487 (CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE=y)
- **What:** initEspNow calls WiFi.mode(WIFI_AP_STA) at :9727 and then esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE) at :9738. When the radio was OFF, WiFi.mode() reaches espWiFiStart() -> esp_wifi_start(), which queues WIFI_EVENT_STA_START to the `sys_evt` task (core 0, prio 20); Arduino bridges that to the `arduino_events` task (core 1, prio ESP_TASKD_EVENT_PRIO-1 = 19, NetworkEvents.cpp:75-84), whose _onStaArduinoEvent unconditionally calls esp_wifi_set_ps(WiFi.getSleep()). `WiFi.setSleep()` is called nowhere in components/hardwareone, so the cached value is the S3 default WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM. Nothing synchronises the two writes and nothing re-asserts PS afterwards. Separately, the comment at :9732-9737 claims MIN_MODEM "stays in effect on a device that never associates" — IDF states the opposite: "Modem-sleep mode works in station-only mode and the station must connect to the AP first... When station disconnects from AP, Modem-sleep will stop" (docs/en/api-guides/wifi.rst:1791,1797). The real disconnected-state sleep is CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE, which is =y on both targets and is not controlled by esp_wifi_set_ps at all.
- **Why it matters:** Paths that bring the radio up from OFF via ESP-NOW — `radiopower on` with gRadioPrevEspNow set (System_WiFi.cpp:545), `openespnow` after a `closewifi` that fully powered the radio down (System_WiFi.cpp:444), or boot with wifiAutoStart off and espnowAutoStart on (HardwareOne.cpp:2077-2094) — leave the final power-save state decided by a two-task race. If MIN_MODEM lands last and the STA later associates through the driver's own auto-reconnect (rather than through connectWiFiIndex, the only site that re-sets PS_NONE at System_WiFi.cpp:1094), modem-sleep engages and unicast ESP-NOW RX is only serviced around the AP's DTIM — intermittent, load-dependent mesh packet loss with no log line. The misleading comment additionally invites a future maintainer to "fix" ESP-NOW RX reliability with the wrong lever: IDF also notes that in coexist mode (always on here — BT enabled plus CONFIG_ESP_COEX_SW_COEXIST_ENABLE=y) Wi-Fi sleeps during non-Wi-Fi slices "even if esp_wifi_set_ps(WIFI_PS_NONE) is called".
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Route the intent through Arduino (WiFi.setSleep(WIFI_PS_NONE)) so the cached _sleepEnabled matches and the STA_START handler re-applies the same value, or re-assert PS_NONE from the GOT_IP/STA_CONNECTED handler in wifiEventLogger. Correct the comment to name CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE and the coex time-slicing as the actual mechanisms.

### [MEDIUM] saveEspNowDevices() aborts mid-JSON-object on a key-encryption failure, committing a syntactically corrupt devices file
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:577`
- **What:** The loop writes `{"mac":"...","name":"...","encrypted":true` (:560-565), and only THEN encrypts the device key (:573). If encryptString() returns empty it does `skipped++; continue;` (:575-577) — leaving the object unterminated and `count` not incremented, so the next iteration emits no separating comma and starts a fresh `    {"mac":"...` immediately after the dangling fragment. The file is then closed (:600) and the function returns `skipped == 0` (:602). Contrast the MAC-encryption failure at :554-558, which continues BEFORE any object bytes are written — that one is clean, as is the sibling saveMeshPeers() (:506-510).
- **Why it matters:** encryptString() returns "" on PSRAM allocation failure (System_Settings.cpp:365, :382, :409) or an mbedtls error (:400) — reachable under the memory pressure this firmware routinely runs at. The result is /espnow_devices.json containing malformed JSON that no loader can parse, so the whole paired-device list is lost on next boot. The function does return false, but it has already destroyed the file: this is the "swallows an error and continues with invalid state" pattern, and the false return arrives too late to help.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW=1 by default)
- **Direction:** Compute encMac AND encKey up front, before emitting any bytes for that device — mirroring the clean shape already used at :554 and in saveMeshPeers. Alternatively build into a buffer and only commit whole objects.

### [MEDIUM] 47 CLI report builders in System_ESPNow.cpp size their snprintfs against a hardcoded 1024 while getDebugBuffer() is 4096 — the mismatch is the only thing preventing an overflow
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11692`
- **Also:** System_Maps.cpp:2140/2145/2187/2197/2229/3368/3596; System_ImageManager.cpp:581-601; System_MQTT.cpp:1679/1684; BLE_Peers.cpp:644-665 (same shape with `cap = 1024` as a local).
- **What:** Report builders do `char* buf = getDebugBuffer(); int pos = 0; pos += snprintf(buf + pos, 1024 - pos, ...)`. `getDebugBuffer()` returns `gDebugBuffer`, allocated at `GLOBAL_DEBUG_BUFFER_SIZE = 4096` (System_Debug.h:272, System_Debug.cpp:603). `1024 - pos` is `int` arithmetic converted to `size_t` at the call, so once `pos` passes 1024 the size argument wraps to ~4.29e9 and every later snprintf in the chain is effectively unbounded. Writes still land inside the 4096-byte allocation only because the literal is 4x too small.
- **Why it matters:** In `cmd_espnow_devices` the loop breaks at `pos >= 900`, so a line can start at pos=899. Peer `friendlyName[48]` + `room[32]` + a 34-byte zone buffer — all remote-supplied via ESP-NOW METADATA — make one line ~130 bytes, pushing pos to ~1029. The `", %lus ago"` and `")\n"` appends and the trailing `"Total: %d devices"` then each run with a negative-turned-huge size at buf+1029..1060. Today those land in the unused 1024–4096 region; the moment anyone "fixes" the constant to match the buffer, or the buffer shrinks, or a longer field is added, the same code writes off the end. The class is latent-by-accident, and it is 47 sites in this file alone plus 7 in System_Maps.cpp, 7 in System_ImageManager.cpp, 2 in System_MQTT.cpp.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Replace the literal with `GLOBAL_DEBUG_BUFFER_SIZE` AND add the clamp at the same time — swapping the constant alone converts a latent bug into a live one, because the guards (`pos >= 900`) are calibrated to the 1024 literal. A small `appendf(buf, cap, pos, ...)` helper that clamps (the one at System_VFS.cpp:29 is already correct) would retire the whole class.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW TIME_SYNC is a no-op that reports success: the offset transmitted is the sender's uptime, `gTimeOffset` has no reader, and `gLastTimeSyncMs` is never written
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:12281`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:2390 (int32 param vs int64 wire field), :2831 (dead write), :12295 (read of never-written global), System_ESPNow_Wire.h:275-279 (payload struct).
- **What:** Four defects in one path. (1) `cmd_espnow_timesync` calls `v4_broadcast_time_sync(epoch, millisAtEpoch)` where `millisAtEpoch = millis()` — the SENDER's uptime. The receiver stores it as `gTimeOffset`, documented at :343 as "Offset to add to millis() to get epoch time (milliseconds)". Adding one board's uptime to another board's `millis()` produces neither an epoch nor an offset; the correct value is `(int64_t)epoch*1000 - millis()`. (2) The wire field `V4PayloadTimeSync::timeOffset` is `int64_t` (System_ESPNow_Wire.h:277) but `v4_broadcast_time_sync`'s parameter is `int32_t` (:2390) — a correct epoch-ms offset (~1.75e12) could not be passed even if computed. (3) `gTimeOffset` is written at :2831 and read nowhere: the only other reference is the DEBUGF at :2830 that prints the PREVIOUS value. The handler never calls `settimeofday`, so the receiver's clock does not move. (4) `gLastTimeSyncMs` (:345) is declared and read at :12295 but never assigned anywhere in the tree.
- **Why it matters:** On the master, `espnowtimesync` returns "Time sync broadcast sent". On each receiver, `v4h_time_sync` sets `gTimeIsSynced = true`, so `espnowtimestatus` prints "Synced: Yes" — while the device's clock is untouched. The "Epoch:" line it then prints comes from the receiver's own `time(nullptr)`, so an unsynced worker reports "Synced: Yes, Epoch: 41". And because `gLastTimeSyncMs` is never written, "Last sync: N seconds ago" always equals uptime. An operator using this to bring an offline mesh worker's clock up gets a green status and no clock change. Note this also contradicts docs/ESPNOW_ARCHITECTURE.md:171 ("TIME_SYNC (moves the clock)") and docs/ESPNOW_RELAY_RESTORE_PLAN.md:144 ("v4h_time_sync applies any verified offset unconditionally") — the receiver applies nothing, so the relay plan's freshness-check premise needs restating.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Decide whether this feature is live or dead. If live: send `(int64_t)epoch*1000 - (int64_t)millis()`, widen the builder parameter to int64_t, have `v4h_time_sync` actually `settimeofday()` (gated on a sane epoch range and a bounded backward-step rule), and assign `gLastTimeSyncMs`. If dead: delete the opcode path rather than leave a command that lies about its result.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW USER_SYNC creates an account without posting SYSEVT_USER_ADDED, so user_added automation triggers and the notification feed never see mesh-created accounts
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3717`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:940 (canonical poster), components/hardwareone/System_Events.h:172 (event definition), components/hardwareone/System_Events.cpp:255 (automation wake)
- **What:** `adminCreateUser` closes with `systemEventPost(SYSEVT_USER_ADDED, u.c_str(), createdBy...)` (System_User.cpp:940) — the only poster of that event in the tree. The ESP-NOW sync copy substitutes `logSystemEvent("USERS", "user '%s' (id=%d) created via mesh user-sync from '%s'", ...)` at System_ESPNow.cpp:3717 and never posts the typed event. The two are different systems: `logSystemEvent` appends to the durable system-events log, while `systemEventPost` writes the typed ring AND calls `automationOnSystemEvent(kind)` to wake the scheduler (System_Events.cpp:255).
- **Why it matters:** An automation configured with a `user_added` event trigger fires when an account is created locally (CLI/web/OLED/G2, all of which reach adminCreateUser) and silently does not fire when the same account arrives over the mesh — which is precisely the case an operator would want an alert for, since the account was created by a remote device. The notification overlay and the event-history feed are likewise blind to it. `approvePendingUserInternal` gets this right (System_User.cpp:1460 posts SYSEVT_USER_APPROVED), so the omission is specific to the sync copy.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Have the sync path post SYSEVT_USER_ADDED alongside its logSystemEvent line, with the mesh device name as the `by`/detail field so the audit trail still distinguishes local from remote creation.

### [MEDIUM] The receiving side of an ESP-NOW remote command broadcasts the raw inner command line to every output sink unredacted, while the sending side was explicitly hardened to redact it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5838`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5781 (bond-token variant)
- **What:** v4_handle_cmd emits BROADCAST_PRINTF("[ESP-NOW] Remote command from %s (user=%s): %s", deviceName, username, actualCmd) at :5838 and BROADCAST_PRINTF("[ESP-NOW] Bonded command from %s (session token): %s", deviceName, actualCmd) at :5781. actualCmd is the verbatim inner command; neither call passes it through redactCmdForAudit(). BROADCAST_PRINTF (System_Debug.h:734) fans out to MSG_ROUTE_SERIAL|WEB|FILE|BLE, and the FILE tee (System_Debug.cpp:254-269) appends to the live system log on flash when `log start` is active. The sender side of the same feature was fixed for exactly this reason — System_Utils.cpp:4666-4674 carries a SECURITY comment saying `@login bob hunter2` used to be printed verbatim and that broadcastOutput writes the password to unencrypted flash — so only one half of the round trip is protected.
- **Why it matters:** An operator on device A runs `espnowremote B alice pw useradd bob s3cretpw 0 admin` (or `@useradd bob s3cretpw 0 admin` over a bond). On A the line is redacted. On B, the moment the frame is authenticated, `useradd bob s3cretpw 0 admin` is printed in cleartext to B's serial console, to every open web-console SSE stream on B, to B's OLED/G2 console, and — if system logging is running — appended to /system/sys_logs/<log> on B's unencrypted flash. The same applies to `login`, `wifiadd`, `mqttpassword`, `blesecret`, `espnowsetpassphrase` and `userresetpassword` sent remotely. B's own command-audit.log gets the redacted copy from logCommandExecution, so the leak is invisible in the place an auditor would look.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW; :5781 additionally needs ENABLE_BONDED_MODE)
- **Direction:** Run actualCmd through redactCmdForAudit() before both BROADCAST_PRINTFs, the same call the sender side already makes at System_Utils.cpp:4671.

### [MEDIUM] SENSOR_BROADCAST and SENSOR_STATUS accept unauthenticated, unpaired, plaintext frames from any device in radio range
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4975`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:4974 (SENSOR_STATUS row, flags 0), :3093-3129 (v4_ingest_sensor_reading), :3073-3092 (v4h_sensor_status), :5153 (meshFingerprint==0 skips the mesh gate); System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:269-296 (findOrCreateCacheEntry), :893-915 (the only slot-reclaim path), :65-66 (MAX_REMOTE_DEVICES 8 x MAX_SENSORS_PER_DEVICE 8)
- **What:** Both rows are registered with flags = 0 — no V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED, no REQ_AUTHENTICATED, no REQ_SESSION_ENC. The upstream mesh-scope check is `if (h->meshFingerprint != 0 && meshByFingerprint(...) == nullptr) return true;`, so a frame stamped with fingerprint 0 bypasses it entirely. v4_ingest_sensor_reading then memcpys up to REMOTE_SENSOR_BUFFER_SIZE-1 = 255 bytes of caller-supplied JSON into a cache entry keyed on recv_info->src_addr and ctx.deviceName, and marks it valid/enabled/connected. Unlike the bond twin at :3801, sensorType is not range-checked against REMOTE_SENSOR_MAX.
- **Why it matters:** A stranger with no passphrase, no pairing and no session can (a) write arbitrary sensor JSON attributed to any MAC it chooses, which then flows into the web sensor cards, MQTT external-sensor publishes, the OLED/G2 remote-sensor views and automation condition evaluation; and (b) fill all 64 cache slots by cycling spoofed source MACs, after which real peers' readings are silently dropped at the "Cache full" branch. The exhaustion is durable: the only code that ever sets connected = false is the presence-aging loop inside getRemoteDevicesListJSON(), a web-page JSON builder — if nobody loads that page, the poisoned cache survives until reboot. The dedup ring is no obstacle either, because it keys on the attacker-supplied h->origin. The header comments describe this RX as "vestigial" and "harmless", but it is a live, registered, completely ungated write path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Give SENSOR_BROADCAST and SENSOR_STATUS the same V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC as SENSOR_ENVELOPE — the encrypted replacement already exists and the plaintext TX side was already retired — or delete the rows outright. Add the sensorType >= REMOTE_SENSOR_MAX guard that the bond handler already has.

### [MEDIUM] saveEspNowDevices() rewrites a LittleFS file inline on espnow_task, inside the 8-slot RX-ring drain loop
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:7492`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:9963, :9972, :11424, :11432, :11488, :12314 (saveMeshPeers); System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:397
- **What:** `processMetadata` (:7449) is reached from `onEspNowRawRecv`, which `espnowHeartbeatTaskFn` calls synchronously while draining the inbound ring (`:8488-8515`). When peer metadata changes it calls `saveEspNowDevices()` (:7492), a truncate-and-rewrite of `/system/espnow/devices.json` (:536). The ring is 8 slots (`ESPNOW_RX_RING_SLOTS = 8`, :393); the WiFi-task callback drops silently on overflow (`gEspNowRxDrops++`, :1097-1099); and `espnow_task` polls with `vTaskDelay(10 ms)` per pass (recon_tasks.md row 13, prio 5, core 0). Sibling writers on the same task family: `saveMeshPeers()` (:9963, :9972, :11424, :11432, :11488, :12314) and the per-peer identity file at `System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:397`.
- **Why it matters:** The ring is already sized for only ~10 ms of inbound traffic between drains. A single sector erase inside the drain loop (finding 1, ~50 ms typical) extends the un-drained window 5-6×; the WiFi task keeps calling `onEspNowDataReceived`, fills the 8 slots, and silently increments `gEspNowRxDrops`. On a multi-hop or file-transfer-adjacent link those drops present as unexplained protocol gaps rather than as an FS problem. Note this is the one place the codebase gets it right elsewhere: `System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:574-618` deliberately moves the file-stream write off espnow_task onto cmd_exec, and its comment (:552-561) names this exact hazard.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Route these persistence calls through the same `submitDeferredToCmdExec` path that `System_ESPNow_Files.cpp` already uses for its writes, or set a dirty flag drained on cmd_exec — nothing on the RX drain path needs the write to complete inline.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW inbound drain loop's termination depends on the arrival rate, not the ring size, so a frame flood can pin espnow_task on core 0
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8491`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:1092-1111 (producer), :393-395 (8-slot ring), :9385-9400 (espnow_task body)
- **What:** `processMeshHeartbeats()` drains with `while (gEspNowRxHead != gEspNowRxTail)` and re-reads the volatile head on every iteration. The producer is the ESP-NOW RX callback `onEspNowDataReceived` (:1092), which runs on the WiFi task — priority 23, core 0 — and advances head, dropping only when the 8-slot ring is full (:1098-1099). The consumer is `espnow_task`: priority 5 (TASK_PRIORITY_HIGH), pinned to core 0, whose entire body is `for(;;) { processMeshHeartbeats(); vTaskDelay(10ms); }` (:9385-9390). There is no yield and no iteration cap inside the drain; `onEspNowRawRecv` (full parse, dispatch, AEAD verify, file-slot handling) is called once per frame with nothing between calls.
- **Why it matters:** The loop exits only when the consumer momentarily catches the producer. Under a sustained inbound frame rate whose inter-arrival time is shorter than the per-frame processing cost, head keeps advancing and the drain never terminates, so espnow_task never reaches its `vTaskDelay(10)`. At priority 5 on core 0 that starves everything below it on that core — `main` (the whole UI/automation loop), `cmd_exec_task` and `debug_out`, all at priority 1 — and starves IDLE0, which trips the TWDT into a warning-only loop (PANIC is off, so no reset). The frames need no authentication to reach the callback: ESP-NOW delivers anything addressed to the device MAC or to broadcast, and every firmware auth check happens downstream of the enqueue. I could not measure the per-frame processing cost, so whether the threshold is actually reachable in the field is unproven — hence PLAUSIBLE — but the loop's bound being producer-controlled is structural regardless.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Cap the drain at ESPNOW_RX_RING_SLOTS iterations per pass and fall through to the existing `vTaskDelay(10)`; frames beyond that are already covered by the next 10 ms tick, and the ring's own drop counter (gEspNowRxDrops, surfaced by `espnowstats` as "RX Ring Drops") already reports overflow honestly. That makes the loop bounded by a constant instead of by whatever a peer chooses to transmit.

### [MEDIUM] espnowApplyChannel() commits gEspNow->channel before and regardless of the radio calls that would make it true
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:979`
- **What:** espnowApplyChannel() sets `gEspNow->channel = ch;` first (System_ESPNow.cpp:981), then attempts esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_AP, &ap_config) (:987) and esp_wifi_set_channel(ch, WIFI_SECOND_CHAN_NONE) (:988), discarding both esp_err_t values. The same pattern repeats at initEspNow (:986-988).
- **Why it matters:** gEspNow->channel is what every status surface reports as the device's ESP-NOW channel. If either radio call fails — esp_wifi_set_channel returns ESP_ERR_WIFI_NOT_STARTED / ESP_ERR_WIFI_IF, and its effect is undefined while the AP interface owns the channel, which the code's own comment at :974-978 admits is unverified — the reported value and the actual radio channel diverge permanently and silently. Two field devices both configured to channel 6 will each display 'channel 6' while sitting on different channels and never hearing each other, which is precisely the failure mode the espnowchannel feature was added to eliminate. This matters now because the channel work is recorded as HW-test-pending: a failing bench test would be misdiagnosed, since the diagnostic output cannot distinguish 'set succeeded' from 'set was rejected'.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW 1 everywhere); prior art: Adjacent to the open project_espnow_channel_field_reliability work (espnowchannel + channel-0 peers, HW test pending) — the discarded returns are not noted there.
- **Direction:** Capture both esp_err_t values, only assign gEspNow->channel when the radio actually accepted the change, and surface the failure in espnowstatus so the pending hardware test can tell rejection from divergence.

### [MEDIUM] Bond settings/schema/manifest temp files are written unchecked and then streamed to the peer as valid payloads
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:6760`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:6889 (schema send), 6986 (bond manifest send)
- **What:** The pairing flow builds a JSON String, writes it with `f.print(settings)` (System_ESPNow.cpp:6760) discarding the return, closes, logs "Wrote %d bytes" using the String's length rather than anything the filesystem reported, and then hands the path to sendFileToMac(). The schema (:6889) and bond-manifest (:6986) senders are identical.
- **Why it matters:** sendFileToMac re-opens the temp and streams whatever is on disk. A truncated temp becomes a truncated but well-formed transfer: the receiver's v4 file path validates the transfer, saves it under /espnow/received/<mac>/, and only then fails to deserialize the partial JSON. The sender meanwhile logs "[SETTINGS_SEND] SUCCESS: File transfer initiated (N bytes)" and, because `sent` is true, proceeds to push the automations file too (:6796-6805). The peer ends up bonded with no usable settings and no error attributable to the real cause.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW 1 everywhere)
- **Direction:** Compare f.print's return against the String length before calling sendFileToMac, and log the value the filesystem returned rather than the source length.

### [MEDIUM] esp_wifi_set_channel() is called while the STA is scanning or connecting, which ESP-IDF explicitly forbids
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:981`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8451 (auto re-sync call site), :11312 (manual resync — same lack of a mid-connect gate)
- **What:** espnowApplyChannel()'s only gate before touching the radio is `if (WiFi.status() != WL_CONNECTED)` (981). That predicate is TRUE for the entire scan/associate window (WL_IDLE_STATUS / WL_DISCONNECTED), so the guard does not exclude the forbidden state. The automatic re-sync at 8444-8455 is triggered by every ARDUINO_EVENT_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED via wifiEventLogger → espnowNoteWifiChannelMayHaveChanged (System_WiFi.cpp:1665-1666), and espnow_task drains that flag within 10 ms. esp_wifi.h:772-775 states: "When device is in STA mode, this API should not be called when STA is scanning or connecting to an external AP" and repeats it for STA+softAP.
- **Why it matters:** connectWiFiIndex runs up to 3 attempts of 12 s each, and each failed attempt raises WIFI_EVENT_STA_DISCONNECTED. That event flags a resync; within 10 ms espnow_task computes `want` from the live (mid-scan) radio channel, finds it differs from gEspNow->channel, and calls esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_AP) + esp_wifi_set_channel() while esp_wifi_connect() is still in flight. Two consequences: the in-flight scan/association can be disrupted (contributing to the "IDLE bug" the code already works around at 1013-1031), and the ESP-NOW channel latches onto a meaningless mid-scan value. The author already knew this hazard — cmd_espnow_channel's comment (11303-11307) says auto-yanking "would fight the driver's background WiFi (re)connect attempts" — but only the manual `espnowchannel resync` path was reasoned about; the automatic path at 8444 has no such gate, while the drift-corrector 20 lines below it does have a careful 2-strike gate.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Add an explicit "STA is busy" predicate (status is WL_IDLE_STATUS/WL_SCAN_COMPLETED, or a connect-in-progress flag set by connectWiFiIndex) and skip or defer the apply while it holds; the existing 15 s safety-net tick will pick it up once the STA settles.

### [MEDIUM] Inbound-command echo bypasses the shared redactCmdForAudit chokepoint on the BLE and ESP-NOW surfaces; serial and web use it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5838`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:5781 (bond path, same pattern); Bluetooth.cpp:704 (BLE_DEBUGF of the raw line) and Bluetooth.cpp:711 (raw line copied into bleAddMessageToHistory, which the OLED Bluetooth screen renders). Correct implementations: appendCommandToFeed HardwareOne.cpp:487-498 (used by serial :2621 and web :3222/:5079).
- **What:** The project has one canonical redactor for command lines, redactCmdForAudit (System_Utils.cpp:1180) with a rule table at :1115 covering login/useradd/wifiadd/blesecret/espnowsetpassphrase/userresetpassword/etc. Serial and both web CLI endpoints route their "echo the command that just arrived" step through appendCommandToFeed, which applies it. The BLE and ESP-NOW receive handlers re-implement that echo inline and print the raw line: System_ESPNow.cpp:5838 `BROADCAST_PRINTF("[ESP-NOW] Remote command from %s (user=%s): %s", deviceName, username, actualCmd)` and :5781 for the bond path; Bluetooth.cpp:704 `BLE_DEBUGF(DEBUG_BLE_DATA, "Processing command: %s", cmdStart)` plus :711 which snprintf's the same raw line into the OLED-visible BLE history ring.
- **Why it matters:** BROADCAST_PRINTF (System_Debug.h:734) is not debug-flag gated — it fires whenever any of MSG_ROUTE_SERIAL|WEB|FILE|BLE is set, i.e. always. So `espnowremote <peer> <u> <p> wifiadd MyNet hunter2` writes `[ESP-NOW] Remote command from A (user=bob): wifiadd MyNet hunter2` verbatim into the target's persistent system log and out to every connected web console, while the audit line for the identical command two frames later reads `wifiadd MyNet ***`. Same for a remoted `login`, `useradd`, `blesecret`, `espnowsetpassphrase` or `userresetpassword`. On the BLE side the raw `login alice hunter2` reaches broadcastOutput whenever DEBUG_BLE_DATA is on and, unconditionally, the OLED BLE message history where it is displayed on the device screen. The credential is exposed on lanes that the redaction design was specifically built to keep clean.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: ESP-NOW site: ENABLE_ESPNOW (live on FeatherS3). BLE sites: ENABLE_BLUETOOTH.; prior art: Partially: docs/HEAP_OFFLOAD_SWEEP_2026-07-24.md §4 mentions bleMessageHistory holding a raw inbound `login <user> <pass>` as a reason not to move it to PSRAM, but treats it as a memory-placement note
- **Direction:** Wrap the three echo sites in redactCmdForAudit(...) (the ESP-NOW ones already have the plaintext command in a String-able form), and redact before the bleAddMessageToHistory copy at Bluetooth.cpp:711 rather than after. While there, add espnowroomcmd/espnowtagcmd to kRules — they carry peer credentials and are still missing (security backlog item 5, still open).

### [MEDIUM] isSelfMac() reads an uninitialized stack MAC when esp_wifi_get_mac fails — 30 call sites including a frame-acceptance gate and both registry persist filters
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.h:1169`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:522; System_ESPNow.cpp:1008,2143,2355,2458,4837,6346,6382,8490,9951,11138-11139,11473,11608,12156,12188,13968-13969,14111-14112,14332-14333,14547-14548; OLED_Mode_Remote.cpp:302; OLED_ESPNow.cpp:1421; OLED_Mode_Network.cpp:836
- **What:** `inline bool isSelfMac(const uint8_t* mac) { uint8_t myMac[6]; esp_wifi_get_mac(WIFI_IF_STA, myMac); return memcmp(mac, myMac, 6) == 0; }` — myMac is never initialized and the esp_err_t is discarded. esp_wifi_get_mac returns ESP_ERR_WIFI_NOT_INIT without touching the output buffer whenever the driver is deinitialized.
- **Why it matters:** There are real windows where the WiFi driver is down while ESP-NOW state is still live: the IDLE-bug recovery in connectWiFiIndex does esp_wifi_disconnect/stop/deinit + delay(500) before reinit (System_WiFi.cpp:1035-1039), and the exhausted-attempts path reinitializes after finding esp_wifi_get_mode != ESP_OK (:1149-1157) — both leaving a multi-hundred-millisecond gap during which any other task calling isSelfMac gets a garbage comparison. The 30 dependents include System_ESPNow.cpp:4840 `if (isSelfMac(senderMac)) return false;` (an inbound-frame acceptance gate), :490 and :549 (the self-entry filters inside saveMeshPeers/saveEspNowDevices — a false positive silently drops a real peer from the persisted registry), :10350 ("refusing to block our own MAC"), and the automation condition variables at System_Automation.cpp:2889/3050/3065. The same discard-with-uninitialized-buffer shape appears at 27 other esp_wifi_get_mac sites, notably System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:522 where the garbage would be memcpy'd into V4PayloadKeyExHello.senderMac and fed to computeKeyExHmac — transmitting six bytes of uninitialized stack over the air and persisting a bond against a bogus MAC.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW 1 everywhere)
- **Direction:** Zero-initialize the buffer and return false when esp_wifi_get_mac != ESP_OK, so a driver-down state can never make an arbitrary MAC compare equal to self. Consider caching the STA MAC once at ESP-NOW init instead of re-querying on every call.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW FS_LIST reply entry names and header path are never NUL-terminated on receipt, so consumers strlen past the field
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:300`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:511 (`V4PayloadFsStatReply`) and :532 (`V4PayloadFsGetAck`) memcpy whole wire structs and pass them to callbacks with the same missing termination.
- **What:** `fsListOnReplyReceived` validates payload length, memcpy's the header, and hands `entries` (a `const V4PayloadFsEntry*` aliasing the wire bytes) to the callback without terminating `hdr.path[128]` or any `entry.name[64]`. The wire struct only *documents* termination ("Null-terminated filename", System_ESPNow_Wire.h:694) — nothing enforces it. The request side of the same module does enforce it (`sDeferred.req.list.path[sizeof(...)-1] = '\0'` at :444, and again at :450/:456), so the asymmetry is an omission, not a design choice.
- **Why it matters:** `OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:396` copies up to 32 entries into a PSRAM cache verbatim, then `peerActivate()` builds the FS_GET path with `snprintf(joined, sizeof(joined), "%s/%s", sPeerPath, e.name)` (:539/541) and the renderer prints `snprintf(line, sizeof(line), "%s/", e.name)` (:992). A peer that sends 64 non-NUL bytes in `name` makes both `%s` conversions scan past the 64-byte field: through the following 12 bytes of the entry and into the next entry, and for entry index 31 past the end of the 2432-byte PSRAM cache block entirely. The over-read bytes are then displayed and used to construct the path sent back in the FS_GET request.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW + OLED for the reachable consumer)
- **Direction:** Terminate on receipt, mirroring the request path: NUL the last byte of `hdr.path` right after the header memcpy, and NUL the last byte of each entry's `name` when copying into the caller cache. Doing it in `fsListOnReplyReceived` fixes every consumer at once.

### [MEDIUM] Remote filesystem read is not bond-gated and runs under an unscoped SYSTEM identity with no path restriction
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:841`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:640-646 (list side, same pattern); System_ESPNow.cpp:5007-5012 (handler rows); contrast System_ESPNow.cpp:4183 and :4435, where the file-WRITE path correctly uses VFS::systemAuth(VFS::Scopes::ESPNOW_RECEIVED, ...)
- **What:** processGetDeferred installs SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE("espnow.fs_get_ack"/"espnow.fs_get_send"), which builds the *unscoped* system identity — the scoped variant VFS::systemAuth(scope, reason) exists (System_VFS.cpp:916) and is used on the receive path, but not here. req.path goes through VFS::normalize and straight into existsGuarded/openGuarded with no allowlist, denylist or prefix confinement, then sendFileToMac streams the whole file back.
- **Why it matters:** Any MAC present in gEspNow->devices[] that also holds an ACTIVE session can enumerate and download every file on the device: /system/users/users.json, /system/settings.json, /system/espnow/identity.json, command-audit.log (which per the standing security backlog still records espnowroomcmd/espnowtagcmd peer passwords in cleartext because those verbs are missing from the kRules redaction table), plus captures, GPS tracks and photos. One compromised or stolen mesh member therefore reads the entire filesystem of every peer it is paired with. Mitigating factors that keep this MEDIUM rather than HIGH: users.json passwords are PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with a device-key salt (System_User.cpp:703-718) and settings.json secrets including the mesh passphrase and its stretched key are AES-encrypted at rest under the eFuse/flash-UID device key (System_ESPNow.cpp:1385-1409), so neither is directly reusable off-device.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN — see docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §7 ("comment claims REQ_BOND_MODE; the rows carry only REQ_PAIRED|REQ_SESSION_ENC"). Status unchanged; the unscoped-SYSTEM detail below is new.
- **Direction:** Switch these two call sites to the scoped systemAuth (VFS::systemAuth(scope, reason)) confined to a browsable subtree, and/or add an explicit deny-prefix list for /system so remote peers cannot pull identity, users and audit files. If device-level trust for paired peers is genuinely intended, say so in the row comment instead of the current claim that these are bond-gated.

### [MEDIUM] REKEY handler never checks that the frame is addressed to this device; the variable that would do it is fetched and unused
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1251`
- **What:** runDeferredRekey does `uint8_t selfMac[6]; esp_wifi_get_mac(WIFI_IF_STA, selfMac);` and then never reads selfMac. msg->receiverMac is copied straight into the transcript at :1253-1255 and is never compared with selfMac; msg->senderMac is likewise never compared with w->srcMac (the real radio source used for the session and identity lookups). Both SESSION_OPEN (:757) and SESSION_CONFIRM (:913) do perform the equivalent check, so this is an omission, not a design choice — the dead selfMac is the fingerprint of the missing line.
- **Why it matters:** A REKEY that peer P legitimately signed for device X verifies unmodified at device Y whenever Y happens to hold a session with P under the same 16-bit sessionId (sessionFindBySessionId, :1235, plus peerIdentityFindByMac on srcMac). sessionIds are 16-bit random (:1066), so this is ~1/65535 per attempt but is attacker-driven and free to retry. On a hit, Y is pushed through the whole rekey path against a transcript that names a different receiver — i.e. the signature no longer proves the peer intended to rekey with Y.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Add the two symmetry checks the sibling handlers already have: reject unless memcmp(msg->receiverMac, selfMac, 6) == 0 and memcmp(msg->senderMac, w->srcMac, 6) == 0.

### [MEDIUM] Unauthenticated SESSION_OPEN flood allocates and enqueues onto the shared cmd_exec queue before any identity check (radio-range DoS, no credentials, no mesh key)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1003`
- **Also:** Table row System_ESPNow.cpp:4947 (SESSION_OPEN flags=0); identity check deferred to runDeferredSessionOpen (System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:763, on cmd_exec_task); enqueue System_Utils.cpp:5010 (xQueueSend timeout 0 -> non-blocking drop); queue depth HardwareOne.cpp:1541 (xQueueCreate(6,...))
- **What:** v4hSessionOpen (flags=0, so reachable with meshFingerprint=0 and no key) does only a size check, then ps_alloc(DeferredSessionOpenWork), memcpy, and submitDeferredToCmdExec — enqueuing a job onto gCmdExecQ. The peer-identity/Ed25519 verification happens later in runDeferredSessionOpen, AFTER the allocation and enqueue. There is no per-source rate limit before the enqueue (sessionRejectLogCount only throttles the log line).
- **Why it matters:** An attacker in radio range, rotating source MAC per frame, forces a PSRAM alloc + a cmd_exec_task job per frame — and gCmdExecQ (depth 6) is the SAME queue that serializes every command surface (serial/web/MQTT/automations, via submitAndExecuteSync with a 2 s enqueue timeout). During a sustained flood, legitimate commands contend for the 6 slots and can time out. The enqueue is non-blocking (drops when full) and the RX ring is only 8 slots, so it is self-limited to PSRAM churn + command-queue occupancy rather than a hard wedge — but it is an unauthenticated, credential-free degradation of all command processing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: KNOWN — PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md 3.4; sharpened here
- **Direction:** Do a cheap per-source-MAC token-bucket (or require the frame's meshFingerprint to be non-zero and known) before the ps_alloc/enqueue in v4hSessionOpen; reject meshFingerprint==0 for SESSION_OPEN as it carries no legitimate meaning for an established peer.

### [MEDIUM] Unauthenticated inbound frames can flood the 6-slot cmd_exec queue that serializes every command on the device
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:998`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1017 (v4hSessionConfirm, same shape); System_ESPNow.cpp:3740 (v4h_user_sync, also flags 0, also defers); HardwareOne.cpp:1541 (gCmdExecQ = xQueueCreate(6, sizeof(ExecReq*))); System_ESPNow_Saturation.h (measurement only, no throttling)
- **What:** v4hSessionOpen validates only the payload length, then ps_allocs a work struct and calls submitDeferredToCmdExec. The peer-identity check, the signature check and every other rejection live in the deferred body that runs on cmd_exec_task. The handler-table row for SESSION_OPEN carries flags = 0, so no pairing, authentication or encryption is required to reach it.
- **Why it matters:** cmd_exec_task is the single serialization point for every command on the device — serial CLI, web /api/cli, OLED taps, G2 lens taps, MQTT, voice, automations — and its queue is 6 entries deep. Any device in radio range can push SESSION_OPEN/SESSION_CONFIRM/USER_SYNC frames as fast as the radio allows and keep that queue saturated, stalling the entire command pipeline. There is no inbound rate limiting anywhere in the ESP-NOW RX path; System_ESPNow_Saturation.* only samples counters for the espnowsaturation report. Memory is not the concern (the queue drops when full) — head-of-line blocking of the device's only command executor is.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Move the cheap rejections into the RX handler before the enqueue: drop SESSION_OPEN whose initiatorMac has no PeerIdentity and whose responderMac is not us (both checks already exist, just on the wrong side of the queue). Add a per-source-MAC token bucket for handshake opcodes.

### [MEDIUM] Pre-NTP pairing permanently poisons the immutable `bondedAtSec` field in persisted peer identities
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:483`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:221 (`generateFreshIdentity` -> `createdAtSec`), :485 (`lastSeenSec`), System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:397 (REPLY path).
- **What:** `peerIdentityPersist` deliberately treats `bondedAtSec` as write-once: `if (!p.valid || p.bondedAtSec == 0) p.bondedAtSec = bondedAtSec;`. Its callers compute that value as `uint32_t bondedAt = (uint32_t)((now > 0) ? now : 0);` (System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:292 and :397). Because pre-sync `time()` returns uptime seconds (see the `now > 0` finding), the value written is a small non-zero number like 47 rather than the intended 0 sentinel.
- **Why it matters:** ESP-NOW pairing is routinely done without WiFi/NTP — `closewifi` is the documented way to free the radio for ESP-NOW, and KEY_EX_HELLO/REPLY run on espnow_task with no clock dependency. A peer paired in that state gets `bondedAtSec = 47` written to its identity JSON (serialized at :392). Because 47 != 0, the write-once guard at :483 refuses to ever correct it, even after NTP syncs and the peer re-pairs. The bond date is permanently 1970-01-01 00:00:47 in the persisted record and in every UI/JSON that surfaces it. The same pattern hits `p.lastSeenSec` (:485) and the local identity's `createdAtSec` (:221).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Gate on `Clock::isSynced()` so an unsynced pairing genuinely writes 0 and stays eligible for correction on the next persist; the write-once guard at :483 already has the right shape once the sentinel is honest.

### [MEDIUM] espnowsensorstream returns "OK: Sensor streaming started" while checking only one of the four conditions required to put data on the air
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:1086`
- **What:** cmd_espnow_sensorstream validates transport, arg count, sensor name, on/off, and mesh role, then calls startSensorDataStreaming() and returns the literal "OK: Sensor streaming started". transmitSensorData (:584-609) requires four further conditions that the command never inspects: gSensorBroadcastEnabled (:584), meshEnabled() (:592), non-master role (:597 — the only one checked), and a non-zero gSensorControllerMac lease (:606). Each failing condition returns from a DEBUG_ESPNOW_STREAMF that is off by default.
- **Why it matters:** On a worker with ESP-NOW closed, mesh disabled, sensor broadcast off, or simply no controller lease, `espnowsensorstream thermal on` returns "OK: Sensor streaming started" and BROADCAST_PRINTFs "[ESP-NOW] Started streaming thermal sensor data to master" while zero frames are transmitted. The web ESP-NOW panel drives this same command (WebPage_ESPNow.h:2131) so the toggle flips green.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: project_v0_99_1_known_gaps.md item 1 — knowingly shipped, but only for the gSensorControllerMac condition. Status unchanged; the other three preconditions are unreported.
- **Direction:** Have the command re-evaluate the same four gates transmitSensorData uses and return an Error: naming the missing one ("no controller lease", "mesh not enabled", ...). All four are cheap boolean reads at the call site.

### [MEDIUM] sessionAllocate's reuse path zeroes the replay window while leaving the previous RX key valid, opening a ≤5 s replay hole
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:68`
- **Also:** sessionClear (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:96) does the full memset correctly; the free-slot branch at :81 does too. Struct fields at System_ESPNow_Sessions.h:79-97.
- **What:** sessionAllocate has two paths that must leave the slot in the same state. The free-slot path (:81) does `memset(&s, 0, sizeof(s))`. The reuse path (:68-77) sodium_memzero's only aeadKeyTx and aeadKeyRx, then resets state/txSeqNext/rxSeqHighWater/rxSeqBitmap/establishedAtMs/lastUseMs. It leaves untouched every field added later for rekey and bonding: aeadKeyRxPrev[32], prevKeysValidUntilMs, rekeyEphPrivKey[32], rekeyInitiatedAtMs, rekeyTxSeqAtInit, bondToken[16] and bondTokenValid.
- **Why it matters:** Peer P's session is torn down and re-established (link loss, espnowsessionopen, a peer reboot). sessionAllocate finds the existing slot and reuses it. If a rekey had run within kRekeyPrevKeysWindowMs, prevKeysValidUntilMs is still a future deadline and aeadKeyRxPrev still holds the OLD session's receive key, so frames encrypted under the superseded key continue to decrypt into the NEW session — a replay window that spans a session boundary the protocol treats as a trust reset. Independently, bondTokenValid stays 1 with the previous shared secret's bondToken until sessionDeriveAeadKeys overwrites it, so during the ESTABLISHING gap the bond token from the prior secret is still the one on file. rekeyEphPrivKey also survives as live X25519 private key material in a slot the code believes it just reinitialised.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; prior art: KNOWN — ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §6 records the uncleared fields as "stale key material outlives a re-handshake". The concrete replay-window interaction is new.
- **Direction:** Replace the reuse branch's field-by-field reset with sodium_memzero over the key fields plus a memset of the whole record (preserving peerMac/meshId), i.e. make it structurally identical to the free-slot branch. Any future field added to SessionState then cannot be forgotten in one of the two paths.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW session slot can be key-zeroed or reallocated between sessionWrapFrame's state check and its AEAD seal — the TX guard covers the counter but not the slot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:256`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:63-102 (unguarded mutators); System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:829, :1057; System_ESPNow.cpp:1737, :13074, :8797
- **What:** sessionWrapFrame() tests `if (s->state != SESSION_ACTIVE) return false;` at :256, and only then takes `EspNowTxGuard txGuard("sessionWrap")` at :295 before reading s->aeadKeyTx at :318. sessionAllocate() (:63-94) and sessionClear() (:96-102) — the two functions that invalidate a slot — take no guard at all. sessionAllocate on an existing slot does sodium_memzero(aeadKeyTx), sets state = SESSION_ESTABLISHING and resets txSeqNext = 0; sessionClear memsets the entire 16-slot entry to zero and marks it SESSION_FREE, making the slot reusable for a different peer. Callers hold a raw SessionState* returned by sessionFindByPeer() with no reference count.
- **Why it matters:** The clear/allocate side runs on espnow_task (core 0) via the inbound SESSION_OPEN handler (System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:829, :1057, plus five sessionClear error paths at :837-970) and on cmd_exec_task via espnowunpair (System_ESPNow.cpp:13074) and the peer-offline sweep (:8797). The wrap side runs on espnow_tx (core 0) draining espnowtx::sendAead, and on cmd_exec_task. Because espnow_tx and espnow_task are separate tasks that both block, and because the EspNowTxGuard acquisition at :295 is itself a yielding mutex take, the window between the :256 state check and the :318 key read is not instantaneous. If a peer re-initiates a handshake in that window, the seal runs against an all-zero ChaCha20-Poly1305 key — the frame goes out on the air encrypted under a key any passive listener can guess, which is a confidentiality failure in the one subsystem whose whole job is confidentiality. The receiver rejects it, so it presents locally as unexplained frame loss. The sessionClear variant is worse in kind: the slot can be reallocated to a different peer, so the held pointer starts describing someone else's session and the state re-check would pass on it. The code comment at :257-259 shows the author already knows the state can flip under the check and treats it as a benign fallback.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW (FeatherS3 included); prior art: CORRECTION to docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md "Verified clean (A8)" — A8 states "The ESP-NOW session nonce counter is properly serialized... this was the highest-consequence cross-core RMW in the tree and
- **Direction:** Move the state test inside the guard, and make sessionAllocate/sessionClear take the same EspNowTxGuard so a slot cannot be invalidated while a sender holds a pointer into it. Handing out raw SessionState* from a reusable 16-slot table is the underlying shape to retire — a slot generation counter validated inside the guard would close the reuse case that a state test alone cannot.

### [LOW] ACK is transmitted before dedup and before any authentication, from an attacker-chosen source address
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5506`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5457 (unauthenticated ACK resets the desync counter)
- **What:** For non-SESSION_FRAME frames the ACK_REQ branch fires at :5506-5515, ahead of the dedup check (:5531), ahead of the gEspNow-initialized check (:5539) and ahead of every dispatch-table gate. v4_send_ack targets recv_info->src_addr, which is the 802.11 source and is covered by no tag (the BROADCAST_AUTH HMAC and the SESSION_FRAME AAD both cover header.origin at offset 12-17, not the radio source). Separately, the inbound ACK path calls sendStatusMarkDelivered → sessionNoteSendResult(delivered=true) at :5457 with no authentication of the ACK itself.
- **Why it matters:** An attacker sends unauthenticated 32-byte V4 frames with ACK_REQ set and a spoofed source of victim Y; every device in range answers to Y — a persistent airtime/battery drain and a reflection primitive that costs the attacker one frame per reflected frame. The second half is the more useful one: spoofed ACKs matching an observed msgId reset consecutiveSendTimeouts, suppressing the (already narrow) session self-heal described above.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Move the plaintext ACK emission below the dedup check at minimum, and consider only honouring ACK_REQ for frames that reached a handler. For the ACK-driven session counter, require the ACK to arrive inside a SESSION_FRAME or drop that side effect.

### [LOW] Bond session token compared with plain memcmp while the rest of the crypto path uses constant-time compares
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:838`
- **What:** validateBondSessionToken does `memcmp(token, s->bondToken, 16) == 0`. The KEY_EX HMAC check uses a hand-rolled constant-time ctMemcmp32 (Handlers_Crypto.cpp:126) and the BROADCAST_AUTH tag check uses sodium_memcmp (System_ESPNow.cpp:5207) — both with explicit comments about not leaking match length.
- **Why it matters:** The bond token is the credential that resolves to kBondAdminUser, which isSuperAdminUser() grants unconditionally. Practical exploitation over ESP-NOW is implausible (each guess costs a session-encrypted round trip and RF jitter swamps a 16-byte memcmp), so this is a consistency/defence-in-depth gap rather than a live hole — but it is the one credential compare in the subsystem that is not constant-time.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Use sodium_memcmp (already linked in this TU's dependency set) for the 16-byte token compare.

### [LOW] ESP-NOW METADATA_REQ/RESP/PUSH dispatch with flag mask 0 — no pairing, no session encryption, no authentication
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4986`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4982-4984 (TOPO_*), :5118 (dispatcher gate); components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:372
- **What:** The V4 handler table gives ESPNOW_V4_TYPE_METADATA_REQ, METADATA_RESP and METADATA_PUSH a flags value of 0 (System_ESPNow.cpp:4986-4988), so the dispatcher's gate at :5118 requires neither V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED, nor REQ_AUTHENTICATED, nor REQ_SESSION_ENC. Contrast the rows immediately below: every FS_* and BOND_* opcode carries REQ_PAIRED|REQ_SESSION_ENC at minimum. TOPO_REQ/START/PEER (:4982-4984) are also 0.
- **Why it matters:** Any device in radio range can push arbitrary deviceName/room/zone/tags into the peer-metadata cache with no relationship to this device. That cache is surfaced by /api/espnow/metadata, which is on the guest allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:372), and by the ESP-NOW web page and OLED/G2 device lists — so unauthenticated radio input becomes stored content rendered to authenticated browsers.
