I did not re-derive the escaping half of the chain; the prior finding that WebServer_Utils.h's esc() is a JS-string escaper rather than an HTML escaper (making ~15 render sites unsafe) still stands unrefuted, and there is no Content-Security-Policy header anywhere in the tree.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: KNOWN — memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 3 (ESP-NOW metadata stored XSS -> admin RCE). RE-VERIFIED STILL OPEN: the flag mask is still 0 at :4986-4988.
- **Direction:** Metadata is peer-descriptive data about a relationship, so REQ_PAIRED at minimum is the natural gate — the discovery/pairing beacon path already exists for the pre-pair case. That is a one-word change per row and does not require touching the render side, which should be fixed independently.

### [LOW] ESP-NOW command handler leaves the peer password and a full bond-token candidate unzeroed on the task stack, and logs the token candidate at full length on a parse failure
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5830`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5762-5765 (tokenStr), :5788 (WARN logging tokenStr in full), :5836 (password memcpy)
- **What:** In the username:password branch, `char password[32]` is filled from the frame at :5836, passed to isValidUser, and then simply goes out of scope — no sodium_memzero, unlike the disciplined zeroing throughout System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp and System_BleSecureChannel.cpp. In the bond branch, `char tokenStr[33]` holds the 32-hex session token; the success path deliberately logs only `%.8s` at :5765, but the parse-failure path at :5788 logs the whole candidate with `WARN_ESPNOWF("[BOND_AUTH]   parse failed for: %s", tokenStr)`.
- **Why it matters:** The password lands in the espnow_task stack, which is a permanent internal-DRAM allocation reused by every subsequent frame — a stale peer credential persists there until overwritten. The token log is narrower: parseSessionToken already failed, so the value logged is not a valid token, but WARN_ESPNOWF reaches the durable /system/sys_logs/errors.log and the shared web mirror, and the failure mode that produces it (a truncated or mistyped token) can leave most of a real token in the log. This is a hygiene gap against an otherwise careful zeroing convention in the same subsystem, not an exploitable hole on its own.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards building ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** sodium_memzero the `password` buffer immediately after the isValidUser call on both the success and failure paths, and truncate the :5788 log to the same `%.8s` the success path already uses.

### [LOW] Fragment-index bound check uses the unclamped wire fragCount, so have[] is indexed up to 254 on a 21-entry array
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5353`
- **Also:** Clamp that is ignored: System_ESPNow.cpp:202 `gV4Reasm[i].fragCount = (fragCount <= V4_FRAG_MAX) ? fragCount : V4_FRAG_MAX;`. Guard that uses the wire value: :5346 `if (h->fragIndex >= h->fragCount)`. Write site: :5370 `e->have[h->fragIndex] = true;`
- **What:** `v4_reasm_find_or_alloc` clamps the stored `fragCount` to `V4_FRAG_MAX` (21), but the duplicate check at :5353 and the write at :5370 index `e->have[]` — declared `bool have[V4_FRAG_MAX]` — with `h->fragIndex`, validated only against the *unclamped* `h->fragCount` from the wire (up to 255). The offset check at :5361 catches the write for every index above 21, so this is memory-safe: `have[21..254]` aliases the first 234 bytes of the adjacent `buffer[4200]` inside the same struct, and the only reachable write is `have[21]` (offset 4200 + payloadLen 0 passes the `>` comparison exactly).
- **Why it matters:** An unauthenticated peer sends a header-only frame (payloadLen 0, so the CRC check at :5141 is skipped) with `fragCount = 255, fragIndex = 21`. `e->received` increments without a real fragment, letting a 21-fragment reassembly complete with only 20 of them, and `e->buffer[0]` is set to 0x01. Because `v4_reasm_reset` clears only `have[]` (21 bytes) and never `buffer`, that byte stays 1 for the life of the slot, so the aliased `have[21]` reads as "already received" from then on. No memory outside the struct is touched and no legitimate sender uses index 21, so the practical impact is a corrupted first payload byte on one reassembly. Reported because the guard does not do what it reads as doing, and the next change to V4_FRAG_MAX or to the offset check turns it into a real out-of-bounds write.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Validate against `e->fragCount` (the clamped value) rather than `h->fragCount`, or add `h->fragCount > V4_FRAG_MAX` to the early reject. This costs nothing and removes the only unvalidated index in the reassembler.

### [LOW] onEspNowDataSent's doxygen block claims it runs in interrupt context; the same file documents the opposite 5,700 lines earlier
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:6098`
- **What:** The block comment above onEspNowDataSent says "@note Called in interrupt context - keep processing minimal" and "@warning Do not call blocking functions or allocate memory". ESP-NOW send/recv callbacks run on the WiFi task in ESP-IDF v5, not in an ISR. The declaration comment for the RX ring at :394-399 gets it right ("onEspNowDataReceived runs on the WiFi task and can neither allocate nor fault"), so the file contradicts itself.
- **Why it matters:** Failure scenario: an author auditing or extending the ESP-NOW TX accounting reads :6098, concludes the callback is an ISR, and either (a) files/accepts a bogus ISR-safety defect against task-context code, or (b) applies real ISR constraints that are unnecessary here, or (c) worse, trusts the inverse elsewhere. This comment is the single most likely source of false-positive ISR findings in the ESP-NOW subsystem — I hit it during this audit and had to disprove it from the registration site at :9825-9826 and IDF's callback contract. It is not covered by docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md (no 'interrupt context' or 'ISR' hits in that file).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Replace the @note with the accurate context (WiFi task) and keep the 'keep it minimal' guidance, which is still correct for a different reason — the WiFi task is latency-critical. The wording already used at :396-399 is a good template.

### [LOW] `espnowApplyChannel` commits `gEspNow->channel` before the unchecked `esp_wifi_set_channel`, so every status surface can report a channel the radio is not on
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:988`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1665-1666 (STA_DISCONNECTED flags the re-sync); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8444-8452 (drains it); esp-idf/components/esp_wifi/include/esp_wifi.h:773 ("When device is in STA mode, this API should not be called when STA is scanning or connecting to an external AP")
- **What:** On ARDUINO_EVENT_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED the WiFi event handler flags gEspNowChannelResyncPending; processMeshHeartbeats drains it on espnow_task and, if the computed channel differs from the cached one, calls espnowApplyChannel -> esp_wifi_set_channel. The only guard inside espnowApplyChannel is `WiFi.status() != WL_CONNECTED`, which is true for the entire duration of an auto-reconnect hunt. The 15 s safety-net path has an explicit comment about not fighting a WiFi connect and gates the drift-heal accordingly, but the event-driven path has no such gate. The esp_err_t is discarded.
- **Why it matters:** `esp_wifi_set_channel` fails with ESP_ERR_WIFI_NOT_STARTED or while the driver is mid-scan/mid-connect, and the cached value has already been written. Consumers then report a value the hardware does not hold: `espnowstatus` JSON (:10174) and text (:10203/:10206), the OLED ESP-NOW page (OLED_ESPNow.cpp:372), the G2 page (G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:283/:526/:529), the discovery banners (:15820/:15823/:15853), the capability advert `cap.wifiChannel` (:6360) that is transmitted to peers, and `cmd_espnow_channel`'s "radio now %d" reply, which reads the cached value at :11340 rather than reading back. The `processMeshHeartbeats` re-sync also logs "channel re-synced %u -> %u" at :8452 purely from the cached value. Impact is contained because the design already compensates: the no-arg `espnowchannel` checker reads the true channel with `esp_wifi_get_channel` and prints MISMATCH (:11277-1290), and the drift self-heal at :8468-8472 re-pins after two consecutive mismatches. So this is a reporting-integrity defect, not a connectivity one — but the wrong number is what an operator debugging an off-grid pairing failure is looking at.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ESP-NOW builds); prior art: Adjacent to docs/WIFI_RADIO_OWNERSHIP_PLAN.md (P32/P33 lane), which does not cover this call.
- **Direction:** Assign `gEspNow->channel` only after `esp_wifi_set_channel` returns ESP_OK, or better, set it from a `esp_wifi_get_channel` read-back — the read-back idiom is already used twice in this file and is the honest source.

### [LOW] Mesh peer metadata `tags` is copied 63 bytes out of a 54-byte wire field
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:7459`
- **Also:** The other four fields in the same block (name/friendlyName/room/zone at :7450-7457) do match their wire sizes; tags is the only mismatch.
- **What:** `strncpy(meta->tags, metadata->tags, sizeof(meta->tags) - 1)` copies up to 63 bytes because `MeshPeerMeta::tags` is `char[64]` (System_ESPNow.h:246), but the source `V4PayloadMetadata::tags` is only `char[54]` — trimmed from 64 with the comment "was 64 — trimmed to fit encrypted-frame budget" (System_ESPNow_Wire.h:376). The in-RAM struct was never trimmed to match.
- **Why it matters:** A peer that sends 54 non-NUL tag bytes makes strncpy run past the field into `stationary`, `reserved[3]`, and 5 bytes beyond the end of the 202-byte struct. It does not corrupt or fault — the deferred copy lives in a 216-byte array (`deferredMetadataPayload[216]`, System_ESPNow.h:837) so the read stays in bounds — but the stored `tags` string then contains adjacent struct bytes, which are rendered into `espnowdevices`, the web device list, and the MQTT `suggested_area`. It is a size-mismatch that only the 14-byte slack in the staging array keeps benign.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Bound the copy by the source field: `strncpy(meta->tags, metadata->tags, sizeof(metadata->tags))` with an explicit terminator, or shrink `MeshPeerMeta::tags` to 54 so the two agree. A static_assert tying the pairs together would catch the next trim.

### [LOW] espnowMeshesWriteJson compacts the mesh array while espnowMeshesReadJson reloads it positionally, so an empty slot ahead of a configured one silently renumbers every mesh — and meshId is a persisted foreign key
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:1382`
- **What:** The writer skips fully-empty slots (`if (!m.enabled && m.label.length() == 0) continue;`, :1382) so the emitted array is compacted, while bondsByMesh is written for all N_MESHES slots unconditionally (:1413-1418). The reader resets every slot and then fills slot idx from array element idx (:1427-1461), and reads bondsByMesh positionally too (:1468-1474). Slot index is a persisted foreign key: devices.json stores `meshId` per peer (:593, read back at :7945-7946) and gSettings.bondModeEnabledMesh/bondRoleMesh/bondPeerMacMesh are indexed by the same number.
- **Why it matters:** If slot k is empty while slot k+1 is configured, the save writes [slot0..slot k-1, slot k+1] and the next load puts the mesh formerly at k+1 into slot k. Every paired device whose meshId is k+1 then points at an unconfigured slot, and bondsByMesh[k+1] (still positional) is paired with the wrong mesh. I could not reach that state through the shipped CLI: meshesCmd_remove deliberately never clears the label (:12853-12859), findFreeMeshSlot only claims empty-label slots, and initPrimaryMeshFromLegacySettings always bootstraps slot 0 (:1493-1499) — so slots fill 0,1,2,3 and never gap. The asymmetry is therefore latent today but is a live trap for any future 'wipe mesh slot' subcommand or a hand-edited settings.json.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Persist the slot index explicitly (`e["slot"] = i`) and load by that value rather than by array position, or stop skipping empty slots so the array is always N_MESHES long.

### [LOW] TIME_SYNC broadcasts 4 bytes of uninitialized stack over the air on every send
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:2391`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Wire.h:275-279 (dead field). Contrast System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:189 `V4PayloadKeyExConfirm conf = {};` which does zero-initialize.
- **What:** `v4_broadcast_time_sync` declares `V4PayloadTimeSync payload;` with no initializer, assigns only `epochTime` and `timeOffset`, then transmits `sizeof(payload)` = 16 bytes. The third member `senderUptime` (System_ESPNow_Wire.h:278, `uint32_t`) is never written — grep across the tree shows the identifier appears in the struct declaration and nowhere else.
- **Why it matters:** Every `espnowtimesync` broadcast puts 4 bytes of whatever was on the espnow/cmd_exec stack at that address onto the air, to all mesh peers. The frame carries a BROADCAST_AUTH HMAC but is not encrypted, so the bytes are readable by anyone in range with a promiscuous sniffer. Low value per frame, but it is a repeatable, attacker-triggerable-by-timing stack read. The field is also dead weight in the wire format.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** `V4PayloadTimeSync payload = {};` at minimum; better, either populate `senderUptime` (it is the one field that would make the payload useful) or drop it from the struct.

### [LOW] espnowCollapsedAllMessages truncates by peer index before the timestamp sort, so the "most recent" global inbox can omit the newest messages; the log line blames "older" messages
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:16692`
- **What:** `espnowCollapsedAllMessages` (System_ESPNow.cpp:16676) walks peers in slot order, collecting collapsed refs until `w >= maxN` (loop condition at :16680), and only THEN bubble-sorts the collected set by timestamp (:16682-16690). The diagnostic at :16692 reads "collapsedAll hit cap maxN=%d; older logical messages dropped".
- **Why it matters:** The cap is applied in peer-slot order, before any temporal ordering exists, so what gets dropped is "everything belonging to peers whose slot index is high" — not "older" anything. On a mesh where the first peer slots hold a lot of history, a caller that shows the tail of the returned array for "most recent" can display an inbox in which the genuinely newest messages, arriving from a later-indexed peer, are absent entirely and without warning. The log line actively misdirects anyone investigating that.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN — docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §14 item 8 (recorded as a log-string inversion at the old line :15497). Still open; the finding here is that the consequence is broader than a wrong log string — t
- **Direction:** Collect without a cap into a bounded scratch set (or track a running minimum timestamp and evict the oldest as you go), sort, then truncate — so the cap and the word "older" mean the same thing. At minimum correct the log line to say the truncation is by peer order.

### [LOW] Pair-control handlers trust the raw 802.11 source address, which the BROADCAST_AUTH HMAC does not cover
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4823`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:4767-4785 (v4h_pair_beacon, uses ctx.recv_info->src_addr), :4866-4913 (pair request/accept/reject), :1671-1677 (HMAC input = header bytes 0..29 + payload only), :13996-14004 (cmd_espnow_pairsecure writes the MAC into gEspNow->devices[] before KEY_EX runs)
- **What:** The BROADCAST_AUTH tag authenticates the V4 header (including the HMAC-covered h->origin at offset 12) and the payload, but not the 802.11 source address. pairCtrlForUs and v4h_pair_beacon both derive the peer's identity from ctx.recv_info->src_addr rather than from the covered h->origin field.
- **Why it matters:** A replayed pair-control frame can therefore carry an attacker-chosen source MAC and still verify. While a victim's discovery window is open, that lets an attacker inject arbitrary MACs into the pairing candidate list (with the captured frame's display name) or raise an accept prompt attributed to a MAC of their choosing. If the operator accepts, cmd_espnow_pairsecure writes that MAC straight into gEspNow->devices[] — which is exactly what the isPaired flag at System_ESPNow.cpp:5541-5548 reads — and opens a one-shot re-key window for it, before any KEY_EX has succeeded. Exploitation stops there: the follow-on KEY_EX_HELLO still requires an HMAC under the mesh bootstrap key, which the attacker cannot forge, so no session and no command channel results. The residue is a spoofable pairing UI plus a MAC that passes REQ_PAIRED-only gates.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Use h->origin (which the HMAC covers) as the peer identity in v4h_pair_beacon and pairCtrlForUs, and additionally require it to equal recv_info->src_addr so a mismatch is a drop rather than a silent identity swap.

### [LOW] ESP-NOW stamps the raw remote command into AuthContext.path, which feeds logAuthAttempt's "/login" path matcher and writes an unredacted command into the security log
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5908`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1481 and :1508 (the substring match and the unredacted insert)
- **What:** `v4_handle_cmd` sets the auth context's *path* field to the command text itself:

    cmd.ctx.auth.path = actualCmd;   // :5908

Every other transport puts a route identifier there — `"/api/cli"` (WebServer_Server.h:249 `ctx.path = req->uri`), `"/mqtt/command"` (System_MQTT.cpp:534), `"/voice"` (System_ESPSR.cpp:196), `"/automation"` (System_Automation.cpp:103).

`executeCommand` passes that field to `logAuthAttempt(true, ctx.path.c_str(), ...)` (System_Utils.cpp:4750, :4831). `logAuthAttempt` (WebServer_Server.cpp:1467) decides whether to persist by testing `cleanPath.endsWith("/login")` (:1481) and then writes `line += " | "; line += cleanPath;` (:1508) — the path is inserted **unredacted**, unlike the `reason` field which at least went through `redactCmdForAudit`.
- **Why it matters:** A paired peer with a valid session sends any ESP-NOW command whose text ends in `/login` — e.g. `fileview "/system/x/login"`. `isSecurityAuditEvent` becomes true purely because of attacker-chosen content, and the whole command is appended verbatim to `/system/sys_logs/successful_login.log` with a SUCCESS status, in a log whose only purpose is recording authentication events.

Combined with finding 1 (no control-character filter on `executeCommand`), an embedded newline in the same command lets the peer write arbitrary additional lines into successful_login.log. Unlike the `reason` path this one is not even redacted, so an ESP-NOW `login bob hunter2 …/login` would land the plaintext credential in the log file.

Separately, `ctx.path` is what `appendAutoLogEntry` and several [PERM] denial lines print as the operation identifier, so remote commands produce log lines where the "path" column is a command string — the field is unusable for filtering.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Set `cmd.ctx.auth.path` to a stable route identifier such as `"/espnow/command"` (the command already travels in `cmd.line`), matching every other transport. `logAuthAttempt` should also stop deciding what is a security event by string-matching caller-supplied text — take an explicit event kind from the caller.

### [LOW] CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE, not esp_wifi_set_ps, governs the unassociated ESP-NOW case the code comment claims to be protecting
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:9732`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:1871 (CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE=y); sdkconfig:1252 (CONFIG_ESP_COEX_SW_COEXIST_ENABLE=y); esp-idf components/esp_wifi/include/esp_wifi.h:119,330 (init-time field); esp-idf docs/en/api-guides/wifi.rst:1819-1823; components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1086-1094 (the other PS_NONE site)
- **What:** The comment reads: "Keep the radio awake whenever ESP-NOW is up. The default WIFI_PS_MIN_MODEM sleeps the modem between beacons, which silently drops asynchronous ESP-NOW RX — and it stays in effect on a device that never associates ... so set it here too so it also holds while unassociated." The disconnected-state sleep is not controlled by esp_wifi_set_ps at all: it is wifi_init_config_t.sta_disconnected_pm, fixed at esp_wifi_init() from CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_STA_DISCONNECTED_PM_ENABLE (=y here), and IDF's own docs say it is "selected forcefully in Menuconfig at coexistence mode" — CONFIG_ESP_COEX_SW_COEXIST_ENABLE=y is set, so it cannot be turned off in this build. What actually keeps the radio up while unassociated is the hidden softAP started three lines later (WiFi.mode(WIFI_AP_STA) at :9728, AP beaconing at :9745-9755), because an active AP interface prevents the RF/PHY/BB shutdown.
- **Why it matters:** The outcome today is correct, but for a reason nobody has written down, and the written reason is wrong. The hidden AP is configured ssid_hidden=1 and max_connection=0 — it reads as vestigial, and its stated purpose in the comment is only "park the radio on the effective channel". A future change that drops it (or moves ESP-NOW to STA-only to save the AP's memory) removes the only thing actually holding the radio up while unassociated, and the PS_NONE call the comment credits will not compensate. That is precisely the out-and-about case the comment identifies as the one that matters. Separately, under software coexistence the radio is time-sliced with BLE regardless of WIFI_PS_NONE, so "keep the radio awake whenever ESP-NOW is up" overstates what that call buys on a build with G2/R1 BLE active.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ESP-NOW; prior art: docs/ESP32_PITFALL_AUDIT.md:243-247 (P33) flags the class; the coexistence half is partially covered by A11's scope.
- **Direction:** The comment should name the softAP as the mechanism that covers the unassociated case, so the AP is understood as load-bearing rather than decorative. Whether ESP-NOW RX loss under BLE coexistence is real here is a hardware question worth measuring rather than reasoning about.

### [LOW] setMeshRole() documents "does not persist" but writes a registered SETTING_U8, so a transient failover promotion becomes permanent on the next unrelated settings save
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:8230`
- **What:** setMeshRole() (:8226-8231) is prefixed with the comment "Set mesh role at runtime with logging. Does not persist - reboot restores saved role." and does `gSettings.meshRole = (uint8_t)role;`. The bare assignment avoids an immediate flash write, but meshRole is a registered setting (:16036, SETTING_U8, section espnow group mesh) so buildSettingsJsonDoc serializes whatever is in RAM on the NEXT writeSettingsJson from any cause.
- **Why it matters:** A backup master promotes itself when the master goes silent past meshFailoverTimeout (:8708-8710 region) via setMeshRole(MESH_ROLE_MASTER,...). If any settings write happens while promoted - a user changing any setting, a WiFi (re)connect (System_WiFi.cpp:850 saveWiFiNetworks -> writeSettingsJson), `savesettings`, a feature toggle - meshRole=1 lands on disk. The demotion path (:3058, "master_returned") only runs while this device stays up; if it reboots while promoted it comes back as MASTER alongside the real master. The comment actively misleads anyone reasoning about failover safety.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW builds); prior art: KNOWN - docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.10 (LOW). Status unchanged.
- **Direction:** Either keep the promoted role in a separate runtime variable that the persistence layer cannot see, or drop the false comment and make the promotion explicitly persistent with a documented demote-at-boot rule.

### [LOW] KEY_EX HMAC is a static per-device credential and KEY_EX_CONFIRM carries no authentication at all
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:80`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:143-149 (verifyKeyExPayload compares h->origin to the payload MAC, never to recv_info->src_addr), :231-341 (v4hKeyExHello persists identity and replies), :439-470 (v4hKeyExConfirm), System_ESPNow.cpp:4941-4943 (all three rows registered with flags 0)
- **What:** computeKeyExHmac covers only (meshFingerprint || senderMac || senderPubEd25519) — three values that never change for a given device on a given mesh. The resulting KEY_EX_HELLO payload is therefore byte-identical on every send, forever. KEY_EX_CONFIRM has no HMAC at all; the handler checks only that h->origin equals the payload's confirmerMac (both attacker-supplied) and that the MAC is a known peer.
- **Why it matters:** A captured KEY_EX_HELLO replays indefinitely from any radio source: the target re-persists the identity, calls esp_now_add_peer for that MAC and emits a KEY_EX_REPLY. That is an unauthenticated peer-table/identity write and a reply-amplification lever without holding the passphrase. Separately, a forged KEY_EX_CONFIRM naming any known peer calls keyExClearInFlight() (:463), cancelling the HELLO retry timer and stalling a legitimate in-progress pairing. Both are bounded — neither yields a session, because SESSION_OPEN requires an Ed25519 signature under the peer's long-term secret — so this is a nuisance/DoS class rather than a compromise.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Include a freshness value (nonce or coarse timestamp) in the KEY_EX HMAC input so a HELLO cannot be replayed, and require the HELLO's origin to match recv_info->src_addr. Give CONFIRM an HMAC under the same bootstrap key rather than treating it as trusted because a prior REPLY was authenticated.

### [LOW] ESP-NOW session slots are zeroed without the TX guard that the sender holds while using them
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:96`
- **What:** `sessionWrapFrame` takes `EspNowTxGuard` at :295 to cover the ++txSeqNext and the AEAD seal, with a comment naming SENSOR_BCAST_TASK and cmd_exec_task as the racing senders. But `sessionClear()` (:96-102) — which does sodium_memzero on aeadKeyTx/aeadKeyRx and then memset(s,0,sizeof(*s)) — takes nothing, and `sessionAllocate()`'s existing-slot reset (:69-79, zeroes both keys and sets txSeqNext=0) takes nothing either. The `s->state != SESSION_ACTIVE` gate at :256 is also evaluated before the guard is acquired.
- **Why it matters:** sensor_bcast is pinned to core 1 and reaches sessionWrapFrame at 10 Hz. sessionClear is reached on core 0 from sessionNoteSendResult (:672-686, after kSessionSendTimeoutsBeforeReestablish consecutive send timeouts) and from the establishing/rekey sweeps. Core 0 zeroing s->aeadKeyTx while core 1 is inside espnowCryptoAeadSeal(..., s->aeadKeyTx) produces a frame sealed under a half-zeroed key: the peer's AEAD open fails and the frame is dropped with no local error, so the sender believes it sent. This is the same shape as the A8 gRing finding — the clearing side never takes the lock, so the sender's re-check buys nothing.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW — including FeatherS3
- **Direction:** No memory-safety exposure here: the slot table is a fixed 16-entry PSRAM array allocated once and never freed, and this is not a nonce-reuse path because a re-established session derives fresh keys. The gap is simply that the retire side of the session lifecycle does not honour the gate its own send path takes.

### [LOW] ESP-NOW rekey previous-key retention window can silently stretch from 5 s to ~49.7 days across a millis() rollover
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:352`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:837
- **What:** sessionApplyRekeyedKeys sets `s->prevKeysValidUntilMs = (uint32_t)millis() + kRekeyPrevKeysWindowMs` (:777, window = 5000 ms per System_ESPNow_Sessions.h:44). Both consumers compare absolutely: sessionUnwrapFrame accepts the retired RX key while `(uint32_t)millis() <= s->prevKeysValidUntilMs` (:352), and the GC only zeroes the key when `nowMs > s.prevKeysValidUntilMs` (:837).
- **Why it matters:** If a rekey lands in the ~5 s immediately before the millis() wrap, prevKeysValidUntilMs holds a near-0xFFFFFFFF value. After the wrap millis() is small, so the accept test at :352 stays true and the sweep test at :837 stays false — both point the same way, so nothing clears it. The retired aeadKeyRxPrev remains a valid decryption key for the session for the next ~49.7 days instead of 5 s. Replay protection still applies (replayCheckAndInsert at :368), so this is a key-lifetime widening rather than a direct forgery path, and the trigger window is ~5 s in 49.7 days.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all ENABLE_ESPNOW builds
- **Direction:** Store the arming timestamp instead of the deadline and test `(uint32_t)(millis() - prevKeysArmedMs) <= kRekeyPrevKeysWindowMs`, which is wrap-correct in both directions and lets the sweep use the identical expression.

### [NOTE] OLED ESP-NOW settings index map in the header omits the eleventh entry present in the label table
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_ESPNow.h:84`
- **What:** The comment above `settingsMenuIndex` (OLED_ESPNow.h:82-86) documents the index-to-setting mapping and says it "must stay in step with that table", listing 0=Device Name through 9=Backup MAC. `espnowSettingsLabels[ESPNOW_SETTINGS_COUNT]` (OLED_ESPNow.cpp:1924-1936) has eleven entries — index 10 is "Channel", which the comment does not mention.
- **Why it matters:** The index is used both to render the label and to select which setting an edit applies to (the switch at OLED_ESPNow.cpp:1979 onward), so anyone inserting a row using the header comment as the map will place it at the wrong index and silently retarget the Channel edit. Low impact today because the drift is a missing tail entry rather than a misordered one.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any build with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY && ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: Related — docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §8 previously reported this comment as having "4 of 5 indices wrong". Status changed: indices 0-9 are now correct; only the missing index 10 remains.
- **Direction:** Add 10=Channel, or better, derive the comment away entirely by keying the edit switch off the label table rather than a parallel hand-written map.

### [NOTE] TIME_SYNC is inert: gTimeOffset is written and never read, so the prior audit's replay impact is overstated
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:2831`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:12277 (sender passes millis() where an offset is expected)
- **What:** v4h_time_sync assigns gTimeOffset and gTimeIsSynced and ignores ts->epochTime entirely. A repo-wide grep for gTimeOffset returns four hits, all in this file: the definition (:343), a debug printf (:2830), the assignment (:2831) and a comment — no consumer anywhere. gTimeIsSynced only drives the `espnowtimestatus` text. The sender is also wrong: v4_broadcast_time_sync(epoch, millisAtEpoch) at :12277 passes millis() into the timeOffset field.
- **Why it matters:** PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.4 rates the BROADCAST_AUTH replay as MEDIUM on the grounds that a replayed TIME_SYNC "rolls the mesh clock unconditionally" and "corrupts time-based automations and log timestamps". It cannot: nothing reads the value it writes. The underlying observation — BROADCAST_AUTH has no anti-replay beyond the 5 s dedup ring — is correct and still applies to PAIR_BEACON/PAIR_REQUEST/PAIR_ACCEPT/PAIR_REJECT and HEARTBEAT, but this particular impact claim should be corrected so the fix does not get prioritised on a false premise.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN — PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.4, but the stated impact is wrong.
- **Direction:** Either wire gTimeOffset into the time accessors (and then the replay is genuinely MEDIUM and needs a monotonicity check on epochTime), or delete the opcode, the handler and cmd_espnow_timesync. Update §3.4 either way.

### [NOTE] v4h_time_sync is a dead handler — mesh time sync silently does nothing on the receiver
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:2822`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:343-344 (gTimeOffset / gTimeIsSynced definitions), :12293 (the single other reader, in cmd_espnow_timestatus), :2389-2396 (v4_broadcast_time_sync), :4957 (row carries V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_AUTHENTICATED)
- **What:** v4h_time_sync assigns gTimeOffset and sets gTimeIsSynced = true. A whole-tree grep shows gTimeOffset has no reader anywhere outside its own debug print, and gTimeIsSynced is read in exactly one place: the yes/no line printed by cmd_espnow_timestatus. Nothing applies the offset to any clock, RTC, log timestamp or scheduler.
- **Why it matters:** An operator running `espnowtimesync` on the master gets "Time sync broadcast sent" and the receiving devices report "Synced: Yes", but no receiver clock changes. Worth recording twice over: it is a functional dead end, and it means the REQ_AUTHENTICATED gate on this opcode (added to stop on-air clock spoofing) is protecting a no-op — which is fortunate, because as shown in the BROADCAST_AUTH replay finding that gate would not have stopped a replayed TIME_SYNC anyway.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Either wire gTimeOffset into the actual clock path (and then add freshness/monotonicity checks before accepting a remote offset) or delete the handler, the opcode row and the espnowtimesync/espnowtimestatus commands so the fleet does not believe it has working mesh time.

### [NOTE] espnowencstatus prints the first four bytes of the derived mesh key plus the passphrase length
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:13881`
- **What:** cmd_espnow_encstatus emits `Passphrase Length: %d` (:13872) and then hexes gEspNow->derivedKey[0..3] as a 'Passphrase Fingerprint' (:13881-13885). Those four bytes are real key material, not a hash of it.
- **Why it matters:** The output goes wherever the command output goes (web CLI response, serial, BLE, ESP-NOW stream). An attacker who obtains it gets a 32-bit oracle for confirming a guessed mesh passphrase offline, plus the exact passphrase length. The comment at :13887-13890 documents the fingerprint as a deliberate cross-device comparison aid, so this is a design tradeoff rather than an oversight — recorded so it is a decision rather than an accident.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Derive the displayed fingerprint from a separate HKDF label (or SHA-256 of the derived key) rather than from the key bytes themselves; it compares identically across devices and reveals nothing about the key. Dropping the passphrase length costs nothing.

### [NOTE] The ESP-NOW CMD wire format gives the bond token no domain separation from the username field, so the privileged @BOND branch is reachable from the ordinary user:pass sender path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5713`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5754 (branch selection), :828-846 (token validation, no srcMac check), :11873 (roomcmd payload build)
- **What:** The CMD payload is a flat colon-delimited string with no escaping. Senders build it as `"%s:%s:%s" % (user, pass, command)` (System_ESPNow.cpp:11873 roomcmd, and the tagcmd/remote twins), while the bonded sender builds `@BOND:<32-hex token>:<command>` via `buildBondedCommandPayload` (used at System_Utils.cpp:4653).

The receiver chooses the trust path purely on the leading bytes of that same string: `if (strncmp(cmdPayload, "@BOND:", 6) == 0)` (:5754) selects the bond branch, which on success stamps `username = kBondAdminUser` — Super Admin for the life of the session (:5776-5779). Otherwise the first two colon-delimited fields are taken as username/password (:5829-5834).

Because the sender's `<user>` field is free text (`espnowremote <target> <user> <pass> <cmd>`, System_ESPNow.cpp:14298), a caller can set `user = "@BOND"` and `pass = <32 hex chars>` and produce a payload byte-identical to a genuine bond frame. Field re-partitioning is symmetrical: a colon anywhere in `user` or `pass` silently re-splits the frame on the receiver.
- **Why it matters:** Today the escalation is blocked by the token check itself: `validateBondSessionToken` (System_ESPNow.cpp:828) requires an exact 16-byte match against a per-session X25519-derived token that is never transmitted outside the AEAD layer, so a forged `@BOND:` frame from a non-bond peer fails at :846 and is logged. The defect is structural, not currently exploitable — the wire format has no field the receiver can use to tell "this is a credential" from "this is a bond token", so the security of the privileged branch rests entirely on the token's secrecy with no second factor.

Two things make it worth recording rather than dismissing: (a) `validateBondSessionToken` does not check that the frame's `srcMac` is the bonded peer — it only checks that the token matches the bond peer's session — so any paired peer that ever learns the token can drive the super-privileged branch; (b) the sender-side surface that can emit an arbitrary `<user>` field (`espnowremote`/`espnowroomcmd`/`espnowtagcmd`) is exactly the surface that the MQTT mesh-route branch reaches without `authorizeCommand` (System_MQTT.cpp:494-508), so the set of callers able to shape a `@BOND:`-prefixed payload is wider than the admin-only registration suggests.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE
- **Direction:** Move the auth-method selector out of the payload string into a dedicated header field or flag bit on the CMD opcode, so a username can never be mistaken for a bond marker; and have `validateBondSessionToken` take the frame's `srcMac` and require it to equal `gSettings.bondPeerMac`. At minimum, reject `@` as the first character of the `<user>` argument in `cmd_espnow_remote`/`roomcmd`/`tagcmd`.

### [NOTE] sessionWrapFrame's guard-justification comment names a caller that no longer reaches it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:289`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:547; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp:248
- **What:** The comment at :289-294 justifies the EspNowTxGuard with "now SENSOR_BCAST_TASK (core 1, 10 Hz) and cmd_exec_task also reach here". That is no longer true for the sensor path: transmitSensorData (System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:547) calls v4_send_sensor_envelope, which calls espnowtx::sendAead (System_ESPNow.cpp:2633), which memcpy's the payload into PSRAM and enqueues a Job onto sJobQueue (System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp:248-276). The actual wrap therefore runs on the espnow_tx dispatcher task, pinned to core 0 (System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp:153-159), not on sensor_bcast.
- **Why it matters:** The comment is the stated rationale for how wide the guard needs to be, and a future reader auditing this code will use it to reason about which cores can be inside the critical section. It currently overstates the sensor path's reach while understating that the same TU's slot-invalidation functions have no guard at all (filed separately above). Nothing misbehaves because of the comment; the guard is still needed for the espnow_tx / cmd_exec_task pair.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Update the comment to name espnow_tx (core 0) as the sensor path's actual entry, and note that the guard covers the counter bump and seal but not slot invalidation.


### Core system / tasks — 55 findings (26 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] `remote:` / `@` command wrapper is invisible to findCommand, so authorizeCommand's admin and super-admin gates never fire — every surface can run super-admin commands on the bonded peer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4505`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1388 (/api/bond/exec), :1514 (/api/bond/cli/batch); components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Remote.cpp:454,458,484,491; components/hardwareone/OLED_RemoteSettings.cpp:262; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5779
- **What:** executeCommand strips a `remote:` / `remote ` / `@` prefix at System_Utils.cpp:4505-4515 and forwards the inner command to the bonded peer. authorizeCommand ran first (:4481) on the WHOLE wrapper line. findCommand (System_Command.cpp:110) does longest-prefix matching over the registry and there is no command named `remote` (verified: zero registrations), and even if there were, the `:` after it fails the word-boundary check at System_Command.cpp:132. findCommand therefore returns nullptr, and commandRequiresAdmin (System_Utils.cpp:3244) / commandRequiresSuperAdmin (:3258) both `return false` for an unresolvable line — the gate fails OPEN. The code states the intent explicitly at :4531: "Bonding treats the two devices as ONE unit, so the bond session token IS the trust — the local caller's role is NOT re-checked here". On the peer, v4_handle_cmd (System_ESPNow.cpp:5779) stamps username = kBondAdminUser, and isSuperAdminUser(kBondAdminUser) returns true for the life of the bond session (System_User.cpp:362-367). The sharpest instance is WebPage_Bond.cpp:1388 (`/api/bond/exec`): the handler's only gate is WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN (:1347) — any authenticated non-guest, no admin check — and it prefixes an attacker-supplied 256-byte `cmd=` param with "remote:".
- **Why it matters:** A plain `user`-tier web account POSTs `cmd=factoryreset` to /api/bond/exec. Locally: authorizeCommand sees "remote:factoryreset", findCommand -> nullptr, no admin/super requirement, allowed. Remotely: the peer runs it as kBondAdminUser = super-admin, deletes /system/users/users.json and reboots into the setup wizard. The same wrapper is reachable from serial with serialRequireAuth off (ctx.user = "AuthBypass", which is not guest so gate 3 passes, HardwareOne.cpp:2575), from the OLED (OLED_Mode_Remote.cpp:454,458,484,491), from BLE, from G2 taps, from MQTT, from voice, and from an ESP-NOW peer holding any non-guest account. Only guests are blocked, and only incidentally (gate 3 fires before the wrapper is parsed).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE); prior art: recon_surfaces.md G1 — but no docs/*.md covers it, and the /api/bond/exec reach (plain user, no admin check on the endpoint) is not documented anywhere.
- **Direction:** Resolve the wrapper BEFORE authorizing: strip `remote:`/`@` first, then run authorizeCommand against the inner command line so the local caller's role is checked against what will actually execute on the peer. Independently, /api/bond/exec and /api/bond/cli/batch should require admin locally — the bond token proves device identity, not caller privilege.

### [CRITICAL] writeText() returns true unconditionally, so writeTextAtomic() can commit an empty/truncated file over a good one and report success — 11 of its 18 call sites are the credential store
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:813`
- **Also:** All 18 writeTextAtomic call sites: System_User.cpp:916,1050,1130,1361,1429,1675,1799 (+4 more), System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:275,908, System_ESPNow.cpp:3694, WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:565 (whole-backup restore), System_Notifications.cpp (NOTIF_POLICY_FILE), G2_Pet (PET_FILE), AUTOMATIONS_JSON_FILE, BOOT_ANCHORS_FILE. WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:586 calls writeText directly.
- **What:** writeText() (System_Utils.cpp:813-826) opens the target, calls f.print(in) with the return DISCARDED, then f.flush() and f.close() — both of which are `void` in Arduino's VFSFileImpl and throw away fflush()/fclose() results — and then `return true;` on every path where the open succeeded. writeTextAtomic() (:828-848) is built entirely on that: `if (!writeText(tmp, content)) return false;` then `if (VFS::rename(tmp, path)) return true;`. Neither function ever inspects a single byte count.
- **Why it matters:** On any write failure (LittleFS ENOSPC on the 2796 KB data partition, LFS_ERR_CORRUPT, an ECC/IO error) writeText leaves a 0-byte or truncated .tmp and returns true; VFS::rename is a metadata-only commit that still succeeds, so the truncated file is renamed over the good one and writeTextAtomic returns true. updateUserLastSeen() (System_User.cpp:1125-1131) runs this on EVERY successful login against USERS_JSON_FILE, whose own in-code comment says the atomic write exists because this is 'the highest-frequency chance to shred the auth DB'. Result: users.json becomes 0-byte, nobody reports an error. getUserIdByUsername() then fails for every account, so isValidUser() (System_User.cpp:1137-1164) returns false on serial, web, OLED, G2 and ESP-NOW; and detectFirstTimeSetupState() (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:82-86) only tests file EXISTENCE, so it reports SETUP_NOT_NEEDED and the recovery wizard never runs. The device is permanently locked out and needs a reflash. Boot JSON validation (System_Filesystem.cpp:1913-1925 region) does not help — its `content.length() > 0` guard skips a zero-byte file.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: CORRECTS docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:157, which states "writeTextAtomic explicitly *refuses* a truncate fallback — the credential store is genuinely safe." That is true only for the power-loss cas
- **Direction:** Make writeText return `f.print(in) == in.length()` AND, because Arduino's File hides fflush/fclose errors (see the next finding), have it verify the committed size by reopening and comparing f.size() before writeTextAtomic renames. Only rename when the temp file's on-disk size matches.

### [CRITICAL] executeCommand() returns true for every dispatched command, including handler errors and unknown commands — the bool is a constant, not a result
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4834`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:544; components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:677,682,687,691,696,701; components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4009,4032 (the comment at :4008 literally says "Rely on the dispatcher's success bool rather than the (now status-stamped) prose" — that assumption is false); components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1326,1392,1439; components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:3017 (executeOLEDCommandWithResult propagates it); components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:206 (voice)
- **What:** executeCommand computes `bool success = (strncmp(out,"Error",5) != 0) && (strncmp(out,"ERROR",5) != 0)` at :4743 and :4802, but uses it ONLY for logCommandExecution() and stampOkStatus(). The function then falls through to an unconditional `return true;` at :4834. The command-not-found branch (:4810-4816) also falls into that same `return true`. The only paths that return false are pre-dispatch: authorizeCommand failure (:4549), empty command (:4563), nested remote wrapper (:4594), and the four bond-send errors (:4628/:4635/:4643/:4678). Once a registry handler runs, the return value carries no information.
- **Why it matters:** Every consumer that keys on the bool reports success on failure. Concretely: `mosquitto_pub -t hw/command -m '{"user":"u","pass":"p","cmd":"nosuchcommand"}'` → System_MQTT.cpp:544 gets success=true → :548 publishes `{"ok":true,"result":"Unknown command: nosuchcommand..."}`. `POST /api/waypoints action=delete index=99` → cmd_waypoint returns "Error: Invalid waypoint index" (System_Maps.cpp:3396) → WebPage_Maps.cpp:690 ok=true → :691 emits `{"success":true}`. `POST /api/files/create` on a denied path → WebServer_Server.cpp:4009 success=true → `{"success":true}`. In all three the author wrote an `else` error branch that is unreachable dead code.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Return the computed `success` instead of `true` at the end of executeCommand (both the help-mode-exit path at :4753 and the main path at :4834), and return false from the command-not-found branch. Then re-audit the handful of call sites that currently compensate with their own `strncmp(out,"Error",5)` check so they do not double-report.

### [HIGH] Confirm-mode `yes` is dispatched after authorizeCommand and the pending slot is a single global with no owner — any User-tier identity on any surface can complete another user's super-admin-only factoryreset
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_CLIConfirm.cpp:18`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_CLIMode.cpp:21,95; components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:2241,4630; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:2053; components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1183
- **What:** sConfirm (System_CLIConfirm.cpp:18-29) is one file-static slot holding onConfirm/onCancel/userData with no requester identity recorded, and sActiveMode (System_CLIMode.cpp:21) is likewise a single file-static. cliModeDispatchInput is invoked from inside executeCommand at System_Utils.cpp:4630 — i.e. 149 lines AFTER authorizeCommand at :4481. So the gate sees the literal string "yes": findCommand("yes") returns nullptr, commandRequiresAdmin/SuperAdmin both return false, and confirm_onInput (System_CLIConfirm.cpp:42) fires the stored callback for any of yes/y/true/1/on. There is no check that the confirmer is the requester. The requesters are factoryreset (System_Utils.cpp:2241, registered requiresSuperAdmin=true at :2592), userdelete (System_User.cpp:2053, requiresAdmin=true) and filedelete (System_Filesystem.cpp:1183). factoryreset_confirmed does its delete under VFS::systemAuth("factory.reset") (System_Utils.cpp:2181), so the VFS permission layer offers no backstop either.
- **Why it matters:** A super-admin types `factoryreset` on the serial console and walks away (or a browser tab is left on the CLI page). Because cmd_exec_task serializes every surface through the same sActiveMode, the pending prompt is now answerable from ANY surface. A plain `user` on the web CLI, an MQTT client holding any non-guest account, an ESP-NOW peer, or an anonymous physical OLED tap (ctx.user = "AuthBypass", which isGuestUser() rates as User tier because no such account exists in users.json) sends `yes` -> /system/users/users.json is deleted and the device reboots into the setup wizard, where the next person to touch serial or the OLED becomes the new superadmin owner. Guests are blocked only because commandAllowedForGuest (System_Utils.cpp:4289) allows only login/logout.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: recon_surfaces.md G3. Not in any docs/*.md.
- **Direction:** Record the requester's AuthContext (user + transport) in sConfirm alongside the callbacks and reject a resolution from a different principal, the way filedelete already captures s_pendingFiledeleteCtx for permissions. Cheaper alternative that also fixes the cross-surface leak: re-run the ORIGINATING command's privilege requirement (stored as originatingCmd, already in the struct at :27) through commandRequiresAdmin/SuperAdmin against the confirmer's ctx before invoking onConfirm.

### [HIGH] Confirm-mode `yes` has no owner check and is evaluated after authorizeCommand, so a plain User can complete a Super-Admin's pending `factoryreset` (deletes users.json, reboots to the setup wizard)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_CLIConfirm.cpp:43`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_CLIConfirm.cpp:18-30 (single global slot, no owner field); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4630 (cliModeDispatchInput runs inside executeCommand, after authorizeCommand at :4481), :2225-2249 (cmd_factoryreset), :2210-2218 (factoryreset_confirmed deletes users.json and schedules reboot); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1176 (filedelete captures the requester's ctx)
- **What:** `sConfirm` is one file-static slot holding only the callbacks, prompt and originating command line — it records no requester identity (:18-30). `confirm_onInput` fires `sConfirm.onConfirm` for any line matching yes/y/true/1/on (:53-60) with no comparison against who armed the prompt. Because `cliModeDispatchInput` is called from inside `executeCommand` at System_Utils.cpp:4630 — 149 lines AFTER the `authorizeCommand` call at :4481 — the gate only ever sees the literal string `yes`. `findCommand("yes")` returns nullptr (no command named `yes` is registered), so `commandRequiresAdmin`/`commandRequiresSuperAdmin` both return false (System_Utils.cpp:3244-3262) and no admin or super check applies. The confirm slot is global across transports: cmd_exec_task serializes every surface through the same slot.
- **Why it matters:** A super-admin types `factoryreset` (registered requiresSuperAdmin=true, System_Utils.cpp:2597) on the serial console and steps away, or is interrupted. A plain `user` on /api/cli sends `yes`. `factoryreset_confirmed` deletes `/system/users/users.json` and schedules a reboot into the first-time setup wizard, whose first account is written `role="superadmin"`. The same hole lets a low-privilege confirmer complete a pending `filedelete`, which executes against `s_pendingFiledeleteCtx` — the ORIGINAL admin's captured AuthContext (System_Filesystem.cpp:1176) — so the permission layer is satisfied by the absent admin while the decision was made by the attacker. Note that `userdelete` is the one action that is safe here, because its confirm callback re-runs `userMutationAllowed()` with the confirmer's identity (System_User.cpp:1958-1960).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Independently confirmed; also surfaced as G3 in this run's shared recon map scratchpad/recon_surfaces.md (not in any docs/*.md)
- **Direction:** Store the requesting AuthContext (or at least user + transport) in `sConfirm` at `cliRequestConfirm` time and have `confirm_onInput` refuse — and audit — a resolution from a different principal, the way `filedelete`/`userdelete` already capture the requester for the action itself.

### [HIGH] Raw remote-command text — which routinely contains cleartext credentials — is teed verbatim into /system/sys_logs/events.log, on by default
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Events.cpp:508`
- **Also:** posters: components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5928, components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:542; acknowledging-but-unfixed comment: components/hardwareone/System_Automation.cpp:3686-3689
- **What:** systemEventLogTick() formats every ring event as "[EVLOG] #seq kind | source:who | subject | detail" and appends it to LOG_EVENT_STREAM_FILE via appendLineWithCap. SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX is posted with detail = the *entire* remote command string: System_ESPNow.cpp:5928 systemEventPost(SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX, deviceName, actualCmd) and System_MQTT.cpp:542 systemEventPost(SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX, username, command). detail[] is 80 bytes (System_Events.h:304). The only transform applied is sanitizeField (System_Events.cpp:449), which replaces non-printables with space and '|' with '/' — it performs NO redaction. redactCmdForAudit is never called on this path. gSettings.eventLogEnabled defaults to true (System_Settings.h:315).
- **Why it matters:** An operator drives a mesh node with `espnowremote node1 admin pw login bob hunter2` (or `useradd eve pw superadmin`, `wifiadd MyNet <psk>`, `espnowsetpassphrase primary <phrase>`, `userresetpassword bob newpw`). On the receiving node the inner command lands in detail[] and, within 2 s, is written as a plaintext line into /system/sys_logs/events.log. Flash encryption is OFF (sdkconfig:490-494) and there is no secure boot, so anyone who dumps the flash — or any admin who runs `filecat /system/sys_logs/events.log` — recovers the cleartext password. The file is capped at 256 KB and holds thousands of lines, so exposure persists for a long time. The codebase already KNOWS this: System_Automation.cpp:3686-3689 says verbatim that "SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX carries the raw remote command text in detail[], so a `login <user> <pass>` arriving over ESP-NOW/MQTT would land in plaintext on a probeable chip (flash encryption is off)" — but that reasoning was only used to keep the automation scratch buffer out of PSRAM. The identical field is written to unencrypted flash by the event-log tee, which nobody guarded.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (feature-gated on ENABLE_ESPNOW / ENABLE_MQTT; applies to the primary FeatherS3 build)
- **Direction:** Route the detail field of SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX through redactCmdForAudit at the post site (both posters), or store only the command's leading verb in detail[] since the full text already goes to command-audit.log through the redacted path. Do not rely on sanitizeField — it is a control-character scrubber, not a redactor.

### [HIGH] events.log persists remote command text verbatim, bypassing the audit-log redaction that covers the identical command locally
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Events.cpp:508`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:14368 (REMOTE_CMD_SENT); `events` CLI command renders the same detail and is registered requiresAdmin=false (System_Utils.cpp:2646), so any authenticated non-guest can read it live
- **What:** systemEventLogTick formats `[EVLOG] #seq KIND | source:who | subject | detail` and hands it to debugQueueLine; debugOutputTask appends every line whose text starts with `[EVLOG]` to LOG_EVENT_STREAM_FILE = /system/sys_logs/events.log (System_Debug.cpp:329-333, 256 KB cap). The only gate is gSettings.eventLogEnabled, which defaults to **true** (System_Settings.h:315). SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_RX carries the raw inbound command in detail[] (80 bytes): System_MQTT.cpp:542 posts the MQTT `cmd` field, System_ESPNow.cpp:5928 posts `actualCmd`. SYSEVT_REMOTE_CMD_SENT does the same on the sender (System_ESPNow.cpp:14368, `#reqId <command>`). No redaction is applied anywhere on this path.
- **Why it matters:** Publish `{"user":"admin","pass":"x","cmd":"useradd bob s3cretpw 0 admin"}` to <base>/command, or run `espnowremote <dev> u p wifiadd HomeNet S3cret123` from a paired peer. events.log gets `[EVLOG] #N remote_cmd_rx | mqtt:admin | admin | useradd bob s3cretpw 0 admin` in plaintext on unencrypted flash. The exact same command typed at the local CLI is masked to `useradd bob *** 0 admin` in command-audit.log by kRules (System_Utils.cpp:1138). The file is admin-read-only via the VFS rules, but it is retrievable by a bond-admin `espnowfetch` and by anyone with physical flash access (flash encryption is off). The codebase already states this rule — docs/CRASH_HISTORY_DESIGN.md §4 'Never store command arguments' — and System_Automation.cpp:3686 plus docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md §270 already call this exact field unsafe to externalise, but only reason about PSRAM, never about the flash file it is written to by default today.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — docs/LAZY_ALLOCATION_AUDIT.md §270 and System_Automation.cpp:3686 note detail[] carries raw command text, but only as a PSRAM-placement argument. The events.log flash sink is not covered a
- **Direction:** Post only the resolved command VERB (found->name after findCommand) into detail[] for the REMOTE_CMD_* kinds, or run detail through redactCmdForAudit before systemEventPost. Deciding this at the post site also fixes the live `events` command, which is not admin-gated.

### [HIGH] The `AuthBypass` sentinel can become a real, promotable account — first-time setup and ESP-NOW USER_SYNC both skip isValidPublicUsername's reserved-name check
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:891`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:262 and :323-359 (post-restore path); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3658 (USER_SYNC)
- **What:** `isValidPublicUsername` (System_User.cpp:786-810) rejects the reserved names `{"system", "AuthBypass", kBondAdminUser}` case-insensitively, and `adminCreateUser` (:834) and web self-registration (WebServer_Server.cpp:3817) both call it. Three account-creation paths do NOT: (a) the main first-time-setup wizard collects the owner name at System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:759-778 with only `u.trim()` and a blank check, then writes `admin["username"] = u` (:891) with `admin["role"] = "superadmin"` (:893); (b) `runPostRestoreCredentialSetup` collects it at :323-337 with only `u.trim()`, then calls `createInitialAdminUser` (:359) which writes `admin["role"] = "superadmin"` (:262); (c) ESP-NOW USER_SYNC writes `newUser["username"] = targetUser` (System_ESPNow.cpp:3658) straight from the wire payload with no validation. `cmd_user_promote` (System_User.cpp:1876-1907) then operates purely by username with no reserved-name recheck.
- **Why it matters:** `AuthBypass` is the identity stamped whenever a transport's require-auth toggle is off: Bluetooth.cpp:202 (`bleRequireAuth` off), HardwareOne.cpp:2575 (serial), OLED_Utils.cpp:2980 (`displayRequireAuth` off). Today that resolves to an unknown name — `isAdminUser` (System_User.cpp:278) and `isSuperAdminUser` (:358) both miss the roster and fall through to false, so it is deliberately a plain-user identity. If a real account named `AuthBypass` exists with role "superadmin", `isAdminUser`/`isSuperAdminUser` return true for it and EVERY unauthenticated peer on that transport becomes super-admin — `hasSuperAdminPrivilege` (HardwareOne.cpp:659) is a bare `isSuperAdminUser(ctx.user)`, and `resolveRole` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1560) hands it FsRole::SUPER = PERM_ALL on the whole filesystem. Two reachable chains: the owner types "AuthBypass" at the setup prompt (self-inflicted, low probability, but the check exists precisely to stop it); or a post-compromise attacker holding admin credentials plants the name via USER_SYNC (role forced to "user") and then `userpromote AuthBypass admin` — a persistent backdoor identity that survives password rotation and never appears as a login.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all — FTS paths are board-independent; the USER_SYNC path additionally requires ENABLE_ESPNOW (=1 in the current build); prior art: KNOWN — project_security_review_backlog memory item 1 ("FTS AuthBypass"), snapshot 2026-07-20. Re-verified against the current working tree: unchanged, still open. This — not the ESP-NOW filesystem fi
- **Direction:** Call `isValidPublicUsername(u, &err)` in both FTS username loops (re-prompt on failure) and reject the USER_SYNC `target_user` the same way before writing users.json. A belt-and-braces recheck in `setUserRoleInternal` would also stop an already-planted sentinel from being promoted.

### [HIGH] First-time setup writes the owner account without isValidPublicUsername, so the reserved sentinel `AuthBypass` can be a real superadmin account
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:891`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:261; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:786,803; components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2575; components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:2980; components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:201
- **What:** Both owner-creation paths accept any non-blank string. The Basic/Advanced wizard loop (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:757-780) only rejects empty input, then writes admin["username"] = u and admin["role"] = "superadmin" at :891/:893. createInitialAdminUser (the post-restore path, :252) does the same at :261/:262. Neither calls isValidPublicUsername (System_User.cpp:786), whose kReserved list at :803 is exactly { "system", "AuthBypass", kBondAdminUser } and which adminCreateUser (:834) and cmd_userrequest (:2712) both honour.
- **Why it matters:** Name the owner `AuthBypass`. That string is the exact sentinel three transports stamp when their require-auth toggle is off: serial (HardwareOne.cpp:2575), OLED (OLED_Utils.cpp:2980) and BLE (Bluetooth.cpp:201). isAdminUser/isSuperAdminUser then find a real users.json row with role="superadmin" and return true (System_User.cpp:317, :406), so an unauthenticated serial console (serialrequireauth off), an unauthenticated OLED (displayrequireauth off) or ANY BLE peer in range (blerequireauth off, and there is no link-layer pairing) executes as super-admin. Naming the owner `bond-admin` similarly makes isAdminUser return true unconditionally only while a bond session is live, but permanently poisons the audit trail. Naming the owner `system` is comparatively harmless — resolveRole (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551) requires transport == SOURCE_INTERNAL as well as the name, and authorizeCommand's bypass at :4303 does the same.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; prior art: KNOWN — memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 1 (snapshot 2026-07-20). RE-VERIFIED STILL OPEN at the current working tree: neither creation site calls isValidPublicUsername.
- **Direction:** Call isValidPublicUsername at both owner-creation sites and re-prompt on rejection — the validator already exists and already knows the reserved list. Belt-and-braces: have isAdminUser/isSuperAdminUser hard-return false for any name in kReserved before touching users.json, so a roster restored from an older backup can't reintroduce this.

### [HIGH] G2 protobuf length-varint wraparound bypasses bounds check → OOB read / crash on a crafted BLE frame
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_G2_Protocol.cpp:1513`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_G2_Protocol.cpp:1554 (g2ParseSettingSilentMode), :1334 (g2DecodeHeartbeatAckTail), :1632 (g2DumpSettingFields); g2PbSkipField at :199 wraps `*pos += (size_t)sz` similarly (misparse/loop rather than direct OOB)
- **What:** The G2 protobuf sub-message parsers read a length as a 64-bit varint (`uint64_t sublen`) then guard with `if (pos + sublen > payloadLen) return false;`. `pos`/`payloadLen` are `size_t` (32-bit on ESP32-S3) but the addition is promoted to `uint64_t`, so for `sublen` near 2^64 (a legal 10-byte varint) `pos + sublen` wraps modulo 2^64 to a small value that passes the check. The code then loops `while (subpos < (size_t)sublen)` where `(size_t)sublen` truncates the near-2^64 value to a ~4 billion 32-bit bound, walking `sub[subpos]` far past the payload buffer.
- **Why it matters:** A BLE peer posing as the glasses sends a G2 settings envelope (sid=0x09) whose pb payload is `[tag field=3 wire=2][10-byte varint ~= 2^64-11]` plus a valid CRC-16/CCITT (keyless, attacker-computable, so g2ParseEnvelope's CRC gate at System_G2_Protocol.cpp:70-74 does not stop a deliberate attacker). handleEnvelope (G2_Glasses.cpp:7172) dispatches it to g2ParseSettingBattery→findInnerField, which reads sub[] for ~4 billion iterations starting one byte past env.payload → out-of-bounds read across the heap → LoadProhibited crash (remote DoS). Same defect at g2ParseSettingSilentMode (check 1554, loop 1557; reached at G2_Glasses.cpp:7201) and g2DecodeHeartbeatAckTail (check 1334, loop 1341; reached at G2_Glasses.cpp:7121).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** Reject oversize lengths before the add and before truncation, e.g. `if (sublen > payloadLen || pos + sublen > payloadLen) return false;` (compare in a width that cannot wrap, or bound sublen to the frame size first). Apply the same clamp everywhere `(size_t)sublen`/`(size_t)sz` is used as a loop bound.

### [HIGH] `imagedelete` deletes any absolute path as FsRole::SYSTEM, giving an ordinary Admin a route to Super Admin by wiping users.json and re-running first-time setup
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ImageManager.cpp:436`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ImageManager.cpp:608-621 (cmd_imagedelete, no path constraint beyond quoting), :663 (registration requiresAdmin=true); components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:86-87, :262
- **What:** `ImageManager::deleteImage(path)` calls `VFS::removeGuarded(path, VFS::systemAuth("imgmgr.delete_image"))` (:441/:445) with no check that the path is an image, is under an image directory, or is within any scope. `cmd_imagedelete` (:608) only requires the argument be a quoted token. The command is `requiresAdmin=true` (:663), so an admin — whose own FS role grants PERM_READ and nothing else on `/system/` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1378) — can delete any file on the device by laundering the request through the system identity.
- **Why it matters:** An account with role `admin` (not superadmin) runs `imagedelete "/system/users/users.json"`. The delete succeeds because the VFS layer is asked with the system identity, not the admin's. On the next boot `detectFirstTimeSetupState()` reports SETUP_REQUIRED and the setup wizard mints the next account as `role="superadmin"` — the admin has escaped the tier that `commandRequiresSuperAdmin` and `userMutationAllowed` exist to enforce. The same primitive also deletes `/system/certs/*`, `/system/settings.json`, or `/system/espnow/devices.json`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Constrain deleteImage to the image/capture directories (the scoped `VFS::systemAuth(scope, reason)` overload used by System_Camera_Video.cpp:508 is the in-tree precedent), or pass the caller's AuthContext so an admin's real PERM_DELETE grant is what decides.

### [HIGH] Always-on system log caps overcommit the entire LittleFS partition on every 8 MB board
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Logging.h:54`
- **Also:** partitions_no_sr_8mb.csv, partitions_sr_8mb.csv, System_VFS.cpp:533 LOG_OVERFLOW_DEFAULT_RESERVE
- **What:** The six registered capped logs plus the hard-coded command-audit cap total 2,757,906 B: LOG_CAP_BYTES 696969 x2 (successful_login, failed_login), LOG_ERROR_CAP/LOG_EVENTS_CAP/LOG_EVENT_STREAM_CAP 262144 x3, LOG_I2C_CAP 65536, and `appendLineWithCap("/system/sys_logs/command-audit.log", entry, 500*1024)` at System_Utils.cpp:940. `/battery.csv` adds 65536 x3 = 196,608 B (System_Battery.cpp:638-640, enabled by default at a 60 s interval). Total 2,954,514 B. `partitions_no_sr_8mb.csv` gives littlefs 0x2BB000 = 2,863,104 B. There is no global storage budget anywhere — each cap is independent.
- **Why it matters:** On an 8 MB board a device left running long enough for the login/audit/error logs to reach their caps consumes more than 100% of the data partition before settings.json, users.json, automations.json, sensor logs, /maps or photos are accounted for, and before LittleFS block/metadata overhead. Once free space falls under the 100 KB reserve, resolveOverflowPath latches to the SD mirror; with no SD card it explicitly falls back to the primary path and accepts silent loss (System_VFS.cpp:626-631), so the reserve that protects state writes is never actually preserved. Combined with the undetectable-commit finding above, the end state is settings.json and users.json being replaced by truncated files with no error reported. On `partitions_sr_8mb.csv` (littlefs 0x20000 = 131,072 B) the situation is degenerate: the caps are 21x the partition and LOG_OVERFLOW_DEFAULT_RESERVE (102,400 B) alone is 78% of it, so the overflow latch fires during the first cleanupLogOrphan call at boot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: 8 MB boards only: boards/xiao_s3.defaults (ESP32-S3), boards/qtpy_esp32.defaults and boards/feather_esp32_v2.defaults (ESP32). NOT the primary FeatherS3 16 MB layout, where 2.95 MB of 10,858,496 B is 27%.; prior art: recon_persist.md notes the sr_8mb 128 KB case; the no_sr_8mb arithmetic and the battery-log contribution are new
- **Direction:** Scale the caps from the actual partition size at init (LittleFS.totalBytes()) instead of hard-coding absolute byte counts, and add a global log budget so the sum can never exceed the partition minus the state reserve.

### [HIGH] Credential material is routed through explicitly-forced PSRAM (heap_caps_malloc(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)) and freed without zeroing, at sites the prior audit did not cover — including the cleartext login password on the web path and every users.json parse
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MemUtil.h:327`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1112, :1149, :766, :781, :876, :927, :1376, :1443, :2721 (users.json / user_settings parses); components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3194, :5040 (raw command bodies); components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:443; components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:364, :490 (KNOWN)
- **What:** PSRAM_JSON_DOC(name) expands to JsonDocument name(psramJsonAllocator()), whose allocate() calls heap_caps_malloc(size, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM) unconditionally when PSRAM is present, and whose deallocate() is a bare free() with no wipe. This is not the 'small allocations land internal by accident' case the prior audit reasoned about — the cap is forced. System_User.cpp uses PSRAM_JSON_DOC at 32 sites, including getUserIdByUsername (:1112, parses all of users.json) and isValidUser (:1149, loads the per-user settings doc and reads settings["password"] / settings["gamepad_password"]) — both on the login hot path, both also reached from isAdminUser/resolveRole on every command. Separately, the raw HTTP request bodies are ps_alloc'd with AllocPref::PreferPSRAM: WebServer_Server.cpp:3194 ("http.cli.exec") and :5040 ("http.batch") hold `cmd=login%20bob%20hunter2` in cleartext, and System_MQTT.cpp:443 parses {"user","pass","cmd"} — cleartext account password — into a PSRAM JsonDocument. None of these are memset before free. Contrast System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:155, which deliberately uses a plain JsonDocument and hand-wipes the decrypted hex.
- **Why it matters:** Flash encryption is off and PSRAM is an external, physically probeable SPI/OPI device on this hardware — that is exactly why the project rule says never put secrets in PSRAM. Every web login leaves the plaintext password in a freed PSRAM block; every command executed by any user leaves the full users.json password-hash table (and the per-user hash) in freed PSRAM blocks. An attacker with the device in hand and a bus tap, or any future in-firmware PSRAM-dump/heap-walk primitive, recovers live credentials rather than the AES-wrapped at-rest copies the storage layer was built to produce.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with PSRAM (FeatherS3 primary; XIAO octal); prior art: Partially — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.1 and priority row 9 cover only gSessions (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:26) and the AES plaintext buffers (System_Settings.cpp:364/:490). The PSRAM_JSON_DO
- **Direction:** Give the credential paths a non-PSRAM JsonDocument (plain JsonDocument, as System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp already does) and allocate the /api/cli, /api/cli/batch and MQTT payload buffers with AllocPref::PreferInternal, wiping before free. A shared 'secret arena' allocator that forces MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL and memsets on release would make the rule mechanically enforceable instead of per-site discipline.

### [HIGH] espnowroomcmd / espnowtagcmd are missing from the audit redaction table, so the target-device password is written cleartext to command-audit.log and echoed to a guest-visible OLED screen
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1114`
- **Also:** file sink components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:939; console echo :951-959; command shapes components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:15922-15923
- **What:** kRules (System_Utils.cpp:1114-1140) covers espnowremote, espnowbrowse and espnowfetch via redactPeerCredCmd, plus login/useradd/userresetpassword/usersync/wifiadd/mqttpassword/blesecret/espnowsetpassphrase. It does NOT cover espnowroomcmd or espnowtagcmd, whose usage strings are `espnowroomcmd <room> <target-user> <target-pass> <command>` and `espnowtagcmd <tag> <target-user> <target-pass> <command>` (System_ESPNow.cpp:15922, 15923) — the exact same credential shape the three covered commands use. redactCmdForAudit (:1179) matches on a lowercase prefix, so an unmatched command is returned verbatim and flows into logCommandExecution and into the five logAuthAttempt sites inside authorizeCommand (:4319, 4330, 4343, 4359) and executeCommand (:4675, 4756).
- **Why it matters:** An admin runs `espnowroomcmd kitchen admin hunter2 reboot`. Two things happen: (a) the literal line lands in /system/sys_logs/command-audit.log; (b) `[CMD] alice@serial: espnowroomcmd kitchen admin hunter2 reboot -> OK` is broadcast with route mask MSG_ROUTE_ALL & ~MSG_ROUTE_BLE = 0x2F, which broadcastOutputCore uses directly as the sink mask (System_Debug.cpp:896-901). 0x2F includes MSG_ROUTE_OLED (0x08) → gOledConsole (System_Debug.cpp:337) → the OLED_CLI_VIEWER mode, which is on the guest allowlist (OLED_Utils.cpp:2927). It also includes MSG_ROUTE_WEB (0x02) → gWebMirror → GET /api/cli/logs (WebServer_Server.cpp:2890), which requires only *any* non-guest session, not admin. So an admin's peer credential is readable by a plain `user`-tier web account and by a view-only Guest standing at the device — neither of whom can read the audit file itself (/system/sys_logs/ is admin-read-only, System_Filesystem.cpp:1367). The live echo defeats the file-permission containment.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 5 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW builds); prior art: KNOWN — memory file project_security_review_backlog.md item 5 ("espnowroomcmd/espnowtagcmd missing from the audit redaction table kRules"). Status UNCHANGED: still absent. Reported because the guest-O
- **Direction:** Add two rows next to the existing espnow entries: `{ "espnowroomcmd ", CALL_HANDLER, 0, &redactPeerCredCmd }` and the same for `espnowtagcmd ` — redactPeerCredCmd already handles the `<target> <user> <pass> <cmd>` shape used by espnowremote.

### [HIGH] Cleartext passwords are written into PSRAM on every credential path, violating the standing no-secrets-in-PSRAM rule — and the prior audit's claim that they stay internal is wrong
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4792`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:3492 (`ps_alloc(total_len+1, PreferPSRAM, "http.login")` holds the raw login POST body `username=..&password=<cleartext>`); System_MQTT.cpp:444-445 (PSRAM_JSON_DOC holding the cleartext `pass` field of every MQTT command); System_ESPNow.cpp:3570-3585 (PSRAM_JSON_DOC holding cleartext `recv_admin_pass` + `target_pass` from USER_SYNC); System_Debug.cpp:624 (`gDebugPoolPrimary` message ring is ps_alloc PSRAM, so any debug line carrying a password lands there too); System_Utils.cpp:4892 (submitCommandAsync), :4774 (early-boot cmd.out.direct)
- **What:** submitAndExecuteSync allocates `ExecReq` via `ps_alloc(sizeof(ExecReq), AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "cmd.exec.req")` (:4792). ps_alloc with PreferPSRAM calls `heap_caps_malloc(size, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)` directly (System_MemUtil.h:136), so this is unconditionally PSRAM and is NOT subject to CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL. The full command line is then `strncpy`'d into `r->line[2048]` (:4810). ExecReq has no user-declared destructor, so `r->~ExecReq(); free(r);` (:4868-4869) returns the block to the PSRAM heap with the plaintext still in it.
- **Why it matters:** Flash encryption is OFF and PSRAM is externally probeable. Every credential-bearing CLI line transits this buffer verbatim: `login <user> <pass>` (from serial when serialRequireAuth is off, from BLE via the synthesized `login %s %s bluetooth` at Bluetooth.cpp:756, from web /api/cli, OLED CLI, G2 lens, ESP-NOW, voice), plus `useradd <u> <pass> ...`, `userresetpassword <u> <newpass>`, `userchangepassword <cur> <new> <confirm>`, `wifiadd <ssid> <pass>`, `blesecret <passphrase>`, `espnowsetpassphrase <mesh> <passphrase>`.
