The existence of the redaction table kRules (System_Utils.cpp:1114-1140) proves these lines are known to be secret-bearing; the audit log is redacted but the PSRAM copy is not, and is never zeroed.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with PSRAM (FeatherS3 quad, XIAO S3 octal); on a no-PSRAM build ps_alloc falls back to malloc and the issue disappears; prior art: docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md:47 and HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md:89 claim typed passwords are internal "by accident" because they are small, and HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md:418 states ExecReq::out "can 
- **Direction:** Either allocate ExecReq (or at minimum its `line` member) with heap_caps_malloc(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL), or give ExecReq a destructor that explicitly zeroes `line` and `out` before free — a volatile-write loop, since a plain memset before free is a legal optimization target. Same treatment for the `http.login` body buffer and the two credential-bearing PSRAM_JSON_DOCs. Also correct docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md §418 and the MECHANICS §4 secrets list before anyone acts on the ALWAYSINTERNAL recommendation on the strength of that claim.

### [HIGH] The `remote:` / `@` prefix runs the wrapped command on the bonded peer as kBondAdminUser (Super Admin) with the local caller's role deliberately not re-checked — reachable by any non-guest, including the unauthenticated `AuthBypass` sentinel
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4505`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4515-4537 (nested-wrapper reject is the only guard; the comment at :4530-4537 states the local role is intentionally not checked), :4303/:4325/:4338/:4350 (the four authorizeCommand gates the wrapper line skips because findCommand returns nullptr for it); components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:290-293, :363-366 (kBondAdminUser rates admin AND super for the life of a bond session); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5779 (the peer stamps ctx.user = kBondAdminUser)
- **What:** `executeCommand` strips a `remote:` / `remote ` / `@` prefix at :4505-4513 and forwards the inner command to the bonded peer. The wrapper line resolves to no registry entry (there is no command named `remote`, and `@X` matches nothing), so `commandRequiresAdmin` and `commandRequiresSuperAdmin` both return false at System_Utils.cpp:3244-3262 and gates 4 and 5 of `authorizeCommand` never fire. On the receiving device `v4_handle_cmd` stamps `ctx.user = kBondAdminUser` (System_ESPNow.cpp:5779), which `isAdminUser` and `isSuperAdminUser` both grant for the life of a valid bond session (System_User.cpp:290-293, :363-366). The only check on the sending side is the nested-`remote:remote:` reject at :4519-4529.
- **Why it matters:** An attacker holding a plain `user` account on the master sends `@userpromote attacker superadmin` (or `@factoryreset`, `@espnowsetpassphrase ...`, `@blesecret ...`) over /api/cli, serial, BLE, MQTT, voice or the OLED. Gate 2 passes (non-empty user), gate 3 passes (not a guest), gates 4 and 5 are structurally skipped, and the command executes on the bonded peer with Super Admin privilege. The same applies to the `AuthBypass` sentinel identity stamped on serial/OLED/BLE when the corresponding require-auth toggle is off (HardwareOne.cpp:2575, OLED_Utils.cpp:2980, Bluetooth.cpp:202) — an unauthenticated local console gets Super Admin on the paired device. Guests alone are blocked, by gate 3.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: Any build with ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE (all current boards); prior art: Independently confirmed; also surfaced as G1 in this run's shared recon map scratchpad/recon_surfaces.md (not in any docs/*.md)
- **Direction:** Apply the local tier check to the INNER command before forwarding — resolve `actualCommand` through findCommand and require the local caller to satisfy `commandRequiresAdmin`/`commandRequiresSuperAdmin` for it — rather than relying on the bond token as a blanket trust. If the "two devices are one unit" model is intentional, at minimum require the local caller to be super, since that is the tier the peer will execute at.

### [HIGH] Interactive-confirm resolution: three requesters use three different identity models, and two never consult the confirmer's privileges
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:2180`
- **Also:** The setup wizard is a fourth CLIMode requester (System_SetupWizardMode.cpp:937, entered by admin-only `featuresetup`, System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:683). Its `wizardMode_onInput` accepts free text from any transport via the same unauthorized dispatch, and `wizardMode_onExit` persists `sWizard.result.wifiSSID`/`wifiPassword` via `upsertWiFiNetwork`+`saveWiFiNetworks` (System_SetupWizardMode.cpp:827-830) — so a non-admin can seed credentials into an admin's open wizard.
- **What:** `cliModeDispatchInput` runs inside `executeCommand` at System_Utils.cpp:4630 — AFTER `authorizeCommand` (:4481), which saw only the literal token ("yes"), got nullptr from `findCommand` and therefore applied no admin/super gate (`commandRequiresAdmin` returns false for unresolved lines, System_Utils.cpp:3244-3252). The three confirm callbacks then run under three DIFFERENT identities: (1) `factoryreset_confirmed` (System_Utils.cpp:2166) explicitly builds `VFS::systemAuth("factory.reset")` at :2180 and deletes users.json with it — no identity check of any kind, and the FS permission layer is deliberately bypassed because admin has only PERM_READ on /system/*; (2) `filedelete_confirmed` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1126) uses `s_pendingFiledeleteCtx`, the ORIGINAL requester's AuthContext captured by value at :1176 — so the permission layer is satisfied by the requester, never the confirmer; (3) `user_delete_confirmed` (System_User.cpp:1950) re-runs `userMutationAllowed()` at :1960, which reads `currentExecUser()` (System_User.cpp:1558) = the confirmer — this one is correct.
- **Why it matters:** An admin (or superadmin) types `factoryreset` on serial and walks away. Any authenticated non-guest on ANY transport — web /api/cli, BLE, MQTT, ESP-NOW, a G2 tap, or a serial/OLED session running as the `AuthBypass` sentinel when serialRequireAuth/displayRequireAuth is off (HardwareOne.cpp:2575, OLED_Utils.cpp:2980) — sends `yes`. `authorizeCommand` passes it (non-empty user, not a guest, no registry entry), `cliModeDispatchInput` hands it to `confirm_onInput`, `/system/users/users.json` is deleted under systemAuth and the device reboots into first-time setup, where anyone can claim ownership. The same shape lets a low-privilege confirmer complete an admin's `filedelete` under the admin's captured permissions. Symmetrically, ANY command line from any transport is silently swallowed by the active mode (returning "Cancelled...") — an automation sub-command (System_Automation.cpp:116 -> cmd_exec -> executeCommand) or a G2/OLED tap firing while a confirm is pending is eaten and cancels the prompt.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: recon_surfaces.md gap G3 (this run's shared recon map) covers the authorization hole. NEW here: the three-way split in WHICH identity the callback actually executes under, that factoryreset's systemAu
- **Direction:** Capture the requester's AuthContext (or at minimum their username+transport) in the CLIMode/`sConfirm` slot and have `cliModeDispatchInput` refuse input whose `currentAuthContext()` does not match; or re-run `authorizeCommand` against `sConfirm.originatingCmd` (not the literal "yes") before firing `onConfirm`. Make the fix at the framework level so the wizard is covered too.

### [HIGH] The remote:/@ command wrapper defeats every audit-redaction rule, leaking typed credentials into the shared web console ring that any authenticated non-guest can poll
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1185`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4505-4513 (prefix strip), :4599 (broadcastOutput of raw inner command), :4600/:4604 (early return, before logCommandExecution/redactCmdForAudit at :4729/:4756); components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:497 (appendCommandToFeed -> gWebMirror); components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3222 (appendCommandToFeed call), :2890 + :5359 (/api/cli/logs serves the ring)
- **What:** redactCmdForAudit matches its rule table with `cl.startsWith(r.prefix)` (System_Utils.cpp:1185). Every rule in kRules is anchored at the start of the line ("login ", "wifiadd ", "useradd ", "blesecret ", "espnowsetpassphrase ", …). executeCommand strips a `remote:` / `remote ` / `@` prefix only at :4505-4513, i.e. AFTER authorizeCommand and long after the web layer has already recorded the line. Three consequences, all traced: (1) `appendCommandToFeed` (HardwareOne.cpp:497) calls redactCmdForAudit on the raw web-CLI line and writes the result straight into the global 8 KB gWebMirror ring — for `@login bob hunter2` no rule matches, so the cleartext password is stored verbatim. (2) On the remote branch, executeCommand does `broadcastOutput("[REMOTE] Sent to bonded device: " + actualCommand)` at :4599 with the unredacted inner command, which broadcastOutputCore (System_Debug.cpp:901-971) fans out to serial, the web mirror, the live system-log FILE sink, OLED, the G2 lens, and the ESP-NOW stream. (3) The remote branch returns at :4600/:4604, before logCommandExecution and before the redactCmdForAudit audit call at :4756 — so remote commands never reach /system/sys_logs/command-audit.log at all.
- **Why it matters:** An admin on the web CLI types `@login admin <peer-password>` or `@wifiadd HomeNet <psk>` or `@espnowsetpassphrase mesh1 <passphrase>` (the normal way to drive a bonded peer, also reachable from OLED_Mode_Remote.cpp:454/458/484/491). appendCommandToFeed writes the unredacted line into gWebMirror. handleLogs (WebServer_Server.cpp:2890) gates only on tgRequireAuth + webGuestAccessAllowed; `/api/cli/logs` is not on the guest allowlist so guests are blocked, but any plain USER-tier account is allowed. That user polls GET /api/cli/logs (the CLI page polls it every 500 ms) and reads the admin's cleartext password out of the shared ring. Note the appendCommandToFeed half fires before submitAndExecuteSync, so it leaks even when the bond session is invalid and the command never goes out. Separately, `@factoryreset` leaves no line in command-audit.log.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards; needs ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER (leak sink) — the broadcast half additionally needs ENABLE_ESPNOW + ENABLE_BONDED_MODE. All enabled on the primary FeatherS3 build.
- **Direction:** Strip the remote:/@ wrapper before redaction rather than after — either normalise the line once at the top of executeCommand and hand the unwrapped form to both authorizeCommand and redactCmdForAudit, or make redactCmdForAudit itself peel a leading remote:/remote /@ before matching kRules. The :4599 broadcast and the :4598 `out` string should carry the redacted form, and the remote branch should call logCommandExecution before returning so remote commands appear in the audit log.

### [HIGH] Enabling debugcli or debugcommandflow dumps every command's raw arguments to all sinks, bypassing redactCmdForAudit entirely
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4690`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4478, :4689, :4765; sink routing components/hardwareone/System_Debug.cpp:800
- **What:** executeCommand emits four unredacted traces of the live command line: :4478 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF("[execCmd] user=%s ip=%s path=%s cmd=%.80s", ..., cmd) — 80 raw bytes; :4689 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF and :4690 DEBUGF(DEBUG_CLI, "[registry_exec] executing: %s (args: %s)", found->name, args.c_str()) — the complete argument string; and submitAndExecuteSync :4765 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF("[submitSync] cmd='%.80s' ..."). None calls redactCmdForAudit. debugQueuePrintf hardcodes MSG_ROUTE_ALL when enqueuing (System_Debug.cpp:800), so these do NOT honour the command's own outputMask. The DEBUGF_QUEUE_DEBUG level gate (System_Debug.h:451) needs getLogLevel() >= LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG, and gLogLevel is initialised to LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG (System_Debug.cpp:84) — so only the flag bit is required. Both flags are ordinary runtime toggles: DEBUG_CLI = bit 5 / `debugcli`, DEBUG_CMD_FLOW = bit 6 / `debugcommandflow` (System_DebugFlags.h:162-163).
- **Why it matters:** An operator turns on `debugcli` to troubleshoot. From that moment every `login alice hunter2`, `useradd bob s3cret admin`, `wifiadd MyNet <psk>`, `blesecret <phrase>`, `espnowsetpassphrase primary <phrase>` and `userresetpassword bob newpw` is printed with its arguments intact to serial, to the web mirror (/api/cli/logs — any non-guest account), to the guest-viewable OLED console, to any BLE client with `outble 1`, and — if `log start` is running — appended to the persisted system log file. The entire kRules redaction table is bypassed because these are DEBUG traces, not audit writes. Answering the assigned question directly: yes, two shipped debug flags dump credentials.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Feed redactCmdForAudit(command) into these four traces (it is already declared at System_Utils.cpp:4261 and used a few lines away at :4675/:4756), or print only found->name and the argument count.

### [HIGH] `writeText()` discards `f.print()`'s return and always returns true; `writeTextAtomic()` then renames the truncated tmp over the good file — the users.json path ends in a wiped auth database
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:820`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ImageManager.cpp:228,242,263,278; components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:2428,2963; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:470,536 (saveMeshPeers/saveEspNowDevices have no write RV at all and return skipped==0)
- **What:** writeText opens "w", calls `f.print(in)` discarding the returned byte count, calls flush() and close() (both `void` in this Arduino FS build — components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:294,352 — so fsync/fclose errors are structurally unobservable), and returns true unconditionally. VFSFileImpl::write (:336) returns the raw fwrite() count, so a short write on a full LittleFS is detectable and is being thrown away. writeTextAtomic (:828) then treats that complete-looking .tmp as valid and VFS::rename()s it over the live file.
- **Why it matters:** Chain, on a full or near-full LittleFS: `writeTextAtomic(USERS_JSON_FILE, out)` (System_User.cpp:916/1050/1130/1361/1429/1675/1799, FirstTimeSetup.cpp:275/908, ESPNow.cpp:3694) → `f.print()` short-writes or the sync fails → `writeText` returns true → `VFS::rename` puts a truncated/zero-length file over a healthy `users.json`. Next boot, `loadAndIncrementBootSeq` (System_User.cpp:3455-3475) parses it, `deserializeJson` fails, and the file is renamed to `users.json.bad` — every account is gone and the device re-arms first-time setup, i.e. it becomes claimable by whoever reaches the serial console or OLED first. The boot corrupt-check at System_Filesystem.cpp:172-189 does not catch it either: it only tests `content[0] != '{'`, which a truncated JSON object still passes. Same mechanism silently loses `automations.json`, `pending_users.json`, `notifications.json`, `pet.json`. Every caller except two checks the returned bool — the bool is simply not telling the truth.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; prior art: Adjacent to docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.7, which covers only settings.json's `bytesWritten == 0` check and its fallback path. `writeText`/`writeTextAtomic` and the users.json chain are not in 
- **Direction:** Have `writeText` compare `f.print()`'s return against `in.length()` and fail, and pair that with the checked-close helper from finding 1 — the length check alone still misses a sub-512-byte sync failure. Consider making `writeTextAtomic` re-stat the tmp before the rename as a cheap independent confirmation.

### [HIGH] executeCommand() returns true for every handler-level failure — the bool means "dispatched", not "succeeded"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4760`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4872 (submitAndExecuteSync); components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:742,796; components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3254,3288-3296; components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:206-209 (voice); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4991-4993 (executeUnifiedWebCommand)
- **What:** executeCommand() computes a local `success` at :4728 (`strncmp(out,"Error",5)!=0 && strncmp(out,"ERROR",5)!=0`) and uses it ONLY for logCommandExecution() and stampOkStatus(). The function itself returns an unconditional `true` at :4760. Even the command-not-found branch (:4736-4742) writes "Unknown command: ...", logs the audit failure, then falls through to `return true`. The only paths that return false are authorizeCommand denial (:4481), empty command (:4496), and the bond-only remote: errors. submitAndExecuteSync() faithfully propagates this (`return ok` at :4872, `r->ok` at HardwareOne.cpp:742), so every downstream consumer inherits it.
- **Why it matters:** Run `espnowremote badpeer u p status` over /api/cli: the handler returns "Error: ...", executeCommand returns true, so WebServer_Server.cpp:3288-3296 — whose own comment says the mapping exists "so API consumers can distinguish success from permission/input errors without parsing the body" — emits HTTP 200 with an error body. Only auth denials and literal "Unknown command"/"Empty command" ever produce 4xx. Four call sites in the codebase (OLED_Utils.cpp:523/:534, OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:187, OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:92) already work around this by re-checking `strncmp(out,"Error",5)` themselves, and G2_Glasses.cpp:5899-5907 string-sniffs for '\"ok\":true' — proof the bool is known-untrustworthy but never fixed at the source.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Make executeCommand return the `success` it already computes (and false for command-not-found), or better, give handlers a real out-param status instead of inferring it from the result string. Every workaround site can then be deleted.

### [HIGH] Success is inferred from an "Error" string prefix, so all JSON failures and many human failure strings audit as OK and get stamped "OK: "
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4728`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:14142,14146,14150,14166,14182,14200,14222,14226,14230,14263,14279,14301,14306,14310,14322,14356; components/hardwareone/System_LLM.cpp:2782,2823; components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:926 (audit "OK"/"FAIL" string)
- **What:** The single success test is `strncmp(out,"Error",5) != 0 && strncmp(out,"ERROR",5) != 0`. stampOkStatus() (:4417-4441) then structurally exempts anything starting with '{' or a JSON '[' from the OK: prefix — but the success bool that drove logCommandExecution() was already computed as TRUE for those same payloads. So a `{"schema":1,"ok":false,"error":"..."}` result is audited as "-> OK". Separately, many handlers return failure text with no "Error" prefix and DO get a literal "OK: " glued on.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete: `espnowremote` with ESP-NOW down returns "ESP-NOW not initialized. Run 'openespnow' first." (System_ESPNow.cpp:14301) → delivered to the user as "OK: ESP-NOW not initialized. Run 'openespnow' first." and written to /system/sys_logs/command-audit.log as "-> OK". Same for "Failed to send remote command" (:14356), "Failed to send V3 browse request" (:14200), "Failed to send V3 fetch request" (:14279), "Target device '%s' not found or not paired" (:14322, :14166), "Rejected (mesh): device not paired" (:14182, :14263), and the multi-line Usage strings (:14150, :14230, :14310). Sibling lines in the very same functions (:14140, :14186, :14220, :14299, :14333) DO use the "Error:" prefix, so the intent is unambiguous. On the JSON side there are 44 `\"ok\":false` returns in System_ESPNow.cpp alone plus 10 in System_LLM.cpp, 8 in System_LLM_Menu.cpp — none can ever be audited as a failure. The audit log is the security record; it currently cannot show a failed command that wasn't auth-denied.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially: docs/LLM_SETTINGS_AUDIT.md R5 records one instance (System_LLM.cpp:2823 "Generation error"). Still present and unfixed; the ESP-NOW instances and the whole JSON class are new.
- **Direction:** Either normalize every failure return to the "Error:"/"ERROR" prefix (mechanical, and the espnowAckErr call sites make it a one-line change each), or add an explicit failure signal so JSON payloads can report failure without the parser guessing. Do not extend the prefix heuristic to sniff for '"ok":false' — that just moves the guess.

### [HIGH] Guest-allowed `logout g2` re-homes the G2 lens identity to the device owner (usually Super Admin) and persists it to flash
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4356`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:5317 (cmd_logout, `g2` arg accepted); components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:502 + :515 (logoutTransport SOURCE_G2_GLASSES -> g2PairedUserClear + bleStampPairedByIfBlank); components/hardwareone/BLE_Peers.cpp:145 (bleResolveStampUsername fallback chain), :176, :206 (setSetting persist)
- **What:** `commandAllowedForGuest()` splits the line on the first space and returns true for any command whose first token is `login` or `logout`. Arguments are never inspected. `cmd_logout` accepts `serial|display|bluetooth|g2`; the `g2` branch calls `logoutTransport(SOURCE_G2_GLASSES)`, which does `g2PairedUserClear()` (blanks `gBlePeerData[BLE_PEER_G2_GLASSES].pairedByUser`) and immediately calls `bleStampPairedByIfBlank()` to re-stamp it. The re-stamp resolver `bleResolveStampUsername()` prefers `currentAuthContext().user` but explicitly rejects guests (`if (isGuestUser(u)) return false;`, BLE_Peers.cpp:154), then falls through to a live OLED session, a live serial session, and finally `getDeviceOwnerUsername()`. It then does `setSetting(d.pairedByUser, who)` — a persisted settings write — and `bumpIdentityGeneration()`.
- **Why it matters:** Guest account `bob` logs in on serial (or BLE/MQTT/ESP-NOW — `isValidUser` never consults role) and sends `logout g2`. authorizeCommand gate 3 permits it because the first token is `logout`. The lens's `pairedByUser` — say `carol`, a plain user who paired it — is cleared and re-stamped. With no live OLED/serial session by a non-guest, the resolver lands on `getDeviceOwnerUsername()`, the first users.json entry, which `isSuperAdminUser` treats as super under the no-explicit-super fallback (System_User.cpp:418). The G2 lens has NO per-session credential — `g2HijackAuthContext()` (G2_HijackCmd.cpp:177) reads `pairedByUser` verbatim and `tgRequireAuth` short-circuits SOURCE_G2_GLASSES to "pairing IS auth". So every subsequent lens tap now executes as the device owner instead of as `carol`. A view-only account has (a) mutated persistent settings and (b) escalated the privilege level of another transport.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH + ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** Make `commandAllowedForGuest` inspect arguments, not just the first token — permit only the bare `logout` (self-scoped) and `login`, and reject any `logout <transport>` / `login ... <transport>` form from a guest. Alternatively route transport-targeted logout through a separate command with `requiresAdmin=true`.

### [HIGH] A newline inside any command line is written verbatim into command-audit.log and (with one trigger word) successful_login.log — an authenticated non-guest can forge arbitrary audit records
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:936`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4750 and :4831 (logAuthAttempt with the raw command as `reason`); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4397, :4404, :4413, :4429 (the four authorizeCommand denial paths, same auditBuf); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1828 (the unescaping sink, shared by errors.log / system-events.log / event-stream)
- **What:** `executeCommand` applies no character filter to `cmd`. `String command = cmd; command.trim();` strips only leading/trailing whitespace, so interior `\n`/`\r` survive. Both audit writers then format the raw line into a single log record and hand it to a writer that does no escaping:

- `logCommandExecution` (System_Utils.cpp:922 `redactCmdForAudit(cmd)`, :936 `snprintf(entry, 512, "[%lu] %s@%s %s -> %s", ...)`, :940 `appendLineWithCap("/system/sys_logs/command-audit.log", entry, ...)`). It is called on BOTH the found-command path (:4805) and the *unknown-command* path (:4820), so the command does not even have to exist.
- `logAuthAttempt(true, ctx.path, ctx.user, ctx.ip, auditBuf)` at System_Utils.cpp:4750 and :4831, with `auditBuf = "cmd=%.170s"` of the redacted line.
- The sink, `appendLineWithCap` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1828) is a bare `a.println(line)` — no newline stripping, no length-prefix, no escaping.

`logAuthAttempt` (WebServer_Server.cpp:1467) normally drops non-login events, but its gate is a *substring* test on the reason string: `reason.indexOf("Login successful") >= 0` (:1486). Because `reason` is `cmd=<the attacker's command text>`, putting the literal words `Login successful` in the command makes the gate fire and the line is appended to `/system/sys_logs/successful_login.log` (LOG_OK_FILE, System_Debug.cpp:2297).

Proof that this is an oversight and not accepted design: the BLE ingress path DOES sanitise. `processBleCommandLine` (Bluetooth.cpp:677-688) rebuilds the line into `cmdBuf[512]`, dropping NULs, mapping `\r`/`\n`/`\t` to a space and discarding everything outside 0x20-0x7E. `executeCommandThroughRegistry` (System_Command.cpp:200-210) also rejects non-printables — but explicitly *allows* `\t`, `\r`, `\n`. Neither filter sits on the shared `executeCommand` chokepoint, so every other transport is unprotected.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete, single-request forgery from any authenticated non-guest account (a plain `user` is enough — `findCommand()` returns nullptr for an unrecognised line so the admin/super gates in `authorizeCommand` never fire):

  POST /api/cli
  cmd=zz+Login+successful%0A%5B99999%5D+admin%40serial+factoryreset+-%3E+OK

`urlDecode` (System_Utils.cpp:588) turns `%0A` into a real newline; `extractFormField`+`urlDecode` keep it; `uc.line = cmd`; `submitAndExecuteSync` `strncpy`s it into `ExecReq::line[2048]` (System_Utils.cpp:4884) unchanged; `cmd_exec_task` calls `executeCommand` (HardwareOne.cpp:743). `findCommand` fails (the boundary test at System_Command.cpp:133 requires a space, and `\n` is not one), so the else branch runs `logCommandExecution(ctx, cmd, false, out)`. command-audit.log now contains a second physical line reading `[99999] admin@serial factoryreset -> OK` that no admin ever ran. The tail of `executeCommand` then calls `logAuthAttempt(true, ...)` whose reason contains `Login successful`, so successful_login.log gains an attacker-shaped record too (the 170-byte `%.170s` cap still leaves ~100 bytes, enough for a full forged `[ts] | SUCCESS | user=admin | ip=10.0.0.9 | web/login | reason=Login successful`).

Same primitive reachable from: MQTT (`doc["cmd"]`, System_MQTT.cpp:452 — a JSON string may contain `\n`), `/api/cli/batch` (WebServer_Server.cpp:5074 `String cmd = v.as<String>()`), and ESP-NOW CMD (System_ESPNow.cpp:5726 `actualCmd = secondColon + 1`, arbitrary bytes). A *guest* is stopped by gate 3 of `authorizeCommand`, but that denial path also calls `logAuthAttempt(false, …, auditBuf)` at System_Utils.cpp:4397 — so a guest can still forge lines in failed_login.log.

Net effect: the device's three security logs (command-audit, successful_login, failed_login) cannot be trusted after any non-guest touches the CLI, and the forged lines are indistinguishable from real ones because they carry the same timestamp/format the real writer emits.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (feature-gated only: any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER, ENABLE_MQTT or ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Sanitise once at the `executeCommand` chokepoint rather than per-sink: after `command.trim()`, either reject a line containing a control character (mirroring System_Command.cpp:200-210 but WITHOUT the `\t`/`\r`/`\n` exemption) or collapse them to spaces the way Bluetooth.cpp:681-682 already does. Independently, `appendLineWithCap` should refuse to write a `line` containing an embedded newline, and `logAuthAttempt`'s `isSecurityAuditEvent` test should key off an explicit event enum from the caller instead of a substring search over caller-supplied text.

### [HIGH] `lightsleep` enters light sleep with WiFi, Bluedroid BLE and ESP-NOW all running, which ESP-IDF documents as unsupported
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1455`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1452 (esp_sleep_enable_timer_wakeup, up to 3600 s)
- **What:** cmd_lightsleep (1400-1468) arms a timer wakeup for 1..3600 s and calls esp_light_sleep_start() directly. It stops nothing first: no esp_wifi_stop(), no esp_bluedroid_disable(), no esp_bt_controller_disable(), no esp_now_deinit(). CONFIG_PM_ENABLE is not set (sdkconfig:1647), so there is no power-management integration that would let the radios survive. The wake path (1457-1467) restores only the OLED. IDF sleep_modes.rst:40: "Before entering Deep-sleep or Light-sleep modes, the application must disable Wi-Fi and Bluetooth using the appropriate calls ... Wi-Fi and Bluetooth connections are not maintained in Deep-sleep or Light-sleep mode, even if these functions are not called."
- **Why it matters:** `lightsleep 60` on a device with glasses + ring connected and ESP-NOW up: the RF is powered down for 60 s while both the WiFi driver and the BT controller still believe they are running. The AP association is dropped without a clean deauth; all three BLE links blow their supervision timeout (G2_CONN_TIMEOUT_REQ = 500 ×10 ms = 5 s, G2_Glasses.cpp:9272) within the first 5 seconds; ESP-NOW is deaf for the whole window, so any peer's unicast is lost and any in-flight fragmented transfer aborts. On wake nothing re-arms any radio — recovery depends entirely on incidental paths (WiFi auto-reconnect if armed, BLE reseek backoff at 5/15/45/90/180 s). The command returns the flat string "Woke from light sleep" and says nothing about what it just tore down.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — docs/G2_HIJACK_FEATURE_EXPANSION.md:133 notes "a successful light sleep drops the BLE hijack link"; docs/CLI_HELPTEXT_AUDIT.md:130 notes only the undocumented cooldown. The WiFi / ESP-NOW 
- **Direction:** Either stop the radios before sleeping and restart them on wake (mirroring what radioPowerOff/radioPowerOn already do correctly in System_WiFi.cpp:487-562), or refuse the command while any radio is up and say why. At minimum the return string must state that WiFi, BLE and ESP-NOW links are dropped.

### [HIGH] `certgen` writes the HTTPS cert and key with unchecked `print()` and no staging, then reports success — a truncated key silently downgrades the admin web UI to plain HTTP
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1540`
- **What:** After a successful mbedTLS keygen (every mbedTLS return is checked, :1498-1528), the two file writes are not: `cf.print((const char*)certPem)` at :1540 and `kf.print((const char*)keyPem)` at :1548, each into a `"w"` truncate-in-place open, no `flush()`, `close()` return discarded, no staging file and no rollback. Control then falls through to the unconditional "Self-signed %s certificate generated and saved" message and `systemEventPost(SYSEVT_CERT_GENERATED, ...)`.
- **Why it matters:** An RSA-2048 PEM key is ~1.7 KB and the cert ~1.2 KB; a short write on either leaves a syntactically-plausible but unusable PEM. At the next boot `startHttpServer` (WebServer_Server.cpp:5182-5196) reads both files and gates only on `sHttpsCertData.length() > 0 && sHttpsKeyData.length() > 0`, so `certsOk` becomes true, `httpd_ssl_start` fails on the malformed key, and the code takes the `else` branch at :5271 — falling back to **plain HTTP on port 80** while `gSettings.httpsEnabled` stays 1. Every subsequent login, session cookie and `/api/cli` command crosses the network in the clear, and the operator's last signal was "certificate generated and saved". This is also the only genuine two-file invariant in the tree: a reset between :1544 (cert closed) and :1548 (key written) leaves a cert that does not match the key, with the same end state.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Write both PEMs to `.tmp` siblings with the byte count verified, then rename both only after both succeed; and make the success message and `SYSEVT_CERT_GENERATED` conditional on that. Separately, `startHttpServer` should validate the key parses (it already calls `mbedtls_x509_crt_parse` on the cert at :5243) before deciding `certsOk`, and the HTTP fallback should be a loud persistent warning rather than one broadcast line.

### [MEDIUM] Voice arming never expires — one `voicearm` from an admin permanently delegates that admin's privileges to anyone within earshot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:160`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:116,148,165,1000,1081,1117,3867; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:206
- **What:** voiceArmFromContextInternal (System_ESPSR.cpp:151) records gVoiceArmedUser and stamps gVoiceArmedAtMs = millis() at :160. gVoiceArmedAtMs is write-only — the only other references in the file are its declaration (:116) and its zeroing on disarm (:148). isVoiceArmed (:165) checks the bool alone. executeVoiceCommandAsArmedUser (:176) submits the mapped CLI line with ctx.auth.user = gVoiceArmedUser and ctx.transport = SOURCE_VOICE; tgRequireAuth's else-branch (System_User.cpp:206-221) passes SOURCE_VOICE straight through, so authorizeCommand evaluates the armed user's full privileges. Three call sites (:1000, :1081, :1117) feed it commands from the recognition FSM. `voicearm` itself is registered requiresAdmin=false (:3867).
- **Why it matters:** Every other authenticated transport in this firmware ages out: serial has sessionIdleExpired (HardwareOne.cpp:2489), the OLED has gLocalDisplayLastInteractionMs, web sessions have expiresAt + a boot-ID check, BLE has per-conn session tokens. Voice has nothing. An admin runs `voicearm` once to try a feature; from that moment until `voicedisarm` or a reboot, any person, radio, or TV in the room who utters a mapped phrase executes CLI commands as that admin, with no re-authentication and no audit distinction from the admin's own use. The arm survives arbitrarily long idle periods and every disconnect of the transport that armed it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ESP-SR builds)
- **Direction:** gVoiceArmedAtMs is already there — read it. Auto-disarm after an idle window (mirroring sessionIdleExpired's shape so the policy is uniform), and consider a hard cap regardless of activity. Surfacing the remaining window in voicestatus would also make the delegation visible.

### [MEDIUM] First-time setup accepts a one-character admin password and any username, bypassing both public intake validators
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:801`
- **Also:** System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:332-360 (the post-restore credential flow has the same gap), :761-780 (username loop)
- **What:** The FTS password loop only rejects blank: `while (p.length() == 0)` with the sole error "Password cannot be blank" (:787-808). `isValidPublicPassword` (System_User.h:161, System_User.cpp:813) — which enforces kPublicPasswordMinLen 6 and kPublicPasswordMaxLen 64 — is never called. The username loop (:761-780) likewise never calls `isValidPublicUsername`, so the reserved-name table at System_User.cpp:803 ("system", "AuthBypass", "bond-admin") is not applied, and the account is written `role = "superadmin"` (:893, and :262 in createInitialAdminUser).
- **Why it matters:** The single most privileged account on the device — the superadmin owner, which every other tier is measured against — is the one account created with no strength or name validation, while `useradd`, web self-registration and admin creation all enforce 6-64 characters and the reserved-name list. A one-character owner password is accepted; so is naming the owner `AuthBypass`, which is the sentinel that serial (HardwareOne.cpp:2575), OLED (OLED_Utils.cpp:2980) and BLE (Bluetooth.cpp:202) stamp when their require-auth toggle is off — turning every unauthenticated local command into superadmin.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards; prior art: The username half is memory/project_security_review_backlog.md item 1 ("AuthBypass can be a real account") — re-verified STILL OPEN at the cited lines. The password-strength half is not recorded there
- **Direction:** Call isValidPublicUsername and isValidPublicPassword in both FTS credential loops (the main one at :761/:787 and the post-restore one at :332/:351) and re-prompt with the returned errorOut string on rejection. They already produce short, user-safe messages.

### [MEDIUM] A stuck bus that reports ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT never triggers automatic recovery on any bus with fewer than three registered devices — which is every bus in the shipping config
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_I2C_Manager.cpp:480`
- **What:** There are exactly two escalation routes to `performBusRecovery`. (1) `I2CErrorType::BUS_ERROR` (wire status 4) escalates immediately (line 877). (2) `NACK` and `TIMEOUT` escalate only via `checkBusRecoveryNeeded()`, which is gated by `if (total < 3 || degradedCount < 2) continue;` (line 480) followed by `degradationPercent > 66.0f`. `classifyI2CError` maps wire status 5 — the value `Wire.cpp:471` produces for `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT`, which is what `i2c_master_transmit`/`i2c_master_receive` return when the controller cannot complete a transfer — to `TIMEOUT`, explicitly NOT to `BUS_ERROR` (the comment at lines 722-727 documents this as a deliberate fix). So a timeout-flavoured wedge depends entirely on the quorum path.
- **Why it matters:** The in-tree FeatherS3[D] config has `CUSTOM_ENABLE_OLED 1` + `CUSTOM_ENABLE_GAMEPAD 1` and everything else 0: bus 0 carries the seesaw gamepad (0x50) and bus 1 carries the OLED (0x3D), plus the MAX17048 fuel gauge on bus 0. Neither bus reaches `total >= 3` registered devices. If a slave holds SDA low and the driver reports ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT, `checkBusRecoveryNeeded()` logs "below quorum, no bus recovery" and returns — forever. The device's own health path degrades it, retries every 30 s (`isDegraded()`, line 898), fails, and re-degrades; the bit-bang SDA-release routine that would actually fix it is never reached. The quorum floor was added (per the comment at lines 474-479) to stop a 1-device bus from tearing itself down on a single degraded device, but the cure removed the only automatic path for exactly the scenario P26 is about.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all; acute on the in-tree FeatherS3[D] config where both buses have 1-2 registered devices
- **Direction:** Add a separate, narrow escalation for the stuck-bus signature that does not depend on device count: e.g. if a single device accumulates N consecutive TIMEOUTs (not NACKs — a NACK means the bus is clocking fine and the device is just absent), run `performBusRecovery(bus)` once, guarded by the existing `busRecoveryThrottled()` cooldown. Keep the quorum floor for the NACK cascade where it belongs.

### [MEDIUM] `ImageManager::saveImage` sets `success = true` without checking any of its four `f.write()` calls, and the free-space precheck only logs on the BOTH path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ImageManager.cpp:228`
- **Also:** System_ImageManager.cpp:242, :263, :278
- **What:** Four write sites — :228 (LittleFS, BOTH), :242 (SD, BOTH), :263 (SD only), :278 (LittleFS only) — all do `f.write(data, len); f.close(); success = true;` with the byte count discarded. This is the one writer in the tree with a genuine pre-write free-space gate (:180-208), but for `IMAGE_STORAGE_BOTH` both branches only `ERROR_STORAGEF` and fall through: the SD check at :185-192 explicitly comments "For BOTH, fall through to try LittleFS", and the LittleFS check at :202-207 only returns "" when `location == IMAGE_STORAGE_LITTLEFS`.
- **Why it matters:** A JPEG is typically 20-60 KB, well past the 4 KB stdio buffer, so an ENOSPC genuinely does show up as a short fwrite — the check would work, it just is not made. Instead saveImage returns the path, posts SYSEVT_PHOTO_SAVED (:294-297) and logs the full byte count, and then calls enforceMaxImages(location) at :300, which counts files on disk — the truncated one included — and calls deleteOldestImages() for the overflow (:473-483). Each silently-failed capture therefore costs one previously-good photo, and both the CLI and the web UI report the capture as successful. The LittleFS free-space precheck at :195-206 reduces but does not close the window (it is a whole-partition estimate taken before the write, and it does not apply to the SD branches).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: all (camera builds)
- **Direction:** Gate `success`/`savedLittleFS`/`savedSD` on `f.write(...) == len`, delete the partial file when it isn't, and make the BOTH path honour its own free-space verdict per tier instead of falling through on both.

### [MEDIUM] `WaypointManager::save` and `GPSTrackManager::saveTrack` discard their write returns, truncate in place with no staging, and return true
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:2427`
- **Also:** System_Maps.cpp:2962 (`f.print` of the CSV header, also unchecked); :3307 (merge path)
- **What:** `WaypointManager::save` opens `waypoints_<base>.json` with `"w"` at :2420 then `serializeJson(doc, f); f.close(); return true;` at :2427-2429 — the serialize count is not even captured. `GPSTrackManager::saveTrack` opens `track-<ts>.csv` with `"w"` at :2955 then loops `f.write((const uint8_t*)line, len)` per point at :2965 with the return discarded, `f.close()`, `INFO_MAPSF("Saved GPS track: %d points")`, `return true`.
- **Why it matters:** Both are truncate-in-place with no tmp+rename, so on a short write the destination is left mid-record and the previous contents are gone. For waypoints that is silent loss of every marker for that map — the file is the only copy, and the loader will fail to parse it — while the UI reports a successful save. For tracks, `_pointCount` can be in the thousands and the file is written after a session, so a full FS turns a completed recording into a truncated CSV with a success message and a log line stating the full point count. Both also run under `currentAuthContext()` rather than system auth, so a permission-shaped failure would surface the same way (open failure is checked, at least).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (maps builds)
- **Direction:** Route both through `writeTextAtomic` (waypoints already builds a JsonDocument, so serializing to a String first is cheap relative to the loss) and gate the success message and return value on the byte count.

### [MEDIUM] 12 of the 140 system-event kinds can never have a notification policy: the device-policy masks are 128 bits wide while SYSEVT_COUNT is 140, and the setter reports success anyway
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Notifications.cpp:137`
- **Also:** The same 128 ceiling silently truncates the JSON listing path at :435-438 and the text listing at :445-451, so `notifydevicekind list` cannot even display those kinds' true state.
- **What:** gNotifOffMask/gNotifAdminMask are uint32_t[4] = 128 bits (:126-127). maskSet() returns silently for kind >= 128 (:137) and maskTest() returns false for kind >= 128 (:134). notifPolicyLoad() explicitly skips any persisted entry with k >= 128 (:170). But cmd_notifydevicekind validates only `k <= 0 || k >= SYSEVT_COUNT` (:468), and SYSEVT_COUNT is 140 (139 X() rows in SYSEVT_KIND_LIST + SYSEVT_NONE, System_Events.h:250-296).
- **Why it matters:** Concrete: `notifydevicekind automation_deleted off`. systemEventKindFromName returns 138, which passes the k < 140 guard. maskSet(gNotifOffMask, 138, true) is a no-op. gNotifPrefsGen is bumped, notifPolicySave() succeeds (it writes nothing for that kind because maskTest is false), and the command returns "[Settings] Configuration updated" plus a broadcast "automation_deleted = off". Reading it back immediately (`notifydevicekind automation_deleted`) prints "all". Write -> reboot -> read loses the setting because the write never happened. Affected kinds are indexes 128-139: video_record_started, thermal_hot_alert, tof_object_detected, fm_tuned, ei_continuous_started, imu_walking, voice_disarmed, llm_model_unloaded, display_init_failed, automation_added, automation_deleted, automation_action_dropped. The `admin` level is equally inert, so those kinds stay visible to every viewer regardless of configuration. Corroborating drift signal: the comment at :431 still says "134 kinds", so the list grew past 128 after that code was written and nothing caught it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Widen the two masks to 8x uint32 (256 bits) to match the automation subscription mask that System_Events.h:298 already static_asserts against, and add a static_assert tying the mask width to SYSEVT_COUNT so the next kind added past the boundary is a build error rather than a silent no-op.

### [MEDIUM] Cleartext credentials reach log, broadcast and OLED sinks on four distinct paths
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4599`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:4478 / :4689 / :4765 / :4835 / :4843 / :4879 / :4946 (DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF prints the raw, unredacted command line and args); System_MQTT.cpp:441 (DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDSF prints the whole JSON payload including "pass"); Bluetooth.cpp:704 and :707-713
- **What:** (a) System_Utils.cpp:4599 `broadcastOutput("[REMOTE] Sent to bonded device: " + actualCommand)` echoes the inner command verbatim to serial/web/file/OLED/G2 with no redaction, and the function returns at :4600 before both logCommandExecution and the redacted logAuthAttempt at :4756 — so a `remote:` command is never audit-logged but its payload IS printed. (b) DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF at :4478 logs `cmd=%.80s` and at :4689 logs `(args: %s)`, both raw. (c) System_MQTT.cpp:441 logs the full inbound JSON. (d) Bluetooth.cpp:707-713 copies the first 28 characters of every inbound BLE line into `bleMessageHistory[4][32]` and renders it on the OLED — unconditionally, not debug-gated.
- **Why it matters:** `remote:login bob hunter2`, `@ useradd bob s3cret admin`, `remote:wifiadd MyAP wifipass` all print the secret in the clear and, when logging is running, write it to the log file. Every DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF site is behind `debugcommandflow`, a runtime-toggleable persisted flag (System_DebugFlags.h:163), so an operator turning on command tracing starts writing every typed password to serial, the web console and the log file — and those lines pass through the PSRAM debug pool (System_Debug.cpp:624) on the way. `RX:login bob hunter2` fits entirely in the 32-byte BLE history slot and appears on the physical display.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: remote:/@ path needs ENABLE_ESPNOW+ENABLE_BONDED_MODE; BLE history needs ENABLE_BLUETOOTH+ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY; the debug-flag sites are all boards; prior art: The MQTT payload log is memory/project_security_review_backlog.md item 6 — still open, unchanged. The other three sites are new.
- **Direction:** Route all four through the existing redactCmdForAudit chokepoint. For the remote path, redact the inner command in both the broadcast and the `out` string, and add the missing logCommandExecution call. For DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF, log `redactCmdForAudit(cmd)` rather than `cmd`. For BLE history, redact before snprintf. While there, note `espnowroomcmd`/`espnowtagcmd` are still missing from kRules (backlog item 5) and System_MQTT.cpp:493 builds an argBuf containing the cleartext MQTT password for them.

### [MEDIUM] `login <user> <pass> display` from any remote transport takes over the physical OLED console session
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:5214`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:439,461,467; components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:2977
- **What:** cmd_login (System_Utils.cpp:5202, registered requiresAdmin=false at System_User.cpp:3383) takes a free-text third argument and maps it to SOURCE_SERIAL / SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY / SOURCE_BLUETOOTH (:5219-5226) with no check that the caller's own transport matches. loginTransport (System_User.cpp:439) then writes the GLOBAL session state for the named transport: gLocalDisplayAuthed/gLocalDisplayUser + oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged() at :467-475, or gSerialAuthed/gSerialUser at :461.
- **Why it matters:** A command arriving over ESP-NOW, MQTT, BLE, the web CLI, a G2 tap or voice can carry `login alice hunter2 display`. The physical OLED in front of a completely different person is now logged in as alice, and every subsequent button press executes with alice's privileges (oledAuthContext, OLED_Utils.cpp:2977, reads gLocalDisplayUser). The mirror case is worse in the other direction: gSerialUser can be set the same way, silently converting an unattended serial console into an authenticated one. The idle-logout at HardwareOne.cpp:2489 does not help, because sessionStampNow() was just refreshed by the login.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY); prior art: recon_surfaces.md G4. Not in any docs/*.md.
- **Direction:** Reject a transport argument that differs from currentAuthContext().transport unless the caller is already an admin on that device — or drop the argument entirely and have each transport's own login front-door (the serial gate at HardwareOne.cpp:2497, the OLED login mode, bleFillCommandAuth) be the only way to mint that transport's session.

### [MEDIUM] remote: / @ forwarded commands are broadcast unredacted and are never written to the command audit log at all
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4599`
- **What:** On the remote-routing branch executeCommand does broadcastOutput("[REMOTE] Sent to bonded device: " + actualCommand) at :4599 and snprintf(out, outSize, "Remote command sent: %s", actualCommand.c_str()) at :4598, with no redactCmdForAudit call. It then returns at :4600 (success) or :4604 (failure) — before ever reaching logCommandExecution (:4729/:4741) or logAuthAttempt (:4757). The nested-wrapper reject at :4519 and the bond-token gate at :4558 are the only checks on this path.
- **Why it matters:** `@wifiadd MyNet <psk>`, `remote:useradd eve <pw> admin` or `@espnowsetpassphrase primary <phrase>` from any surface produces `[REMOTE] Sent to bonded device: wifiadd MyNet <psk>` in cleartext. broadcastOutput with routeOverride 0 resolves the route from the calling command's ctx.outputMask (System_Debug.cpp:896-899), which always contains MSG_ROUTE_FILE for serial/web/G2/BLE/MQTT origins — so the unredacted line is appended to the persisted system log whenever `log start` is active, plus serial or the web mirror depending on origin. Meanwhile command-audit.log records nothing at all for the remote path, so the surface that leaks the most is also the one with no audit trail.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE
- **Direction:** Wrap actualCommand in redactCmdForAudit for both the broadcast and the returned result string, and add a logCommandExecution call on the remote branch so forwarded commands are audited like local ones.

### [MEDIUM] remote:/@ forwarding still runs after authorizeCommand at HEAD — the escalation step for both remote chains
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4499`
- **Also:** authorizeCommand call at System_Utils.cpp:4481; the comment that documents the design decision at :4530; peer-side identity stamp at System_ESPNow.cpp:5779 (`strncpy(username, kBondAdminUser, ...)`), which `isSuperAdminUser` treats as super via System_User.cpp:365.
- **What:** `authorizeCommand(ctx, command, ...)` runs at :4481 on the full raw line. The `remote:` / `remote ` / `@` prefix is stripped afterwards at :4499-4515. `findCommand()` cannot resolve a line beginning with the prefix, so `commandRequiresAdmin` and `commandRequiresSuperAdmin` both return false (System_Utils.cpp:3244) and gates 4 and 5 never fire. The only guard is the nested-wrapper reject at :4519.
- **Why it matters:** This converts any credential a remote attacker obtains — through the lockout-eviction bypass on the web, or the unthrottled BLE login path — from User tier into Super Admin on the bonded peer in one step. `@factoryreset`, `@userdelete`, `@fileread "/system/users.json"` all execute as `kBondAdminUser`. Guests are correctly blocked (gate 3 fires first), so the prerequisite is exactly one non-guest account, which is what the two chains above deliver.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE for the forwarding leg; the gate hole itself is unconditional); prior art: KNOWN — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.2 ("remote:/@ forwarding skips the local role check — MEDIUM") and priority action #10. Re-verified unchanged at HEAD; reported only because it is the escala
- **Direction:** Already prescribed by §4.2: authorize the unwrapped inner command against the local identity, or require super to forward at all. Noting only that the fix now also closes the tail of two credential-guessing chains, which raises its priority relative to the MEDIUM it currently carries.

### [MEDIUM] ESP-NOW senders report "sent" when the frame was only parked in the pending ring, and executeCommand's remote: path drops the status out-param that exists to distinguish the two
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4597`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:1845-1846,1891-1892 (return true on failed kick); :14211, :14621, :14359 (comment)
- **What:** v4_send_encrypted_or_queue() has an explicit `outStatus` out-param whose whole purpose is to report "sent" vs "queued" vs "queued; KEY_EX kick failed (no enabled mesh?)" vs "queued; SESSION_OPEN kick failed" (System_ESPNow.cpp:1820-1907). It returns TRUE for all of the queued variants, including :1845-1846 where the kick failed and the frame is guaranteed to be eaten by the timeout sweep. executeCommand's remote: branch calls it without passing outStatus at all (:4594-4595) and answers "Remote command sent: %s" (:4598). The same conflation runs through espnowtx::sendAeadSync — whose success is v4_send_payload_smart's, i.e. wire-send OR queue-acceptance — into cmd_espnow_remote/browse/fetch ("File browse request sent to %s", :14211) and cmd_espnow_send ("Encrypted message sent via SESSION_FRAME", :14621). System_ESPNow.cpp:14359 comments "wire-delivery confirmed above" over a sendAeadSync result that may be pure queue acceptance.
- **Why it matters:** With no enabled mesh, the KEY_EX kick fails, the frame is dropped by the pending-ring timeout, and the user is told "OK: Remote command sent". The status string that would have said "queued; KEY_EX kick failed (no enabled mesh?)" was computed and thrown away at the call site.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all for the espnow* commands; the remote:/@ branch is additionally gated on ENABLE_BONDED_MODE, which is 0 by default (System_BuildConfig.h:320); prior art: docs/ESPNOW_MESH_SYSTEM.md §303/§320 documents the general "wire-delivery is mistaken for command success" flaw and names sendAeadSync. The specific fact that executeCommand's bond path discards an av
- **Direction:** Pass a status buffer at System_Utils.cpp:4594 and fold it into the reply ("queued for handshake" vs "sent"). Consider making the failed-kick paths return false — the frame provably cannot be delivered.

### [MEDIUM] remote:/@ command forwarding is authorized on the un-stripped wrapper line, so any non-guest local principal runs commands as bond-admin (super) on the paired peer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4505`
- **Also:** authorizeCommand runs on the raw line at System_Utils.cpp:4481 before the prefix strip at :4505; peer stamps kBondAdminUser (super) at System_ESPNow.cpp:5779; comment admitting no re-check at :4535
- **What:** executeCommand strips a leading remote:/remote /@ AFTER authorizeCommand has already run. findCommand() cannot resolve the wrapper token, so commandRequiresAdmin/SuperAdmin both return false and gates 4/5 never fire. On the bonded peer the forwarded command executes as kBondAdminUser, which is treated as Super Admin.
- **Why it matters:** A plain User (or a non-super Admin, or an AuthBypass identity on a box with require-auth off) can send '@factoryreset' or '@useradd ...' and have it run at Super Admin on the bonded peer — a confused-deputy cross-device escalation. Guests are blocked (guest gate fires first on the wrapper), so this is a User-tier issue, not a guest->admin path; it matters most as the cross-device privilege amplifier once any non-guest foothold exists.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_ESPNOW + ENABLE_BONDED_MODE; prior art: KNOWN — PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md 4.2 / recon_surfaces.md G1
- **Direction:** Parse and strip the remote:/@ prefix first, then run authorizeCommand on the UNWRAPPED inner command against the local caller's identity; or gate remote forwarding behind hasSuperAdminPrivilege(local).

### [MEDIUM] Automation logging writes the raw sub-command line unredacted while the parallel command-audit log redacts the identical string
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4556`
- **Also:** System_Automation.cpp:100 DEBUGF(DEBUG_AUTOMATIONS, "[autos queue] Queueing cmd='%s' ...") prints the same raw line
- **What:** Inside executeCommand, when automation logging is active: snprintf(logBuf, sizeof(logBuf), "[%s] %s", cmdCtx->automationName, cmd); appendAutoLogEntry("COMMAND", logBuf). `cmd` is the verbatim command line. Roughly 190 lines later the same function hands the same string to logCommandExecution (:4747), which does apply redactCmdForAudit before writing /system/sys_logs/command-audit.log. appendAutoLogEntry (System_Utils.cpp:973) appends to gAutomationLogFile under the captured owner's AuthContext, and the log path is operator-chosen at `autolog start`.
- **Why it matters:** An automation whose action is a credential-bearing command — `wifiadd "HomeNet" "correcthorse"`, `login svc svcpw display`, `userresetpassword bob newpw`, `mqttpassword …` — writes that password in cleartext into the automation log, on unencrypted flash, while the command-audit log for the same execution shows `wifiadd "HomeNet" ***`. Because the destination path is operator-supplied, it can easily be somewhere the rule table exposes more widely than /system/sys_logs/ (which is admin-read-only): anything under the default catch-all rule, e.g. /autolog.txt at the LittleFS root, is PERM_ALL for every authenticated user. The output half of the same feature (:4744, :4820) has the same gap.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_AUTOMATION)
- **Direction:** Pass redactCmdForAudit(cmd) into the COMMAND entry and redactOutputForLog(out) into the OUTPUT entries, so the automation log matches the audit log's disclosure level.

### [MEDIUM] Guest can seize or destroy the physical OLED session and revoke all BLE sessions via `logout display` / `login … display` / `logout bluetooth`
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4356`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:5202 (cmd_login accepts a transport arg), :5317 (cmd_logout); components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:438 (loginTransport SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY), :502 (logoutTransport), :527 (bleRevokeAllSessions); components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:3153 (oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged)
- **What:** Because `commandAllowedForGuest` ignores arguments, a guest may run `logout display`, `logout bluetooth`, and `login <user> <pass> display`. `logoutTransport(SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY)` clears `gLocalDisplayAuthed`/`gLocalDisplayUser`; `logoutTransport(SOURCE_BLUETOOTH)` calls `bleRevokeAllSessions()`. `loginTransport(SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY, …)` sets the *global* display session to whatever account's credentials were supplied. `oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged()` only forces `OLED_LOGIN` when `shouldBlockForDisplayAuth()` is true, and only pushes to `OLED_MENU` when `currentOLEDMode == OLED_LOGIN`; on a guest login from another transport it clears the mode back-stack but leaves `currentOLEDMode` exactly where it was.
- **Why it matters:** An admin is logged in at the OLED, sitting on OLED_SETTINGS. A guest on serial (or BLE/MQTT/ESP-NOW) sends `login guestuser guestpass display`. The admin's display session is silently replaced with a guest session, the mode stack is cleared, but the screen stays on OLED_SETTINGS. The admin's console is now a guest console. `logout display` alone is a pure denial-of-service against the physical operator; `logout bluetooth` kills every BLE session. All three are state mutations on transports the guest does not own, performed by an account whose documented contract is "authenticated but view-only".
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY / ENABLE_BLUETOOTH)
- **Direction:** Same root fix as the `logout g2` finding: have `commandAllowedForGuest` reject any argument-bearing `login`/`logout` form. Additionally, `oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged` should re-run the guest mode gate (force `OLED_MENU` when the new session is a guest and the current mode is not guest-allowed), so a session downgrade can never leave a guest parked on a privileged screen.

### [MEDIUM] Several debug flags dump full command lines and arguments — including passwords — to every sink and to the persistent system log
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4763`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:321/368/373 print the full session sid under DEBUG_AUTH; System_ESPNow.cpp:896 prints the inner bonded command in full (the token itself is correctly cut to 8 hex chars)
- **What:** executeCommand logs the resolved arguments verbatim: `DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF("[registry_exec] executing: %s (args: %s)", found->name, args.c_str())` at :4763 and the identical DEBUGF(DEBUG_CLI, ...) at :4764. Also :4545 (`cmd=%.80s`), :4839, :4953 (`cmd.line='%s'`, untruncated), :5020 (`cmd=%s`, untruncated); System_Command.cpp:262; WebServer_Server.cpp:3205 (raw POST body, `buf[0-79]`) and :3218 (decoded command); System_MQTT.cpp:441 `DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDSF("Command payload: %s", payload.c_str())` where payload is the whole `{"user":..,"pass":..,"cmd":..}` JSON; System_Automation.cpp:100. DEBUGF_QUEUE_DEBUG requires getLogLevel() >= LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG, and the persisted logLevel defaults to 3 = DEBUG (System_Settings.cpp:1860) — so only the flag matters. debugQueuePrintf enqueues with MSG_ROUTE_ALL (System_Debug.cpp:800), and MSG_ROUTE_FILE messages are written to the open system log when `log start` is active (System_Debug.cpp:254-283).
- **Why it matters:** Enable DEBUG_CLI (or DEBUG_CMD_FLOW) and run `log start`. A subsequent `login alice hunter7` emits `[registry_exec] executing: login (args: alice hunter7)` to serial, the web mirror, and permanently into the log file on unencrypted flash. With DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDS, every inbound MQTT command emits the broker password holder's cleartext credentials the same way. WebServer_Server.cpp:3205 leaks the URL-encoded POST body before decoding, which contains the same secret.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: The MQTT one is KNOWN — memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 6 (recorded at ~:385; it has drifted to :441). The rest are not documented.
- **Direction:** Route these through redactCmdForAudit before formatting — the redaction already exists and is cheap relative to a debug build. For System_MQTT.cpp:441, log only the parsed `cmd` field rather than the raw payload, since `pass` sits in the same JSON object.

### [MEDIUM] Command success is inferred from an "Error" string prefix, so ~13 ESP-NOW failure paths (and others) are stamped "OK: " and audited as OK
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4802`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:10717,14144,14148,14152,14184,14202,14224,14228,14232,14265,14281,14303,14307,14312,14358; components/hardwareone/System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:590; components/hardwareone/System_Automation.cpp:2533,2541
- **What:** The single success determination for both the always-on audit log and the OK:/Error: stamp is `strncmp(out,"Error",5) != 0 && strncmp(out,"ERROR",5) != 0` (:4743, :4802). Any handler whose failure text does not start with those five bytes is recorded as a success. In System_ESPNow.cpp the espnowAckErr() helper pairs each human string with a JSON sibling: the JSON side correctly emits ok:false while the human side does not carry the prefix at :14144, :14148, :14152, :14184, :14202, :14224, :14228, :14232, :14265, :14281, :14303, :14307, :14312, :14358 — with :14188 in the same function correctly using "Error:", proving the inconsistency is accidental.
- **Why it matters:** `espnowbrowse gold u p` with ESP-NOW closed returns "ESP-NOW not initialized. Run 'openespnow' first." → stampOkStatus prepends "OK: " (System_Utils.cpp:4505) → the user sees "OK: ESP-NOW not initialized" and command-audit.log records `[123] admin@web espnowbrowse ... -> OK`. Same for `espnowprobe <peer>` on timeout (:10717 "no response within 500ms from ... peer offline"), `features nosuch on` (System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:590 "Unknown feature."), and the automation parser's "Missing condition after 'IF'" (System_Automation.cpp:2533/:2541). The audit log — the artifact you reach for after an incident — cannot distinguish these from real successes.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/LLM_SETTINGS_AUDIT.md R5 identified one instance of this class (llm sync generation). The ESP-NOW cluster and the systemic framing are new.
- **Direction:** Prefix-checking a prose string is the wrong contract for a machine signal. Short term, sweep the non-conforming returns onto "Error: ". Long term, give CommandEntry handlers a way to report status out of band (a thread-local status flag set by a helper, or a return struct) so the audit log stops inferring it from the first five bytes of English.

### [MEDIUM] WiFi IDLE-bug recovery discards every `esp_wifi_*` return into debug-only logs; the final "ensure usable state" reinit discards them entirely, so the radio can be left permanently deinitialized with no error surfaced
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1044`
- **Also:** System_WiFi.cpp:1153-1155 (bare statements); :748, :902, :1025, :1027, :1037 (disconnect/stop/connect)
- **What:** The nuclear-reinit branch (System_WiFi.cpp:1035-1060) calls `esp_wifi_disconnect()`, `esp_wifi_stop()`, `esp_wifi_deinit()` with returns fully discarded, then assigns `esp_wifi_init`/`set_mode`/`start`/`set_config`/`connect` to `err` — but the only consumer of `err` is `DEBUG_WIFIF`, which compiles to nothing when `DEBUG_WIFI` is off (the normal state). The recovery block at :1150-1156 doesn't even assign: `esp_wifi_init(&cfg); esp_wifi_set_mode(WIFI_MODE_STA); esp_wifi_start();` as bare statements.
- **Why it matters:** The function has already destroyed the driver with `esp_wifi_deinit()` at :1039 before attempting `esp_wifi_init()` at :1044. `esp_wifi_init` returns ESP_ERR_NO_MEM when it cannot claim its internal-DRAM buffers — and this file's own comment at :1119-1122 documents exactly that risk ("the next attempt's deinit/reinit can hit malloc-buffer-fail when BLE is also active"). If it fails, nothing notices: the loop keeps polling `WiFi.status()` against a nonexistent driver for the remaining 12 s timeout, then :1150 sees `esp_wifi_get_mode() != ESP_OK`, retries the same three unchecked calls, and if those fail too the function returns false with the generic "Connection failed after 3 attempts". The radio is now gone for the rest of the session — `openwifi`, `wifiscan`, ESP-NOW re-init and the HTTP server all fail downstream with unrelated-looking errors, and nothing in any log says the driver was never re-created.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Distinct from docs/WIFI_RADIO_OWNERSHIP_PLAN.md D4, which is about `esp_wifi_deinit` without a prior `esp_now_deinit`. This is the discarded-return half of the same block.
- **Direction:** At minimum promote the reinit failures to `WARN_WIFIF` + `logSystemEvent` + a `SYSEVT`, and set a sticky "wifi driver dead" flag that `wifistatus` and the OLED/G2 network pages read, so the operator sees the real cause instead of three layers of downstream symptoms. A failed `esp_wifi_init` should also abort the attempt loop rather than continuing to poll `WiFi.status()`.

### [MEDIUM] wifiscan and the openwifi connect ladder channel-hop the shared radio for seconds-to-minutes with no ESP-NOW guard, warning, or post-scan re-pin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:614`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:283-320 (connectToBestWiFiNetwork loops every saved network); :856-1010 (connectWiFiIndex: WIFI_FAST_SCAN, 3 attempts x 12 s with 1.5/3/6 s backoff); :427 (closewifi disables the runtime hunt — the fixed half)
- **What:** cmd_wifiscan calls WiFi.scanNetworks(async=false, hidden=true) unconditionally after ensureWiFiInitialized(), with no check of isEspNowInitialized(). connectWiFiIndex configures WIFI_FAST_SCAN and then runs up to 3 attempts x 12 s per SSID, and connectToBestWiFiNetwork iterates every saved network with a 2 s gap. All of this moves the radio off the ESP-NOW channel. Nothing warns the operator, nothing forces a re-pin when the scan finishes, and the periodic re-sync (System_ESPNow.cpp:8444) is on a 15 s timer with the hardware-reading branch disabled in the default auto mode.
- **Why it matters:** Running `wifiscan` on a node holding an ESP-NOW mesh blacks the mesh out for the whole blocking scan — peers registered with channel 0 transmit on whatever channel the scan happens to be on, so TX is lost as well as RX. `openwifi --best` with several saved-but-absent SSIDs hops channels for minutes. Combined with finding 1, in the default auto channel mode the position the radio happens to land in after the scan then becomes the node's new "correct" ESP-NOW channel, so the outage does not self-heal — it is ratified. The user sees a mesh that stopped working shortly after they ran a scan, with no log line connecting the two.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Adjacent to docs/WIFI_RADIO_OWNERSHIP_PLAN.md D1 ("that reconnect scan hops channels"), which was fixed for closewifi only — the explicit scan/connect paths were never covered
- **Direction:** Have cmd_wifiscan and the connect ladder emit a one-line warning when isEspNowInitialized() is true, and force an unconditional espnowApplyChannel(espnowComputeChannel()) against the hardware channel (not the cached one) once the scan or the connect ladder finishes.

### [MEDIUM] cmd_wifiscan blacks out the mesh for seconds and neither warns nor flags an ESP-NOW channel re-sync afterwards
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:615`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Network.cpp:776 (same scan from the G2 lens), components/hardwareone/System_SetupWizard.cpp:1279, components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:185, components/hardwareone/OLED_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:364
- **What:** cmd_wifiscan calls WiFi.scanNetworks(/*async=*/false, /*hidden=*/true) with no reference to ESP-NOW state anywhere in the function (604-662). A synchronous all-channel active scan dwells ~120 ms per channel across the country's 11 channels (IDF wifi.rst:652-654), so the radio is off the ESP-NOW channel for roughly 1.5-3 s. After the scan the function does not call espnowNoteWifiChannelMayHaveChanged() and does not re-pin the channel; recovery is left entirely to the 15 s safety-net tick in processMeshHeartbeats (8444).
- **Why it matters:** Running `wifiscan` while ESP-NOW is up silently drops every inbound frame for the scan duration, and any in-flight fragmented transfer exceeds its ~1 s per-fragment ACK budget (System_ESPNow.cpp:2145-2146) and aborts outright (2227-2232) with no resume. If the scan leaves the radio parked off the ESP-NOW home channel, the mesh stays down for up to a further 15 s in pinned mode — and in the default auto mode the next tick adopts the post-scan channel as the new permanent mesh home instead of correcting it (see the auto-channel finding above). The command's output says nothing about any of this. It is also registered non-admin (1606), so any authenticated user can trigger it repeatedly.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Call espnowNoteWifiChannelMayHaveChanged() immediately after every scanNetworks() so the mesh channel is re-derived on the next 10 ms tick rather than up to 15 s later, and mention the brief mesh interruption in the command's response when isEspNowInitialized().

### [LOW] `millis() > deadline` absolute-deadline comparisons — ~20 sites that misbehave for one interval every 49.7 days
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:1283`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPSR.cpp:1283, :2280; OLED_UI.cpp:175; OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:238, :1173; OLED_Mode_ChangePassword.cpp:160; OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:114, :208, :237, :280, :328; OLED_Utils.cpp:3076, :3065; G2_Pet.cpp:470, :490; G2_Glasses.cpp:7998, :8755, :9075, :22212, :22393, :22605; System_ESPNow.cpp:3362, :3421, :8325; System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:352, :837; System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712; System_ESPNow.cpp:2961 and System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:215 (LRU eviction picking by absolute `millis()`).
- **What:** A cluster of sites compare `millis()` directly against a stored `millis() + N` deadline instead of using the unsigned/signed-difference form the rest of the codebase uses. Representative: System_ESPSR.cpp:1283 `if (millis() > gSrSnipSessionDeadlineMs)` and :2280 `if (millis() > commandTimeoutMs)`; OLED_UI.cpp:175 `if (active && millis() > gOledToast.expireMs)`; System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:837 `if (s.prevKeysValidUntilMs != 0 && nowMs > s.prevKeysValidUntilMs)`; System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712 `(uint32_t)now >= gSensorLeaseExpiresAt[i]`; System_ESPNow.cpp:3362/3421/8325 (topology window). I traced each: because the deadline is always `millis() + small`, the wrapped deadline is always SMALL while `millis()` is still LARGE, so every one of these fails in the SAFE direction — the timeout is treated as already elapsed.
- **Why it matters:** For the ~N ms straddling the 49.7-day wrap: a voice-command session or snippet window aborts instantly instead of running its timeout; an OLED toast vanishes the frame it is drawn; the ESP-NOW rekey previous-key retention window (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:837) is zeroed the moment it is created, so a peer that had not yet applied a REKEY has its in-flight frames rejected instead of being decrypted under the retained key; a remote-sensor lease (System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712) expires immediately and stops the stream; an in-flight topology discovery finalizes with partial results. All self-correct on the next cycle. No hang, no security relaxation — I checked the reverse direction (small `millis()` vs large stored deadline) and it is not reachable from `deadline = millis() + N`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Convert to the house forms already used correctly at System_ESPNow.cpp:4643 (`(int32_t)(until - (uint32_t)millis()) > 0`) and System_Utils.h:205 (`everyMs`). Priority order if you only fix some: System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:837 (crypto retention window), System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712 (lease), then the UI ones.

### [LOW] Three OLED input handlers perform direct, non-command mutations with no oledGuestBlocksMutate() guard, relying solely on the transition-time mode gate
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Microphone_OLED.h:89`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Sensors.cpp:487 (i2cDiagInputHandler — direct blocking bus sweep, no guard); components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_SetPattern.cpp:174 (handleSetPatternInput — no guard); components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:3162 (requestOLEDMode guest gate fires only on a mode change)
- **What:** `microphoneInputHandler` calls `initMicrophone()`/`stopMicrophone()` and `startRecording()`/`stopRecording()` directly — `startRecording()` opens a WAV file and spawns a record task. `i2cDiagInputHandler` arms a blocking two-bus 1..126 address sweep. `handleSetPatternInput` writes a gamepad pattern password. None of the three is command-routed, so the `authorizeCommand` guest gate cannot help, and none calls `oledGuestBlocksMutate()`. Their only protection is `requestOLEDMode`'s guest gate, which rewrites a non-allowlisted target to OLED_MENU — but that gate fires only on a mode *transition*.
- **Why it matters:** Chained with the session-seizure finding above: an operator is on OLED_MICROPHONE; a remote guest sends `login guestuser guestpass display`; `oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged` clears the back-stack but leaves `currentOLEDMode == OLED_MICROPHONE` because it is not OLED_LOGIN. The display is now a guest session parked on a mode that would never have been reachable by navigation, and `microphoneInputHandler` will happily power the mic and open a WAV recording on the next Y press. Every other mutate-capable OLED mode I checked (settings editor, quick settings, user manager, automations wizard, LED, logging, power, network, bluetooth, ESP-NOW, LLM, remote, unified menu, FM radio, R1 health, file browser) guards at the top of its handler or routes through executeOLEDCommand — these three are the outliers.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY (+ PDM mic / ENABLE_I2C_SYSTEM respectively)
- **Direction:** Add `if (oledGuestBlocksMutate()) return true;` at the top of all three handlers, matching the convention used by the other 20+ modes. Belt-and-braces: have `oledNotifyLocalDisplayAuthChanged` re-assert the guest mode gate on any session change, not just when arriving from OLED_LOGIN.

### [LOW] remote:/@ command forwarding still skips the local role check — but the code now documents this as deliberate, and the path is compiled out in the current build
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4531`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4521-4527 (the nested-wrapper reject that partially mitigates)
- **What:** `authorizeCommand` runs at System_Utils.cpp:4481 on the FULL raw line, before the `remote:`/`@` prefix is stripped at :4548/:4552. `findCommand` cannot resolve a leading `@`, so `commandRequiresAdmin`/`commandRequiresSuperAdmin` both miss and the line passes. On the peer the payload executes under `kBondAdminUser`, which `isSuperAdminUser` (System_User.cpp:363-367) grants super for the life of a valid bond session. A nested-wrapper reject was ADDED at :4521-4527 to stop the origin→peer→origin loop, and the comment at :4531-4537 now explicitly states the local caller's role is not re-checked and explains why ("the bond session token IS the trust").
- **Why it matters:** With ENABLE_BONDED_MODE=1, a plain non-admin User (Guests are still blocked, since the guest gate at :4323 is name-independent) types `@factoryreset` and it executes as super on the bonded peer — a confused deputy. The nested-wrapper reject closes the loop-back-to-origin escalation but not the forward one.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all — currently INERT: System_BuildConfig.h:320 sets `#define ENABLE_BONDED_MODE 0`, so the `#if ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE` block at :4539 compiles to the `Error: Bond mode not available` stub at :4607; prior art: KNOWN — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.2, rated MEDIUM.
