STATUS CHANGED twice since: the nested-wrapper escalation it did not mention is now blocked, and the residual behaviour is now explicitly do
- **Direction:** If the design decision stands, record it in the threat model rather than leaving it to a code comment. If not, authorize the UNWRAPPED inner command against the local identity before forwarding.

### [LOW] /api/cli silently truncates the command line at 2047 bytes and the reply at 4095 bytes
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4810`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:4899 (`submitCommandAsync`) is the identical copy; System_Utils.cpp:4658 (help-mode dispatch) uses `snprintf(out, outSize, "%s", commandResult)` and silently truncates results where the main path at :4710 reports an explicit error.
- **What:** `submitAndExecuteSync` copies the caller's line with `strncpy(r->line, cmd.line.c_str(), sizeof(r->line) - 1)` at :4810 into `ExecReq::line[2048]` (System_CommandTypes.h:105), and the reply comes back through `ExecReq::out[CMD_RESULT_MAX]` = 4096 (:97, :107). Neither the truncation of the input nor the truncation of the output sets a flag, returns an error, or appends a marker; `handleCLICommand` (WebServer_Server.cpp:3253) reports the result as success. `submitCommandAsync` has the identical strncpy at :4900.
- **Why it matters:** The inbound ceilings are larger than 2047: ESP-NOW reassembles up to V4_REASM_MAX_MSG = 4200 bytes and `/api/cli` accepts 4095. A remote command longer than 2047 bytes runs as a prefix of itself. The outbound side of the same pipeline explicitly refuses over-long payloads with "Error: result too large for this transport" (System_Utils.cpp:4712); the inbound side is silent, so the contract is enforced in one direction only.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards; affects every surface that uses submitAndExecuteSync, /api/cli included; prior art: The 4095 output cap is described in docs/LLM_GUIDED_MENU_SPEC.md:157 and docs/COMMAND_RESULT_DELIVERY_PLAN.md:16 as a known reference capacity; the 2047 INPUT truncation is not documented anywhere I f
- **Direction:** Reject at submit time when `cmd.line.length() >= sizeof(r->line)` and return false with the same loud shape the result path already uses, so callers can report it rather than executing a mangled line.

### [LOW] submitAndExecuteSync is labelled "deadlock avoidance" but has no self-submission guard, unlike the equivalent ESP-NOW TX path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4838`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:5025 (execCommandUnified), :5049 (runUnifiedSystemCommand), :5065 (executeUnifiedWebCommand) all funnel here
- **What:** The function is headed "Queued command execution with deadlock avoidance", but it unconditionally posts an ExecReq to gCmdExecQ and then blocks 60 s on r->done (:4920). There is no `xTaskGetCurrentTaskHandle() == gCmdExecTaskHandle` check. cmd_exec_task is single-threaded (HardwareOne.cpp:704 pulls one request at a time), so a submission from a handler already running on cmd_exec_task can only be satisfied by the task that is blocked waiting for it. Compare espnowtx::submitSync (System_ESPNow_Tx.cpp:196-199), which does have exactly that guard.
- **Why it matters:** Any future call chain that reaches submitAndExecuteSync / execCommandUnified / runUnifiedSystemCommand / executeUnifiedWebCommand from a command handler stalls cmd_exec_task for a full 60 s, during which every CLI, web, BLE, OLED and G2 command queues behind it and every ESP-NOW deferred crypto job (SESSION_OPEN/CONFIRM/REKEY, System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1011/:1030/:1343) is dropped once the 6-slot queue fills. I could not find a live path today — the automation engine deliberately uses submitCommandAsync (System_Automation.cpp:116) and System_Automation.cpp:1072-1074 documents avoiding exactly this recursion — so the exposure is latent, not active. It is worth closing because the header comment currently tells a reader the protection exists.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Add the same early-return guard espnowtx::submitSync uses: if the caller is gCmdExecTaskHandle, fall through to the direct executeCommand path already implemented at :4842-4862 for the pre-queue boot case, rather than enqueueing and waiting.

### [LOW] readText() returns true on a silently truncated read — the length is never verified against the file size
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:795`
- **Also:** System_Filesystem.cpp:1758 readTextLimited has the same unconditional `return true`
- **What:** `readText` does `out = f.readString(); f.close(); return true;` with no comparison against `f.size()`. `f.readString()` is the inherited Stream::readString, which accumulates one byte at a time via `String::operator+=`; String::concat returns 0 on a failed reserve and readString ignores it, so an internal-DRAM shortage yields a short String and no error. With CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384 every String under 16 KB is internal DRAM, and the audit history records steady-state free internal heap of 29-39 KB with LLM loaded.
- **Why it matters:** Every caller treats `readText(...) == true` as "I have the whole file". I traced the state-file consumers and they happen to fail closed — setUserRoleInternal (:1594-1599) and deleteUserInternal bail on the missing `]`, sanitizeAutomationsJson (System_Automation.cpp:407-413) parses with ArduinoJson and returns false, isAdminUser/isSuperAdminUser fail closed — so I could not confirm a live data-loss path through readText specifically. The residual risk is any future read-modify-write caller that does not re-validate, and the wrong contract is the bug.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — prior art: HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md item 1 covers readText's per-byte cost, not its truthfulness
- **Direction:** Capture f.size() before the read and return false when out.length() != size. Fixing the per-byte inefficiency (bypassing Stream::readString) and the truncation contract is the same edit.

### [LOW] Log overflow routes writes to an SD card that is mounted but proven non-writable, discarding them entirely
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_VFS.cpp:628`
- **Also:** System_ImageManager.cpp:185 and System_SensorLogging.cpp:1183 make the same mounted-implies-writable assumption.
- **What:** After the overflow latch fires, the tier decision is `if (!isSDAvailable()) { ...primary... }` — `isSDAvailable()` returns `gSdMounted` only (:274). The module already maintains a separate, properly probed `gSdWritable` / `isSDWritable()` (write + readback + delete of /HWPROBE.TMP, :219-274) with lazy re-probe, and that is what the rest of the code uses to decide whether SD writes will work.
- **Why it matters:** With a read-only or bad-sector SD inserted and LittleFS below the 100 KB reserve, the overflow latch redirects errors.log, system-events.log, events.log, the login logs, i2c_errors.log and the sensor log to /sd/..., where every open or write fails. The latch is one-way per boot, so from that point until reboot the device produces no persisted logs at all — worse than the no-SD fallback, which at least keeps writing to flash. The probe result that would have prevented it was already computed at mount time.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards with SD_CS_PIN defined
- **Direction:** Use isSDWritable() rather than isSDAvailable() in resolveOverflowPath's :628 check (it already has a lazy re-probe), so a non-writable card falls back to the primary path instead of latching onto a dead tier.

### [LOW] `cmd_httpstop` discards `httpd_stop()` and clears the handle regardless, so a failed stop leaves the web server serving with no way to reach it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1276`
- **Also:** System_WiFi.cpp:411, :505
- **What:** `cmd_httpstop` (System_WiFi.cpp:1268-1285) calls `httpd_stop(server)` at :1276 with the `esp_err_t` discarded, then unconditionally `server = NULL; gServerIsHttps = false;` and returns "[HTTPS]/[HTTP] Server stopped successfully". Same pattern at :411 (`cmd_wifidisconnect`) and :505 (radio power-down).
- **Why it matters:** `httpd_stop` returns ESP_FAIL when `cs_send_to_ctrl_sock` cannot deliver the shutdown datagram to the control socket (esp-idf/components/esp_http_server/src/httpd_main.c:557-561) — reachable under lwIP socket pressure, and this build deliberately runs near the limit (`max_open_sockets = 8` against `LWIP_MAX_SOCKETS = 16`, per the sizing comment at WebServer_Server.cpp:5216-5223). In that case the httpd task keeps running with all ~117 handlers attached and the listener still bound, while the application has thrown away the only handle that could ever stop it. `httpstatus` reports `running: false`, the authenticated admin UI stays reachable, and a subsequent `httpstart` binds a second server or fails on the port. An operator who runs `httpstop` to take the web surface down is told it worked.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all (HTTP server builds)
- **Direction:** Only NULL the handle when `httpd_stop` returns ESP_OK; on failure report the error and keep the handle so a retry is possible. Note the HTTPS/plain distinction is *not* a bug here — I checked esp-idf/components/esp_https_server/src/https_server.c:437-444 and `httpd_ssl_stop` is `httpd_stop` plus an event dispatch, with the SSL context freed by `httpd_stop`'s `global_transport_ctx_free_fn` call at httpd_main.c:578-586.

### [NOTE] gCLIState (help mode) is a process-global shared by every command surface, so one surface's help navigation changes another's dispatch
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4645`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:296; components/hardwareone/System_Command.cpp:250
- **What:** gCLIState is a single global read inside executeCommand at :4645 (and by executeCommandThroughRegistry at System_Command.cpp:250). Because cmd_exec_task serializes every transport through one executeCommand, a `help` typed on serial leaves gCLIState non-normal for the next MQTT, ESP-NOW, G2 or web command, which is then routed through the exit-help branch instead of the normal one. This is the same single-global-shared-across-surfaces defect class as the confirm-mode finding, but for help.
- **Why it matters:** No privilege consequence today: the only privilege-relevant consumer of gCLIState is adminRequiredForLine (System_Utils.cpp:4270), which exempts the module names system/wifi/automations/espnow/sensors/settings from the admin check while in help mode — and that function has ZERO callers (only a stale declaration at HardwareOne.cpp:296). authorizeCommand calls commandRequiresAdmin directly and never consults gCLIState. So the exemption is dead code. Filing it because the dead exemption is a loaded gun: wiring adminRequiredForLine back in as the gate would let any caller run `settings`/`espnow`/`wifi` module commands without admin simply by preceding them with `help`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Delete adminRequiredForLine and its declaration — it is unreachable and its help-mode exemption is not a policy anyone would choose deliberately. Making gCLIState per-transport is a larger change and is only cosmetic today, but is worth noting alongside sActiveMode when the confirm-ownership fix is done, since both are the same shared-slot pattern.

### [NOTE] wifitxpower silently changes ESP-NOW and softAP range too, with no mention and no persistence
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:675`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:684-696 (wifigettxpower — the only way to notice the revert)
- **What:** cmd_wifitxpower clamps the requested value to the raw range 8..84 (roughly 2..21 dBm) and calls esp_wifi_set_max_tx_power(), which is a PHY-wide setting shared by the STA, the softAP and ESP-NOW. The response text ("TX power set to %.2f dBm") names only WiFi. The value is runtime-only and is not restored after the esp_wifi_deinit/esp_wifi_init cycles in connectWiFiIndex (1039, 1153).
- **Why it matters:** `wifitxpower 2` to save battery cuts ESP-NOW mesh range by roughly 18 dB at the same time, with nothing in the output connecting the two. The mesh then appears to have "lost peers" for reasons that no ESP-NOW diagnostic surfaces (espnowstatus reports channel and peer health, not TX power). Conversely, after the driver reinit path runs, the setting silently reverts to the sdkconfig default of 20 dBm, so a user who lowered it finds it back at maximum with no notice.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Say in the response that this also affects ESP-NOW range and the hidden softAP, and surface the live value in espnowstatus alongside the channel.


### Web / HTTP — 42 findings (15 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] Pre-auth 1-byte stack overflow in decodeBasicAuth: out_buf[out_len]='\0' can write index 256 of a 256-byte array
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1350`
- **Also:** docs/STACK_TO_PSRAM_CANDIDATES.md:110 lists this same `unsigned char out_buf[256]` purely as a stack-size candidate; the overflow itself is not recorded anywhere in docs/.
- **What:** decodeBasicAuth declares `unsigned char out_buf[256];` (:1346), calls `mbedtls_base64_decode(out_buf, sizeof(out_buf), &out_len, ...)` (:1347), and on success unconditionally writes `out_buf[out_len] = '\0';` (:1350). Verified against the in-tree mbedtls at /Users/morgan/esp/esp-idf/components/mbedtls/mbedtls/library/base64.c: the size check is `dlen < *olen` (line 220), so *olen == dlen == 256 is accepted, 256 bytes are written, and the function returns 0. out_buf[256] is then one byte past the array.
- **Why it matters:** An attacker sends `Authorization: Basic <344 base64 chars ending in ==>` — 344 chars decode to exactly (344/4)*3 - 2 = 256 bytes. Total header is ~350 bytes, well under CONFIG_HTTPD_MAX_REQ_HDR_LEN=1024 (sdkconfig:1448). Reachability is unauthenticated and unthrottled: isAuthed() calls decodeBasicAuth whenever the request carries no session cookie (:552-556), on every URI that goes through tgRequireAuth, and it does so BEFORE the isLoginLocked() check at :558 — so even a locked-out or repeatedly-failing IP still reaches the write. The result is a one-byte NUL written past a 256-byte buffer on the httpd task stack (30 KB plain HTTP / 44 KB HTTPS). The build has CONFIG_COMPILER_STACK_CHECK_MODE_NORM=y (sdkconfig:733), and -fstack-protector places the canary immediately above local arrays, so the likely landing sites are the canary (-> __stack_chk_fail -> abort/reboot, i.e. a trivially repeatable remote pre-auth DoS) or an adjacent local/saved register.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1 (FeatherS3 + XIAO S3 defaults)
- **Direction:** Size the decode destination one byte larger than the value passed as `dlen` (e.g. `unsigned char out_buf[257]` with `mbedtls_base64_decode(out_buf, sizeof(out_buf)-1, ...)`), or reject `out_len >= sizeof(out_buf)` before writing the terminator. Note docs/STACK_TO_PSRAM_CANDIDATES.md:110 already lists this buffer as a stack-size candidate but does not mention the overflow.

### [CRITICAL] Full live web session tokens are written to the /api/cli/logs mirror, letting any authenticated non-admin steal an admin's session
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:376`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:322 (reusedSession, same full sid); WebServer_Server.cpp:368 (DEBUG_AUTHF, flag-gated so lower risk); System_Debug.cpp:246 (the ungated web-mirror sink — the shared root cause); WebServer_Server.cpp:2890 handleLogs (no admin gate); WebServer_Server.cpp:5155 (mirror allocated at server start)
- **What:** `setSession()` ends with `BROADCAST_PRINTF("[auth] setSession user=%s, sid=%s, exp(ms)=%lu", u.c_str(), s.sid.c_str(), ...)` at :376, and the session-reuse branch does the same at :322. BROADCAST_PRINTF (System_Debug.h:734) is gated only on `gOutputFlags` having ANY route bit, not on a debug flag. broadcastOutputCore (System_Debug.cpp:902) sees no command context on the httpd task, so it routes MSG_ROUTE_ALL. In the debug_out drain, the Serial sink checks `gOutputFlags & MSG_ROUTE_SERIAL` (System_Debug.cpp:242) and the file sink checks `gOutputFlags & MSG_ROUTE_FILE` (:253), but the web-mirror sink checks only `gWebMirror.buf` (:246) — and `ensureWebRuntimeBuffers()` (WebServer_Server.cpp:5155) allocates that 8 KB buffer every time the HTTP server starts. `handleLogs` (:2890) then hands the entire mirror to any caller who clears `tgRequireAuth` + `webGuestAccessAllowed`, i.e. every non-guest account. `redactOutputForLog` (System_Utils.cpp:1212) only rewrites JSON `"sid":"…"`, never the `sid=<hex>` form, and is applied to command output only — never to broadcastOutput. DEBUG_AUTHF at :321/:368/:373 prints the same SID and also routes MSG_ROUTE_ALL via debugQueuePrintf (System_Debug.cpp:800) when DEBUG_AUTH is on.
- **Why it matters:** A low-privilege account (role "user", e.g. one created through /register + admin approval) logs in and polls `GET /api/cli/logs` — the same endpoint the CLI page polls every 500 ms. When the admin or superadmin next signs in, the response contains `[auth] setSession user=admin, sid=<32 hex chars>`. `isAuthed` (WebServer_Server.cpp:538) resolves a request purely by `findSessionIndexBySID` with no IP, User-Agent, or origin binding, and SESSION_TTL_MS is 24 h (:192). Setting `Cookie: session=<stolen sid>` yields a full admin session from any host — which reaches /api/cli, /api/files/write, /api/admin/approve and the bond RCE endpoints. Prior doc rated this LOW because it believed the line only reached serial and only reached a file if file logging was enabled; the web-mirror sink is unconditional, so the leak is on by default on every HTTP build.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1 — the default on FeatherS3 and XIAO); prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:98 — flags these same two log lines but rates them LOW on the premise "Default is serial-only, but enabling file logging persists live SIDs to a web-downloadable file."
- **Direction:** Truncate the sid in both broadcasts the way authSuccessUnified already does ("%.8s...", :417-421) — that is the pattern the codebase itself established. Separately, the sink asymmetry at System_Debug.cpp:246 is worth fixing on its own merits: gate the web mirror on gOutputFlags & MSG_ROUTE_WEB like serial and file are, so "web output off" actually means off. Consider whether /api/cli/logs should be admin-only given it mirrors everything.

### [CRITICAL] Web session cookie (sid) is broadcast in cleartext to every output sink on every login — no debug flag required
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:376`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:322 (reuse branch, same BROADCAST_PRINTF); WebServer_Server.cpp:321, 368, 373 print the full sid under DEBUG_AUTH
- **What:** setSession() ends with `BROADCAST_PRINTF("[auth] setSession user=%s, sid=%s, exp(ms)=%lu", u.c_str(), s.sid.c_str(), s.expiresAt)` and the session-reuse branch does the same at :322. BROADCAST_PRINTF (System_Debug.h:734) has NO debug-flag and NO log-level gate — it fires whenever any of SERIAL/WEB/FILE/BLE is in gOutputFlags. setSession runs on the httpd task, which has no CommandContext in its TLS slot, so broadcastOutputCore falls to `route = MSG_ROUTE_ALL` (System_Debug.cpp:954): serial, the gWebMirror ring, the system log file, the OLED console, BLE and G2 all receive the full token. `s.sid` is the literal bearer credential — writeSessionCookie(req, s.sid) sets it as the cookie and every later request authenticates via findSessionIndexBySID(getCookieSID(req)).
- **Why it matters:** Alice has the plain 'user' role. Bob (admin) signs in at the web UI. Alice GETs /api/cli/logs — handleLogs (WebServer_Server.cpp:2890) only calls tgRequireAuth + webGuestAccessAllowed, and webGuestAccessAllowed returns true immediately for any non-guest (WebServer_Utils.cpp:403), so Alice is allowed. The mirror contains `[auth] setSession user=bob, sid=<full token>, exp(ms)=...`. Alice sets that cookie and is now bob (admin). If `log start` is active the same line is also written to /system/sys_logs/*.log on unencrypted flash, and it is echoed on the OLED console and over BLE. Note the surrounding code proves this is unintended: the other three sid log sites truncate to `%.8s...` (:419, :1231, :1232) and redactOutputForLog explicitly masks `"sid"` in JSON command output (System_Utils.cpp:1242-1250).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER)
- **Direction:** Truncate to the `%.8s...` form already used at :419/:1231/:1232 at both BROADCAST_PRINTF sites, and at the three DEBUG_AUTHF sites. A session identifier in an operator log should never be the whole token.

### [HIGH] Bond remote-filesystem web endpoints have no local role check, and the peer serves FS_LIST/STAT/GET under SYSTEM identity with no credential in the request
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:2066`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1827,1976; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5004-5012; components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644,784,842,896
- **What:** handleBondFsGet (WebPage_Bond.cpp:2066), handleBondFsList (:1827) and handleBondFsStat (:1976) gate on WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN only — session auth plus the guest allowlist, no admin check — then take an arbitrary `path` query param and call fsGetSendRequest/fsListSendRequest straight at the peer. On the wire, FS_LIST_REQ / FS_STAT_REQ / FS_GET_REQ carry NO username or password; the dispatcher rows (System_ESPNow.cpp:5007-5012) require only V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_PAIRED | V4_OPC_FLAG_REQ_SESSION_ENC. On the peer, the handlers open the file under SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644 list, :784 stat, :842 and :896 get), which resolveRole maps to FsRole::SYSTEM and permsForRole maps to rule.systemPerms — the top filesystem tier. This is deliberate (the table comment at :5004 says "served under SYSTEM identity"), but nothing narrows who on the MASTER may drive it.
- **Why it matters:** A plain `user`-tier web account on the master issues GET /api/bond/fs/get?path=/system/users/users.json and the peer transfers its credential database (password hashes) to the master's filesystem; then GET /api/bond/fs/list?path=/system enumerates certs and keys. The account has no admin rights on either device and never presents a credential valid on the peer. Contrast the CLI equivalents: espnowbrowse/espnowfetch (System_ESPNow.cpp:14137, registered requiresAdmin=true at :15929/:15930) build a credential-bearing `user:pass:files "path"` CMD payload that v4_handle_cmd validates with isValidUser — the web bridge skips that path entirely.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE && ENABLE_WEB_BOND); prior art: Partially — docs/ESPNOW_COMMENT_AUDIT.md §7 records that FS_LIST/STAT/GET carry no bond gate (the inline comment at System_ESPNow.cpp:3692 lies about it). The SYSTEM-identity service combined with a n
- **Direction:** At minimum require admin on the three /api/bond/fs/* handlers so the local privilege needed matches espnowbrowse/espnowfetch (requiresAdmin=true). The deeper fix is the one the FsList comment already anticipates ("per-user identity propagation can be layered on later via a token in the request's reserved bytes") — until then, serving under SYSTEM rather than the requester's tier means device-level pairing is the only thing between a peer and every file.

### [HIGH] /api/bond/exec and /api/bond/cli/batch give any authenticated non-guest Super-Admin command execution on the bonded peer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1345`
- **What:** `handleBondExec` (:1345) and `handleBondCliBatch` (:1471) are gated by `WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN` only — authentication plus the guest gate, with no `isAdminUser` check. Both prefix the caller's string with `"remote:"` (:1391, :1516) and hand it to `executeUnifiedWebCommand`. `executeCommand` (System_Utils.cpp:4505-4515) strips that prefix and forwards the inner command to the bonded peer, where it runs as `kBondAdminUser` (Super Admin). The comment at System_Utils.cpp:4535 states the local caller's role is deliberately not re-checked; the only guard is the nested-wrapper reject at :4519.
- **Why it matters:** A plain `user` account POSTs `cmd=useradd attacker pw admin` (or `sdformat`, `filedelete`, `certgen`, `espnowsetpassphrase`) to /api/bond/exec. `findCommand("remote:useradd …")` returns nullptr, so gates 4 and 5 of `authorizeCommand` never fire locally, and the peer executes it with Super Admin privilege. Separately, `handleBondRole` (:1419) — same gate — sends `remote:bondrole <peerNewRole>` FIRST (:1436) and only then attempts the local `bondrole`, which IS `requiresAdmin=true` (System_ESPNow.cpp:15951) and therefore fails for a non-admin. Net effect for a non-admin caller: the peer's role flips, the local role does not, leaving the pair in a split-brain both-master or both-worker state.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_WEB_BOND && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE && ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: General `remote:` escalation is item G1 in the shared recon map (scratchpad/recon_surfaces.md); it is not in docs/. The web-endpoint expression and the /api/bond/role split-brain below are new.
- **Direction:** Require admin (or superadmin, matching the privilege the forwarded command receives on the peer) on every /api/bond/* mutating endpoint, and re-order handleBondRole so the local role change is attempted first and aborts before touching the peer.

### [HIGH] /api/bond/fs/get|list|stat lets any authenticated non-guest read arbitrary files off the bonded peer with no admin gate
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:2066`
- **What:** `handleBondFsGet` (:2066), `handleBondFsList` (:1827) and `handleBondFsStat` (:1976) are gated by `WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN` only. `handleBondFsGet` takes an arbitrary `?path=` (:2076), applies no `isAdminOnlyPath` check and no VFS guard, and calls `fsGetSendRequest(peerMac, path, …)` (:2096). The peer's FS_GET handler installs `SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE` (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:853), so on that side the path is unrestricted.
- **Why it matters:** Compare the local equivalent: `handleFileRead` (WebServer_Server.cpp:1635) rejects `isAdminOnlyPath(path) && !isAdminUser(ctx.user)` at :1691 and then goes through `VFS::openGuarded` with the caller's identity. The bond bridge has neither check, so a plain `user` can request `/system/users/users.json` (PBKDF2 hashes) or `/system/settings.json` from the peer and have it transferred onto the local filesystem — a strictly higher privilege than the same account has against its own device.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_WEB_BOND && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE; prior art: Peer-side SYSTEM identity on FS_LIST/FS_STAT/FS_GET is docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:135; that entry does not name the web bridge as the unprivileged initiator.
- **Direction:** Mirror the local file endpoints: run `isAdminOnlyPath` + `isAdminUser` on the requested path before sending the FS_GET/FS_LIST frame, and require admin on the whole /api/bond/fs/* group.

### [HIGH] Bond role-swap split-brain guard is structurally inert — `remote:` returns on enqueue, never on peer execution
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1439`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1392 (/api/bond/exec returns {"success":true,"result":"OK: Remote command sent: ..."} — never the peer's output); :1517-1529 (handleBondCliBatch hardcodes respDoc["ok"]=true and every per-command result is the local "sent" echo, so the bonded settings panel reports every write applied regardless of what the worker did)
- **What:** handleBondRole sends `remote:bondrole <peerNewRole>` first and guards the local change with `if (!remoteOk) { abort with "Remote role change failed" }`. remoteOk comes from executeUnifiedWebCommand → executeCommand's remote branch (System_Utils.cpp:4661-4674), which returns true as soon as v4_send_encrypted_or_queue() accepts the frame. That helper returns true for "sent", "queued" (no active session yet), "queued; SESSION_OPEN kick failed", and even "queued; KEY_EX kick failed (no enabled mesh?)" (System_ESPNow.cpp:1846/:1892/:1906). There is no correlation back to the peer's CMD_RESP anywhere on this path.
- **Why it matters:** If the peer never applies the role change — frame parked in the pending ring and eaten by kPendingFrameTimeoutMs, peer offline, or the peer's own `bondrole` handler errors — remoteOk is still true, so the code proceeds to change the LOCAL role at :1452 and reports `{"success":true,"role":N}`. That produces exactly the master/master or worker/worker split-brain the guard's own comment at :1431-1434 says it exists to prevent, and the UI shows it as a clean success.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW && ENABLE_BONDED_MODE); prior art: docs/ESPNOW_MESH_SYSTEM.md:115,320 documents that the synchronous return is wire-delivery-only, but does not identify this consumer as relying on it for a correctness guard.
- **Direction:** A guard that must observe the peer's outcome needs the reqId/CMD_RESP correlation the mesh already has (sendStatusRegister + espnowmessages), not the enqueue bool. Either make handleBondRole wait for the peer's CMD_RESP before touching the local role, or invert the order and add an explicit reconciliation step.

### [HIGH] Unauthenticated ESP-NOW metadata injection reaches the admin web UI as unescaped innerHTML -> admin-privileged command execution (radio-range, no credentials, no mesh key)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_ESPNow.h:739`
- **Also:** Ingest: System_ESPNow.cpp:3974 (v4h_metadata_resp_push, flags-0) -> 7452 (processMetadata strncpy of attacker deviceName/friendlyName/room/zone/tags into gMeshPeerMeta for ANY src MAC) ; name resolver System_ESPNow.cpp:8127 (getEspNowDeviceName returns gMeshPeerMeta.name first) ; peer-list emit System_ESPNow.cpp:8201/8155 ; presence slot System_ESPNow.cpp:2981+8057 (v4h_heartbeat flags-0 -> noteMeshPeerRxActivity createIfMissing) ; one-click tab sink WebPage_ESPNow.h:1817-1837 (loadPeerMetadata)
- **What:** The V4 opcodes METADATA_PUSH/RESP and HEARTBEAT are registered with flags=0 (kV4HandlerTable, System_ESPNow.cpp:4958/4987-4988) -> reached with NO pairing, NO session, NO mesh key when the frame carries meshFingerprint=0 (the mesh-scope gate at System_ESPNow.cpp:5153 only rejects NON-zero unknown fingerprints). v4h_metadata_resp_push stores the attacker's deviceName[32]/friendlyName[48]/room[32]/zone[32]/tags[54] wire fields verbatim into gMeshPeerMeta for the attacker's chosen MAC. getEspNowDeviceName() returns that attacker string, and buildMeshStatusPeersJson emits it as the peer 'name'. renderUnifiedDeviceList concatenates it into innerHTML with NO escaping at line 739 (mesh-peer row) — and loadPeerMetadata does the same at 1817-1837 (metadata tab).
- **Why it matters:** Attacker in radio range with no account and no mesh passphrase sends (1) a plaintext HEARTBEAT (meshFingerprint=0) to create a gMeshPeers slot so their MAC shows as a peer row, and (2) a plaintext METADATA_PUSH with deviceName = a 31-char '<img src=x onerror=...>'. When an admin opens the ESP-NOW page (which polls espnowmeshstatus every ~10 s and re-renders), the payload executes same-origin in the admin's session with ZERO clicks; the JS then POSTs to /api/cli at admin privilege (e.g. 'useradd evil pass admin'). Server-side JSON escaping (espnowSerializeMeshPeerMeta) does NOT stop this because JSON-escaping is not HTML-escaping. The 31-char name is enough to prove arbitrary JS execution; the metadata-tab fields (tags up to 53 chars, one admin click) hold a full fetch('/api/cli',{method:'POST',credentials:'include',...}) payload.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: any board with ENABLE_ESPNOW + ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER + ENABLE_WEB_ESPNOW, device in mesh mode; prior art: Sharpens PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md 2.2 (which stated the wrong precondition)
- **Direction:** Set peer/name/metadata display values via textContent or HTML-escape them in renderUnifiedDeviceList (739, 717/758) and loadPeerMetadata (1817-1837); the existing escHtml/esc helper is already used elsewhere on this page. Separately, do not populate gMeshPeerMeta from unauthenticated flags-0 METADATA frames — require REQ_AUTHENTICATED (mesh group key) or an active session, matching PAIR_BEACON. A baseline CSP would contain the class.

### [HIGH] Guest accounts can start the GPS radio and create files through /api/gps/tracks — a guest-allowlisted GET that reaches executeCommandThroughRegistry and a SYSTEM-impersonating file write
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:128`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:133-135, :141, :147; the underlying primitive is System_Command.cpp:263 (executeCommandThroughRegistry with no AuthContext), also called from WebPage_Maps.cpp only among web handlers.
- **What:** `/api/gps/tracks` is on the guest read-only allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:392) and `webGuestAccessAllowed` (:401-420) admits any GET whose path (query stripped) is on that list. `handleGPSTracksAPI` then parses `?live=` and, for `start`, calls `executeCommandThroughRegistry("opengps")` (:128), `("sensorlog format track")` (:133), `("sensorlog sensors gps")` (:134) and `("sensorlog start /logging_captures/tracks/live.csv 1000")` (:135); for `stop`, `("sensorlog stop")` (:141); for `save`, `GPSTrackManager::saveTrack` (:147). `executeCommandThroughRegistry` (System_Command.cpp:191-288) invokes `found->handler(resolvedArgs)` directly at :263 with no AuthContext and no `authorizeCommand`. `saveTrack` (System_Maps.cpp:2929-2971) deliberately impersonates SYSTEM — `const AuthContext sys = VFS::systemAuth("gpstrack.save")` at :2938 — so the caller's FsRole::GUEST read-only mask is irrelevant.
- **Why it matters:** A logged-in Guest issues `GET /api/gps/tracks?live=start`, which powers on the GPS sensor and starts a periodic sensor logger writing /logging_captures/tracks/live.csv; then `?live=save` creates a further track file as SYSTEM. Guests are documented and enforced everywhere else as view-only (`commandAllowedForGuest` in System_Utils.cpp permits only login/logout, and `permsForRole` masks FsRole::GUEST to `userPerms & PERM_READ`). This is a state change, a radio power-on and a filesystem write from the bottom role, through a GET, with no audit record.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_WEB_MAPS derives to 1 from CUSTOM_ENABLE_WEB_MAPS=1 + ENABLE_MAPS=1) — LIVE; prior art: Backlog memory item 2 flags it in one clause. It directly CONTRADICTS project_guest_role_audit memory, which recorded the HTTP layer as "COMPLIANT (re-run). No guest-reachable mutation/file/camera/adm
- **Direction:** Split the mutating query verbs (`live=start|stop|save`) out of the allowlisted GET — either move them to POST (which webGuestAccessAllowed already 403s for guests) or add an explicit `isGuestUser(ctx.user)` reject before the `live=` block. Separately, the other 24 entries in kAllow deserve the same end-to-end trace; only this one was verified.

### [HIGH] Guest GET on `/api/gps/tracks?live=…` starts the GPS sensor, reconfigures and starts sensor logging, and writes a track file to flash with SYSTEM filesystem authority
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:109`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:387 (`/api/gps/tracks` in kAllow); components/hardwareone/System_Command.cpp:263 (executeCommandThroughRegistry calls found->handler() with no AuthContext); components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:2929 (saveTrack uses VFS::systemAuth); components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:894, :1149 (sensorlog opens files with VFS::systemAuth)
- **What:** `webGuestAccessAllowed()` (WebServer_Utils.cpp:401) allows guests any GET whose path is in `kAllow[]`, and `/api/gps/tracks` is on that list. The handler then treats query params as verbs. `?live=start` calls `executeCommandThroughRegistry("opengps")`, `GPSTrackManager::clearTrack()`, then `executeCommandThroughRegistry` three more times for `sensorlog format track`, `sensorlog sensors gps`, `sensorlog start /logging_captures/tracks/live.csv 1000`. `?live=stop` runs `sensorlog stop`. `?live=save` calls `GPSTrackManager::saveTrack()`. `executeCommandThroughRegistry` invokes `found->handler(args)` directly — it takes no AuthContext and never reaches `authorizeCommand`, so the guest gate is skipped entirely.
- **Why it matters:** A logged-in guest issues `GET /api/gps/tracks?live=start`. The 403 gate passes (GET + allowlisted path). GPS hardware is powered on, the in-RAM track of whoever was recording is destroyed by `clearTrack()`, the global sensor-logging config is overwritten (format=track, sensor mask replaced with gps), and a logger starts appending to flash every 1000 ms. `GET ?live=save` then writes a brand-new `/logging_captures/tracks/track-<ts>.csv`. The guest's own FsRole::GUEST read-only mask does NOT stop this: `saveTrack` explicitly uses `VFS::systemAuth("gpstrack.save")` and the sensor logger uses `VFS::systemAuth("senlog.append")`, so both impersonate SYSTEM. Net result: a "view-only" account powers hardware, clobbers another user's logging configuration, and writes files to flash — three distinct mutations from a GET.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_WEB_MAPS (current config: WEB_FEATURE_LEVEL 4 / CUSTOM_ENABLE_WEB_MAPS=1); prior art: docs/WEB_API_INVENTORY.md:122 rates this endpoint's auth "adequate" and :2188 flags only its response-shape overloading — the guest-mutation issue is not documented there. Independently surfaced as G6
- **Direction:** Split the mutating verbs off `/api/gps/tracks` into POST routes (which the existing non-GET guest rule already blocks), or gate the `live=start|stop|save` branches on `!isGuestUser(ctx.user)` before dispatch. Longer term, `executeCommandThroughRegistry` should not be reachable from an HTTP handler at all — route through `executeUnifiedWebCommand` so authorizeCommand runs.

### [HIGH] Migration restore writes arbitrary bundle-chosen absolute paths verbatim, including /system/users/users.json — the attacker-reachable way to plant an "AuthBypass" superadmin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:519`
- **Also:** The confirm gate that mitigates it: applyStagedMigrationRestore (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:594).
- **What:** The restore loop iterates `doc["files"]` and takes the JSON key as a destination path with the only check being `path[0] == '/'` (:521), then calls `writeTextAtomic(path, content)` at :577 with content taken straight from the bundle. There is no allowlist of restorable paths, no `..` rejection at this layer, and no schema validation of the restored users.json — no `isValidPublicUsername`, no role sanity check. In `compatible` (same-device) mode the `/system/users/` skip at :530 does not apply, so users.json and the per-user settings files are written from the bundle as-is.
- **Why it matters:** An operator who confirms a restore of an attacker-supplied bundle installs whatever roster the bundle contains — e.g. `{"users":[{"id":1,"username":"AuthBypass","role":"superadmin"}]}`. That turns the reserved sentinel into a live account, and every transport that stamps `AuthBypass` when its require-auth toggle is off (Bluetooth.cpp:202, HardwareOne.cpp:2631, OLED_Utils.cpp:2980) then resolves to super-admin via `isSuperAdminUser` → `hasSuperAdminPrivilege` → `authorizeCommand` passes everything. This is the practical version of backlog item #1: no owner mistake required, only one confirmed restore. The prior audit's own note that the confirm prompt shows no bundle identity (no hostname/fingerprint/size) is what makes that confirmation uninformed.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: All boards with ENABLE_MIGRATION_TOOL (=ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER, which is 1 in the current config).; prior art: Not in the backlog as such. docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md and the backlog cover the stage-then-confirm gate and the missing bundle identity on the confirm prompt, but not the unvalidated path/conten
- **Direction:** Validate on apply, not just on stage: restrict destination paths to a fixed allowlist matching the backup writer's own constants, reject any key containing "..", and run the restored roster through `isValidPublicUsername` + a role allowlist before it becomes the live auth database.

### [HIGH] GET /api/automations and /api/automations/export bypass the VFS permission table; /api/automations is additionally on the guest allowlist
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3340`
- **What:** `handleAutomationsGet` (:3335) and `handleAutomationsExport` (:4852) read `/system/automations.json` with `readText()` at :3340, :4863 and :4919. `readText` → `readTextLimited` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1758) opens via unguarded `VFS::open` and documents itself as "policy-free byte shovel" that relies on callers having gated access. Neither handler gates: both use only `WEB_AUTH_OR_RETURN`. The rule table (System_Filesystem.cpp:1350) gives `/system/automations.json` `userPerms = 0` — a non-admin has no READ permission on it — and `/api/automations` is in the guest allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:376). Automation command strings are stored verbatim, only JSON-escaped (System_Automation.cpp:1243-1263).
- **Why it matters:** A guest account — the role whose entire contract is "view-only", enforced elsewhere by webGuestAccessAllowed — GETs /api/automations and receives every automation's raw command string. Those strings routinely embed credentials, because the CLI takes them positionally: `wifiadd <ssid> <password>`, `espnowremote <peer> <target-user> <target-pass> <cmd>`, `login <user> <pass>`, `mqttpassword <pw>`. Nothing on this path applies `redactCmdForAudit` (which the command-feed path at HardwareOne.cpp:497 does apply). The same GET also writes flash: :3347 calls `sanitizeAutomationsJson(body)` and :3348 `writeAutomationsJsonAtomic(body)` on any file with duplicate IDs, so a view-only role triggers a flash rewrite.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_AUTOMATION
- **Direction:** Read through `VFS::openGuarded(AUTOMATIONS_JSON_FILE, "r", ctx)` so the rule table applies, drop /api/automations from the guest allowlist (or serve a redacted projection to non-admins), and move the sanitize+writeback out of the GET path.

### [HIGH] Cleartext passwords and PBKDF2 hashes are staged in PSRAM on every login, registration, password change and web CLI command, and freed without zeroing
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3492`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3789 (http.reg.post), :2427 (http.pwchg), :4809 (http.passwd), :3194 (http.cli.exec), :5040 (http.batch); components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1149 and :766 (PSRAM_JSON_DOC holding the hash); components/hardwareone/System_MemUtil.h:287-296 and :327 (PsramJsonAllocator / PSRAM_JSON_DOC); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:754 (fileview body buffer)
- **What:** handleLogin reads the form body into `ps_alloc(total_len + 1, AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "http.login")` — a std::unique_ptr whose deleter is plain `free`. ps_alloc with PreferPSRAM calls heap_caps_malloc(size, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM) unconditionally (System_MemUtil.h:151-166), so CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384 does not keep these small allocations internal the way it does for plain malloc. The buffer holds `username=admin&password=hunter2` verbatim and is released without a memset. The same pattern repeats for registration, the password-change page, the admin password-reset endpoint, and both CLI endpoints (which carry `login u p`, `wifiadd ssid psk`, `blesecret <passphrase>`, `espnowsetpassphrase <mesh> <passphrase>`). Separately, PSRAM_JSON_DOC forces MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM through PsramJsonAllocator::allocate regardless of size, and isValidUser (System_User.cpp:1149) parses the per-user settings file — including the PBKDF2 hash — into one on every single authentication, with PsramJsonAllocator::deallocate being a bare free().
- **Why it matters:** The project rule is explicit: never put secrets in PSRAM, because flash encryption is off and the external PSRAM bus is probeable. Nothing here is transient state that gets reused quickly — a login password and the corresponding hash sit in an unzeroed PSRAM block until the allocator happens to hand that region out again and something overwrites it. An attacker with the device in hand and a bus probe (or any future firmware bug that reads uninitialised PSRAM back to a caller) recovers live cleartext credentials rather than the AES-wrapped at-rest forms the design intends. Note this defeats the at-rest layer entirely: settings.json goes to lengths to AES-wrap the WiFi PSK, and then the same PSK crosses PSRAM in the clear whenever it is typed into the web CLI.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with PSRAM (primary FeatherS3 quad, XIAO octal). ps_alloc falls back to internal DRAM only when PSRAM is exhausted.; prior art: Class is known — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §3.1 names gSessions and the AES plaintext buffers at System_Settings.cpp:364/:490. The HTTP credential bodies and the PSRAM_JSON_DOC hash residency be
- **Direction:** Switch the credential-bearing HTTP body allocations to AllocPref::PreferInternal and zero them before free — a scope guard that memsets `received` bytes would cover all six sites uniformly. For the JSON docs, either use a plain internal-heap JsonDocument for isValidUser/setUserPassword/loadUserSettings, or read only the one field needed rather than the whole file. Keep it consistent with the §3.1 fix so gSessions and the AES buffers move at the same time.

### [HIGH] Brute-force lockout is bypassable: the 8-slot login-attempt table evicts the oldest entry, and a locked entry is always the oldest
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:787`
- **Also:** isLoginLocked WebServer_Server.cpp:815; recordFailedLogin :841; consumers HardwareOne.cpp:2515 (serial, key "local"), WebServer_Server.cpp:3518 (POST /login), :558 (HTTP Basic Auth), :3766 (/register/submit)
- **What:** `sLoginAttempts` holds `MAX_LOGIN_ATTEMPT_ENTRIES = 8` rows (WebServer_Server.h:121). `findLoginEntry(ip, createIfMissing=true)` scans for a free slot, and if there is none it picks `oldestSlot` — the row with the smallest `windowStart` — and `memset`s it to zero. `windowStart` is stamped at the *first* failure of a window, so an actively locked-out attacker's row is, by construction, the row with the oldest `windowStart` and therefore the first eviction target. `isLoginLocked()` (:815) looks the IP up with `createIfMissing=false`; once the row is gone it returns `false` and both `lockedUntil` and `failCount` are lost. Nothing persists lockouts, and nothing auto-bans (`banIp` has exactly one caller, the manual `ipban` command at System_User.cpp:2591).
- **Why it matters:** Attacker on the LAN fails 5 logins from 10.0.0.5 and is locked for 30 s. They then send one failed login from 8 other source addresses — a second NIC alias, or 8 addresses out of their own IPv6 /64 (`LoginAttemptEntry::ip` is `char[40]`, sized for IPv6, and getClientIP handles AF_INET6). That fills the table and evicts the 10.0.0.5 row. Returning to 10.0.0.5, `isLoginLocked` returns false and `failCount` restarts at 0. The tier-1/2/3 escalation can never be reached, so guessing proceeds at wire speed indefinitely. The filler rows do not even need credentials: `POST /register/submit` calls `recordFailedLogin` on eight distinct unauthenticated failure branches (:3778, :3782, :3791, :3810, :3814, :3818, :3822, :3840). Cross-transport side effect: the serial console shares this table under the literal key "local", so a web attacker silently clears a serial-console lockout too.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER builds; the same table also backs the serial console gate); prior art: Contradicts docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §2 'Already solid: tiered per-IP lockout keyed on the real socket IP via getpeername'. The key is indeed unspoofable; the table backing it is not durable.
- **Direction:** Never evict a row whose `lockedUntil` is still in the future — prefer an expired-window row, and if every row is live, fail closed (treat an unknown IP as locked, or fall back to a global throttle). Sizing the table to the socket limit rather than 8 would also remove the pressure.

### [HIGH] Same-LAN attacker with no credentials: plaintext HTTP is the default, so a passive sniff yields the admin session cookie/password -> full admin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5309`
- **Also:** Default System_Settings.h:357 (httpsEnabled(false)); HTTPS only if httpsEnabled AND user-installed certs exist, else silent fallback to port 80 with gServerIsHttps=false which also drops ;Secure from the session cookie
- **What:** CONFIG_ESP_HTTPS_SERVER_ENABLE is unset and httpsEnabled defaults false; the server binds port 80 and issues session cookies without ;Secure. Every mutating/secret endpoint IS correctly gated (all handlers call tgRequireAuth / WEB_AUTH_*; the /api/cli unbounded-alloc reboot at :3194 is reached only AFTER tgRequireAuth at :3175, i.e. authenticated-only; /register is admin-approval-gated; /api/restore is first-time-setup-gated). So no endpoint 'falls' to an anonymous request.
- **Why it matters:** The web app's own authz is solid, which means the first thing a same-LAN no-cred attacker attacks is the transport, not an endpoint: sniff the admin's HTTP request on the shared LAN/AP and replay the HttpOnly session cookie (or read the login password in the clear). No crypto work, no account. This is the realistic 'which endpoint falls first' answer for threat model 2: none — the LAN link does.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER); prior art: KNOWN — PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md 2.3
- **Direction:** Document the plaintext exposure prominently and make the silent HTTP fallback loud (surface gServerIsHttps=false in status). On-device TLS is a large lift; at minimum stop reporting a protected posture when running on port 80.

### [MEDIUM] Web CLI persists every typed command — including `login <user> <pass>` — into browser localStorage, surviving logout
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_CLI.h:215`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_CLI.h:218, :221 (cliOutputHistory)
- **What:** The CLI page's execute() handler pushes the raw typed string into commandHistory and writes it to localStorage under key 'cliHistory' (:213-216), and separately appends '$ ' + command to the on-screen transcript which is written to localStorage key 'cliOutputHistory' (:218, :221). Neither copy is redacted — the firmware's redaction only applies to the server-side feed (appendCommandToFeed, HardwareOne.cpp:497). clearHistory() (:225) exists but is a manual user action.
- **Why it matters:** A user types `login bob hunter2`, `useradd eve s3cret admin`, `wifiadd MyNet <psk>` or `blesecret <phrase>` at the web CLI. The plaintext lands in the browser's localStorage for that origin and stays there across logout, tab close and browser restart. Any subsequent user of that browser profile can read it from devtools, and any same-origin script can exfiltrate it — the security backlog already records that esc() in WebServer_Utils.h is a JS-string escaper misused as an HTML escaper at ~15 sites and that there is no CSP anywhere, so stored XSS on this origin is a live concern.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** Apply a client-side equivalent of the kRules prefix list before pushing to commandHistory/localStorage, or simply skip persistence for lines whose first token is in a small credential-command deny-list (login, useradd, userrequest, userresetpassword, userchangepassword, wifiadd, blesecret, mqttpassword, espnowsetpassphrase, espnowremote/browse/fetch/roomcmd/tagcmd, usersync).

### [MEDIUM] POST /api/llm/load and /api/llm/unload skip the admin requirement the equivalent CLI commands enforce
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_LLM.cpp:195`
- **What:** `handleLLMLoad` (:195) and `handleLLMUnload` (:255) use `WEB_AUTH_OR_RETURN` and then call `llmLoadModel(modelPath, maxCtx)` (:239) and `llmUnload()` (:259) directly — they never go through `executeCommand`, so `authorizeCommand` never runs. The CLI equivalents are registered `requiresAdmin = true`: `{ "llmload", …, true, cmd_llm_load, … }` and `{ "llmunload", …, true, cmd_llm_unload }` at System_LLM.cpp:3040-3041.
- **Why it matters:** A plain `user` account POSTs `{"model":"foo.bin"}` to /api/llm/load, or an empty body to /api/llm/unload, and performs an operation the same account is denied on every other surface (serial, OLED, BLE, G2, ESP-NOW). `llmUnload()` frees the multi-MB PSRAM weight and KV blocks, so it is also a cheap way for a low-privilege user to kill an admin's in-flight generation and force a multi-second reload.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM
- **Direction:** Add `if (!isAdminUser(ctx.user)) return 403;` to both handlers, or route them through `executeUnifiedWebCommand("llmload …")` so the registry's requiresAdmin flag is the single source of truth.

### [MEDIUM] Guest-allowlisted GET /api/gps/tracks?live=start reaches executeCommandThroughRegistry, which has no auth gate at all
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:129`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Command.cpp:191,263; components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:392,401
- **What:** `handleGPSTracksAPI` (:108) treats query parameters as verbs. `?live=start` calls `executeCommandThroughRegistry` four times at :129, :133, :134, :135 (`opengps`, `sensorlog format track`, `sensorlog sensors gps`, `sensorlog start /logging_captures/tracks/live.csv 1000`) and `?live=stop` once at :141. `executeCommandThroughRegistry` (System_Command.cpp:191) invokes `found->handler(...)` directly at :256/:263 — it takes no AuthContext and never calls `authorizeCommand`. `?live=save` calls `GPSTrackManager::saveTrack` at :146. The path `/api/gps/tracks` is in the guest allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:392), and `webGuestAccessAllowed` (:401) only blocks non-GET methods for guests.
- **Why it matters:** A view-only guest account powers up the GPS sensor and starts a 1 Hz filesystem write loop with a single GET. It is a guest/view-only violation and an unauthenticated-write-amplification vector against the shared 2796K LittleFS partition, not an admin bypass — both commands happen to be requiresAdmin=false (i2csensor_pa1010d.cpp:737, System_SensorLogging.cpp:2074). The structural problem is that executeCommandThroughRegistry is a permanent gate-free door into the registry; if any future caller passes it an admin command, there is nothing to stop it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_GPS_SENSOR); prior art: recon_surfaces.md G6; memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 2 mentions /api/gps/tracks in passing. docs/WEB_API_INVENTORY.md:2173 covers the uneven-granularity theme but names /espnow and bon
- **Direction:** Have handleGPSTracksAPI go through submitAndExecuteSync with the ctx it already builds (it installs ExecIdentityGuard(ctx) at :117 for exactly this reason on the FS side), and drop /api/gps/tracks from the guest allowlist or split the read-only query params from the ?live=start/stop verbs into separate paths. The 8 other executeCommandThroughRegistry call sites are internal literals (System_Settings.cpp:1538,2572,2575; System_EdgeImpulse.cpp:2156,2157) and are fine.

### [MEDIUM] Guest can power on the GPS sensor and start filesystem logging via a GET, bypassing authorizeCommand
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Maps.cpp:129`
- **Also:** Guest GET allowlist WebServer_Utils.cpp:392 (/api/gps/tracks); executeCommandThroughRegistry invokes found->handler() with no AuthContext (System_Command.cpp:263); commands 'opengps'/'sensorlog start ...' at WebPage_Maps.cpp:129,133,134,135,141
- **What:** /api/gps/tracks is on the guest GET allowlist and webGuestAccessAllowed permits GET on it. handleGPSTracksAPI treats ?live=start as a verb and calls executeCommandThroughRegistry('opengps'), ('sensorlog format track'), ('sensorlog sensors gps'), ('sensorlog start /logging_captures/tracks/live.csv 1000') — none of which pass through authorizeCommand (executeCommandThroughRegistry calls the handler directly).
- **Why it matters:** A view-only guest account actuates hardware (GPS radio on) and starts the sensor-logging engine writing capture files — a clear breach of the guest 'view-only' contract. It is NOT an escalation to admin/secrets/RCE (the invoked commands are hard-coded and requiresAdmin=false; the /logging_captures write may even fail under the guest FsRole), which is why threat model 3 has no guest->admin path — but this is the concrete guest-tier policy violation.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER); prior art: KNOWN — recon_surfaces.md G6
- **Direction:** Route these side-effecting query verbs through executeCommand/authorizeCommand under the caller's identity (as /api/cli does), or block ?live=start/stop/save for guests; do not use executeCommandThroughRegistry for anything reachable from a guest surface.

### [MEDIUM] mustChangePassword is enforced only on HTML page routes — every API and every non-web transport ignores it
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1159`
- **Also:** System_User.cpp:777 (the only implementation), WebServer_Server.cpp:3598 (post-login redirect); no call site in System_Utils.cpp cmd_login, Bluetooth.cpp, OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp, System_ESPNow.cpp, System_MQTT.cpp
- **What:** `userMustChangePassword()` has exactly three references in the whole tree: its definition (System_User.cpp:777), the gate inside streamPageWithContent (WebServer_Server.cpp:1159) and the post-login redirect target choice (:3598). streamPageWithContent is the HTML page renderer only. Nothing on the command pipeline, /api/cli, /api/cli/batch, serial, BLE, ESP-NOW, MQTT, OLED or G2 consults it.
- **Why it matters:** `useradd bob temp123 1` sets `defaults["mustChangePassword"] = true` (System_User.cpp:930) and the operator reasonably believes bob must rotate the admin-chosen temporary password before doing anything. In fact bob can log in over serial or BLE, or POST to /api/cli in the browser, and run the full command surface indefinitely with the temporary credential the admin chose — a credential the admin also knows. The forced-rotation control is decorative outside HTML navigation.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards
- **Direction:** Check it in authorizeCommand alongside the guest gate (System_Utils.cpp:4325), allowing only `login`, `logout` and `userchangepassword` while the flag is set, and add it to the /api/cli and /api/cli/batch handlers. That covers every transport in one place.

### [MEDIUM] /api/cli/batch returns unredacted command output in the HTTP response while only the log and broadcast sinks get the redacted copy
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5096`
- **Also:** contrast with components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3259/:3296. Also stale comment: components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:1215-1216 says the rule matches "password":"HASH:xxxxx" — the real stored prefix is PBKDF2: (System_User.cpp:736); the code is prefix-agnostic so it still works, but the comment misleads anyone extending it.
- **What:** handleCliBatch computes String redacted = redactOutputForLog(out) at :5093, broadcasts `redacted`, then does results.push_back(out) at :5095 — the raw output — and serializes `results` straight into the JSON response at :5117-5124. handleCLICommand (the single-command sibling) does the opposite: it computes redactedOut at :3258 and sends *that* to the client at :3296. redactOutputForLog masks "password":"…" values entirely and truncates "sid":"…" to its first 8 characters, so the batch endpoint is the only surface that hands a client full session identifiers and password-hash fields.
- **Why it matters:** This confirms the claim docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md flagged as unverified (cited there as WebServer_Server.cpp:4811; the code has drifted to ~5093). Concretely: an admin web client POSTing {"commands":["sessionlist json"]} to /api/cli/batch receives every active session's complete `sid` — the bearer cookie value — for every logged-in user, whereas the same command through /api/cli returns them truncated to 8 chars. The redaction was clearly intended to apply to the response as well (the comment at :3256 says 'This applies to CLI history, logs, and all broadcast sinks'), and the batch path is a hole in it. Reach is limited to non-guest accounts, and `sessionlist` itself is requiresAdmin=true, so this is a defence-in-depth failure rather than direct privilege escalation — but it is the one surface that will hand a full SID to any code (or XSS payload, per the three stored-XSS sinks in the prior hardening audit) running in an authenticated browser session.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 9 finders; board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER); prior art: KNOWN as an UNVERIFIED claim in docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md (recorded there against the old line number 4811, drifted to 5096). This audit CONFIRMS it — the prior doc left it explicitly unverified.
- **Direction:** Push `redacted` into `results` in the batch handler so both endpoints have identical response semantics; if the raw value is genuinely wanted for a legitimate consumer, make that an explicit opt-in flag on both endpoints rather than an accident of one.

### [MEDIUM] /api/files/view serves .svg inline as image/svg+xml — stored XSS reaching same-origin /api/cli as the viewer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4385`
- **What:** `handleFileView` classifies `.svg` as an image at :4351 and sets `Content-Type: image/svg+xml` at :4385 with no `Content-Disposition`, no `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and no CSP anywhere in the server. `.svg` is absent from `isImageFile()` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1443, which lists jpg/jpeg/png/gif/bmp/webp/ico/avif/heif) so the edit-block does not cover it, and absent from `hasSensitiveExtension()` (:1417) so the upload/write block does not either. The non-image text path immediately below correctly escapes through `streamHtmlEscaped` at :4535.
- **Why it matters:** A non-guest user uploads `evil.svg` via /api/files/upload or /api/files/write into any directory the rule table's catch-all grants them (System_Filesystem.cpp:1393 gives userPerms = PERM_ALL for non-/system paths). When an admin clicks View in the file browser, `/api/files/view?name=/evil.svg` renders as a top-level document, and `<script>` inside it executes same-origin with the admin's session cookie — which is SameSite=Strict but same-site, so it is sent. The script can then POST to /api/cli and run arbitrary admin commands.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards built with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1; prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:87. Re-verified: UNFIXED.
- **Direction:** Serve .svg with `Content-Disposition: attachment` (or text/plain), add it to hasSensitiveExtension/isImageFile, and set `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` on the file-view responses.

### [MEDIUM] `saveIpBans()` commits on `written > 0`, so a truncated ban list silently drops every IP ban at the next boot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:963`
- **What:** `saveIpBans` (WebServer_Server.cpp:940-965) opens `IP_BANS_FILE` with `"w"` (truncate in place, no staging), calls `size_t written = serializeJson(doc, f)` at :962, `f.close()`, and returns `written > 0`. ArduinoJson's counting writer never aborts on a short write, so any partial serialization ≥ 1 byte reports success.
- **Why it matters:** The destination is truncated at open, so a short write leaves cut-off JSON. `loadIpBans` (:895-939) then hits `DeserializationError` at :907, logs a `DEBUG_AUTHF` line (invisible unless `DEBUG_AUTH` is on), and returns with `sIpBans` fully memset to zero and `sIpBansLoaded = true`. Every permanently-banned IP is silently unbanned, `isIpBanned()` returns false for all of them, and `banlist` shows an empty list with no error anywhere. Ban records are also the only persistent trace of who was banned and why, so the audit trail goes with them. The 100 KB overflow reserve does not protect this file — state files deliberately never route to the SD mirror (System_VFS.h design contract), so `saveIpBans` writes into whatever is left.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Compare against `measureJson(doc)` and use a tmp+rename so a failed save leaves the previous ban list intact; and make `loadIpBans`'s parse failure loud (broadcast + `logSystemEvent` + SYSEVT) rather than a debug-gated line, since silently unbanning everyone should never be quiet.

### [MEDIUM] /api/cli/batch discards every per-command result and hardcodes {"ok":true}
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5115`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Bond.cpp:1517,1529
- **What:** The batch handler loops over the command array calling `submitAndExecuteSync(uc, out);` at :5093 with the return value not even assigned, then builds the response with a literal `respDoc["ok"] = true;` at :5115 and `count` = number of commands attempted. There is no per-command status in the results array — only raw output strings.
- **Why it matters:** A non-admin POSTing `{"commands":["reboot","factoryreset"]}` gets HTTP 200 with `{"ok":true,"count":2,"results":["Error: Admin access required","Error: Admin access required"]}`. Any client that checks the top-level `ok` before parsing `results` concludes the batch applied. handleBondCliBatch (WebPage_Bond.cpp:1529) has the identical hardcode, and there every result is the local "Remote command sent" echo rather than the worker's outcome — so the bonded settings panel reports a fully-applied write for a batch the worker never received.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; prior art: docs/WEB_API_INVENTORY.md line 2165 flags inconsistent response envelopes across /api/* generally, but does not identify that this endpoint's ok field is a literal.
- **Direction:** Bind the bool, AND it across the loop (or emit a per-command ok array), and set respDoc["ok"] from that. Fixing executeCommand's return value is a prerequisite for either to mean anything.

### [MEDIUM] Web admin approve/deny endpoints bypass executeCommand, losing the command-audit line and mis-attributing the approval event to "system"
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4783`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4829 (deny twin); CLI twins at components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1842,1859
- **What:** `handleAdminApproveUser` (WebServer_Server.cpp:4752) and `handleAdminDenyUser` (:4793) call `approvePendingUserInternal` / `denyPendingUserInternal` directly (:4783, :4829) behind an inline `isAdminUser(ctx.user)` check, instead of going through `executeUnifiedWebCommand("userapprove …")`. The CLI twins `cmd_user_approve` / `cmd_user_deny` (System_User.cpp:1842/:1859, registered requiresAdmin=true at :3388/:3389) reach the same two internals through `executeCommand`, which runs `logCommandExecution(ctx, cmd, success, out)` and `logAuthAttempt(true, ctx.path, ctx.user, ctx.ip, auditBuf)` (System_Utils.cpp:4670-4676). The web path runs neither.
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete losses. (1) An admin approving or denying an account request from the web UI leaves no `[CMD]` line in command-audit.log and no entry in the auth-attempt log; the same action from serial or the OLED does. Reconstructing who approved an account after the fact only works for non-web admins. (2) `approvePendingUserInternal` posts `SYSEVT_USER_APPROVED` at System_User.cpp:1460, but the httpd task installs no `CommandIdentityScope`, so `systemEventPost` falls through to `ev.source = NOTIF_SOURCE_SYSTEM` and `ev.who = ""` (System_Events.cpp:233-242). The event therefore records the approval as firmware-initiated with no actor — while the identical CLI action, running under executeCommand's identity scope, attributes it to the admin. The privilege check itself is equivalent (both require admin), so this is an accountability gap rather than an access-control hole.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Route both handlers through `executeUnifiedWebCommand("userapprove <name>")` / `"userdeny <name>"` the way the other web mutation endpoints do — that restores the audit line, the auth-attempt record and the actor attribution in one move, and removes the second hand-rolled admin check.

### [MEDIUM] The debug lane logs raw command lines and raw MQTT payloads containing cleartext passwords, bypassing redactCmdForAudit entirely
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3263`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3217; components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4541; components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:440; components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:955
- **What:** The codebase has a careful redaction table (System_Utils.cpp:1115-1141 kRules, applied in logCommandExecution and appendCommandToFeed), but several DEBUG_* emitters print the same strings before redaction ever runs: WebServer_Server.cpp:3263 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF("[web.cli] cmd_first_80='%.80s'", cmd.c_str()) and :3217 which dumps the first 80 raw body bytes; System_Utils.cpp:4541 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF("[execCmd] … cmd=%.80s", cmd) inside executeCommand, which sees every command from every transport; System_MQTT.cpp:440 DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDSF("Command payload: %s", payload.c_str()) which prints the whole inbound {"user","pass","cmd"} object; and System_WiFi.cpp:955 DEBUG_WIFIF("  Password: '%s'") which prints the PSK. All route through the normal debug fan-out to serial, the web console ring, and the flash file tee when `log start` is active. gSettings.debugCommandFlow and gSettings.debugMqttCommands both default false (System_Settings.h:86, :96).
- **Why it matters:** The moment an operator enables `debugcommandflow` (or `debugmqttcommands`) to troubleshoot — a routine, documented action — every subsequent `login <user> <pass>`, `wifiadd <ssid> <psk>`, `useradd <u> <pw> <role>`, `blesecret <phrase>`, `espnowsetpassphrase <mesh> <phrase>` and MQTT credential is written verbatim to the console ring that /api/cli history serves and, if system logging is on, to unencrypted flash where it survives reboots and is retrievable by any of the file surfaces. The whole point of kRules is that these strings never reach a durable sink; the debug lane silently opts every one of them back in. System_WiFi.cpp:955 is a known instance (docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §1.1, still present at HEAD) — the finding here is that it is a class, not a one-off, and the fix proposed for §1.1 (delete that one line) does not address the others.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §1.1 covers only System_WiFi.cpp:955 (verified still present). The CMD_FLOW and MQTT sites are not documented anywhere.
- **Direction:** Route command-bearing debug emitters through redactCmdForAudit() the same way the audit path does — the cost is paid only when the flag is on. For the MQTT payload, log the parsed user/cmd fields rather than the raw object.

### [MEDIUM] HTTP chunk-streaming loops ignore httpd_resp_send_chunk errors, so an aborted download streams a whole file into a dead socket while cycling the global filesystem lock per chunk
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4225`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:1721-1730, 4262, 4391-4396, 4460-4465, 4488-4493
- **What:** The file-view/download loops read a chunk under fsLock, release the lock, call httpd_resp_send_chunk with the esp_err_t discarded, retake the lock, and repeat until EOF — e.g. WebServer_Server.cpp:4220-4227. The loop's only exit is `bytesRead <= 0`. The same shape appears at :1721-1730, :4262, :4391-4396, :4460-4465 and :4488-4493.
- **Why it matters:** When the client disconnects mid-transfer, httpd_resp_send_chunk returns ESP_ERR_HTTPD_RESP_SEND on every subsequent call but the handler keeps going: it reads the entire remaining file and takes/releases the global fsLock once per chunk, for hundreds of iterations on a multi-hundred-KB sensor CSV or map file. fsLock is the single serialization point for all filesystem users, so for the duration of that pointless drain the sensor logger, settings writes and ESP-NOW file transfers are contending against a transfer whose bytes are going nowhere. Repeatedly opening and aborting a large-file download turns into a cheap way to monopolize storage for an authenticated client.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (WEB_FEATURE_LEVEL 4)
- **Direction:** Break out of each loop as soon as httpd_resp_send_chunk returns anything other than ESP_OK, then close the file and return — the connection is already gone, so there is nothing to salvage.

### [MEDIUM] getCookieSID matches "session=" at any byte offset, so a cookie whose name merely ends in "session" overrides the real session cookie
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:304`
- **Also:** getCookieSID is called on every request via isAuthed (:542) and again directly at WebServer_Server.cpp:496, :2819, :2841, :2867, :2929, :3226, :5061 and WebPage_Sensors.cpp:728 — all inherit the same parse.
- **What:** The hand-rolled parser scans the raw Cookie header one byte at a time — `while (*s) { if (strncmp(s, "session=", 8) == 0) { sidStart = s + 8; break; } ++s; }` (:304-311) — with no notion of "; " token boundaries and no check that the match starts at the beginning of a cookie name. It then takes everything up to the next ';' as the SID. The correct ESP-IDF helper is already wrapped in this same file (getCookieValue -> httpd_req_get_cookie_val, :247-258) but is not used here.
- **Why it matters:** A Cookie header of `xsession=ATTACKER; session=REAL` yields SID "ATTACKER": strncmp fails at the 'x' but succeeds one byte later. Cookie ordering is attacker-controllable — browsers send longer-Path cookies first, so `xsession=...; Path=/api` sorts ahead of the real `session=...; Path=/`. HttpOnly protects the real cookie from being read or overwritten by script, but it does not stop script from creating a *differently named* cookie, so any of the already-documented same-origin XSS sinks (docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md sections on the .svg and filename XSS) upgrade from "run as the victim" to "pin the victim's browser onto an attacker-chosen session id", and on plaintext HTTP a LAN attacker who can inject one Set-Cookie achieves the same. Practical outcomes are session confusion and a persistent denial of service on the victim's browser (every request resolves to a nonexistent SID and 401s); it is not by itself a privilege escalation, because the attacker must already know a valid SID to gain anything.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1)
- **Direction:** Replace the hand-rolled scan with the httpd_req_get_cookie_val path that getCookieValue already wraps, which parses on real token boundaries. If the hand-rolled version must stay, only accept a match at offset 0 or immediately after a "; " separator.

### [LOW] /api/espnow/status runs three requiresAdmin commands under the "system" identity and returns the output to guests
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:556`
- **What:** `espnowRunInternal` (:556) hard-codes `transport = SOURCE_INTERNAL, user = "system"` and calls `executeCommand` (:563), which short-circuits `authorizeCommand` entirely at System_Utils.cpp:4303. `handleEspNowStatus` (:569) runs a fixed list of eight commands through it (:573-582). Three of those are registered `requiresAdmin = true`: `espnowmode` (System_ESPNow.cpp:15895), `espnowmeshrole` (:15900) and `espnowmeshes` (:15894, invoked as `espnowmeshes listjson`). `/api/espnow/status` is in the guest allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:369). The handler's own comment states this is deliberate ("so guests can load the page without /api/cli").
- **Why it matters:** A guest account reads configuration that the role model otherwise reserves for admins. I verified the payloads are not secret — `meshesCmd_listjson` (System_ESPNow.cpp:12783) emits only `hasPassphrase` as a boolean and never the passphrase or the PBKDF2-stretched key — so the impact is role-consistency and reconnaissance (mesh labels, fingerprints, mesh role, direct/mesh mode), not credential disclosure. The same pattern in `runInternalStatusCmd` (WebServer_Server.cpp:2989) is also argument-free.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_WEB_ESPNOW && ENABLE_ESPNOW
- **Direction:** Either mark these three status reads as non-admin in the registry (they are pure reads) or filter the results array by `isAdminUser(ctx.user)` before serializing, so one policy governs both surfaces.

### [LOW] Guest UI-hide sweep never reached /settings, /sensors, /battery and /files — guests see a fully-populated control surface that 403s on every write
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebPage_Settings.h:1`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_Sensors.h (0 data-guest-hide), components/hardwareone/WebPage_Battery.h (0), components/hardwareone/WebPage_Files.h (2, effectively unswept); components/hardwareone/WebServer_Utils.cpp:718 (the .guest-view CSS + applyGuestViewOnly injection that these pages opt into but never tag for)
- **What:** `streamBeginHtml` injects the `.guest-view [data-guest-hide]{display:none}` rule, the guest banner and the `hw.applyGuestViewOnly()` bootstrap into every authenticated page. Pages opt in by tagging their mutating controls with `data-guest-hide`. WebPage_Settings.h, WebPage_Sensors.h and WebPage_Battery.h contain zero such attributes, so for a guest the sweep hides nothing. Both /settings and /sensors submit through `hw.postForm('/api/cli', …)` / `/api/cli/batch`, which `webGuestApiAllowed` does not list — the POST is rejected 403 by `webGuestAccessAllowed` before any handler runs.
- **Why it matters:** A guest loads /settings, sees the full settings editor with live-looking Save controls and the complete schema panel, edits a value, and gets a silent 403. No data is changed (backend is genuinely safe — this is a presentation defect only), but the page contradicts the 'View-only guest session' banner rendered directly above it, and the same is true of every open/close sensor button on /sensors.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER; prior art: Prior guest audit recorded the sweep as covering Bluetooth/Automations/Logging/LLM/Speech/Maps/Bond/MQTT/Dashboard; these four pages were not in that list and still carry zero markers.
- **Direction:** Tag the write controls on /settings, /sensors, /battery and /files with `data-guest-hide`, mirroring what WebPage_Bluetooth.h and WebPage_Logging.h already do. A lint/grep check that every page calling `hw.postForm('/api/cli'…)` also contains at least one `data-guest-hide` would stop the next page from drifting.

### [LOW] findFreeSessionIndex' "evict oldest" branch can only return -1, so a full session table always silently evicts slot 0
