- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:226`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:345-346 (the `idx < 0` fallback that masks it)
- **What:** After the free-slot scan fails, the eviction loop is `unsigned long tOld = 0xFFFFFFFFUL; ... if (gSessions[i].expiresAt == 0 || (long)(gSessions[i].expiresAt - tOld) < 0) { oldest = i; tOld = ...; }` (:223-230). Every occupied slot has a nonzero expiresAt (set at :353), and `expiresAt - 0xFFFFFFFF` is `expiresAt + 1` in 32-bit wraparound arithmetic — a small positive number for any uptime under ~24 days — so the signed comparison is never < 0 on the first iteration, tOld is never lowered, and the function returns -1. setSession then does `if (idx < 0) idx = 0;` (:346).
- **Why it matters:** With MAX_SESSIONS=2 the practical damage is small (slot 0 happens to be the oldest in the common case), but the stated policy is not implemented and the failure is silent: the evicted user's slot is overwritten by `gSessions[idx] = s;` at :363 with no storeLogoutReason() and no enqueueTargetedRevokeForSessionIdx(), so that user just starts getting "unknown SID" 401s with no explanation, unlike every other revocation path which stores a reason. Raising MAX_SESSIONS — the obvious response to the 2-session limit — makes the bug bite properly, since a 5-slot table would still always evict slot 0.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1)
- **Direction:** Seed the search from the first occupied slot rather than 0xFFFFFFFF, and compare with wrap-safe signed deltas between the two candidates rather than against a sentinel. Store a logout reason for whoever gets evicted so the eviction is visible.

### [LOW] Two dead login-flow fossils are latent session-token exposures: /login/setsession mints a session with no authentication, and streamLoginSuccessContent writes the SID into page JavaScript
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3643`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebPage_LoginSuccess.h:73 and :79 (document.cookie with the raw sid); WebServer_Server.cpp:5327 (route registration); WebServer_Server.cpp:3662-3684 (a second inline copy of the same document.cookie polling page, also only reachable through the dead handler)
- **What:** handleLoginSetSession is registered as an unauthenticated GET route (:5327) and, if the global gSessUser is non-empty, calls setSession(req, user) (:3655) — minting a session and setting the cookie for whoever made the request, with no credential check anywhere in the handler. It is inert only because gSessUser is never assigned a non-empty value anywhere in the tree (verified: WebServer_Server.cpp:182 defines it, :3645/:3654/:3656 read and clear it, nothing writes it). Separately, WebPage_LoginSuccess.h:69-79 builds a page that does `document.cookie = 'session=<sid>; Path=/'` — a script-set cookie is by construction not HttpOnly and not SameSite=Strict — and embeds the raw SID in the served HTML. streamLoginSuccessContent has zero callers (the header is included at HardwareOne.cpp:50 and never used).
- **Why it matters:** Neither is reachable today, so this is a latent-hazard report rather than a live hole. The risk is resurrection: one assignment to gSessUser anywhere turns /login/setsession into an unauthenticated session-mint endpoint, and one call to streamLoginSuccessContent undoes the HttpOnly and SameSite=Strict guarantees that writeSessionCookie (:284-297) is careful to apply uniformly, while also putting the token in the DOM where any XSS can read it. The guest gate already treats /login/setsession as dangerous (WebServer_Utils.cpp:426 explicitly 403s guests on it, commented "Block CLI and session minting"), which shows the hazard was noticed without the route being removed.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1); prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_CODE_HEALTH_AUDIT.md:1134-1137 records /login/setsession as a dead route and recommends deleting it, but frames it purely as dead code — the unauthenticated-session-mint and the non-HttpO
- **Direction:** Delete the route, the handler and the gSessUser global — the current login flow does not use them. If the two-step redirect is wanted later, the pending value must be keyed to the client (nonce in the redirect URL, or the requesting IP + a short TTL), never a bare process-global username.

### [LOW] Public self-registration is dead: /register/submit is denied by authorizeCommand's own empty-identity gate, and each attempt burns the visitor's IP toward lockout
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3831`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.h:246; components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4311; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:2712 (cmd_userrequest, the intended target)
- **What:** handleRegisterSubmit is deliberately public (no tgRequireAuth). It builds `AuthContext ctx = makeWebAuthCtx(req)` at :3831; makeWebAuthCtx (WebServer_Server.h:246) sets transport/opaque/path/ip and leaves ctx.user default-constructed — empty. It then runs `userrequest <u> <p> <p>` via executeUnifiedWebCommand at :3836. authorizeCommand gate 2 (System_Utils.cpp:4311) rejects any empty-user non-INTERNAL context unless the command name is literally "login", so `userrequest` returns "Error: Authentication required." and ok=false. The salvage check `ok = ok || (out.indexOf("Request submitted for") >= 0)` at :3837 cannot rescue it because that string never appears.
- **Why it matters:** Every account request fails with "Unable to submit request. Please try again later." and, on the failure path at :3839, calls recordFailedLogin(ip). A visitor who retries a few times locks their own IP out of the login page (isLoginLocked is checked at :3765 and at WebServer_Server.cpp:552 for Basic Auth), and behind NAT that lockout is shared. So a broken feature is also a self-service denial-of-service against a whole office's source IP.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER); prior art: recon_surfaces.md G5. Not in any docs/*.md.
- **Direction:** Give the registration path an identity authorizeCommand accepts — either widen gate 2's exception from `login` to `{login, userrequest}` (both are legitimately pre-auth credential submits) or have handleRegisterSubmit call the internal helper directly under systemAuth instead of routing an anonymous ctx through the CLI pipeline. Independently, do not recordFailedLogin on a validation failure the visitor cannot fix.

### [LOW] Unauthenticated /api/ping exposes hostname, MAC, firmware version and restore state with Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3391`
- **What:** `handlePing` (:3391) sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` at :3393 and returns `hostname`, `mac`, `fingerprint`, `firmwareVersion`, `acceptingRestore` and `pendingConfirm` with no auth check at all. It is registered before the auth-bearing routes at :5329/:5386.
- **Why it matters:** Any web page the owner visits on the same LAN can fingerprint the device and read its exact firmware version cross-origin, which is exactly the input needed to pick a version-specific exploit. I confirmed the `fingerprint` field itself is safe — `getDeviceFingerprint()` (System_Settings.cpp:322-344) is SHA256(deviceKey || "HWBACKUP_DEVICE_FINGERPRINT") and is not reversible to the device encryption key — so this is reconnaissance, not key disclosure.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards built with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1
- **Direction:** Drop `firmwareVersion` and `mac` from the unauthenticated response (or gate the whole endpoint behind auth except when `gAcceptingRestore` is true, which is the only state the migration tool needs it for).

### [LOW] Web login broadcasts the submitted password's length, and the pass/fail verdict, to every output sink unconditionally
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3511`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3535
- **What:** BROADCAST_PRINTF("[login] POST attempt: username='%s', password_len=%u", u.c_str(), (unsigned)p.length()) at :3511, followed by broadcastOutput(validUser ? "[login] isValidUser result: true" : "[login] isValidUser result: false") at :3535 and BROADCAST_PRINTF("[login] Login successful for user: %s", ...) at :3577/:3607. BROADCAST_PRINTF is gated only on gOutputFlags (System_Debug.h:734) — no debug flag, no log level. Running on the httpd task there is no command context, so broadcastOutputCore falls back to MSG_ROUTE_ALL.
- **Why it matters:** Every web login attempt prints the attempted username plus the exact password length to serial, the web CLI mirror, the guest-viewable OLED console (OLED_CLI_VIEWER) and the persisted system log file. Password length is a real search-space reducer for an offline or online guessing attack, and the paired true/false line at :3535 turns the OLED console into a live login oracle that a view-only Guest standing at the device can watch. The lockout counter does not suppress the broadcast.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** Drop password_len from the line (username alone is the audit-relevant field), and move the isValidUser result line behind DEBUG_AUTHF instead of an unconditional broadcast.

### [LOW] handleFilesCreate interpolates a raw body field into a quoted command token with quotePath(), which does no escaping
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:4007`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1182 (`"filedelete " + quotePath(path)` — audit string only, not re-executed); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1523 (quotePath itself)
- **What:** `handleFilesCreate` extracts `name` from the POST body by substring scan and decodes only `%20` and `%2F` (:3975-3980), so a literal `"` byte in the body passes through untouched. It then builds `String cmd = "mkdir " + quotePath(path)` (:4007) or `snprintf(cmdBuf, sizeof(cmdBuf), "filecreate \"%s\"", path.c_str())` (:4028). `quotePath` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1523) is a bare `"" + path + ""` with no escaping and no validation, despite `normalizeFsPath` treating a `"` in a path as a hard reject at the other end of the pipeline.
- **Why it matters:** `name=a" confirm` yields the command line `filecreate "/a" confirm"`; `CommandArgs::parse` closes the quoted token at the injected quote and emits `confirm"` as a second positional argument. The attacker therefore controls extra argument slots on a command they did not intend to parameterize. Impact is bounded — I traced every command-submission site and there is no delimiter that starts a SECOND command, and neither `mkdir` nor `filecreate` has a dangerous bare flag — but the same `quotePath` helper is the documented way for "web / ESP-NOW / app glue" to hand a path to the parser (System_Filesystem.h:186), so any future consumer with a bare `confirm`/`--yes` token (as `filedelete` and `userdelete` already have) inherits a real safety-gate bypass.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** Have `quotePath` reject (or backslash-escape) a `"` and control chars rather than blindly wrapping — it is the single producer-side chokepoint and already has a matching consumer-side rule in `normalizeFsPath`. Reject the character in `handleFilesCreate`'s `name` for a clear 400 as well.

### [LOW] 121 of 124 `httpd_register_uri_handler()` calls are unchecked against a 160-slot table that the code's own comment says has silently overflowed before
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:5205`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:5289 (plain-HTTP config); the 121 unchecked sites span WebServer_Server.cpp, WebPage_Bond.cpp, WebPage_LLM.cpp, WebPage_Sensors.cpp, WebPage_ESPNow.cpp, WebPage_Maps.cpp, WebPage_Battery/Bluetooth/DarkRoom/Games/MQTT/R1_Health/Speech.cpp, System_EdgeImpulse.cpp
- **What:** `max_uri_handlers = 160` is set on both the HTTPS (:5205) and plain-HTTP (:5289) configs, with a comment recording that the previous default of 100 "silently drops later registrations — historically that meant /api/backup OPTIONS never attached and the migration tool's CORS preflight got 405". Of the 124 `httpd_register_uri_handler` call sites across the web layer, only three check the return — all in `WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp` (:788, :789, :805), which is where that bug was found and which even prints "URI handler table full? Increase max_uri_handlers." at :792.
- **Why it matters:** `httpd_register_uri_handler` returns ESP_ERR_HTTPD_HANDLERS_FULL and the caller registers the next route regardless, so the failure mode is a route that simply 404s — indistinguishable from a typo in the URL, and only ever noticed by whichever feature happens to sit at the end of registration order. I counted ~117 main-server registrations at current HEAD (WebServer_Server.cpp 51, WebPage_Bond 14, WebPage_LLM 12, WebPage_Sensors 11, WebPage_ESPNow 6, WebPage_Maps 5, MigrationTool 3, plus 15 across the smaller page modules), against the comment's stated "~110". So there are roughly 43 slots left and the count is drifting upward with no detection — which is exactly the situation that produced the original bug, one bump later. I could not run a build, so the exact post-preprocessor count for a given feature-flag combination is unverified; the site count is a floor.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (HTTP server builds)
- **Direction:** Wrap registration in a small helper that counts failures and logs one summary line ("N of M URI handlers failed to register") at the end of `startHttpServer` — that turns a silent 404 into a boot-time signal without touching 121 call sites individually.

### [LOW] ip_bans.json, mesh_peers.json and devices.json are truncate-rewritten with no or weak write checks, and a parse failure silently drops the whole set
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:961`
- **Also:** System_Maps.cpp:2421 (waypoints, serializeJson result discarded), System_ImageManager.cpp:217-284 (IMAGE_STORAGE_BOTH falls through when both tiers are low on space and sets success = true without checking f.write)
- **What:** saveIpBans (:940-965) opens IP_BANS_FILE with "w" and judges success by serializeJson's count > 0 — which, per the buffered-stdio analysis, cannot see an ENOSPC on a file this small. saveMeshPeers (System_ESPNow.cpp:470-533) and saveEspNowDevices (:536-600) are also "w" in place and check no write return value at all, returning skipped == 0 instead. All three loaders treat a parse failure as 'empty set' and continue.
- **Why it matters:** A truncated ip_bans.json parses as nothing at next boot, so every previously banned IP silently regains access — a fail-open on a security control caused purely by a storage failure. The equivalent for devices.json is loss of all pairings and stored AES keys; for mesh_peers.json, loss of topology. None of the three has a backup or a quarantine path, and none surfaces the loss beyond a debug-gated WARN.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Move these three onto the writeTextAtomic pattern (tmp + verified length + rename) like users.json, and log a durable [EVENT] when a load parses to zero entries from a non-empty file so silent set-loss is at least attributable.

### [LOW] Every web login attempt broadcasts the attempted username and the exact password length
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3511`
- **What:** handleLoginPost calls `BROADCAST_PRINTF("[login] POST attempt: username='%s', password_len=%u", u.c_str(), (unsigned)p.length())` before any auth check, and follows it at :3535 with `broadcastOutput(validUser ? "[login] isValidUser result: true" : "...false")`. BROADCAST_PRINTF is un-gated and the httpd task has no command context, so both go out on MSG_ROUTE_ALL.
- **Why it matters:** A plain 'user'-role account polls GET /api/cli/logs (allowed — webGuestAccessAllowed returns true for any non-guest, WebServer_Utils.cpp:403) and harvests `username='admin', password_len=14` for every login attempt on the device, plus the pass/fail outcome. That is a usable oracle for narrowing an offline or online guess, and it also records the usernames of anyone who has ever tried to sign in. When `log start` is active it is persisted to flash as well.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER
- **Direction:** Drop password_len entirely, or bucket it. The username and the pass/fail outcome already reach the proper audit sinks (recordLoginAttempt / logAuthAttempt / SYSEVT_LOGIN_OK|FAIL) which are admin-gated.

### [NOTE] /api/backup debug logging prints the first 20 bytes of the Authorization header into the web log mirror
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:303`
- **What:** `DEBUG_HTTPF("[Backup] Authorization header present (len=%d): %.20s...", hdr_len, auth_hdr)` at :303. DEBUG_HTTPF → DEBUGF_QUEUE_DEBUG → debugQueuePrintf (System_Debug.cpp:775), which enqueues with `MSG_ROUTE_ALL` at :800 — so it reaches the web mirror and therefore `GET /api/cli/logs`.
- **Why it matters:** Requires log level >= DEBUG and the DEBUG_HTTP flag, so it is conditional, not default. When those are on, the migration tool's Basic-Auth header is logged as `Basic <14 base64 chars>` — roughly the first 10 bytes of `user:password` — into a buffer readable by any authenticated non-guest via /api/cli/logs. Basic Auth is a real path here: `isAuthed` accepts it as a fallback at WebServer_Server.cpp:551-566.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: boards with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER && ENABLE_MIGRATION_TOOL
- **Direction:** Log only the scheme and the length, never any of the credential bytes.

### [NOTE] /login/setsession is an unauthenticated session-minting endpoint kept safe only by a global that is never assigned
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3643`
- **Also:** Global declared at WebServer_Server.cpp:182 (`String gSessUser;`), extern re-declared at :1132, read at :3645 and :3654, cleared at :3656. Guest gate explicitly denies the path at WebServer_Utils.cpp:~424.
- **What:** `handleLoginSetSession` (:3643) has no auth check whatsoever. If `gSessUser` is non-empty it calls `setSession(req, user)` at :3655 — minting and cookie-setting a session for whatever username that global holds — with no verification that the requester is the party who just authenticated. `gSessUser` is defined at :182 and, across the whole tree, is only ever read at :3645/:3654 and cleared at :3656; nothing assigns it a non-empty value, so today the handler always redirects to /login. `webGuestAccessAllowed` (WebServer_Utils.cpp:424) explicitly blocks guests from this path, which suggests it was live at some point.
- **Why it matters:** Not exploitable today: the global is permanently empty, so the handler is dead. It is reported because the code is one assignment away from a first-order vulnerability — if any login path were ever wired to stage a pending user in `gSessUser` (which is plainly what the two-step design intended), an unauthenticated attacker polling GET /login/setsession would race the legitimate browser and receive the admin's session cookie. A dead handler that mints sessions from a global is the wrong thing to leave in an HTTP handler table.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards built with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1; prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_CODE_HEALTH_AUDIT.md:1135 (framed as dead code, not as a latent auth hazard)
- **Direction:** Delete the handler, its registration and the `gSessUser` global. The one-step login at :3600 already sets the session and 303s directly, so the two-step path has no remaining purpose.

### [NOTE] MAX_SESSIONS is 2, so any two ordinary accounts can evict an admin's web session
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.h:55`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:218-232 (findFreeSessionIndex), :345-346 (fallback)
- **What:** #define MAX_SESSIONS 2 caps the whole device at two concurrent web sessions. setSession enforces one session per user (:331-343), so two distinct accounts fill the table, and a third login lands in the eviction path — which, per the preceding finding, always overwrites slot 0.
- **Why it matters:** Two low-privilege accounts (or one low-privilege account plus any other user) logging in are sufficient to push an admin out of their web session, repeatedly and at will, with no notice delivered to the evicted admin. It is a nuisance-grade availability issue rather than a confidentiality one — the admin can simply log in again, evicting someone else — but on a device where account creation is admin-approved it is worth knowing that the session table is this shallow.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1)
- **Direction:** If the 2-slot cap is a deliberate RAM decision, document it next to the #define; SessionEntry is ~800 B each, so 4 slots is ~1.6 KB and the array is already PSRAM-allocated. Fix the eviction ordering first either way, and store a logout reason for the evicted session.


### BLE / G2 / R1 — 15 findings (5 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] `blePushEvent` writes and transmits far past a 256-byte stack buffer; reachable from the non-admin `bleevent` command on every command surface
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:2226`
- **Also:** Bluetooth.cpp:2222 (the `details` branch) has the identical defect and fires first when details is non-empty.
- **What:** `char eventJson[256]; int pos = snprintf(eventJson, sizeof(eventJson), "{\"type\":%d,\"msg\":\"%s\"", eventType, message);` then unconditionally `pos += snprintf(eventJson + pos, sizeof(eventJson) - pos, ",\"ts\":%lu}", millis());` and finally `pEventNotifyChar->setValue((uint8_t*)eventJson, pos)`. snprintf returns the length it WOULD have written, so a long `message` leaves `pos` > 256. `sizeof(eventJson)` is `size_t`, `pos` is `int`, so `256 - pos` promotes to unsigned and becomes ~4.29e9 — the size argument stops bounding anything — while `eventJson + pos` already points past the array.
- **Why it matters:** `cmd_bleevent` (Bluetooth.cpp:1809, registered at :2070 with requiresAdmin=false) takes the raw CLI argument tail as `message` with no length check. Any authenticated non-admin user on serial / web `/api/cli` / MQTT / ESP-NOW remote command / G2 / OLED runs `bleevent x <2000 chars>` while a BLE client is connected. With a 2035-char message pos becomes ~2053, so the second snprintf writes ~17 bytes at eventJson[2053..2069] — roughly 1800 bytes past the end of a 256-byte array living in `cmd_exec_task`'s 8192-byte stack — clobbering caller frames' saved registers/return addresses. Then `setValue(..., pos)` hands the BLE stack a 2070-byte length for a 256-byte buffer, copying and notifying up to an MTU's worth of adjacent stack memory to the connected client. Memory corruption plus stack disclosure over the air.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH; primary FeatherS3)
- **Direction:** Both accumulator steps need the standard guard: clamp `pos` to `sizeof(eventJson)-1` after every snprintf and skip any further append once the buffer is full, and pass `strnlen(eventJson, sizeof(eventJson))` (not the snprintf return) to `setValue`. The message also needs a length bound at the `cmd_bleevent` entry, and is currently interpolated into JSON unescaped.

### [CRITICAL] G2 lens is the weakest surface: lens-initiated menu executes CLI as the device owner (super-admin) with zero authentication of any kind
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:177`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/BLE_Peers.cpp:144-174 (bleResolveStampUsername -> getDeviceOwnerUsername), components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:418 (no-explicit-super fallback makes the first account super), components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Files.cpp:615-627 (G2HijackCtxGuard then canRead then readTextLimited), components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1592 (permsForRole SUPER returns PERM_ALL), :1630 (isUnrestrictedRole bypasses the extension guard)
- **What:** g2HijackAuthContext() (G2_HijackCmd.cpp:177) sets ctx.user = gBlePeerData[BLE_PEER_G2_GLASSES].pairedByUser and ctx.transport = SOURCE_G2_GLASSES. tgRequireAuth (System_User.cpp:192-206) short-circuits SOURCE_G2_GLASSES to "pairing IS auth" — it only checks that pairedByUser is non-empty, never a credential. When pairedByUser is blank, bleStampPairedByIfBlank -> bleResolveStampUsername (BLE_Peers.cpp:145) falls through TLS -> live OLED session -> live serial session -> getDeviceOwnerUsername() (BLE_Peers.cpp:171). The owner is users.json entry #1, written role="superadmin" by first-time setup (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:893). isSuperAdminUser then returns true, so authorizeCommand gates 3/4/5 all pass, and VFS resolveRole (System_Filesystem.cpp:1562) returns FsRole::SUPER whose permsForRole is an unconditional PERM_ALL (:1592) — path rules do not apply. The hijack menu is entered by the WEARER (firmware MenuStartUp cmd=17 -> hijackBootstrapBody, G2_Glasses.cpp:6345-6396), not by any local command, and 148 g2SubmitHijackCommand call sites across 12 G2_Page_*.cpp reach userpromote/userdemote/userdelete/useradd (G2_Page_Users.cpp:296), filedelete/filerename (G2_Page_Files.cpp:489), the settings editor (G2_Page_Settings.cpp:565), WiFi join, power and ESP-NOW pages. There is no page-level role check: G2_Page_Users.cpp:93 merely reads isSuperAdminUser(g2HijackAuthContext().user) to decide which role rows to draw.
- **Why it matters:** Physical possession of the paired glasses == super-admin CLI + unrestricted filesystem. There is no per-session credential, no PIN, no confirmation, and no BLE link-layer security anywhere in first-party code (zero hits for BLESecurity/setSecurityAuth/ESP_GATT_PERM_*_ENC under components/hardwareone/), so the link is also not cryptographically bound to the real glasses: the pairing scan matches on the advertised NAME pattern only (classifyG2Name, G2_Glasses.cpp:1172) and the boot auto-reconnect matches on a saved public MAC (g2ConnectSavedSync, :9007) that an attacker-controlled ESP32 can set. Concretely: attacker advertises "Even G2_00001_L_x"/"..._R_x" (or spoofs the saved MAC), the device auto-reconnects at boot via g2ConnectSaved() which calls bleStampPairedByIfBlank FIRST (:9040), then the attacker sends a MenuStartUp + tap notifications and runs `userpromote attacker superadmin` / `filedelete /system/users/users.json` as the owner.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES); prior art: Partially — recon_surfaces.md G7 and docs/G2_INTERACTIVE_SETTINGS_PLAN.md:60 note the owner fallback. The full chain (owner fallback -> FsRole::SUPER unconditional PERM_ALL -> lens-INITIATED hijack en
- **Direction:** The lens needs a credential of its own, not an inherited one. Two independent levers: (1) stop the owner fallback in bleResolveStampUsername — an anonymous boot-reconnect worker should leave the peer UNOWNED rather than homing it to the super-admin, and (2) cap the effective rank of SOURCE_G2_GLASSES inside authorizeCommand/resolveRole (e.g. never above ADMIN, or gate mutating pages behind a lens-entered PIN). Separately, consider requiring an application-layer challenge over the G2 link before the first hijack menu of a session, since MAC/name matching is not authentication.

### [HIGH] G2 text-entry isSecret is defeated downstream — the submit path echoes the assembled credential line at four sites, three of which are on by default
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:138`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:100, :105, :144; components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Users.cpp:122
- **What:** `TextEntryConfig::isSecret` (G2_Page_TextEntry.h:49) does suppress the buffer at all three keyboard debug sites (G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp:189, :203, :216), and is set at the three password call sites (G2_Glasses.cpp:4282, G2_Page_Network.cpp, G2_Page_Users.cpp). But the commit callbacks then assemble a full command line containing the plaintext and hand it to `g2SubmitHijackCommand`, which logs it: `DEBUG_G2F("[G2-Hijack] submit '%.40s' …", line, …)` at G2_HijackCmd.cpp:138, plus `WARN_COMMANDF("g2.hijack.cmd: reject blank pairedByUser line='%s'", line)` at :100, `WARN_COMMANDF("g2.hijack.cmd: queue full line='%s'", line)` at :144, and `ERROR_MEMORYF("g2.hijack.cmd: alloc failed line='%s'", line)` at :105 — all three of the latter print the WHOLE line. G2_Page_Users.cpp:122 additionally does `DEBUG_G2F("[G2-USERS] submit '%s'", line)` with no truncation. The lines are: `login "<user>" "<pass>" display` (G2_Glasses.cpp:4260), `wifiadd "<ssid>" "<pass>" 1 0` (G2_Page_Network.cpp:1260), `useradd "<user>" "<pass>" 0 <role>` (G2_Page_Users.cpp:264).
- **Why it matters:** WARN_COMMANDF/ERROR_MEMORYF expand to `DEBUGF_QUEUE(DEBUG_ALWAYS | …)` (System_Debug.h:628, :632), and `DEBUGF_QUEUE` (:440-445) fires whenever the DEBUG_ALWAYS bit is set REGARDLESS of any debug flag, gated only by `getLogLevel() >= LOG_LEVEL_WARN` — and the shipped default is `logLevel(3)` = DEBUG (System_Settings.h:193). So the three full-line sites leak with no debug flag enabled at all. The `%.40s` site at :138 needs DEBUG_G2 on, but 40 chars covers most of a PSK: `wifiadd "` is 9 characters, so with an 8-character SSID the password begins at offset 20. Every one of these lines is enqueued with MSG_ROUTE_ALL and, when `systemlog` is running, appended verbatim to a file on flash (System_Debug.cpp:254-268) and mirrored to the web console. The audit log itself is clean — `redactCmdForAudit` (System_Utils.cpp:1114-1140) masks login/wifiadd/useradd correctly — so the only leak is this debug path, which bypasses it entirely.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1 && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES=1 (both 1 in the current build) — LIVE; prior art: KNOWN — project_g2_textentry_secret_flag memory records the :138 site as "known remaining leak, flagged as spawned task, not fixed". STATUS CHANGED: it is worse than recorded — three additional sites 
- **Direction:** Carry the secret intent across the hop: add a submit flag (or route these two callers through a variant) that makes all four log sites print redactCmdForAudit(line) instead of the raw line. The redaction table already covers `login ` and `wifiadd ` and nthTokenBounds is quote-aware, so the masking works on these exact strings.

### [HIGH] G2 text-entry isSecret is defeated one hop later: g2SubmitHijackCommand logs the composed command line, password included, on always-on WARN/ERROR paths
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:144`
- **Also:** producers: components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:4260, components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Network.cpp:1260, components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Users.cpp:223
- **What:** g2SubmitHijackCommand prints the raw `line` argument in four places: :100 WARN_COMMANDF("g2.hijack.cmd: reject blank pairedByUser line='%s'", line), :106 ERROR_MEMORYF("g2.hijack.cmd: alloc failed line='%s'", line), :138 DEBUG_G2F("[G2-Hijack] submit '%.40s' ...", line), :144 WARN_COMMANDF("g2.hijack.cmd: queue full line='%s'", line). The three WARN/ERROR macros expand to DEBUGF_QUEUE(DEBUG_ALWAYS | ...) (System_Debug.h:599, :628) — DEBUG_ALWAYS short-circuits the flag check in DEBUGF_QUEUE (System_Debug.h:442), so no debug flag is needed; WARN only needs getLogLevel() >= LOG_LEVEL_WARN, and the default is LOG_LEVEL_DEBUG. The lines they print are built by the lens flows that deliberately set cfg.isSecret = true: G2_Glasses.cpp:4260 `login "<user>" "<pass>" display` (isSecret set at :4282), G2_Page_Network.cpp:1260 `wifiadd "<ssid>" "<psk>" 1 0` (isSecret at :1313), G2_Page_Users.cpp:223 (new-user password). G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp's isSecret flag (:189, :203, :216) only suppresses three DEBUG_G2F lines inside the keyboard itself; it has no effect once the string is handed to the submit bridge.
- **Why it matters:** A wearer logs the OLED in from the lens with password 'hunter2'. If the cmd_exec queue happens to be full, :144 emits `[WARN][CMD] g2.hijack.cmd: queue full line='login "alice" "hunter2" display'` to serial, the web mirror, the OLED console and the system log file — with no debug flag enabled. The same happens on an allocation failure (:106) and on the blank-pairedByUser path (:100). With DEBUG_G2 on, :138 leaks it on the success path too: `login "alice" "hunter2" display` is 31 chars, inside the %.40s window, and `wifiadd "MyNetwork" "hunter2pass" 1 0` is 36. The isSecret flag's comment ("keep out of debug logs") is therefore false in practice.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES; prior art: KNOWN — memory file project_g2_textentry_secret_flag.md records "submit-echo leak still open". Status UNCHANGED; this report pins the four exact sites and shows three of them are NOT debug-gated.
- **Direction:** Print redactCmdForAudit(line) in all four traces — the existing rules already cover `login ` (token 3) and `wifiadd ` (token 3) and are quote-aware (nthTokenBounds, System_Utils.cpp:1055), so the quoted lens forms redact correctly. Alternatively give g2SubmitHijackCommand an isSecret parameter that suppresses the line entirely.

### [HIGH] G2 command-submit echoes log full credential command lines; two of the four sites are DEBUG_ALWAYS and fire regardless of the debug flag
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:100`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:138 (DEBUG_G2F, `%.40s`), :144 (WARN_COMMANDF, full line, queue-full path), components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Users.cpp:122 (DEBUG_G2F `'%s'` — full line, no truncation).
- **What:** The `TextEntryConfig::isSecret` fix works exactly as recorded — G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp:189/:203/:216 log only `len` when set, and the flag is set at the three password prompts (G2_Page_Network.cpp:1313, G2_Glasses.cpp:4282, G2_Page_Users.cpp:223). But the commit callbacks then concatenate the secret into a command line and submit it: `wifiadd "<ssid>" "<psk>" 1 0` (G2_Page_Network.cpp:1260), `login "<user>" "<pass>" display` (G2_Glasses.cpp:4260), `useradd "<user>" "<pass>" 0 <role>` (G2_Page_Users.cpp:264). `g2SubmitHijackCommand` then logs that line at three points. Critically, `WARN_COMMANDF` expands to `DEBUGF_QUEUE(DEBUG_ALWAYS | DEBUG_CLI, ...)` (System_Debug.h:628) and `DEBUGF_QUEUE` short-circuits the flag test whenever `DEBUG_ALWAYS` is present (System_Debug.h:442) — so :100 and :144 emit with no debug flag enabled, at the default WARN log level.
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete paths. (1) Queue full: `submitCommandAsync` fails → :144 logs `g2.hijack.cmd: queue full line='useradd "bob" "hunter2xyz" 0 admin'` verbatim. (2) Blank pairedByUser: :100 logs `reject blank pairedByUser line='login "alice" "s3cret" display'` verbatim — and a blank stamp is precisely the state a user is trying to fix when they reach the login screen. `debugQueuePrintf` routes with `MSG_ROUTE_ALL` (System_Debug.cpp:800), so the line goes to serial, the web console mirror, every attached BLE CLI sink, and — when system logging is on — is appended to `gSystemLogPath` on flash (System_Debug.cpp:254-270). Note `redactCmdForAudit` protects only the *audit* log inside `authorizeCommand`; none of these four sites go through it. The on-disk copy is then readable over the air via the ESP-NOW FS finding above.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: All boards with ENABLE_G2_GLASSES=1 (=1 in the current config, System_BuildConfig.h:228).; prior art: Recorded as the open "submit-echo leak" in the G2 isSecret memory note, which cites only G2_HijackCmd.cpp:138. Status: still open, and WORSE than recorded — three additional sites, two of them not gat
- **Direction:** Redact at the single chokepoint: run `redactCmdForAudit(line)` (System_Utils.cpp, table at :1115) before every log call in `g2SubmitHijackCommand`, and do the same at G2_Page_Users.cpp:122. That reuses the existing `wifiadd`/`login`/`useradd` rules instead of adding a second policy.

### [MEDIUM] A BLE `login <user> <pass>` is stored verbatim in the BLE message history and rendered in cleartext on the guest-viewable OLED Bluetooth screen
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:710`
- **Also:** accessor components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:2148; renderer components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Bluetooth.cpp:169-176; guest allowlist components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:2932
- **What:** processBleCommandLine copies the raw inbound line into the message ring BEFORE it inspects it: :709-711 snprintf(tagged, 32, "RX:%.*s", 28, cmdStart); bleAddMessageToHistory(tagged). The login branch that would have masked the credential is not reached until :716. bleGetRecentMessages (:2148) hands the two newest entries to the OLED, and OLED_Mode_Bluetooth.cpp:169-176 prints them under a "Last:" heading. OLED_BLUETOOTH_STATUS is on the guest allowlist (OLED_Utils.cpp:2932). The 32-byte slot holds "RX:" plus 28 characters, so `login alice hunter2` (19 chars) is retained in full. :704 BLE_DEBUGF(DEBUG_BLE_DATA, "Processing command: %s", cmdStart) leaks the same string to the log sinks when that flag is on.
- **Why it matters:** A phone app authenticates over BLE with `login alice hunter2`. The string is immediately parked in bleMessageHistory. The device then sends its response, which is added as a TX: entry (:1275-1277), so the two displayed slots become [TX:response, RX:login alice hunter2] — both rendered. A view-only Guest logged in at the OLED can navigate to Bluetooth > status and read another user's cleartext password off the screen. The history is RAM-only and holds 4 entries, so exposure is short-lived, but it is a straight Guest -> higher-tier credential disclosure.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY (the history writer is inside #if ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY). Not present on ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY=0 builds.; prior art: docs/HEAP_OFFLOAD_SWEEP_2026-07-24.md §4 notes in passing that bleMessageHistory holds a raw inbound `login <user> <pass>` (as a reason not to move it to PSRAM). The OLED rendering and the guest-allow
- **Direction:** Move the bleAddMessageToHistory call below the login/logout/whoami branches so credential commands never enter the ring, or store redactCmdForAudit(cmdStart) instead of the raw line.

### [MEDIUM] BLE login handler pins the transport to "bluetooth" but rebuilds the command line unquoted, so a password containing a space overrides it — a BLE client can authenticate the physical OLED or serial console
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:756`
- **Also:** Contrast with the correct pattern at components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:4260, which builds `login "<user>" "<pass>" display` with both operands quoted
- **What:** `processBleCommandLine` intercepts any line beginning with `login ` (Bluetooth.cpp:715) precisely so it can control the transport argument. It splits at the FIRST space after the verb: `u` is everything up to that space, `p` is the ENTIRE remainder including any further spaces (Bluetooth.cpp:727-736). It then rebuilds the line with a hardcoded transport:

    snprintf(loginCmd, sizeof(loginCmd), "login %s %s bluetooth", u, p);   // :756

Neither `u` nor `p` is quoted. `cmd_login` (System_Utils.cpp:5276) parses with `CommandArgs`, which splits on spaces, reads `transportStr = a.arg(2)` (:5287) and **ignores every argument past index 2** — there is no arity check. So the trailing literal `bluetooth` is discarded whenever the password contains a space, and `a.arg(2)` becomes the second word of the password.

`loginTransport` (System_User.cpp:439) then sets the *global* session state for whichever transport it was handed: `gSerialAuthed`/`gSerialUser` (:466) or `gLocalDisplayAuthed`/`gLocalDisplayUser` (:472).
- **Why it matters:** A BLE client with valid credentials for account `alice` writes to the CLI characteristic:

    login alice <alice's password> display

BLE splits it as u="alice", p="<password> display". The rebuilt line is `login alice <password> display bluetooth`. `CommandArgs` yields arg0=alice, arg1=<password>, arg2=display, arg3=bluetooth(ignored). `cmd_login` maps "display" to SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY (:5292) and `loginTransport` marks the physical OLED console logged in as alice. It returns "Login successful for 'alice' on display", and `bleLoginAsyncCallback` (Bluetooth.cpp:612-619) tests `strstr(res, "Login successful")` — so the BLE session is bound as well. The attacker gets both sessions from one write.

Substituting `serial` does the same to the physical serial console. This defeats the entire point of the `serialrequireauth` / `displayrequireauth` toggles, which are deliberately `requiresSuperAdmin=true` (System_Settings.cpp:241-242) — a remote BLE actor with only a plain `user` account can now hand a live local-console session to whoever is standing at the device.

The interception at Bluetooth.cpp:715 is what makes this distinct from the general "login takes a transport argument" behaviour: sending `login alice pw display` directly over BLE is supposed to be impossible, because the handler rewrites the line. The unquoted rebuild is exactly what defeats its own mitigation.

Same construction is a plain functional bug for honest users: `isValidPublicPassword` (System_User.cpp:812) imposes only a length check, so any password containing a space makes BLE login fail with "Error: Invalid transport" (System_Utils.cpp:5299) with no hint why.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH (the display half additionally needs ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY)
- **Direction:** Quote both operands in the rebuild (`"login \"%s\" \"%s\" bluetooth"`) — `CommandArgs` handles quoted tokens verbatim and `normalizeFsPath`-style quote rejection is not needed here since the BLE printable-filter at Bluetooth.cpp:684 already drops nothing but keeps `"`; alternatively strip `"` from u/p first. Independently, `cmd_login` should reject arity > 3 rather than silently ignoring extra tokens, so a boundary shift fails loudly instead of picking a different transport.

### [MEDIUM] No automated test catches any defect class in this audit — G2_Page_TestSuite contains zero assertions and is a human-in-the-loop visual bench
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:12`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.h:12-16 (states the no-assertion method explicitly); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Wire.h (32 of the 61 static_asserts)
- **What:** G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp is 3013 lines with no assertion of any kind (grep for assert/expect/PASS/FAIL over the file returns only `pdPASS` from xTaskCreate checks and one "FAILED" string in a log line at :2984). Its own header states the method: "The test pattern is 'if you can see the page, the transport worked'" (G2_Page_TestSuite.h:12-16). It exercises BLE fragment sizes (kBrackets, :172-207), AI-panel pipelines (:277-291), font coverage (:315-440), container geometry (:565-600) and image probes — all judged by a human looking at the lens. The only mechanised checks in the first-party tree are `r1ProtocolSelfTest()` (see the separate finding on its discarded result), the `g2nativenotify selftest` CRC vector (G2_Glasses.cpp:14949), and 61 `static_assert`s — 32 of which are wire-struct layout in System_ESPNow_Wire.h. There is no unit-test target, no host build, and no Unity/CTest harness anywhere under components/hardwareone.
- **Why it matters:** Every finding in this report is invisible to the existing suite, and the gaps are structural rather than incidental: (a) authorization decisions — nothing tests that `isValidPublicUsername` rejects reserved names, that USER_SYNC refuses them, that `authorizeCommand`'s five gates fire in order, or that the guest gate blocks a mutation; (b) opcode-flag policy — nothing asserts the kV4HandlerTable rows carry the flags their comments claim, which is exactly how SENSOR_BROADCAST's flags=0 survived the D2 hardening; (c) return-contract conformance — nothing checks that a command's result satisfies the OK:/Error: contract, which is why the G2 settings surface mis-reads "SUCCESS" as failure; (d) cross-surface parity — nothing compares the four users.json append paths, the two directory-listing filters, or the two settings-commit success predicates. The wire-layout static_asserts are the one thing that works well precisely because the compiler enforces them (matching the codebase's own heuristic that "anything a compiler or static_assert can check is scrupulously accurate").
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** The highest-leverage additions are the ones that need no hardware and no framework: compile-time assertions over the handler table (a constexpr check that every FS_*/SENSOR_*/USER_SYNC row carries its documented flag mask, so a policy comment and its row cannot diverge again), and a small operator-invocable `selftest` command that runs pure-function checks — isValidPublicUsername against the reserved list, stampOkStatus against each exemption, findCommand's longest-match and duplicate-first-wins behaviour — and returns an Error: string on any failure so the existing uniform return contract carries the result.

### [MEDIUM] There is no automated test of any kind in first-party code; the only "test suite" is a manual on-glasses rendering bench that cannot observe any of this audit's defect classes
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1`
- **Also:** tools/command_registry.py (the repo's only executable regression check — `audit` subcommand, cited in docs/DOCS_ACCURACY_AUDIT_2026-07-26.md) and tools/settings_registry.py; 61 static_assert sites across components/hardwareone are the only compile-time invariants
- **What:** G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp/.h (3,062 lines) is a hijack-page UI, not a test harness. Its own header states the contract: "The test pattern is 'if you can see the page, the transport worked'" — a human wears the glasses, taps a bracket, and judges the result by eye. It covers BLE CREATE-list fragmentation at 100 B / 250 B / 500 B / 1 KB / 2 KB, AI-panel pipeline variants, ASCII and Unicode glyph rendering, display geometry, image/animation paths and selection-edge cases. There are no assertions, no pass/fail state, no result aggregation. Outside it: no unity test app, no host test target, no .github directory and no CI YAML anywhere in the repo; the only directories named test*/ belong to managed_components and third_party.
- **Why it matters:** Every finding in this report is invisible to what exists. The bench cannot reach a non-G2 surface, so it cannot observe that the OLED peer file browser authorizes differently from `espnowbrowse`, that loginTransport has no lockout while HardwareOne.cpp's inline copy does, that /api/cli/batch skips redactOutputForLog, or that USER_SYNC skips isValidPublicUsername. More generally, the whole class this audit is about — the same operation implemented on N surfaces and drifting on surface N+1 — is precisely what a cross-surface assertion would catch and what a human looking at a lens cannot. The economics are unusually favourable here because the decision functions are pure string-in/verdict-out and carry no hardware dependency: commandAllowedForGuest (System_Utils.cpp:4356), userRoleRank (System_User.cpp:424), isValidPublicUsername/Password (System_User.cpp:786/813), lookupRule + permsForRole + hasSensitiveExtension + pathWithinScope (System_Filesystem.cpp:1396/1582/1417/1611), normalizeFsPath (:1458), redactCmdForAudit and redactOutputForLog (System_Utils.cpp:1180/1213), CommandArgs (System_Command.h:63) and findCommand (System_Command.cpp:110). A table-driven test over those alone would have caught findings 3, 5, 8 and 10 outright and would pin the role/path matrix that finding 1 violates.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: the bench itself needs ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES; the coverage gap is board-independent
- **Direction:** Add a host-buildable test target for the pure decision functions listed above (table-driven: input line/path/role -> expected verdict), and extend the existing tools/command_registry.py audit into a cross-surface parity check — e.g. assert that every command a UI surface dispatches by string exists in the registry, and that endpoints marked as mirrors of a CLI command carry the same requiresAdmin bit. Do not try to grow G2_Page_TestSuite into this; it is a rendering bench and should stay one.

### [MEDIUM] BLE scans run at 99% RF duty cycle in active mode, for up to 300 s, and repeat indefinitely on auto-reconnect backoff, with no coexistence throttle while WiFi/ESP-NOW share the radio
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:975`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:7986-7992 (3 s scan), :8649-8651 and :8746-8748 with SCAN_DURATION_MS=12000 at :194; components/hardwareone/BLE_Peers.cpp:334-340 (backoff ladder) and :406-446 (bleAutoReconnectTick calls connectSaved, which scans); G2_Ring.cpp:963 (timeout clamp 300 s), :992 and :1043 (help text suggests `ringscan 60`)
- **What:** Every BLE scan in the tree is configured setActiveScan(true) / setInterval(100) / setWindow(99). At the BLE 0.625 ms unit that is a 62.5 ms interval with a 61.9 ms window — 99% RF occupancy, and active (the scanner transmits SCAN_REQ). g2RingScan takes a caller-supplied duration clamped only at 300 s. G2 scans run 3 s (mid-session reseek) or 12 s (SCAN_DURATION_MS). BLE_Peers schedules connectSaved() — which runs one of these scans — on a 5/15/45/90/180 s ladder that caps at 180 s and never stops while the peer is registered with autoReconnect and out of range.
- **Why it matters:** Wi-Fi and BLE time-share one 2.4 GHz front end through the coex scheduler, and ESP-NOW is a "connectionless module" whose RF access is already time-division allocated under coexistence (IDF docs/en/api-guides/wifi.rst:1888-1891). A 99%-duty active scan pushes the coex arbiter hard toward BLE for the whole scan. Concrete cases: (1) glasses go out of range with autoReconnect on — a 12 s near-continuous active scan fires every 3 minutes, forever, each one a mesh brownout; the ring backs off on its own independent ladder so the two overlap. (2) `ringscan 300`, or the `ringscan 60` the code's own hint text recommends, is a user-reachable multi-minute ESP-NOW/WiFi brownout with no warning. A marginal STA link can miss enough beacons in that window to hit supervision timeout. The code already reasons carefully about intra-BLE contention (the connection-priority arbiter at G2_Ring.cpp:1000-1012 explicitly drops glasses to BALANCED so a third link can be established) but there is no equivalent awareness of the WiFi/ESP-NOW side.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH (FeatherS3 primary; QT Py ESP32 excluded — boards/qtpy_esp32.defaults:35 sets CONFIG_BT_ENABLED=n); prior art: Principle stated for a hypothetical future feature in docs/DEFLOCK_CAR_DETECTOR_PLAN.md §6.2 (which mandates PASSIVE scan); never applied to the shipped G2/Ring scans
- **Direction:** Drop the scan duty cycle (e.g. window ~30 of interval 100) and prefer passive scan whenever wifiRadioOn() is true, and cap the user-supplied ringscan duration far below 300 s while ESP-NOW or an STA association is up.

### [MEDIUM] BLE scans run at 99% radio duty cycle with no coordination against WiFi/ESP-NOW; a scan longer than ~1 s aborts any in-flight ESP-NOW fragmented transfer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Ring.cpp:1006`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:7986-7988 (recovery scan), :8649-8651 (init scan); components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:615 (WiFi scan, same collision)
- **What:** Every BLE scan in the project is configured setInterval(100) / setWindow(99) (G2_Ring.cpp:1005-1007, G2_Glasses.cpp:7986-7988 and 8649-8651). BLEScan.cpp:122-143 converts those milliseconds to 0.625 ms units, giving scan_interval=160 / scan_window=158 — 99 ms of radio-on per 100 ms, i.e. a 99% duty active scan. `ringscan <sec>` accepts up to 300 seconds (G2_Ring.cpp:992-993). There is no radio arbiter anywhere: grepping G2_Ring.cpp, BLE_IDF.cpp and the scan paths in G2_Glasses.cpp for isEspNowInitialized / WiFi.isConnected / wifiRadioOn returns nothing (one unrelated hit at G2_Glasses.cpp:3637). SW coexistence is enabled (sdkconfig:1252) so the stack arbitrates, but arbitration under a 99%-duty BLE claim leaves very little for WiFi.
- **Why it matters:** ESP-NOW fragmented TX is stop-and-wait with ACK_TIMEOUT_MS = 200 and MAX_RETRIES = 3 (System_ESPNow.cpp:2145-2146). A fragment therefore has roughly a 1-second total budget (3 × 200 ms ACK wait plus 50/100/150 ms send-failure backoff) before the entire transfer is aborted at 2227-2232. Any BLE scan is at least 3 s (glasses recovery), 15 s (ring self-scan, G2_Ring.cpp:1072) or up to 300 s (`ringscan 300`), so a user who starts a scan while a file is streaming loses the whole transfer with no resume. The same 1-second budget is also blown by `wifiscan` (System_WiFi.cpp:615, a synchronous all-channel scan taking roughly 11 × 120 ms). Nothing warns, defers, or retries at the transfer level.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Widen the scan interval relative to the window (e.g. 100/40) when ESP-NOW or WiFi is up, and/or gate long scans behind a check for an active ESP-NOW transfer — the espnow_task already tracks stream state. Alternatively raise the fragment ACK budget so a multi-second radio stall degrades throughput instead of aborting the transfer.

### [LOW] No BLE link-layer security anywhere: the GATT CLI service is reachable by any peer, and with blerequireauth off it executes as the AuthBypass sentinel
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:201`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:794,811,871; components/hardwareone/System_Settings.h:365
- **What:** A grep for BLESecurity / setSecurityAuth / esp_ble_gap_set_security_param / ESP_GATT_PERM_*_ENC across components/hardwareone/ returns zero hits — every match is in the vendored components/arduino BLE library's examples and README. No characteristic sets encrypted access permissions. Peer identity is a bare stored MAC (BLE_Peers.cpp bleSavePeerMac). The application-layer defences that do exist are: bleRequireAuth (default true, System_Settings.h:365) checked at Bluetooth.cpp:794-800; the optional X25519/HKDF secure channel gated by blesecret (bleScRequired at :871); and a hard secure-channel requirement for file-browser verbs at :811. When bleRequireAuth is off, bleFillCommandAuth (:201) stamps user = "AuthBypass".
- **Why it matters:** Defaults are safe, so this is LOW as shipped. It matters because it is the substrate under two higher findings: (a) with blerequireauth off, an unpaired BLE peer in range reaches executeCommand as a User-tier identity, which is enough to answer a pending factoryreset confirm with `yes` and enough to send `remote:<anything>` to the bonded peer as super-admin; and (b) if the owner account is named AuthBypass (see that finding), the same peer is super-admin locally. The absence of link security is also what makes the G2 client-side impersonation in the top finding practical.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH); prior art: KNOWN — memory project_security_review_backlog.md item 4. RE-VERIFIED STILL OPEN: zero first-party uses of any BLE security API.
- **Direction:** Treat blerequireauth=off as a debug-only posture and say so in the command help (it is already super-admin-gated, which is correct). If BLE is meant to be a trusted channel, the secure channel (blesecret) should be the default rather than opt-in — bleScRequired() already exists as the enforcement point, so the change is a policy default, not new machinery.

### [LOW] Latched G2 live-page owner AuthContexts are cross-task Arduino-String globals handed off with only a 300 ms bounded wait
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Glasses.cpp:12372`
- **Also:** `gImgProbeOwnerCtx` (G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:977, written :1072, read :985) is the same shape; `gAutomationLogOwnerCtx` (System_Automation.cpp:172-176) is a latched AuthContext written on cmd_exec and read from the scheduler on the main loop.
- **What:** `gLivePageOwnerCtx` (declared :12190) and `gLiveTextOwnerCtx` (:12454) are file-scope `AuthContext` globals — five Arduino `String` members each. They are ASSIGNED on the tap-dispatcher task at :12372 and :13232 (`= g2HijackAuthContext()`), and copy-constructed FROM on the `g2_session_w` worker task at :12286, :13063 and :13156 (`CommandIdentityScope scope(gLiveTextOwnerCtx)`). The only synchronization is `g2StopLiveListPage` (:12388-12398) / `g2StopLiveTextPage` (:13253-13261), each of which spins at most 30 x 10 ms and then returns unconditionally whether or not the worker actually exited; the start path (:12336, :13202-13203) then proceeds to overwrite the global regardless.
- **Why it matters:** If the outgoing worker is inside a long `renderFn`/`buildFn` — the LLM viewer, Health page and map page all push BLE images and load tiles — it will not observe the stop flag within the 300 ms spin. The new page then assigns the global (String::operator= frees and reallocates the old buffer) while the still-running worker executes the `CommandIdentityScope` constructor, which copy-constructs those same Strings. A torn read of a String whose buffer is being freed underneath yields a bad pointer/length -> heap corruption or a LoadProhibited fault, with no lock or atomic anywhere on the handoff.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES (FeatherS3 default)
- **Direction:** Copy the latched AuthContext into the worker's own frame at job-submit time (pass it through the `g2SessionSubmit` payload) instead of reading a mutable global mid-run, or make the stop path actually block until the worker signals exit rather than giving up after 300 ms. I could not measure a real renderFn exceeding 300 ms on hardware, hence PLAUSIBLE rather than CONFIRMED.

### [NOTE] G2 text-entry renders the secret buffer in cleartext on the lens even when isSecret is set
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp:99`
- **What:** buildTextEntryText emits the buffer row with appendf(out, cap, off, "%s: %s_\n", prompt, gTE.buf) at :99-101 — unconditionally, with no reference to gTE.isSecret. The isSecret flag is consulted only at :189, :203 and :216, where it swaps three DEBUG_G2F messages for length-only variants. The rendered rows are then shipped to the glasses via g2StartLiveListPage.
- **Why it matters:** During OLED-login password entry (G2_Glasses.cpp:4277-4283), WiFi PSK entry (G2_Page_Network.cpp:1307-1313) and new-user password entry (G2_Page_Users.cpp:223), the characters typed so far are displayed in plaintext on the lens. For a head-up display only the wearer sees it, so the practical severity is low — but the flag is named isSecret and its comment says "suppress buf contents", which reads broader than the three log lines it actually covers. Worth recording so nobody assumes the flag masks the UI. (Verified clean on the adjacent worry: the live-page worker at G2_Glasses.cpp:12277-12314 never logs textBuf, so the in-progress buffer is not echoed to any log sink.)
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES
- **Direction:** Either render '*' repeated gTE.len times when isSecret is set, or rename the field to something like suppressInLogs so the narrower contract is obvious at the call sites.

### [NOTE] G2 text-entry leaves the last typed password in its static buffer after commit or cancel
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp:122`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Page_TextEntry.cpp:133 (finishCancel)
- **What:** finishCommit() snapshots gTE.buf into a stack array, clears gTE.active, and calls the commit callback — but never clears gTE.buf or gTE.len. finishCancel() (:133) likewise only flips gTE.active. `static TextEntryState gTE = {}` has no EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR, so it lives in internal .bss. The isSecret plumbing around it is otherwise correct: the debug emitters at :189-195, :203-207 and :215-220 all branch on gTE.isSecret and print only the length, and the three secret callers (WiFi PSK G2_Page_Network.cpp:1313, new-user password G2_Page_Users.cpp:223, login password G2_Glasses.cpp:4282) do set the flag.
- **Why it matters:** After a user types a WiFi PSK or a login password on the glasses, that plaintext remains in .bss until the next text-entry session happens to overwrite it — surviving the page teardown, any subsequent crash dump, and any heap/memory-inspection command. The risk is materially lower than the PSRAM cases because internal DRAM is not externally probeable, which is why this is a NOTE: it is a hygiene gap in an otherwise well-handled path, not an exposure by itself.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** memset gTE.buf (and the local `snap` after the callback returns) at the end of finishCommit/finishCancel when isSecret is set — or unconditionally, since the buffer is small.


### Storage / filesystem — 11 findings (5 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] Permission-check path and I/O path canonicalize differently — a non-canonical path skips every PathRule and lands on the catch-all PERM_ALL grant
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1458`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1706 (read), :1852 (write), :2102 (upload), :3912 (list, variant B only), :4200 and :4329 (view); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:723 (fileview), :1043 (filewrite), :1106 (filerename), :1164 (filedelete); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1392 (catch-all rule), :1396 (lookupRule)
- **What:** `normalizeFsPath()` is the canonical form used for the permission decision. It rejects `..`, rejects `"` and control chars, collapses `//`, and strips a trailing `/`. It does NOT force a leading `/` and does NOT collapse `.` segments. `VFS::normalize()` (System_VFS.cpp:321), the canonical form used for the actual I/O, DOES prepend a leading `/`; and littlefs itself skips `.` path components (managed_components/joltwallet__littlefs/src/littlefs/lfs.c:1507-1510), with no canonicalization in between (IDF `translate_path` at esp-idf/components/vfs/vfs.c:798 only strips the mount prefix; Arduino `VFSImpl::open` only requires `fpath[0]=='/'`). `VFS::openGuarded` (System_VFS.cpp:803) calls `canRead/canEdit(norm, ctx)` on the un-slashed / un-dotted string, then `open(norm, ...)` which re-normalizes. `lookupRule()` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1396) is a raw `path.startsWith(rule.path)` over a table whose every entry begins with `/`, and its last row is `{nullptr, PERM_ALL, PERM_ALL, PERM_ALL}` (:1392) — full access for ANY authenticated non-guest.
- **Why it matters:** Two escape forms, both confirmed by reading the code end to end.

Variant A (no leading slash) — `POST /api/files/write` with body `name=system/users/users.json&content=<forged users.json>` as a plain `user`-role account: `tgRequireAuth` passes (System_User.cpp:161 accepts any session, no role check); `webGuestAccessAllowed` only blocks guests; `isAdminOnlyPath("system/users/users.json")` resolves through the same `lookupRule` → catch-all → `userPerms != 0` → returns false, so the 403 never fires (WebServer_Server.cpp:1839); `VFS::openGuarded(name,"w",ctx,true)` (:1852) → `normalizeFsPath` leaves the string untouched → `canEdit` → catch-all → PERM_ALL → allowed; `hasSensitiveExtension` does not block `.json` and the basename `users.json` matches none of password/secret/credential; `VFS::open` prepends `/` → LittleFS truncates and rewrites the real `/system/users/users.json`. Result: a plain user rewrites the account database and makes themselves `superadmin`. `/api/files/read` (:1706) and `/api/files/view` (:4200, :4329) give the same read primitive over the same paths (dump the hashes first), and `/api/files/upload` (:2102) the same write.

Variant B (a `/./` segment) is broader because it survives `VFS::normalize` too: `?path=/./system` on `/api/files/list` (:3912 → buildFilesListing at System_Filesystem.cpp:242, which calls `VFS::normalize` first — variant A is caught there, variant B is not) enumerates `/system` for a plain user. On the CLI, `requireQuotedPath` (:1500) forces a leading `/` but does not touch `.`, so `filewrite "/./system/users/users.json" 0 <b64>` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1043) is an ordinary-admin → superadmin escalation on serial, web CLI, BLE, G2, OLED, MQTT and ESP-NOW alike; `filedelete "/./system/users/users.json" confirm` (:1164) destroys it. `hasSensitiveExtension` still blocks `.key`/`.pem` reads, so TLS material is not exposed by this path, but `settings.json`, `debug.json`, `automations.json`, `espnow/devices.json` and `users.json` all are.

Defense in depth does not save this: the rule table is the ONLY thing standing between a `user` account and `/system` on these endpoints — there is no second admin check on `/api/files/{read,view,write,upload}`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (feature-gated on ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER for the worst vector; the CLI vector applies to every build); prior art: Contradicts docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:75 ("traversal blocked by normalizeFsPath + role rules") — the `..` half is true, the role-rule half is not. The prior security-review backlog notes `pathWi
- **Direction:** Make `normalizeFsPath` produce the same canonical string the I/O layer will use: force a leading `/`, and collapse or reject `.` segments in addition to `..`. Then have `VFS::open/remove/rename/mkdir` take the already-normalized string rather than re-normalizing, so there is exactly one canonical form. Separately, consider whether the trailing catch-all should grant `PERM_ALL` to `user` at all, or whether an unmatched path should default to deny.

### [HIGH] Every user's PBKDF2 password hash is readable by any plain Admin — and the hashes are not in users.json, contradicting the prior hardening audit
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1343`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:770 (setUserPassword writes settings["password"]), :929, :1445, :2758; :1411 comment "Password now stored in per-user settings file, not here"; components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:3080 (getUserSettingsPath), :3093/:3097/:3121 (all load/save use systemAuth); components/hardwareone/WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:411 (backup bundles USER_SETTINGS_DIR under caller ctx)
- **What:** The credential store is /system/users/user_settings/<userId>.json, holding `password` and `gamepad_password` as `PBKDF2:10000:<64 hex>`. users.json holds only id/username/role/provenance — System_User.cpp:1411 says so explicitly. The PathRule at System_Filesystem.cpp:1343 is {"/system/users/user_settings", userPerms=0, adminPerms=PERM_READ, systemPerms=PERM_ALL}, prefix-matched, so it covers every <id>.json. The comment block at :1334 records that this admin grant was added deliberately ("NEW: admin can now read user_settings, which fixes the original bug that triggered this whole refactor"). But every functional reader and writer — loadUserSettings, saveUserSettings, isValidUser, setUserPassword, mergeAndSaveUserSettings — passes VFS::systemAuth (System_Settings.cpp:3093, :3097, :3121, :3127, :3132, :3134), which resolves to FsRole::SYSTEM and takes systemPerms. Grepping the tree, nothing else opens that directory with a caller-supplied AuthContext except the generic file-browser surfaces. So the adminPerms=PERM_READ column has exactly one observable effect: it exposes the raw credential files to file browsing.
- **Why it matters:** A plain Admin (rank 2) opens the web Files page, GET /api/files/read?name=/system/users/user_settings/1.json — isAdminOnlyPath passes them, VFS::openGuarded -> canRead -> permsForRole(ADMIN) = PERM_READ -> allowed, and hasSensitiveExtension does not fire because the filename is a plain `.json` with no "password"/"secret"/"credential" substring. They receive the SuperAdmin's PBKDF2 hash. One click is enough: GET /api/backup?users=1 (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:411) bundles the whole directory. Because the salt is the device-wide getDeviceEncryptionKey() rather than a per-user random value (System_User.cpp:707) and iterations are 10,000 (:708), and because an Admin already has the device in hand to reconstruct that key, this is an offline Admin -> SuperAdmin escalation, not merely a hash disclosure. It also reveals which accounts share a password, since identical passwords produce byte-identical hashes.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards; prior art: Partially — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.4 covers the weak hash construction but states the hashes are "visible directly in users.json", which is wrong; the location and the admin-read grant are
- **Direction:** Set adminPerms back to 0 on the /system/users/user_settings rule, or split the credential fields out of the per-user prefs file so the browsable file carries no secret. If admins genuinely need to view per-user prefs, serve them through a handler that strips `password`/`gamepad_password` the way handleUserSettingsGet already does (WebServer_Server.cpp:2651-2652) rather than by granting raw file read. Separately, correct the file location claim in docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.4.

### [HIGH] exemptSensitiveExt on /system/certs/ is a no-op for its stated beneficiaries and its only real effect is handing the HTTPS private key to any plain Admin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1355`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1629-1630 (sensitiveBlocked expression), :1545 (isUnrestrictedRole), :1422-1426 (kBlockedSuffixes includes .key/.pem/.crt); components/hardwareone/System_WiFi.cpp:1543 (key written), :1619 (certgen requiresSuperAdmin=true); components/hardwareone/WebPage_Settings.h:2231 and :2258 (admin UI lists /system/certs)
- **What:** The rule is {"/system/certs/", userPerms=0, adminPerms=PERM_READ|PERM_DELETE|PERM_IMPORT, systemPerms=PERM_ALL, exactMatch=false, exemptSensitiveExt=true}. The justification comment at :1354 is ".pem/.crt/.key files live here by design". But checkPerm computes `sensitiveBlocked = sensitiveExtensionApplies && !rule.exemptSensitiveExt && hasSensitiveExtension(path) && !isUnrestrictedRole(role)` (:1629-1630). For FsRole::SYSTEM and FsRole::SUPER, isUnrestrictedRole() is already true, so the final conjunct is false and the extension guard never fires for them regardless of the flag. The flag therefore changes behaviour for exactly one role: ADMIN. Without it, an Admin reading https_server.key would be blocked by the .key suffix; with it, canRead returns adminPerms & PERM_READ = allowed.
- **Why it matters:** `certgen` is registered requiresSuperAdmin=true (System_WiFi.cpp:1619) — only a SuperAdmin may create the TLS keypair. But any Admin can then download the private key: the Settings page already lists /system/certs for them (WebPage_Settings.h:2231), and GET /api/files/read?name=/system/certs/https_server.key passes isAdminOnlyPath and then VFS::openGuarded -> canRead -> allowed. GET /api/backup?certs=1 exports it too. With flash encryption off and the server falling back to plain HTTP when certs are absent, the private key is the only thing separating an Admin from passively decrypting every other user's HTTPS session, including the SuperAdmin's login POST.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards (key is generated only when certgen has been run; the rule is unconditional)
- **Direction:** Split the rule: keep exemptSensitiveExt for the readable half (.crt/.pem chain files an admin legitimately inspects or replaces) and add a more specific preceding rule for the private key giving adminPerms = PERM_DELETE|PERM_IMPORT with no PERM_READ. Since SYSTEM and SUPER bypass the extension guard structurally, dropping exemptSensitiveExt entirely would still leave HTTPS startup (WebServer_Server.cpp:5184-5185, systemAuth) and certgen working.

### [HIGH] `appendLineWithCap()` rotation copies the tail with an unchecked `w.write()`, then `remove()`s the original before renaming — a nearly-full FS silently destroys command-audit.log and errors.log
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1889`
- **Also:** System_Debug.cpp:306, :320, :330 and System_Utils.cpp:939 all discard `appendLineWithCap`'s bool as well
- **What:** The shared log engine (System_Filesystem.cpp:1812-1911) does: append (`a.println(line)` at :1828, return discarded) → if size > cap, stream the last 85% into `<dest>.tmp` with `w.write(sRotateBuf, n)` at :1889, return discarded → `VFS::remove(dest)` at :1899 → `VFS::rename(tmp, dest)` at :1905. It also calls `VFS::resolveOverflowPath(path, line.length() + 512, ...)` at :1820, reserving ~512 bytes.
- **Why it matters:** Two compounding defects. (a) The rotation transiently needs `cap * 0.85` free bytes *while the original still exists* — 425 KB for `command-audit.log` (500 KB cap, System_Utils.cpp:939) and 217 KB for `errors.log`. The overflow latch only fires below `max(100 KB, reserveBytes)` = 100 KB (System_VFS.cpp:533, :598-601), so at 150 KB free the latch stays off, `w.write()` short-writes, the error is discarded, and `VFS::remove(dest)` then destroys the full 500 KB original before the truncated tmp is promoted. If the first block allocation fails you get a 0-byte audit log. The mechanism to prevent this already exists and is documented at System_VFS.cpp:594-597 ("callers can request a LARGER margin — e.g. the sensor log passes its rotation-chunk size so overflow triggers before a full rotation fails"); `System_SensorLogging.cpp:891` does exactly that and `appendLineWithCap` does not. (b) The append at :1828 is unchecked, so `appendLineWithCap` returns `true` for a line that never landed. Consequence is loss of the always-on security record: `command-audit.log`, `failed_login.log`, `successful_login.log`, `errors.log`, `system-events.log`. Worse for `command-audit.log`, which is the one log absent from the `cleanupLogOrphan` list (System_Filesystem.cpp:94-99) and whose directory `/system/sys_logs` is absent from the boot `.tmp` sweep (:139-144) — so its orphan is never recovered either.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: The missing boot-orphan recovery for command-audit.log is already in the run's persistence recon; the unchecked rotation write and the un-passed rotation reserve are new.
- **Direction:** Pass the rotation transient (`capBytes * 85 / 100`) as `reserveBytes` to `resolveOverflowPath` so the latch fires before the copy can fail, check `w.write()`'s count against `n` and abort the rotation (leaving the original intact) on mismatch, and gate the `remove(dest)` on `getStorageType() == SDCARD` — `lfs_rename` replaces an existing destination atomically, only FAT needs the remove.

### [HIGH] Files pulled from an ESP-NOW peer land in /espnow/received/, a path the rule table grants PERM_READ to every authenticated non-admin — so a peer's users.json password-hash database is downloadable by a plain user over /api/files/read
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1388`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:4406 (RAM-path destination), components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:599 (streaming-path destination), components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:1690-1710 (handleFileRead gate), components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:843 (the SYSTEM-identity source side, already documented in docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.1)
- **What:** The PathRule row {"/espnow/", PERM_READ|PERM_WRITE|PERM_DELETE, same, PERM_ALL, prefix, exemptSensitiveExt=false} is the first rule that matches /espnow/received/<peerToken>/<file>. permsForRole() therefore returns PERM_READ|PERM_WRITE|PERM_DELETE for FsRole::USER. Inbound ESP-NOW file transfers write to exactly that tree (System_ESPNow.cpp:4406 builds "/espnow/received/%s" + "/%s" from the peer token and the sender-supplied filename), whatever the file was. hasSensitiveExtension() does not fire on "users.json"/"identity.json"/"settings.json" (only .key/.pem/.crt/.cert/.cer/.credentials/.bin/.hex/.elf and basenames containing password/secret/credential). handleFileRead (WebServer_Server.cpp:1635) skips its 403 because isAdminOnlyPath() is false for this rule (userPerms != 0), then VFS::openGuarded -> canRead -> checkPerm returns true.
- **Why it matters:** Device A is paired+sessioned with device B. Any of the existing pull paths puts B's credential store on A's flash under /espnow/received/: `espnowfetch <B> <u> <p> "/system/users/users.json"` (admin here, B-side credentials), or an FS_GET, which B serves under SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE with no credential at all (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:843-900, the known FTS-AuthBypass). Once the file has landed, a plain non-admin, non-guest web account on A issues GET /api/files/read?name=/espnow/received/AABBCCDDEEFF/users.json and receives every account name, role and PBKDF2 hash from B. The same account also holds PERM_WRITE and PERM_DELETE there, so it can tamper with or destroy a fetched automations.json/settings.json before an admin reviews it. The identical file at /system/users/users.json is correctly 403'd for that same account — the confidentiality class of the data is lost purely by crossing the radio.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW + ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER builds)
- **Direction:** The inbox should not inherit user-tier read. Either give /espnow/received/ its own rule with userPerms=0 / adminPerms=READ|DELETE (matching where the data came from), or quarantine inbound files under a per-requester subtree and drop the blanket user grant on /espnow/. Fixing the source side alone (§4.1) does not close this, because espnowfetch is a legitimate admin action.

### [MEDIUM] Boot log-orphan recovery inspects the SD mirror instead of LittleFS whenever free space is already below the reserve, so the orphan is never recovered and the log stays missing
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:94`
- **Also:** System_Filesystem.cpp:1923-1950 cleanupLogOrphan; System_VFS.cpp:588-604 resolveOverflowPath
- **What:** `cleanupLogOrphan` resolves the path through `VFS::resolveOverflowPath(path, 0, dest, sizeof(dest))` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1927-1930). resolveOverflowPath ignores the caller's 0 and applies the LOG_OVERFLOW_DEFAULT_RESERVE floor of 100 KB (System_VFS.cpp:598-600), so if LittleFS free is under 100 KB the one-way latch fires here — during the very first orphan check at boot, immediately after VFS::init() has mounted SD. Every subsequent cleanupLogOrphan call then examines `/sd/system/sys_logs/<log>` and `/sd/....tmp`.
- **Why it matters:** A device that crashed mid-rotation while its filesystem was nearly full — precisely the condition that makes rotation writes fail — boots, latches overflow, checks the SD tier, finds no orphan there, and returns. The LittleFS `<log>.tmp` holding 85% of the log is never promoted (case c is the only data-preserving recovery) and is never swept either, since /system/sys_logs is not in the .tmp sweep list. The log data is lost permanently and the orphan permanently consumes space on the partition that was already short. On the sr_8mb layout (128 KB partition, 100 KB reserve) this is not an edge case — the latch fires on every boot.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all; guaranteed on 8 MB + ESP-SR (partitions_sr_8mb.csv)
