- **Direction:** Have cleanupLogOrphan check the primary LittleFS path unconditionally (and optionally the SD mirror as well) rather than going through resolveOverflowPath, and give resolveOverflowPath a read-only query mode that cannot set the latch.

### [MEDIUM] ramflush captures ESP-NOW divergence against espnowEnabled but replays it against espnowAutoStart, so a manually-started ESP-NOW is never resumed
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_RamFlush.cpp:254`
- **Also:** Checked all 22 features: every other apply site passes the same field its ramFlushReadIntent case returns (I2C sensors System_I2C.cpp:3075-3163, OLED_Utils.cpp:4600, HardwareOne.cpp:1487/1700/1821/1848/1878/1892/1895/1900/1911/1928/1939/2121/2122). RF_ESPNOW is the only mismatch.
- **What:** ramFlushReadIntent(RF_ESPNOW) returns gSettings.espnowEnabled (:254, with the now-stale comment at :231-233 'espnow is a master-enable'). The apply site passes a different field: HardwareOne.cpp:2079 `gSettings.espnowEnabled && ramFlushResolve(RF_ESPNOW, gSettings.espnowAutoStart)`, under a comment (:2076-2077) that records the split: 'espnowEnabled used to serve as BOTH the master switch and the boot flag. It is now the master switch only; espnowAutoStart is the boot flag.' docs/RAMFLUSH_IMPLEMENTATION_MAP.md:440 prescribed `ramFlushResolve(RF_ESPNOW, gSettings.espnowenabled)` — matching the intent function — so the apply site was updated for the split and ramFlushReadIntent was not.
- **Why it matters:** Config espnowEnabled=1, espnowAutoStart=0 (master switch on, don't start at boot), user runs `openespnow` so ESP-NOW is live. ramFlushCaptureOverlay (:298-312) reads live=true and intent=espnowEnabled=true, sees isLive == intent, and records NO divergence. On the resumed boot, ramFlushResolve(RF_ESPNOW, espnowAutoStart=false) finds no overlay bit and returns the passed intent, false - ESP-NOW does not come back up, which is exactly the state `ramflush` exists to preserve. `ramflush status` also reports nothing, because the bit was never set. The comment at :232-233 shows the author knew espnow was keyed differently but the resolve site was not reconciled.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: docs/RAMFLUSH_IMPLEMENTATION_MAP.md §3 row 3.12 documents the pre-split wiring; the drift is new.
- **Direction:** Make the intent function and the resolve site read the same field. Given the master-switch/boot-flag split documented at HardwareOne.cpp:2120, espnowAutoStart is the one that matches every other feature's semantics.

### [LOW] OLED and G2 file browsers list admin-only subtrees that the web UI and `files json` hide — the directory-listing filter was duplicated without the hideAdminPaths rule
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_FileManager.cpp:476`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_FileManager.cpp:214-223 (getItem copy); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:701-734 (a fourth copy, which additionally skips dotfiles that buildFilesListing shows — its comment at :677-679 says it mirrors "System_Filesystem.cpp:230", a line reference now off by 13 since buildFilesListing sits at :243)
- **What:** `buildFilesListing` (System_Filesystem.cpp:243) skips entries where `isAdminOnlyPath(entryFullPath)` when `hideAdminPaths` is set (:337-344); the web `/api/files/list` handler passes `hideAdminPaths = !userIsAdmin` (WebServer_Server.cpp:3922) and the `files json` CLI/BLE wrapper does the same plus an `isAdminOnlyPath` pre-check (System_Filesystem.cpp:551-556). `FileManager::loadDirectory` (System_FileManager.cpp:420) — which backs the OLED file browser and the G2 Files page — re-implements the same entry loop (prefix strip, nested-path skip, hidden-file skip at :476-502) with no `isAdminOnlyPath` filter at all; grep for isAdminOnlyPath/hideAdmin across System_FileManager.cpp, OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp and G2_Page_Files.cpp returns nothing. `FileManager::getItem`'s >64-entry fallback (:200-249) is a third copy with the same omission.
- **Why it matters:** A non-admin USER or a guest opening the file browser on the OLED (OLED_FILE_BROWSER is on the guest allowlist, OLED_Utils.cpp:2950) or on the G2 lens sees `system` and `logging_captures` listed at the root, whereas the same account on the web UI does not. Descending fails — `VFS::openGuarded("/system", …)` denies because the rule row grants userPerms=0 (System_Filesystem.cpp:1370) — so this is directory-name disclosure and an inconsistent view of the device, not an access bypass. It is filed because the three listing loops are the same operation and the copies have already drifted on one filter, which is how the next drift gets in.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Partially — docs/PRE_1_0_CODE_HEALTH_AUDIT.md §3a flags the directory-entry loop as duplicated across buildFilesListing/loadDirectory/getItem/FsList, but records it as a maintenance concern and does n
- **Direction:** Centralize the per-entry name-normalize + filter decision (nested-path, hidden, admin-only) into one VFS-level helper that every browser/listing path calls, with the admin-only rule as a parameter rather than a per-copy choice.

### [LOW] The sensitive-extension guard is applied to read and write only — canRename/canDelete deliberately skip it, so a .key/.pem can be renamed out from under the guard wherever a rule grants a non-admin PERM_RENAME
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1657`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1732 (getPermissions mirrors the same mask); components/hardwareone/System_FileManager.cpp:315 (reachable renamer)
- **What:** canRead and canEdit call checkPerm with sensitiveExtensionApplies=true; canDelete (:1654), canRename (:1657), canCreate (:1660) and canImport (:1663) pass false, and getPermissions (:1732) mirrors this by masking only PERM_READ|PERM_WRITE. The comment at :1722-1726 states the intent explicitly ('you can still delete a .key file you can't read'). But rename is not delete: renaming /x.key to /x.txt changes what hasSensitiveExtension() matches, and the next canRead on the new name succeeds. The default catch-all rule (:1391) grants FsRole::USER PERM_ALL — including PERM_RENAME — for every path not matched by an earlier row, which is the LittleFS root, /maps/*, /logging_captures is excluded but e.g. /sd/<anything-not-g2_icon_animations> falls through to it.
- **Why it matters:** Concretely: a file /backup.key sitting at the LittleFS root is unreadable to a plain user (extension guard), but VFS::renameGuarded("/backup.key", "/backup.txt", userCtx) succeeds — canRename sees PERM_RENAME from the catch-all and skips the extension check, canCreate on the destination likewise — and the follow-up read is then permitted. Today this is latent rather than exploitable: all six file CLI commands are requiresAdmin=true (System_Filesystem.cpp:1264-1282), there is no web rename endpoint, and the only key material shipped by the firmware lives under /system/certs/ where userPerms=0. The reachable renamer is FileManager::renameItem (System_FileManager.cpp:315) from the OLED/G2 browsers, where a USER-tier identity ('AuthBypass' with displayrequireauth off) can drive it. So the exposure depends entirely on nobody ever dropping key material into a catch-all path — the guard that is supposed to make that safe does not.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Apply the sensitive-extension check to PERM_RENAME as well (renaming changes the classification, unlike delete). Leave delete alone — that reasoning is sound.

### [LOW] readTextLimited() keeps a 512-byte PSRAM scratch buffer alive for the whole boot and never clears it, so the tail of the last file viewed persists in externally-probeable memory
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1771`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1755 (fs.fileview.body); components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Files.cpp:627
- **What:** readTextLimited uses `static char* buf = nullptr; if (!buf) buf = (char*)ps_alloc(chunk, AllocPref::PreferPSRAM, "file.read");` — a function-static, allocated once with MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM, never freed and never zeroed between calls. Its two callers are cmd_fileview (System_Filesystem.cpp:736) and the G2 Files text viewer (G2_Page_Files.cpp:627). The function's own comment notes it is a 'policy-free byte shovel' that deliberately uses unguarded VFS::open, relying on the caller's own permission check.
- **Why it matters:** When an admin runs `fileview "/system/users/users.json"` — or views it on the glasses, where the lens identity resolves to the device owner and therefore FsRole::SUPER — the final 512-byte chunk of the password-hash database is left sitting in a permanently-mapped PSRAM block for the rest of the boot. Same for /system/certs/https_server.key, /system/espnow/identity.json, or any AES blob in settings.json. Flash encryption is off and PSRAM is external, so this is exactly the residue class the project's no-secrets-in-PSRAM rule exists to prevent, and unlike a transient allocation it is not even reclaimed by the allocator. cmd_fileview compounds it by copying the whole (up to 60 KB) content into a second PSRAM buffer at :1755 ('fs.fileview.body').
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with PSRAM
- **Direction:** memset the scratch buffer at function exit, and either move it to internal DRAM or reuse a caller-provided stack chunk. The fileview body buffer should be wiped before its ps_free/reuse too.

### [LOW] getPermissions() omits the ctx.scope confinement that checkPerm() applies, so the two permission oracles disagree for a scoped context
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1707`
- **Also:** checkPerm System_Filesystem.cpp:1624 (`if (!pathWithinScope(path, ctx.scope)) return false;`); getDirPerms :1738 inherits the gap; consumers at :301, :369, :388, System_FileManager.cpp:236/466/514, System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:696/730
- **What:** checkPerm — the single decision point behind canRead/canEdit/canDelete/canRename/canCreate/canImport — applies four filters: ANON reject, capability-scope confinement, sensitive-extension, image-edit. getPermissions is the batched fast path (its own comment at :1707-1711 says it exists so listings don't call canX six times) and reproduces three of the four: it drops the pathWithinScope check entirely. Its comment claims "These special-case denials ... mirror the sensitiveExtensionApplies / imageEditApplies flags that canRead/canEdit pass to checkPerm", which is true as far as it goes and silently omits scope.
- **Why it matters:** For any AuthContext built with the scoped overload VFS::systemAuth(scope, reason) (System_VFS.cpp:915; used with Scopes::ESPNOW_RECEIVED at System_ESPNow.cpp:4185/4428/4435 and Scopes::CERTS at System_MQTT.cpp:1449), a listing produced under that identity reports permission bits for paths outside the scope that every actual canX-guarded operation would refuse. Today no scoped context reaches a listing path, so the impact is a latent inconsistency rather than a live bypass — but it is a permission oracle that over-reports, and getPermissions' output is what populates e.perms on the ESP-NOW FS_LIST wire and the web/OLED button state. If scoped identities are extended to the browse paths (which is the natural fix for the FS_LIST finding above), this becomes a live UI/enforcement mismatch on day one.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Add `if (!pathWithinScope(path, ctx.scope)) return 0;` right after the ANON check in getPermissions, so the fast path and checkPerm apply the same four filters. Consider deriving one from the other to stop them drifting a third time.


### Sensors / I2C / HAL — 4 findings (2 critical/high)

### [CRITICAL] Non-admin `sensorlog start <path>` performs arbitrary-path create/append/rename/delete as FsRole::SYSTEM — a plain User can destroy /system/users/users.json and force the device into first-time setup (new owner = superadmin)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:1109`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:894 (append as systemAuth), :946-951 (rename/remove as systemAuth), :1119-1130 (recursive mkdir as systemAuth), :1148-1149 (create as systemAuth), :2074 (registration requiresAdmin=false); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1551,1593 (SYSTEM role = systemPerms), :1378 (/system/ rule: user=0, admin=PERM_READ, system=PERM_ALL); components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:86-87, :262, :893
- **What:** `sensorlog` is registered `requiresAdmin=false` (:2074), so any authenticated non-guest account reaches it through executeCommand/authorizeCommand. Its `start` subcommand validates the destination path with exactly one check — `filepath.charAt(0) != '/'` (:1109) — and then performs every filesystem operation under `VFS::systemAuth(...)`: recursive parent mkdir (:1119-1130), create (:1148-1149), per-tick append (:894), and on size rollover either `renameGuarded(activePath, activePath+".1")` or `removeGuarded(activePath)` (:946-951). `VFS::systemAuth()` (System_VFS.cpp:903) sets transport=SOURCE_INTERNAL and user="system", which `resolveRole()` maps to `FsRole::SYSTEM` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551), and `permsForRole` then returns `rule.systemPerms` = PERM_ALL for the `/system/` rule (System_Filesystem.cpp:1378, :1593). The path-rule table that denies a plain user (userPerms=0) and even an admin (adminPerms=PERM_READ) any write under /system is therefore never consulted with the caller's identity. For the default TEXT format `resolveSessionTarget` returns the path unchanged (:236) and `sensorLogClassifyActivePath` marks it MANUAL (:189-193), so the literal path is used verbatim. `gSettings.sensorLogEnabled` — the only precondition — defaults to true (System_Settings.h:298). `sensorlog maxsize` (min 10240, :1275) and `sensorlog rotations` (0-9, :1297) are subcommands of the same non-admin command.
- **Why it matters:** A holder of a plain `user` account (rank 1, non-guest, reachable over serial, /api/cli, /api/cli/batch, BLE, MQTT, ESP-NOW or the OLED) runs: `sensorlog rotations 0` / `sensorlog maxsize 10240` / `sensorlog start /system/users/users.json 100`. sensorLogTick() (HardwareOne.cpp:2333) then appends heartbeat/sensor rows to the live auth database every interval — immediately breaking every `deserializeJson` consumer of users.json — and once gApproxSizeBytes crosses 10 KB the rotation branch REMOVES (rotations=0) or RENAMES (rotations>0) users.json out of its canonical path, as SYSTEM. With users.json absent, `isAdminUser`/`isSuperAdminUser`/`isValidUser` all fail closed and nobody can authenticate; on the next boot `detectFirstTimeSetupState()` sees `existsGuarded(USERS_JSON_FILE)==false` (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:86-87), sets SETUP_REQUIRED, and the first-time wizard writes the next account it is given with `admin["role"] = "superadmin"` (:262 / :893). Whoever is at the serial console or OLED at that reboot becomes device owner at the top tier. The same primitive also lets a plain user create directories and files anywhere under /system, /system/certs, /logging_captures and /sd with SYSTEM authority.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Either gate `sensorlog start`'s destination (confine it to the capture tree the way System_Camera_Video.cpp does with `VFS::systemAuth(VFS::Scopes::VIDEOS, ...)`), or adopt the pattern `autolog` already uses: capture `currentAuthContext()` at start and pass that ctx — not systemAuth — to every open/mkdir/rename/remove for the session. The scoped `systemAuth(scope, reason)` overload already exists and `checkPerm`'s `pathWithinScope` already enforces it.

### [HIGH] `sensorlog autostart on` persists a low-privilege user's arbitrary path and re-executes it at boot via a direct `cmd_sensorlog()` call that bypasses executeCommand and authorizeCommand entirely
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:2186`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:1215 (setSetting persists the attacker's path), :1419-1431 (autostart subcommand on the requiresAdmin=false `sensorlog` entry), :2170-2176 (path read back + shapeSessionPath); components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2121 (boot call site)
- **What:** This is the definitive answer to "can a low-privilege user create an autostart entry that later executes with higher privilege". `cmd_sensorlog start` writes the caller-supplied path into persistent settings via `setSetting(gSettings.sensorLogPath, stripSessionShaping(filepath))` (:1215), and the `autostart` subcommand (:1419-1431) of the same `requiresAdmin=false` command sets `gSettings.sensorLogAutoStart`. At boot, HardwareOne.cpp:2121 calls `sensorLogAutoStart()`, which reads the persisted path back (:2170), reshapes it with `shapeSessionPath` (:2176), and invokes `cmd_sensorlog(...)` as a raw C++ call (:2186) — not through `runUnifiedSystemCommand`, not through `submitAndExecuteSync`, not through `executeCommand`. No AuthContext is constructed and `authorizeCommand` never runs. All filesystem work inside then executes under `VFS::systemAuth()` as documented in the previous finding.
- **Why it matters:** A plain `user` runs `sensorlog start /system/certs/x.txt` then `sensorlog autostart on`, and from that boot forward the device — with no session, no credential and no auth gate — recreates the attacker's chosen directory tree and writes files there with SYSTEM filesystem authority on every single power-up. `shapeSessionPath` rewrites the basename into `<dir>/<YYYY-MM-DD|boot-N>/<base>-<stamp><ext>`, so the exact users.json clobber is not reachable through the autostart path specifically, but any parent directory the attacker names is created and written under SYSTEM privilege at boot, indefinitely, and the persisted setting survives the attacker's account being deleted.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Re-validate the persisted `sensorLogPath` against the capture-tree allowlist at autostart time rather than trusting it because it is in settings.json, and consider routing the boot resume through the normal command pipeline so the same policy applies to boot and to runtime.

### [MEDIUM] Sensor-log start gate checks one file's worth of free space while the steady-state footprint is (rotations + 1) files
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:1178`
- **What:** The sensorlog start admission check requires only freeBytes >= gSensorLogMaxSize on LittleFS (or the same on SD). The writer, however, keeps gSensorLogMaxRotations numbered copies plus the live file: on rollover it deletes .N, shifts .i -> .i+1 and moves the base to .1 (:920-948). Defaults are gSensorLogMaxSize = 250 KB and gSensorLogMaxRotations = 3 (:75, :77), i.e. ~1 MB steady state against a 250 KB admission test; the registry (:2123-2124) permits maxsize up to 10 MB and rotations up to 9, i.e. 100 MB steady state against a 10 MB test.
- **Why it matters:** sensorlog start succeeds with 300 KB free, then grows to ~1 MB of rotations and drives LittleFS to the floor. Once free drops below 100 KB the overflow latch fires, and with no SD mounted resolveOverflowPath falls back to the primary path and accepts silent loss (System_VFS.cpp:628-631). The per-row write's return value is not checked (:900-902) and gApproxSizeBytes is incremented regardless, so rows vanish with no error while the filesystem sits at ~0 free — which is also the state that makes every state-file write silently destructive.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all; acute on no_sr_8mb (2796 KB partition, where the default 1 MB footprint is already 36%)
- **Direction:** Scale the admission check by (gSensorLogMaxRotations + 1) * gSensorLogMaxSize, and check f.write's return value so a failing row surfaces instead of being counted as written.

### [MEDIUM] Sensor-log rotation builds rotation filenames with unchecked snprintf into a 128-byte path buffer — a long log path makes the rotation delete or rename the wrong file
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_SensorLogging.cpp:928`
- **Also:** System_VFS.cpp:621/:629/:635/:640/:642 — resolveOverflowPath discards the snprintf return on all five branches, so every caller inherits silent path truncation.
- **What:** `char activePath[128]` at :890 receives the shaped log path via VFS::resolveOverflowPath (System_VFS.cpp:621, itself an unchecked `snprintf(outPath, outPathLen, "%s", primaryPath)`), even though shapeSessionPath produces up to 255 chars (System_SensorLogging.cpp:1817 `char pathBuf[256]`). Rotation then builds `oldestFile[128]`/`fromFile[128]`/`toFile[128]` with `snprintf(..., "%s.%d", activePath, N)` at :928/:939/:940 and ignores every return value.
- **Why it matters:** Two distinct failures. (1) If the shaped path exceeds 127 chars, `activePath` is a silent truncation, so `VFS::openGuarded(String(activePath), "a", ...)` at :894 appends to a *different* file than the one `sensorlog start` created and reported to the user. There is no length check on the `sensorlog start <filepath>` argument (:1109-1111 only checks it is non-empty and starts with '/'), and shapeSessionPath adds ~30 chars of date subfolder + stamp on top of it. (2) If strlen(activePath) == 127, `snprintf(oldestFile, 128, "%s.%d", activePath, N)` truncates back to exactly activePath, and the very next line `VFS::removeGuarded(String(oldestFile))` deletes the live log. At strlen 126 both `fromFile` and `toFile` truncate to the same string and the rotation renames files onto themselves, silently losing the shift.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards
- **Direction:** Check the snprintf returns (>= sizeof(buf) means truncation) and abort the rotation instead of operating on a truncated name; and either size activePath to match shapeSessionPath's 256-byte output or reject an over-long path at `sensorlog start` with a clear error.


### Auth / users — 16 findings (6 critical/high)

### [HIGH] No brute-force lockout on any login path except web and the serial pre-auth gate — cmd_login, BLE, OLED, ESP-NOW and MQTT are unthrottled
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:448`
- **Also:** System_ESPNow.cpp:5841 (per-frame isValidUser, no counter); System_MQTT.cpp:472 (per-message isValidUser, no counter); OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:184 (loginTransport, no counter); Bluetooth.cpp:716-772 (BLE login synthesizes a `login` command, no counter); System_Utils.cpp:5230 (cmd_login -> loginTransport)
- **What:** `recordFailedLogin()` (WebServer_Server.cpp:841) is the only thing that increments the tiered lockout counter. Its complete call-site set is WebServer_Server.cpp:569/3540/3778/3782/3791/3810/3814/3818/3822/3840 plus HardwareOne.cpp:2547 — i.e. web handlers and the serial pre-auth gate only. `loginTransport` (System_User.cpp:439-459) calls `recordLoginAttempt()` on failure, which is an audit-ring write, NOT the lockout counter; it never calls recordFailedLogin. Meanwhile authorizeCommand explicitly lets the literal command `login` through from an empty-identity context on every non-INTERNAL transport (System_Utils.cpp:4312-4322).
- **Why it matters:** An attacker who can reach any of these surfaces can try credentials without limit. Concretely: a paired ESP-NOW peer resends CMD frames with `user:pass:cmd` and gets an unlimited oracle at System_ESPNow.cpp:5841; an MQTT client republishes to <base>/command with a new `pass` each time; a BLE central reconnects and issues `login u p` repeatedly. Nothing counts, nothing locks, and nothing except the ~100 ms PBKDF2 cost slows it down. This is the same lockout the web path takes seriously enough to expose "N attempts remaining" messaging for.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards; the surfaces present depend on ENABLE_BLUETOOTH / ENABLE_ESPNOW / ENABLE_MQTT / ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY
- **Direction:** Move the failure accounting into `loginTransport`/`isValidUser`'s callers rather than the web layer — a per-transport key ("ble", "espnow:<mac>", "mqtt:<user>") through the existing findLoginEntry/recordFailedLogin machinery would cover all of them with the tiers already defined in WebServer_Server.h:127-132. Note the lockout state also sits behind `#if ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER` (System_User.cpp:449-459, HardwareOne.cpp:2513-2549), so a headless build loses serial lockout too.

### [HIGH] User-enumeration timing oracle: isValidUser short-circuits before PBKDF2 when the username does not exist
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1146`
- **Also:** callers: components/hardwareone/WebServer_Server.cpp:3534, components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:5230 (via loginTransport), components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:472, components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:5841
- **What:** isValidUser (:1137) returns false immediately at :1146 when getUserIdByUsername fails, and at :1150 when the per-user settings file will not load — neither path performs any key derivation. When the account exists it calls verifyUserPassword (:1154), which runs hashUserPassword — mbedtls_pkcs5_pbkdf2_hmac_ext with 10,000 iterations of HMAC-SHA256 (:711-718) — and, if a gamepad pattern is also set, a SECOND full derivation at :1160. Every credential surface funnels through this one function (web :3534, CLI cmd_login :5230, MQTT :472, ESP-NOW :5841, BLE via cmd_exec).
- **Why it matters:** All the error strings are uniform ("Invalid username or password" web :3557, "Error: Authentication failed" System_Utils.cpp:5239, "Authentication failed" MQTT :478, "Auth failed" ESP-NOW :5848), so message-based enumeration is correctly blocked — but the response time is not equalised. On an ESP32-S3 a 10k-iteration PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 is on the order of tens of milliseconds, comfortably above LAN jitter, so an attacker can distinguish 'user exists, wrong password' from 'no such user' with a single request per candidate name. The per-IP lockout (isLoginLocked, checked before isValidUser at :3518) rate-limits but does not remove the signal, and the MQTT and ESP-NOW surfaces have no lockout at all. I could not run the firmware to measure the actual delta, so the exploitability tier is estimated, not measured.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards; observable on every credential surface (web, serial, BLE, ESP-NOW, MQTT, OLED)
- **Direction:** Make the non-existent-user path pay the same cost: on lookup failure, run hashUserPassword against a fixed dummy hash and discard the result before returning false. Fold the ban check into the same shape. Also consider collapsing the two verify calls into one constant number of PBKDF2 runs so wrong-password timing does not depend on whether a gamepad pattern is set.

### [HIGH] The OLED 4-direction unlock pattern is a full-strength credential on every remote transport, with a keyspace as small as 256
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1160`
- **Also:** Alphabet: OLED_Utils.cpp:1531-1534 (`PATTERN_UP '^'`, `PATTERN_DOWN 'v'`, `PATTERN_LEFT '<'`, `PATTERN_RIGHT '>'`) and the input handler at :1827-1851. Minimum length: OLED_Mode_SetPattern.cpp:202 (`if (len < 4)`). Setter: System_User.cpp:947 setUserGamepadPassword.
- **What:** `isValidUser()` checks the text password at :1155 and, if that fails, checks `settings["gamepad_password"]` at :1159 with the same `verifyUserPassword`. There is no transport condition on that second check. `isValidUser` is the single credential primitive for POST /login (WebServer_Server.cpp:3534), HTTP Basic Auth (:564), the serial gate (HardwareOne.cpp:2528), `loginTransport` for serial/display/bluetooth (System_User.cpp:451), MQTT (System_MQTT.cpp:472), ESP-NOW remote commands (System_ESPNow.cpp:5838) and ESP-NOW USER_SYNC (:3596). The pattern itself is a string over exactly four characters with a minimum length of 4.
- **Why it matters:** A user who sets a gamepad pattern for convenient on-device unlock silently installs a second credential for their account that is valid over WiFi, BLE, MQTT and ESP-NOW. A minimum-length pattern has 4^4 = 256 candidates; a typical 6-symbol pattern has 4096. Combined with the lockout-eviction bypass above (web) or the complete absence of any throttle on the BLE login path (Bluetooth.cpp:716 -> System_User.cpp:439, no isLoginLocked/recordFailedLogin anywhere on that route), the whole space is exhaustible in minutes to hours even at the ~100 ms/guess cost of PBKDF2-10k. If the account is the device owner, `isSuperAdminUser`'s first-user fallback (System_User.cpp:418) makes that Super Admin.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all boards that compile ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY (pattern is set there) — but exploitable from any board's ESP-NOW/MQTT/BLE/web surface once set; prior art: docs/CLI_HELPTEXT_AUDIT.md notes `setgamepadpassword` has an undocumented OLED-login precondition; the keyspace/cross-transport reuse issue is not recorded anywhere.
- **Direction:** Either scope the gamepad hash to SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY (add a transport parameter to isValidUser, or a separate verifyLocalDisplayCredential path used only by the OLED), or raise the minimum move count substantially and rate-limit local-display attempts. Scoping is the better fix — a gesture is a physical-presence factor and should not be a network credential.

### [HIGH] `logout g2` is guest-allowed and non-admin, and immediately re-homes the lens identity to the device owner — a guest can escalate the entire G2 surface to super-admin
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:515`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4289 (commandAllowedForGuest first-token match), :5242 (cmd_logout); components/hardwareone/BLE_Peers.cpp:145,156,171; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:3384
- **What:** cmd_logout (System_Utils.cpp:5242) accepts `serial|display|bluetooth|g2` and is registered requiresAdmin=false (System_User.cpp:3384). commandAllowedForGuest (System_Utils.cpp:4289) splits on the first space and allows the whole line if the first token is `login` or `logout` — so `logout g2` passes the guest gate. logoutTransport's SOURCE_G2_GLASSES branch (System_User.cpp:515-521) calls g2PairedUserClear() and then IMMEDIATELY bleStampPairedByIfBlank(BLE_PEER_G2_GLASSES). That resolver (BLE_Peers.cpp:145) tries currentAuthContext().user first — but usable() rejects guests (:156) — then live OLED/serial sessions, then getDeviceOwnerUsername() (:171), which is the superadmin owner.
- **Why it matters:** Two distinct escalations from one non-admin command. (a) Guest path: the lens is currently owned by `bob` (plain user). A guest — on serial, BLE, OLED, MQTT, ESP-NOW, or any transport that reaches executeCommand — runs `logout g2`. usable(guest) is false, so the resolver falls all the way through to the device owner and the lens is re-stamped as the SUPER-ADMIN. Every subsequent lens tap now runs with super privilege and FsRole::SUPER. A view-only account has silently upgraded a whole surface. (b) Plain-user path: `mallory` (User tier) runs `logout g2`; her TLS identity IS usable, so the lens is re-homed to her — she steals the lens surface from whoever owned it. Neither path requires admin, and the command is one of exactly two a guest is permitted to run.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES)
- **Direction:** commandAllowedForGuest should allow bare `logout` only, not `logout <transport>` — a guest has no business terminating another transport's session. Separately, logoutTransport(SOURCE_G2_GLASSES) should leave the lens UNOWNED rather than immediately re-homing to the founder; the recovery path (`bleautoreconnect g2-glasses on` from an authenticated CLI) already exists and is the intended re-stamp gesture. Note the same first-token match means a guest can also run `logout display` / `logout bluetooth`, which kicks the physical OLED session and calls bleRevokeAllSessions (:521).

### [HIGH] resolvePendingUserCreationTimes() reads users.json into a fixed 8 KB buffer with no truncation check, then writes the truncated copy back over the live auth database
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:3216`
- **Also:** Call sites: HardwareOne.cpp:1490 (boot), System_Debug.cpp:2347 (first NTP sync), System_User.cpp:1465 (approve), System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:295/931, System_Utils.cpp:2467/2494
- **What:** The routine allocates a static 8192-byte PSRAM buffer (:3206), does `bytesRead = f.readBytes(usersJsonBuf, USERS_JSON_BUF_SIZE - 1)` (:3216), NUL-terminates, and never compares `bytesRead` against `f.size()`. It then string-splices resolved `createdAt` timestamps into that possibly-truncated copy and, when `modified` is true, calls `writeTextAtomic(USERS_JSON_FILE, usersJson)` (:3317). Unlike setUserRoleInternal (:1594-1599) and deleteUserInternal, it does NOT bail on a missing `]` — `usersArrayStart`/`arrayBracket` are both near the start of the file, and the per-user walk simply ends early when the object braces run out. `modified` becoming true is the routine's *normal* outcome after the first NTP sync, not an error path.
- **Why it matters:** users.json grows past 8191 bytes (no user cap exists anywhere in System_User.cpp; each record is ~200-260 B with id/username/role/createdAt/createdBy/createdAtSource/createdMs/ntpAnchorId/bootCount plus later banned/banReason/lastSeen fields, so roughly 32-40 accounts). NTP then syncs; logTimeSyncedMarkerIfReady (System_Debug.cpp:2347) calls this routine; it resolves one pending timestamp, sets modified=true, and atomically renames an 8 KB prefix over the full file. Every account past the 8 KB boundary is permanently and silently deleted. The atomic-write machinery makes it worse, not better: the truncated copy is committed cleanly, so there is no orphan, no error, and no event.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Compare bytesRead against f.size() and abort the whole routine (no write-back) on a short read, the way setUserRoleInternal bails on a missing close bracket. Better: parse with ArduinoJson like every other users.json mutator instead of string-splicing a fixed-size char buffer.

### [HIGH] Brute-force lockout lives in two per-surface inline copies instead of the shared login path, so 4 of 6 credential surfaces accept unlimited password guesses
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:439`
- **Also:** Copies that DO check: HardwareOne.cpp:2571-2603 (serial inline) and WebServer_Server.cpp:3518/:3540 (web /login), :558/:569 (basic auth), :3766 (/register/submit). Paths with NO check: cmd_login System_Utils.cpp:5273->System_User.cpp:439; BLE Bluetooth.cpp:715-770; G2 lens G2_Glasses.cpp:4260; OLED keypad OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:184; MQTT System_MQTT.cpp:472; ESP-NOW v4_handle_cmd System_ESPNow.cpp:5841.
- **What:** The tiered IP lockout (isLoginLocked / recordFailedLogin / clearLoginAttempts, WebServer_Server.cpp:815-874) is invoked from exactly four call sites, all of them hand-rolled login flows inside a transport handler. The function that the codebase itself calls the centralized login front door — loginTransport (System_User.cpp:439, comment: "serial / display / bluetooth are the credential-login transports that funnel through here ... Audit each attempt once via the shared recordLoginAttempt() front-door") — calls isValidUser and recordLoginAttempt but never isLoginLocked and never recordFailedLogin. Everything that reaches authentication through cmd_login therefore has zero rate limiting. The serial console is the only local surface with a lockout, and only because HardwareOne.cpp:2551-2605 re-implements parse+lockout+isValidUser+authSuccessUnified inline rather than dispatching `login`.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete 1 (physical): the OLED login screen calls loginTransport directly (OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:184). A person at the device can try passwords on the on-screen keyboard forever; the serial console two inches away locks out after LOGIN_LOCKOUT_TIER1_COUNT failures against key "local". Same threat model, opposite behaviour. Concrete 2 (radio): BLE handles `login ` before its own auth gate (Bluetooth.cpp:715, gate at :791), re-serializes to `login <u> <p> bluetooth` and submits it; an unauthenticated BLE peer can therefore iterate the entire password space at link speed with no counter incrementing and no SYSEVT_LOGIN_LOCKED ever posted. Concrete 3 (lens): the G2 Config->OLED Login flow dispatches `login "<u>" "<p>" display` (G2_Glasses.cpp:4260), so possession of the paired glasses gives unlimited guesses against the physical console session. MQTT (System_MQTT.cpp:472) and ESP-NOW remote command (System_ESPNow.cpp:5841) likewise call isValidUser with no attempt accounting. sTotalFailedLogins — the counter the OLED Web Stats card presents as the "did anyone try to brute me?" answer (WebServer_Server.cpp:780-784) — stays at zero through all of it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (feature-gated by ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER for the lockout store itself, not by board)
- **Direction:** Move the isLoginLocked pre-check and the recordFailedLogin/clearLoginAttempts bookkeeping inside loginTransport, keyed on a per-transport identifier (the serial copy already uses the literal "local" for this), then delete the inline lockout blocks from HardwareOne.cpp and reduce the serial gate to a cmd_login dispatch. MQTT and ESP-NOW need an equivalent key (broker client id / peer MAC).

### [MEDIUM] Password hashing uses a device-global, attacker-recoverable value as the PBKDF2 salt at only 10,000 iterations
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:707`
- **Also:** System_Settings.cpp:267-283 (deriveDeviceKeyFromIds), System_Settings.cpp:299-320 (getDeviceEncryptionKey)
- **What:** hashUserPassword sets `String salt = getDeviceEncryptionKey()` and runs PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with `const int iterations = 10000` (:708). The "salt" is SHA-256(eFuseMAC : flashUID : "HARDWAREONE_V1") — a single value shared by every account on the device, not a per-user random salt. Its own source comment (System_Settings.cpp:264-266) says the eFuse MAC is "publicly visible via WiFi/BT" and the flash UID "requires physical SPI access to read".
- **Why it matters:** An attacker who can read the hashes must already be reading /system/users/user_settings/<id>.json off the (unencrypted) flash — and the flash UID is a register on that same SPI chip, readable by the same access, while the MAC is broadcast on every frame. So the salt provides no protection against exactly the attacker who obtains the hash. Because it is device-global rather than per-user, two accounts with the same password produce byte-identical hashes (directly visible by comparing the JSON files), and one precomputed table breaks every account at once. 10,000 iterations is the 2010-era NIST SP 800-132 floor cited in the comment; current OWASP guidance for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 is 600,000. libsodium (crypto_pwhash / Argon2id) is already linked and used elsewhere in the tree (System_BleSecureChannel.cpp, System_ESPNow_*.cpp) but is not used here.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards
- **Direction:** Store a per-user random salt (esp_fill_random, 16 B) in the user settings file and encode it in the hash string, and raise the iteration count — the hash format `PBKDF2:<iters>:<hex>` already has a slot for the count. Note that verifyUserPassword (:750-751) re-hashes with the compile-time constant and does a full-string compare, so the iteration field is currently decorative and raising it silently invalidates every account; add a rehash-on-successful-login path or accept the reset (the project's no-backwards-compat rule makes that cheap).

### [MEDIUM] revokeUserSessions' exceptSid is always empty on the web path, so a self-service password change signs the caller out — and the comment asserting otherwise is false
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:577`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:2462-2467 (the false comment); System_User.cpp:2117-2120 (the only exceptSid caller); System_User.cpp:542-547 (header comment documenting the intended behaviour)
- **What:** The web branch of revokeUserSessions skips a session only via `if (exceptSid.length() > 0 && gSessions[i].sid == exceptSid) continue;` (:577); it never consults exceptTransport, unlike the serial (:588), display (:597), G2 (:609) and BLE (:619) branches, which all honor it. But AuthContext::sid is never populated for web requests: makeWebAuthCtx (WebServer_Server.h:246-253) sets only transport/opaque/path/ip, and tgRequireAuth's SOURCE_WEB branch (System_User.cpp:162-175) sets only user and ip. So when userChangePasswordCore passes `caller.sid` as exceptSid (:2119), it passes an empty string and nothing is skipped.
- **Why it matters:** A user changes their own password at POST /account/password-change. ExecIdentityGuard installs the request's ctx (WebServer_Server.cpp:2468), userChangePasswordCore succeeds, then revokeUserSessions marks the caller's own session revoked=true (via enqueueTargetedRevokeForSessionIdx, :581). The handler replies 303 to /dashboard (:2493-2496); the browser follows it, isAuthed hits the `revoked` check at :639 and returns false, and the user is bounced to /login. On the forced-change flow (userMustChangePassword -> redirect at :3598) this makes the rotation loop through a re-login every time. The comment at WebServer_Server.cpp:2462-2467 states the opposite in so many words: "currentAuthContext() returns ctx (so revoke skips ctx.sid + SOURCE_WEB and the user's own browser tab stays logged in)." The exceptSid mechanism is effectively dead code on every web caller, so anything later built on "revoke all sessions except mine" over HTTP will silently revoke all of them.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (any build with ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER=1)
- **Direction:** Either populate ctx.sid in tgRequireAuth's SOURCE_WEB branch (it already calls isAuthed, which resolves the SID, so it is one assignment) or make the web branch honor exceptTransport the way the other four branches do. Whichever way it goes, correct the comment at WebServer_Server.cpp:2465 — it is currently the opposite of the behaviour.

### [MEDIUM] Credential login via loginTransport has no lockout or rate limit, giving BLE/OLED/ESP-NOW an unthrottled online password-guessing oracle
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:448`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/Bluetooth.cpp:735-773 (pre-auth login handling); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:5230 (cmd_login -> loginTransport); components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:135
- **What:** loginTransport() validates with `isValidUser(username, password)` (:448) and, on failure, calls only recordLoginAttempt(...) (:451) — an audit-log writer. It never calls isLoginLocked() or recordFailedLogin(), which are the actual tiered lockout counters (WebServer_Server.cpp:815, :841). Grep of the whole tree shows those two are called from exactly two places: the web login/register handlers (:558, :3518, :3540, :3766-3818) and the serial console gate (HardwareOne.cpp:2515, :2547). Every other credential transport reaches cmd_login -> loginTransport with nothing in between.
- **Why it matters:** On BLE, processBleCommandLine handles the literal `login` verb at Bluetooth.cpp:735-773 and returns — this is BEFORE the `if (gSettings.bleRequireAuth) { if (!bleIsAuthed(...)) { bleSendAuthRequired(); return; } }` gate at :797-803. So an unauthenticated BLE peer in radio range can write `login admin <guess>` to the CLI characteristic as fast as the queue drains, forever, with no lockout, no backoff and no counter — while the same guesses over HTTP are cut off after 5 failures (30 s), 10 (5 min) and 20 (30 min). The same unthrottled path is reachable from OLED auth, ESP-NOW remote commands and MQTT. A recovered credential then mints a web session through the normal /login form, so this directly undermines the session layer the lockout was built to protect.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (BLE reach requires ENABLE_BLUETOOTH=1); prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:75 lists "tiered per-IP lockout keyed on the real socket IP" under "Already solid" without noting that the non-web credential transports bypass it.
- **Direction:** Move the lockout check inside loginTransport so every credential transport inherits it, keyed on a per-transport identifier (BLE connId / peer MAC, "oled", ESP-NOW peer MAC) rather than an IP. The counter table already supports an arbitrary string key (LoginAttemptEntry::ip is char[40]).

### [MEDIUM] isValidUser returns before any PBKDF2 work for an unknown or banned username — a ~100 ms timing oracle for free account enumeration
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:1137`
- **Also:** hashUserPassword System_User.cpp:703 (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 10000 iterations); verifyUserPassword :739; second PBKDF2 for the gamepad pattern at :1159. Reachable from every login surface listed in the gamepad finding.
- **What:** `isValidUser` short-circuits at :1142 (`isUserBanned`) and :1146 (`getUserIdByUsername`) — both of which cost only a LittleFS read and a JSON parse — and at :1149 (`loadUserSettings`). Only when the account exists does it call `verifyUserPassword`, which runs a full PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with 10 000 iterations; when a gamepad pattern is also set, it runs a *second* one at :1159 before returning false.
- **Why it matters:** The response-time difference between a valid and an invalid username is roughly two orders of magnitude. The prior audit's own calibration (§4.4: "HW SHA makes 100k ≈ 1 s") puts 10k at ~100 ms per PBKDF2, against a few milliseconds for the early-return path — trivially separable over LAN, and equally over BLE where there is no throttle at all. An attacker therefore enumerates the account list for free before spending any guesses, and simultaneously learns which accounts have a gamepad pattern set (one PBKDF2 vs two on a wrong password), i.e. exactly which accounts have the weak second credential from the finding above.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4.4 and the "Deliberately lower priority" list dismiss timing on the *hash comparison* ("both operands are PBKDF2 outputs, so there's no steerable oracle"). That reaso
- **Direction:** Make the failure path cost-equivalent: when the username does not resolve, still run one PBKDF2 against a fixed dummy hash before returning false, and collapse the one-vs-two-PBKDF2 split (always evaluate both slots, combine with a branchless OR).

### [MEDIUM] tgRequireAuth's non-web branches are unreachable dead code, and their comments assert ESP-NOW/MQTT/BLE security properties the firmware does not implement
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:214`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:227-252 (the ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER==0 stub carries the same dead serial/display/G2 branches); live gates that these branches duplicate: components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2497, components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:2901, components/hardwareone/G2_HijackCmd.cpp:98
- **What:** `tgRequireAuth()` (System_User.cpp:160-220) is documented as "Require auth across transports" and branches on SOURCE_WEB / SOURCE_SERIAL / SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY / SOURCE_G2_GLASSES / else. Every caller in the tree passes a context built by `makeWebAuthCtx()`, which hard-sets `transport = SOURCE_WEB` (WebServer_Server.h:246-253): WebServer_Utils.h:74/:83 (the WEB_AUTH_OR_RETURN and WEB_AUTH_JSON_OR_RETURN macros), WebServer_Server.cpp:1643/1743/1905/2893/3175/4113/5027, WebPage_Sensors.cpp:551/663, WebServer_Events.cpp:158, WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:316. So only the SOURCE_WEB branch ever executes. The dead branches duplicate gates that really live elsewhere and have drifted from them: the serial branch sets `ctx.user = gSerialUser` (:180) where the live serial gate (HardwareOne.cpp:2497-2575) stamps the `"AuthBypass"` sentinel when auth is off and additionally enforces idle-logout (:2489) and IP lockout (:2513); the display branch (:189) likewise omits the `"AuthBypass"` stamp that `oledAuthContext()` applies (OLED_Utils.cpp:2980).
- **Why it matters:** The `else` branch comment (System_User.cpp:208-219) is the load-bearing problem. It states: (1) "ESP-NOW — v3 protocol signed msgId verification before submit" — grep across the whole tree finds no signed-msgId verification anywhere; the real ESP-NOW gate is mandatory AEAD session encryption plus a bond token or username:password (`v4_handle_cmd`, System_ESPNow.cpp:5737-5850). (2) "SOURCE_MQTT currently has NO such upstream check: MQTT-received commands are administrator-trusted, equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL" — false in both halves: `handleMQTTCommand` does authenticate every message with `isValidUser()` (System_MQTT.cpp:472), and `authorizeCommand` deliberately does NOT treat SOURCE_MQTT as internal (the bypass at System_Utils.cpp:4303 requires `transport == SOURCE_INTERNAL && user == "system"`). A maintainer acting on that comment — e.g. adding a SOURCE_MQTT arm to the internal-bypass to match the stated model — would hand every MQTT client an unauthenticated super-admin channel. (3) "BLE — per-conn session token check in Bluetooth.cpp before submit" is conditional on `gSettings.bleRequireAuth` (Bluetooth.cpp:797); with that toggle off there is no token check at all.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Reduce tgRequireAuth to the web branch it actually serves (or rename it to make the web scope explicit) and delete the transport branches that no longer run. If the multi-transport shape is being kept for a future caller, the else-branch comment must be rewritten against the code as it is — especially the MQTT sentence, which describes a trust model the authorization layer explicitly rejects.

### [MEDIUM] setUserPassword / setUserGamepadPassword ignore a loadUserSettings() failure and rewrite the per-user settings file from an empty document, destroying every other stored preference
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:767`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:959 (setUserGamepadPassword) - identical pattern.
- **What:** Both functions do read-modify-write on /system/users/user_settings/<id>.json but discard the read's return value: `loadUserSettings(userId, settings);  // OK if doesn't exist yet` (:767 and :959), then set one or two keys and call saveUserSettings(). loadUserSettings (System_Settings.cpp:3086) returns false on BOTH failure paths and leaves the document empty in both: open failure returns false after the leading doc.clear() (:3087, :3098), and a parse error does doc.clear() then returns false (:3101-3104). Only the missing-file case returns true with a fresh empty object (:3093-3096), which is the case the comment is describing.
- **Why it matters:** If a user's <id>.json is unparseable (a previously truncated write, flash bit-rot) or cannot be opened (LittleFS is mounted with maxOpenFiles=10, System_Filesystem.cpp:59, so a transient EMFILE under concurrent web/OLED/ESP-NOW load is real), then a password change writes the file back containing ONLY {"password":..., "mustChangePassword":...}. Everything else in that file is gone: theme, dashboard layout, notificationMuted / notificationForced / notifyLevel (System_Notifications.cpp:516, :602), and gamepad_password. setUserGamepadPassword is worse - it writes back only {"gamepad_password":...}, deleting the user's text `password` field, after which that account can no longer log in by password at all. The correct pattern already exists two functions away: mergeAndSaveUserSettings (System_Settings.cpp:3159) bails when loadUserSettings returns false.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Route both through mergeAndSaveUserSettings(), or at minimum distinguish "file absent" from "read failed" (a tri-state return, or an out-param) and abort the write on a genuine read failure instead of committing a one-key document.

### [MEDIUM] tgRequireAuth's transport comment asserts auth mechanisms that no longer exist and declares MQTT equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:214`
- **Also:** The MQTT reality is System_MQTT.cpp:472 (isValidUser per message) and :525-544 (submitAndExecuteSync under SOURCE_MQTT). The ESP-NOW reality is System_ESPNow.cpp:5739 (session-encryption gate), :5771 (bond token) and :5841 (isValidUser).
- **What:** The else-branch comment in tgRequireAuth (System_User.cpp:207-217) documents the security posture of the transports it does not gate. Both of its substantive claims are false against current code. (1) "ESP-NOW — v3 protocol signed msgId verification before submit": there is no V3 protocol left in the tree — grep for ESPNOW_V3/V3_MAGIC returns only two stale comments — and no msgId is signed anywhere; the real gates are mandatory SESSION_FRAME AEAD plus either a bond session token or isValidUser. (2) "SOURCE_MQTT currently has NO such upstream check: MQTT-received commands are administrator-trusted, equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL": MQTT has validated credentials per message since the routing change (System_MQTT.cpp:472), and MQTT commands emphatically do NOT get the SOURCE_INTERNAL treatment — authorizeCommand's bypass at System_Utils.cpp:4369 requires transport==SOURCE_INTERNAL && user=="system", which SOURCE_MQTT never satisfies, so guest/super/admin gates all apply.
- **Why it matters:** This is the one comment in the codebase that tells a maintainer what each un-gated transport's compensating control is. A reader following it would (a) go looking for a signature check on the ESP-NOW path and, not finding one, either add a redundant one or conclude the path is unprotected, and (b) act on "MQTT is equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL" — the single most dangerous thing to believe about that transport, since making it true would hand every MQTT client the authorizeCommand bypass. The instruction to "split this branch and wire MQTT through a proper session/token check" if it is ever opened up is already satisfied and reads as outstanding work.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Rewrite the branch comment to describe the actual gates (BLE per-conn session token; ESP-NOW mandatory SESSION_FRAME + bond-token-or-isValidUser; MQTT per-message isValidUser, still SOURCE_MQTT and still fully subject to authorizeCommand). Deleting the 'equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL' sentence is the load-bearing part.

### [LOW] System_AuthIdentity.h claims the legacy exec-identity globals still exist and are kept in sync; they were deleted
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_AuthIdentity.h:6`
- **Also:** HardwareOne.cpp:391 carries the accurate note ("Per-task identity (formerly gExecUser / gExecIsAdmin / gExecAuthContext) now ...")
- **What:** The file banner states: "Replaces the old globals gExecAuthContext, gExecUser, gExecIsAdmin. During migration the legacy globals still exist and ExecIdentityGuard keeps them in sync; readers can switch to the accessor functions below file-by-file." A grep for those three identifiers across every .cpp and .h in components/hardwareone returns only two hits, both inside comments (System_AuthIdentity.h:6 itself and HardwareOne.cpp:390, which correctly says they "now" live in per-task TLS). No definition, no declaration, and no sync code exists.
- **Why it matters:** This is the header a maintainer reads to understand how per-task auth identity works — the mechanism behind every guarded FS call and every ExecIdentityGuard scope. It tells them a dual-write invariant is in force, so a reader debugging an identity mismatch will look for the sync path that keeps the globals current, find nothing, and be left unsure whether the sync is missing (a bug) or the globals are gone (the truth). The migration it describes is complete.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders
- **Direction:** Delete the second sentence of that paragraph; the first sentence (historical context) is still useful.

### [LOW] Stale comment asserts MQTT has no upstream auth check — a maintainer trusting it could add a genuinely unauthenticated path
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:215`
- **What:** `tgRequireAuth`'s else-branch comment reads: "SOURCE_MQTT currently has NO such upstream check: MQTT-received commands are administrator-trusted, equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL. If/when MQTT is opened to untrusted clients, split this branch and wire MQTT through a proper session/token check." (System_User.cpp:215-218), and the function `return true`s for SOURCE_MQTT. In reality System_MQTT.cpp:472 DOES call `isValidUser` per message, so the comment understates the current posture — while the actual live gap (the `target:` branch skipping authorizeCommand at :486-516) is documented nowhere in this file.
- **Why it matters:** The comment is a trust-model landmine in both directions: it tells a reader MQTT is a trusted internal transport (encouraging future code to skip checks on SOURCE_MQTT), while the transport is in fact reachable by anyone who can publish to the broker. It also points attention away from the branch that genuinely is unauthorized.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4 flags this exact comment as "itself a hazard". Re-verified: unchanged.
- **Direction:** Rewrite to state what is true: MQTT authenticates per message via isValidUser in handleMQTTCommand and routes the local path through executeCommand; the mesh-routing branch does not, and that is the gap to close.

### [NOTE] verifyUserPassword compares with Arduino String operator== rather than a constant-time compare
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:751`
- **What:** `return (inputHash == storedHash);` — Arduino String::operator== resolves to equals(), a length check plus strcmp. Not constant-time. libsodium's sodium_memcmp is linked and is used for exactly this purpose elsewhere (System_ESPNow.cpp:38 and :5207 for the HMAC tag compare, with an explicit comment at System_ESPNow_Handlers_Crypto.cpp:124 about spraying HMAC guesses).
- **Why it matters:** Reporting this as a NOTE rather than a defect, because I traced the exploitability and it is not there: both operands are PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 outputs and the attacker controls only the preimage, so a byte-position oracle cannot be walked — you would have to invert PBKDF2 to steer the compared bytes. The ~100 ms PBKDF2 that precedes the compare also swamps the strcmp delta by several orders of magnitude. Recording it so a future reviewer does not have to re-derive the same conclusion, and because the asymmetry with the ESP-NOW code (which does use sodium_memcmp for a directly-attacker-controlled tag) is worth making deliberate.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards
- **Direction:** Optional hygiene only: swap to sodium_memcmp over the fixed 32-byte decoded hash for consistency with the ESP-NOW tag comparison. Do not treat this as a fix that closes a live hole — the real timing oracle in this file is the existence check at :1146, filed separately.


### Settings / config — 9 findings (5 critical/high)

### [HIGH] Device-key epoch selection is blind to the credential database and can permanently orphan every password hash
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:1287`
- **Also:** System_User.cpp:707 (the salt), System_Settings.cpp:299-320 (the un-validated fallback derivation when readSettingsJson bails before :1390)
- **What:** hashUserPassword uses `String salt = getDeviceEncryptionKey()` (System_User.cpp:707) — the same key used to AES-encrypt WiFi/MQTT passwords at rest. selectDeviceKeyEpoch (System_Settings.cpp:1261) picks among three candidate derivations by counting how many `AES:` blobs from settings.json each one opens (:1287-1292) and adopts the winner (:1294). Password hashes are not AES: blobs, are not in settings.json, and are never consulted. When `nBlobs == 0` the loop leaves `best = 0` and the naive derivation is adopted with zero validation.
- **Why it matters:** Two concrete failure chains. (1) A device with no stored WiFi/MQTT password has no oracle at all; if `esp_flash_read_unique_chip_id` transiently fails (:1270-1271, an explicitly anticipated failure per the comment at :1241) the key silently becomes the mac+0 epoch, every stored PBKDF2 hash stops verifying, and every account is locked out — with serialRequireAuth defaulting true (System_Settings.h:359) that is a total lockout. (2) Worse and permanent: if any secret is re-saved during such a boot, later boots find that the mac+0 candidate opens the AES blobs and mac+flashUID opens none, so the selector deliberately adopts mac+0 — permanently orphaning password hashes computed under mac+flashUID. The selector's own optimization causes the lockout.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards; prior art: Adjacent to memory/project_secret_loss_diagnosis_and_hardening.md, which shipped selectDeviceKeyEpoch. That work validates candidate keys against AES: blobs only; the password-hash blind spot below is
- **Direction:** Give the epoch selector a credential-side oracle: store a small key-fingerprint marker alongside the hashes (deviceKeyFingerprint already exists at :288), or include a canary AES: blob written at first-time setup so nBlobs is never 0. At minimum, log loudly and refuse to adopt a new epoch when nBlobs == 0 and users.json exists.

### [HIGH] handleSettingCommand() ignores writeSettingsJson()'s result and returns "[Settings] Configuration updated" — the entire settings surface reports success on a failed persist
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:2645`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Settings.h:1150,1159,1168 (setSetting overloads); components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:2645,2660,2668,2677; components/hardwareone/System_SetupWizard.cpp:82; components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:1183
- **What:** All four type branches of handleSettingCommand() (:2645, :2660, :2668, :2677) call `if (!gDeferWrites) writeSettingsJson();` with the bool discarded, then unconditionally `return "[Settings] Configuration updated"` (:2648, :2663, :2673, :2685), broadcast "<key> set to <value>", and post SYSEVT_SETTING_CHANGED. stampOkStatus turns the return into "OK: [Settings] Configuration updated" (out[0]=='[' but out[1]=='S', so the JSON-array exemption correctly does not fire). The setSetting() family (System_Settings.h:1147-1171) is `void` by construction, so even a caller that wanted the truth cannot get it.
- **Why it matters:** writeSettingsJson() has four explicit failure exits (:1049 doc.overflowed → PSRAM allocation failure, :1062 temp open failure, :1077 serialize wrote 0, :1099 rename-and-direct-open both failed). Each one logs "settings NOT persisted" to system-events.log and posts SYSEVT_SETTINGS_SAVE_FAILED. The user's command still answers "OK: [Settings] Configuration updated" and the audit log records "-> OK". Reachable today: PSRAM_JSON_DOC is an elastic ArduinoJson 7 doc, so `overflowed()` means allocation failure — with an LLM model loaded (~7.3 MB of 8 MB per the project's own notes) any setting change can hit it. The device then reboots with the old value and the operator has a log line saying the change succeeded. Roughly 276 registered SettingEntry cmdKeys route here (per docs/DOCS_ACCURACY_AUDIT_2026-07-26.md).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.4 records "writeSettingsJson has 5 return-false paths; every call site ignores the result (~20 sites). Violates the OK:/Error: contract." Status unchanged — still 3
- **Direction:** Return the bool from writeSettingsJson through handleSettingCommand and answer "Error: <key> set in RAM but NOT saved (<reason>)" on failure; gLastInitErrorReason-style reason capture already exists as a pattern in this codebase (System_ESPNow.cpp:9516). Making setSetting return bool is the deeper fix but touches many call sites.

### [HIGH] Mesh passphrase and its PBKDF2-stretched key are decrypted BEFORE the device-key epoch is selected, and the mesh writer destroys the merge-read blob before putSecret can preserve it — so one failed decrypt permanently clobbers the mesh secret
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:1386`
- **Also:** The WiFi-networks writer in the same file (System_Settings.cpp:961-977) does this correctly — it captures oldSsid/oldBlob BEFORE to<JsonArray>() with a comment naming this exact hazard. espnowMeshesWriteJson is a missed application of that fix.
- **What:** readSettingsJson() calls espnowMeshesReadJson(doc) at :1386, which decrypts the mesh passphrase and the stretched mesh key via getSecret() (System_ESPNow.cpp:1434, :1441). selectDeviceKeyEpoch(doc) — the function whose whole job is 'before anything decrypts, test the stored blobs against every plausible derivation and adopt the one that actually opens them' (:1244-1247) — does not run until :1390. So those two decrypts use whatever getDeviceEncryptionKey() lazily derives (the default mac+flashUID epoch, :299-320), not the validated one. Separately, getSecret() (:603) has no failure detection at all, so a failed mesh decrypt does not increment gSecretLoadFailures. On the write side, espnowMeshesWriteJson (System_ESPNow.cpp:1376) does `doc["espnow"]["meshes"].to<JsonArray>()`, which clears the array the merge-read just loaded, and then calls putSecret(e,"passphrase",...) at :1389 on a freshly-created object — so putSecretPreserving's prevBlob is always empty and its 'keep the previous stored value' branch (:584-592) is unreachable for meshes.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete chain: the flash-UID read transiently fails or changes representation (exactly the scenario selectDeviceKeyEpoch was written for, per its own comment) → the mesh passphrase blob and passphraseStretchedKey decrypt to "" → gSettings.meshes[i].passphrase = "" and passphraseStretchedKeyValid = false, silently and without incrementing gSecretLoadFailures → the next writeSettingsJson (any setSetting, e.g. a WiFi reconnect) rebuilds the meshes array from scratch and writes `"passphrase":""` over the still-recoverable AES blob. The device permanently loses its mesh passphrase and cached group-key material and cannot rejoin its mesh; selectDeviceKeyEpoch can no longer heal it because the blob is gone. decryptString also returns "" on a plain ps_alloc failure (:475-478, :491-496) and on corrupt padding (:514-529), so a PSRAM-starved boot (LLM loaded) reaches the same end state without any key-epoch problem.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Move espnowMeshesReadJson (and blePeersReadJson, for symmetry) to after selectDeviceKeyEpoch in readSettingsJson; make getSecret bump gSecretLoadFailures when the input starts with "AES:" but decrypts empty; and give espnowMeshesWriteJson the same prevBlob capture the wifi block already has.

### [HIGH] saveUserSettings() truncates the live per-user credential file in place as its rename fallback, then reports success on an unverifiable buffered write
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:3131`
- **Also:** System_Settings.cpp:1097-1110 (writeSettingsJson has the same truncate-in-place fallback, and it additionally ignores the fallback serializeJson return entirely — that half is docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.7)
- **What:** saveUserSettings() (System_Settings.cpp:3113-3148) writes a .tmp, checks only `written == 0`, then renames. If renameGuarded fails it opens the REAL path with mode "w" — which truncates it at fopen — re-serializes, and returns `written > 0`. This is the per-user settings file at /system/users/user_settings/<id>.json, which holds the `password` and `gamepad_password` hashes read by isValidUser() (System_User.cpp:1151-1161).
- **Why it matters:** Two independent failure chains. (1) Rename fails (ENOSPC on the metadata commit, or a stale .tmp) → the fallback truncates the user's live credential file to zero length before writing anything; if that write then also fails, serializeJson still returns the full byte count out of the 4 KB stdio buffer (see the vfs_api finding), so `written > 0` is true and the function returns success while the file on disk is empty. (2) Even without a rename failure, the primary path's `written == 0` guard cannot detect a full flash for a sub-4 KB doc. Either way loadUserSettings() then returns an empty object, `settings["password"]` is null, both password checks in isValidUser() fail, and that account is permanently unable to log in on any surface — with the command that caused it having reported OK.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: CORRECTS docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:191, which asserts "settings.json is the only persist path not using writeTextAtomic" and that it is the sole place with a truncate-in-place rename fallback. s
- **Direction:** Delete the truncate-in-place fallback and fail loudly instead, matching writeTextAtomic's documented policy; verify the committed size after close before renaming.

### [HIGH] CONFIG_MBEDTLS_EXTERNAL_MEM_ALLOC=y puts every TLS secret — session traffic keys and the parsed HTTPS server private key — in plaintext PSRAM, breaking the project's own "never put secrets in PSRAM" rule
- **Where:** `sdkconfig.defaults:142`
- **Also:** sdkconfig:2313 (live value); sdkconfig:494 (CONFIG_SECURE_FLASH_ENC_ENABLED unset); esp-idf components/mbedtls/port/esp_mem.c:18-19 (the mechanism); docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §4 (the incorrect reassurance)
- **What:** sdkconfig.defaults:139-142 sets CONFIG_MBEDTLS_EXTERNAL_MEM_ALLOC=y to move ~40-50 KB/session TLS buffers out of internal DRAM. IDF implements this by making mbedtls_calloc resolve to esp_mbedtls_mem_calloc, which is `heap_caps_calloc(n, size, MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT)` — an explicit SPIRAM cap for EVERY mbedTLS allocation, not just the big content buffers. That includes mbedtls_ssl_transform (the derived read/write traffic keys), mbedtls_ssl_session (the master secret and resumption ticket state, kept alive for 86400 s by CONFIG_ESP_TLS_SERVER_SESSION_TICKETS), and the MPI limb arrays that mbedtls_pk_parse_key allocates for the HTTPS server private key. CONFIG_SECURE_FLASH_ENC_ENABLED is not set, so PSRAM is plaintext on an externally probeable bus.
- **Why it matters:** Bring up HTTPS and complete one handshake: the server's private key material and that session's traffic keys are resident in external PSRAM for the life of the connection (and the private key for the life of the server). An attacker with physical access — the exact threat model the project's no-secrets-in-PSRAM rule exists for — reads the QSPI PSRAM bus and recovers the HTTPS identity, which then decrypts and impersonates the admin web surface. Critically, docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §4 tells a future reader that short secret allocations "are protected by accident — they're small, so ALWAYSINTERNAL keeps them internal". That is true for malloc/new/String, and false here: heap_caps_calloc with an explicit MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM bypasses CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384 entirely, so even a 32-byte key goes external. So the one document that would be consulted before touching this actively points the wrong way.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (FeatherS3 primary; any build with HTTPS or MQTT-over-TLS); prior art: Adjacent, not this. docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:110 (§3.1) files the same class for gSessions/gPending but never mentions mbedTLS. docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §4 explicitly reassures the
- **Direction:** The choice is between DRAM pressure and the secrets rule and it currently resolves silently in favour of DRAM. Either scope the PSRAM routing to the bulk content buffers only (CONFIG_MBEDTLS_CUSTOM_MEM_ALLOC with a size/purpose-aware allocator) or accept the internal-DRAM cost; at minimum, correct the MECHANICS §4 reassurance so the accidental-protection argument is not applied to mbedTLS.

### [MEDIUM] decryptString() copies every decrypted secret into a second heap buffer and free()s it without zeroing, two lines after carefully wiping the first one
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:532`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:548 (the free), :550 (the returned String)
- **What:** After AES-CBC decrypt and PKCS#7 strip, decryptString does: char* result = malloc(plaintextLen+1); memcpy(result, plaintext, plaintextLen); memset(plaintext, 0, ciphertextLen); free(plaintext); String output = String(result); free(result);  — the PSRAM `plaintext` buffer is wiped, but `result` (which holds the identical cleartext) is freed at :548 with no memset, and the String copy it feeds is likewise never wiped by this function. Sub-16 KB mallocs land in internal DRAM under CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384, so this is internal-heap residue rather than PSRAM, but it is unconditional and it defeats the wipe that sits immediately above it.
- **Why it matters:** Every recoverable secret in the system passes through this one function on the read path: WiFi PSKs (System_Settings.cpp getSecret on the networks array), mqttPassword, bleSecureChannelSecret, each mesh passphrase AND its PBKDF2-stretched key — which the code itself annotates at System_ESPNow.cpp:1393-1399 as being the mesh group key material, derivable without the passphrase — and the 64-byte Ed25519 device identity secret (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:180). Each boot therefore scatters un-zeroed cleartext copies of all of them across the internal heap, where any later heap-adjacent overread, a crash dump, or a heap-walking diagnostic re-exposes them. The callers that do try to be careful (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:190-195 hand-wipes its returned hex; hashUserPassword secureClearString's the salt) are undone by the copy this function leaves behind.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** memset(result, 0, plaintextLen) before free(result), and consider constructing the output String directly from the wiped-on-exit plaintext buffer so only one cleartext copy ever exists.

### [MEDIUM] SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE §4.1 is only half-fixed: the merge-read's doc.clear() still lets the first settings write after a failed load serialize pure defaults over every registered section, and gSettingsLoadedOk does not protect the WiFi list in the corrupt-file case
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:1028`
- **What:** The boot auto-trigger IS fixed - HardwareOne.cpp:1407-1408 now uses plain assignment for crashCount/lastResetReason instead of setSetting, and a failed load leaves the file on disk (:1374-1377). But writeSettingsJson's merge-read still does `doc.clear()` on a parse failure (:1024-1028) and then falls straight through to buildSettingsJsonDoc (:1038) with no abort. gSettingsLoadedOk (:960) only gates the WiFi-networks REBUILD; when the merge-read has cleared the document there is no wifi array left to preserve, so skipping the rebuild leaves the array absent from the output entirely.
- **Why it matters:** An empty or unparseable /system/settings.json survives the boot corrupt-check (an empty file fails the length>0 guard at System_Filesystem.cpp:179; and the check is pointed at the wrong path anyway - see the separate finding). readSettingsJson returns false, gSettings runs on defaults, gSettingsLoadedOk stays false. The next settings write from ANY source - a CLI/web/OLED/G2/ESP-NOW setting change, `detect apply` (System_I2C.cpp:1182), `features <x> on` (System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:610), `autostart` (System_I2C.cpp:2112/2132) - takes the same corrupt file down the merge-read path, hits the parse error, clears the doc, and writes out every registered module section at RAM defaults with NO network.wifi.networks array. Saved WiFi networks, mesh passphrases, ESP-NOW identity settings and all module config are gone, and the operation reports success. The guard that was added protects the transient-failure-on-an-intact-file case, not the corrupt-file case the audit rated HIGH.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN - docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.1 (HIGH). recon_prior.md item 10 records this as "status CHANGED to FIXED". That is only true of the boot trigger; the destructive mechanism is intact. The 
- **Direction:** Refuse the save when the merge-read of an EXISTING file fails, the same way writeTextAtomic refuses the truncate-in-place fallback (System_Utils.cpp:841-847) - return false and surface the error rather than committing a defaults document over a file the firmware could not read.

### [MEDIUM] setSetting() is void — every setting command reports OK: after a failed persist
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.h:1150`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:610; components/hardwareone/System_I2C.cpp:1183,2112,2132; components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:3071; components/hardwareone/System_SetupWizard.cpp:82,1782; components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:971; components/hardwareone/System_Camera_DVP.cpp:1292
- **What:** All three setSetting overloads (:1147, :1156, :1165) and setDebugSetting (:1177) call `writeSettingsJson()` / `writeDebugJson()` as bare statements and are declared void, so the five documented failure returns of writeSettingsJson (System_Settings.cpp:1004 filesystem not ready, :1054 JSON overflow, :1068 temp open failed, :1083 serialize wrote 0, :1104 rename+direct-open both failed) cannot reach the handler. All ~30 direct writeSettingsJson() call sites do the same.
- **Why it matters:** With settings.json already past the 5120-byte doc budget, writeSettingsJson returns false at :1054 after logging "save FAILED (JSON build overflow) — settings NOT persisted" and posting SYSEVT_SETTINGS_SAVE_FAILED, but the in-RAM field has already been mutated at :1149. The command still returns its success string and the user sees "OK: ...". After a reboot the setting is back to its old value with nothing linking the two events. Same for `detect apply` (System_I2C.cpp:1182) and `features <id> on` (System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:608), both of which return their own success text.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.4 — status unchanged; framed there as "call sites ignore the result". The OK:/Error: contract consequence is the part that matters for this track.
- **Direction:** Make setSetting return bool and thread it into the handlers' return strings. The failure signal already exists (SYSEVT_SETTINGS_SAVE_FAILED, logSystemEvent) — it just never reaches the caller who asked.

### [NOTE] Thirteen SettingEntry rows tag a uint32_t field as SETTING_INT, so the write path reinterprets the field as signed
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Settings.cpp:2009`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Battery.cpp:801, System_ESPNow.cpp:16040-16043, System_Power.cpp:406-407, System_Settings.cpp:1967-1970
- **What:** A mechanical cross-check of every SettingEntry's declared SETTING_* tag against the real C++ type of its target field in struct Settings found 13 rows where a uint32_t field is tagged SETTING_INT: crashCount and lastResetReason (System_Settings.cpp:2009-2010), the four sessionIdle* rows (:1967-1970), batteryLogIntervalMs (System_Battery.cpp:801), the four mesh timing rows (System_ESPNow.cpp:16040-16043), and powerTransitionCooldownMs / powerSaveTimeoutMinutes (System_Power.cpp:406-407). writeRegisteredSettings' SETTING_INT case does `target[leaf] = *((int*)e->valuePtr)` (:2460); the read case writes back through an int* (:2340).
- **Why it matters:** Benign today - int and uint32_t are both 4 bytes on this target so no adjacent field is clobbered, and every one of these rows carries a maxVal that keeps the value well inside INT_MAX, so no value is ever serialized as negative.
