Filed as a NOTE rather than a defect because the codebase treats this exact class of mismatch as load-bearing: the enum comment at System_Settings.h:1262-1270 records that width-mismatched dispatch previously produced heap corruption and a "free() target pointer is outside heap areas" crash, and the explicit U8/U16/U32 tags exist specifically so future rows cannot regress. These 13 rows sit outside that protection.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Retag these rows SETTING_U32 to match their fields. A build-time check would be stronger than a one-off sweep - the registry rows are static, so a constexpr assertion tying each row's tag to sizeof/signedness of its target is feasible.


### MQTT — 8 findings (2 critical/high)

### [HIGH] MQTT bridge still skips authorizeCommand — the `target:` mesh-routing branch calls three admin-gated handlers directly and never reaches executeCommand
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:486`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:494, :501, :508 (the three direct calls), :544 (the local path, correctly routed through submitAndExecuteSync); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:15922, 15923, 15931 (all three registered requiresAdmin=true)
- **What:** handleMQTTCommand authenticates with isValidUser(username, password) at System_MQTT.cpp:472 — which checks credentials only and never consults role (System_User.cpp:1137-1164: ban check, username lookup, password/gamepad-pattern verify, no getUserRole call). When a `target` field is present and meshRole == MESH_ROLE_MASTER, the handler takes the branch at :483 and calls cmd_espnow_roomcmd (:494), cmd_espnow_tagcmd (:501) or cmd_espnow_remote (:508) directly, then `return`s at :513 — before the submitAndExecuteSync at :544 that every other MQTT command goes through. All three are registered requiresAdmin=true (System_ESPNow.cpp:15922, 15923, 15931). authorizeCommand is never reached on this branch, so neither the admin gate nor the guest gate runs.
- **Why it matters:** An authenticated non-admin — including a Guest, since `isValidUser` (System_User.cpp:1137-1165) checks only credentials and never role, and the guest view-only rule lives exclusively in `authorizeCommand` (System_Utils.cpp:4323-4333) — publishes {"user":"guest1","pass":"x","target":"room:lab","cmd":"..."} and drives mesh command dispatch that is admin-only on every other transport. Three further consequences of skipping the chokepoint: (1) NO audit record — `logCommandExecution` is only called from `executeCommand` (System_Utils.cpp:4670/4729/4741), so these dispatches produce no command-audit.log line and no `[CMD] user@mqtt:` console echo; (2) the target-string is unquoted into the arg buffer, so target="room:X Y Z" re-splits and lets the publisher inject arbitrary target-device credentials into the on-wire `user:pass:cmd` payload; (3) the handlers write into the single shared 4 KB `gDebugBuffer` (System_Debug.cpp:57, System_Debug.h:364) from the esp-mqtt event task, and `cmd_espnow_remote` blocks up to 2000 ms in `sendAeadSync` there — exactly the race the comment at System_MQTT.cpp:521-524 says routing through cmd_exec exists to prevent.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 4 finders; board: all — but LATENT in the current tree: System_BuildConfig.h:104 sets `#define ENABLE_MQTT 0`, so System_MQTT.cpp is compiled out today. The defect is a one-flag flip away from live and the MQTT feature is documented as shipped.; prior art: PARTIAL — docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md line 17 and §4 claim "The MQTT backlog item is resolved… it authenticates per-message via isValidUser and routes through authorizeCommand". That doc is WRONG:
- **Direction:** Route the mesh branch through submitAndExecuteSync the same way the local branch already does — build the `espnowroomcmd <room> <u> <p> <cmd>` line and submit it so authorizeCommand applies. Note also that tgRequireAuth's else-branch comment (System_User.cpp:213-216) still says MQTT is "administrator-trusted, equivalent to SOURCE_INTERNAL", which is no longer how the local path behaves; that comment should be corrected either way.

### [HIGH] MQTT command bridge reports ok:true for mesh-routed commands unconditionally, and ok:true for any locally-dispatched failure
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:496`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:503,510,548-555
- **What:** Two separate defects. (1) The mesh-routing branches build their reply with a literal `{\"ok\":true,...,\"result\":\"%s\"}` at :496, :503 and :510 regardless of what cmd_espnow_roomcmd / tagcmd / remote returned — and they splice the result into a JSON string with no escaping, so any result containing a quote or a newline (e.g. the multi-line usage text at System_ESPNow.cpp:14310) emits invalid JSON. (2) The local branch sets `respDoc[\"ok\"] = success` at :548 from submitAndExecuteSync, which is executeCommand's always-true bool, and then selects the result/error key on it at :551-555.
- **Why it matters:** `{"target":"room:kitchen","cmd":"..."}` when no device is in that room → cmd_espnow_roomcmd returns "No devices found in room 'kitchen'" → published as `{"ok":true,...,"result":"No devices found in room 'kitchen'"}`. A Home Assistant automation keying on `ok` treats a total no-op as a success. Secondarily, `result` is pasted into a JSON string with no escaping, so any quote or backslash in a peer/room name emits malformed JSON and any result over ~200 chars is silently cut by the 256-byte respBuf.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_MQTT; mesh branches also need ENABLE_ESPNOW); prior art: project_security_review_backlog.md item 2 covers the auth bypass on this same code path (bypasses authorizeCommand). The ok:true hardcode is a separate defect.
- **Direction:** Use the ArduinoJson doc for the mesh branches too (it escapes correctly, as the batch endpoint's comment at WebServer_Server.cpp:5113 already notes) and derive ok from the routed command's result rather than a literal.

### [MEDIUM] MQTT command handler debug-logs the entire inbound payload including the cleartext password
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:441`
- **What:** handleMQTTCommand does DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDSF("Command payload: %s", payload.c_str()) at :441, before parsing. The documented payload format is {"user":"username","pass":"password","cmd":"command"} (:420), so the string logged contains the account password verbatim. DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDSF gates on DEBUG_MQTT | DEBUG_MQTT_COMMANDS (System_Debug.h:473) and routes MSG_ROUTE_ALL.
- **Why it matters:** With the MQTT command debug flag enabled for troubleshooting (the exact situation in which someone would turn it on), every device password used over MQTT is broadcast to serial, the web console mirror and every BLE CLI sink via MSG_ROUTE_ALL, and appended to the system log file on flash when system logging is running. `redactCmdForAudit` never sees this string — the password is a JSON field, not a command token, so even the existing `login`/`useradd` rules would not cover it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 3 finders; board: all — LATENT, ENABLE_MQTT=0 in the current build config (System_BuildConfig.h:104); prior art: KNOWN — project_security_review_backlog memory item 6 (recorded as "~:385"; it is now line 441). Re-verified: unchanged, still open.
- **Direction:** Log only the parsed `cmd` field after the auth check, or redact by printing `topic + payload length` at this point and moving any content logging below :481 where `command` is already isolated from `pass`.

### [MEDIUM] MQTT mesh-route branch still calls three admin-only handlers directly, bypassing authorizeCommand — the prior audit's "resolved" verdict is wrong
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:486`
- **Also:** cmd_espnow_roomcmd called at :494, cmd_espnow_tagcmd at :501, cmd_espnow_remote at :508; all three registered `requiresAdmin = true` at System_ESPNow.cpp:15922, :15923, :15931. Also flagged as G2 in the shared recon map scratchpad/recon_surfaces.md and as item 2 of memory/project_security_review_backlog.md.
- **What:** `handleMQTTCommand` authenticates with `isValidUser(user, pass)`, which returns true for a guest account. If a `target` field is present and this device is mesh master, the handler calls `cmd_espnow_roomcmd`, `cmd_espnow_tagcmd` or `cmd_espnow_remote` as raw function pointers and `return`s before the `submitAndExecuteSync` path at :544. All three are registered `requiresAdmin=true`, and none of that is checked because `authorizeCommand` is never reached on this branch. The local-exec path immediately below it was correctly fixed; this branch was not.
- **Why it matters:** A guest account with valid MQTT credentials publishes `{"user":"guest","pass":"…","target":"device:worker1","cmd":"factoryreset"}`. `cmd_espnow_remote` runs locally — it transmits ESP-NOW frames and consumes the radio, an admin-gated local action performed by a view-only account. The supplied credentials are re-verified on the target (System_ESPNow.cpp:5841) and the target then stamps `cmd.ctx.auth.user = username` and routes through `executeCommand`, so a guest is correctly denied *there* — but only if rosters match. Where the same username holds a higher role on the target device (rosters are per-device unless `espnowusersync` is run), this is a cross-device privilege escalation reachable only from the one surface that skips the guest gate.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all (ENABLE_MQTT && ENABLE_ESPNOW, and only when gSettings.meshRole == MESH_ROLE_MASTER); prior art: docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md §4 states "The MQTT backlog item is resolved — it authenticates per-message via isValidUser and routes through authorizeCommand ([System_MQTT.cpp:470],[:529])". That is
- **Direction:** Route the mesh branch through the same `submitAndExecuteSync` path as the local branch — build the `espnowroomcmd <room> <u> <p> <cmd>` line and submit it, so `authorizeCommand` sees a resolvable command name. Then correct the §4 paragraph in docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md.

### [MEDIUM] MQTT mesh-bridge silently truncates the composed remote command at 255 bytes (snprintf return discarded)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:493`
- **Also:** System_MQTT.cpp:500 (tag:) and :507 (device:) are identical; :495/:502/:509 additionally splice the unescaped handler result into a 256-byte JSON response with the same discarded return.
- **What:** `char argBuf[256]; snprintf(argBuf, sizeof(argBuf), "%s %s %s %s", targetStr.substring(5).c_str(), username, password, command);` then `cmd_espnow_roomcmd(String(argBuf))`. The return value is discarded, so an over-long composition is silently cut and the truncated string is executed as a command on every peer in the room. `command` comes straight from the MQTT JSON payload (`doc["cmd"]`, :454) with no length limit; `username`/`password`/`target` come from the same payload.
- **Why it matters:** This is the exact P44 danger case: a truncated *command*. An MQTT client publishing `{"user":"u","pass":"p","target":"room:kitchen","cmd":"<260 chars>"}` gets a differently-shaped command broadcast to the mesh than the one it sent — arguments dropped mid-token, quoting unbalanced, trailing safety tokens (`confirm`) removed — with no error returned. The client's response JSON still reports `"ok":true`.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Check the snprintf return against `sizeof(argBuf)` and publish an explicit error instead of dispatching. The main `executeCommand` path already does exactly this at System_Utils.cpp:4712 ("result too large for this transport") — the bridge should mirror that behaviour. Building the arg via String would also remove the fixed cap.

### [MEDIUM] publishDiscoveryConfig() accumulates snprintf return values with no clamp — a long mqttBaseTopic drives writes past the 1024-byte PSRAM staging buffer
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:388`
- **Also:** publishPeerDiscoveryConfig (System_MQTT.cpp:706-738) is the same nine-call pattern into the same shared buffer, also driven by mqttBaseTopic. publishMQTTSensorData (:1130-1235) uses the identical unguarded pattern against a 16384-byte ps_alloc; its content maxes out around 5 KB so it is not currently reachable, but it has no guard either.
- **What:** Nine chained `pos += snprintf(configJson + pos, sizeof(configJson) - pos, ...)` calls (:363 through :399) into `sMqttDiscoveryConfigJson[1024]` (EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR, :340). `pos` is int and `sizeof(configJson)` is size_t, so the moment any call truncates, `pos` exceeds 1024, the size argument wraps to ~4.29e9 and the destination is already past the buffer. stateTopic and availTopic are both `gSettings.mqttBaseTopic + suffix`, and cmd_mqttbasetopic (:1542) applies no length validation at all — unlike espnowRoom/Zone/Tags which go through metaGetSet's maxLen check (System_ESPNow.cpp:11519).
- **Why it matters:** With mqttBaseTopic ~400 chars: the first call (:363) renders ~660 and fits (pos=660ish); the availability call at :378 wants ~500 with only ~280 left, truncates, and returns 500 -> pos ~1145; the device-info call at :388 then executes `snprintf(configJson + 1145, (size_t)(1024 - 1145), ",\"device\":{...}")` and writes ~110 bytes starting 121 bytes past the end of the 1024-byte PSRAM .bss object, over whatever static follows it. publishDiscoveryConfig is live and called ~40 times per discovery pass (:577-:616).
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: none as shipped — System_BuildConfig.h:104 sets ENABLE_MQTT 0 and the whole file is inside `#if ENABLE_WIFI && ENABLE_MQTT` (System_MQTT.cpp:14). This is latent behind a single flag flip, not board-gated.
- **Direction:** Clamp after each call, or switch this builder to a truncation-aware append helper (System_VFS.cpp:29 appendf is the correct model already in-tree). Independently, cmd_mqttbasetopic should enforce a length bound the way metaGetSet does.

### [LOW] MQTT mesh routing splices attacker-controlled target text into a positional argument list, letting a caller take over the user/pass fields (and reach the @BOND branch)
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:487`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:494, :501 (tag/device variants); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:11869 (roomcmd payload build), and the tagcmd/remote equivalents
- **What:** `handleMQTTCommand` builds the mesh-route argument string with `snprintf(argBuf, sizeof(argBuf), "%s %s %s %s", targetStr.substring(5), username, password, command)` (:487 room, :494 tag, :501 device) and hands it to `cmd_espnow_roomcmd`/`tagcmd`/`remote`. `targetStr` is the raw JSON `target` field and is not checked for spaces, so the caller controls the token boundaries of a space-delimited positional parser (`CommandArgs a(argsInput); a.arg(0..2)` at System_ESPNow.cpp:11849-11852). Those handlers then build the on-air payload as another unescaped positional format, `snprintf(roomCmdPayload, ..., "%s:%s:%s", user, pass, command)` (:11869), which the receiver splits on the first two colons (System_ESPNow.cpp:5714-5723) and routes to the bond-token branch whenever the first field is `@BOND` (:5754).
- **Why it matters:** A publisher holding ANY valid account credential (`isValidUser` at :472 does not consult role, so a guest works — and this branch returns before `submitAndExecuteSync`, so `authorizeCommand` never runs on it) can send `{"target":"room:lab @BOND deadbeef…32hex","user":…,"pass":…,"cmd":…}`. The extra spaces shift the parse so `arg(1)`="@BOND" and `arg(2)`=the 32-hex string, producing an on-air `@BOND:<token>:<cmd>` frame to every peer in the room — which, if the token is right, executes on the receiver as `kBondAdminUser`, i.e. Super Admin. The residual gate is knowledge of the bond token, so this is a framing/escaping defect rather than a standalone escalation, but it means a low-privilege MQTT client can forge the shape of a bond-channel command that the device itself then AEAD-wraps as legitimate traffic. Separately at :489/:496/:503 the handler response is built with `snprintf(respBuf, ..., "\"result\":\"%s\"", result)` with no JSON escaping, so any quote in the command result corrupts the published JSON.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_MQTT && ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: The auth half is KNOWN — recon G2 / project_security_review_backlog item 2 ("MQTT bridge bypasses authorizeCommand", docs/CMD_ROUTING_MQTT_VOICE_2026-07-24.md §2b explicitly leaves this block unchange
- **Direction:** Reject a `target` suffix containing whitespace before formatting, and give the mesh handlers a non-positional entry point (pass room/user/pass/cmd as separate parameters) instead of round-tripping through a space-delimited string. The `user:pass:cmd` wire payload should also refuse a username containing `:` or equal to `@BOND` at the sender.

### [LOW] MQTT mesh-route rebuilds a 4-field command line by unescaped %s concatenation and returns the handler's raw output inside a hand-built JSON string
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:494`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_MQTT.cpp:500-503 (tagcmd) and :507-510 (device/remote) — identical construction
- **What:** When an MQTT command carries a `target` field, the handler re-serialises four independently attacker-controlled JSON values into one space-delimited argument string and calls the ESP-NOW handler directly:

    snprintf(argBuf, sizeof(argBuf), "%s %s %s %s", targetStr.substring(5).c_str(), username, password, command);
    const char* result = cmd_espnow_roomcmd(String(argBuf));       // :494 (and :501 tagcmd, :508 remote)

`cmd_espnow_roomcmd` (System_ESPNow.cpp:11844) then parses that back with `CommandArgs`: room=arg(0), user=arg(1), pass=arg(2), command=remaining(2). A space anywhere in `target`, `user` or `pass` re-partitions all four fields. The reply is then built by hand:

    snprintf(respBuf, sizeof(respBuf), "{\"ok\":true,\"routed\":\"room\",\"result\":\"%s\"}", result);   // :496

`result` is `getDebugBuffer()`, a multi-line human listing containing `\n` and potentially `"` (peer friendly names, room names).
- **Why it matters:** Field re-partitioning: an MQTT client posts `{"user":"alice","pass":"pw","target":"room:kitchen bogus","cmd":"status"}`. Local `isValidUser("alice","pw")` passes (:472), then argBuf becomes `kitchen bogus alice pw status`, so the frame actually put on the wire is room=kitchen, user="bogus", pass="alice", command="pw status" — a command the client never asked for, dispatched to every peer in the room, with the audit/broadcast text at System_ESPNow.cpp:11892 reporting a different command than was sent.

JSON corruption: any successful room/tag dispatch returns `result` = the multi-line `  -> <peer> (<mac>)\n...Sent '<cmd>' to N device(s) in <room>` listing. Embedding those raw newlines and any quote from a peer's friendlyName inside a `"result":"..."` string produces a payload the subscriber cannot parse. Note the *local* MQTT path immediately below (:545-552) does this correctly with ArduinoJson (`respDoc["result"] = cmdResult`), so the mesh branch is the odd one out here too.

This branch is also the one that skips `authorizeCommand` entirely (all three handlers are registered `requiresAdmin=true` at System_ESPNow.cpp:15924/15925/15933) — already captured as G2 in the shared recon map, so it is listed here only as the context that makes the string-building defect reachable.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all builds with ENABLE_MQTT && ENABLE_ESPNOW; prior art: The missing authorizeCommand on this branch is recon_surfaces.md G2 / memory backlog item 2 and docs/CMD_ROUTING_MQTT_VOICE_2026-07-24.md §2b (explicitly left unchanged). The unescaped field concatena
- **Direction:** Reject `target`, `user` and `pass` values containing whitespace before dispatch (or quote each field, since `CommandArgs` de-quotes), and build the response with ArduinoJson the way the local branch at :545 already does. Fixing the authorizeCommand gap on this branch would remove the whole re-serialisation step.


### Other — 6 findings (2 critical/high)

### [HIGH] Arduino File buffers 4096 bytes and discards fflush()/fclose() errors, so every "short write means the flash is full" check in this firmware is structurally inert for sub-4 KB writes
- **Where:** `components/arduino/libraries/FS/src/vfs_api.cpp:336`
- **Also:** System_SensorLogging.cpp:174-185; System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:110,404; System_Settings.cpp:1076,3123; System_ESPNow.cpp:4441
- **What:** VFSFileImpl::write() is `return fwrite(buf, 1, size, _f);` into a fully-buffered stdio stream: the constructor calls setvbuf(_f, NULL, _IOFBF, 4096) (:250/:281), and when littlefs's stat supplies a nonzero st_blksize (esp_littlefs.c:1925 sets st_blksize = cfg.block_size) newlib allocates a block-sized _IOFBF buffer anyway. VFSFileImpl::flush() (:352-358) calls fflush()/fsync() and returns void; VFSFileImpl::close() (:294-305) calls fclose() and returns void. sdkconfig:2878 has CONFIG_LITTLEFS_FLUSH_FILE_EVERY_WRITE unset, so nothing forces a per-write commit.
- **Why it matters:** For any payload smaller than the stdio buffer, fwrite copies into RAM and returns `size` without ever reaching the VFS layer, so an ENOSPC/IO failure cannot appear in File::write()'s return value — it surfaces only at fflush/fclose, whose returns are unreachable through the Arduino API. Concretely: writeHeaderChecked() (System_SensorLogging.cpp:174-185) compares `f.write(...) == h.length()` on a ~100-byte CSV header and the caller at :1155 reports "header write failed for %s (flash full?)" — that branch can never fire on a full flash. writeIdentityFile()'s `written == 0` guard (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:110) and writePeerIdentityFile()'s (:404) are equally inert for their ~300-byte JSON, so a partial/empty ESP-NOW identity gets renamed into place and readIdentityFile()'s documented 'refuse to start, recovery via CLI' path triggers on the next boot. writeSettingsJson()'s `bytesWritten == 0` (System_Settings.cpp:1076) has the same hole. The v4 inbound-file check (System_ESPNow.cpp:4441) only works because those payloads exceed 4 KB.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: Related to docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md §4.7 (weak `bytesWritten == 0` success check), but that doc treats it as an off-by-one in the predicate. The buffering mechanism that makes the whole check 
- **Direction:** Add a single VFS-level helper that, after close(), reopens the path and compares f.size() to the expected byte count — that is the only signal the Arduino File API actually exposes. Route all critical writers (settings, identity, users, sensor headers) through it and drop the `write() == len` idiom for small payloads.

### [HIGH] The `@` / `remote:` command wrapper defeats every redaction rule, and the wrapped credential is written straight into the web-readable command feed
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:498`
- **Also:** WebServer_Server.cpp:3222 and :5079 (batch) pass the raw line to appendCommandToFeed; System_Utils.cpp:4545 DEBUG_CMD_FLOWF prints the raw wrapped line too
- **What:** redactCmdForAudit matches on a lowercase prefix (`cl.startsWith(r.prefix)`, System_Utils.cpp:1186), so `@login bob hunter2`, `remote:login bob hunter2` and `remote login bob hunter2` match nothing and return unchanged. appendCommandToFeed does `String line = String(prefix) + redactCmdForAudit(cmd)` and writes it directly into gWebMirror (HardwareOne.cpp:498-502). Meanwhile executeCommand's remote branch returns at System_Utils.cpp:4674/4678 BEFORE any logCommandExecution call, so this feed line is the only record of the command and it is unredacted.
- **Why it matters:** An admin types `@login bob hunter2` in the web CLI (WebServer_Server.cpp:3222 calls appendCommandToFeed with the raw line) or on serial (HardwareOne.cpp:2621). gWebMirror gains `[web admin@192.168.1.9] $ @login bob hunter2`. Any authenticated non-guest user GETs /api/cli/logs (handleLogs, WebServer_Server.cpp:2890 — reachable by the plain 'user' role) and reads the bonded peer's credentials. Same for `@wifiadd HomeNet S3cret` and `@useradd bob pw 0 admin`. The parallel `[REMOTE] Sent to bonded device:` broadcast at System_Utils.cpp:4673 was previously fixed to redact, which shows the intent — but the feed sink was missed because it sits upstream of executeCommand.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Strip the `remote:` / `remote ` / `@` wrapper before matching in redactCmdForAudit (re-run the table against the inner command and re-attach the prefix), so the prefix-anchored rules cannot be sidestepped by prepending one character.

### [MEDIUM] Async command callbacks run on cmd_exec_task OUTSIDE executeCommand's identity scope, i.e. as ANON — G2 file mutations emit a bogus [PERM] DENY and a wasted rescan on every rename/delete
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:796`
- **Also:** Same exposure for every `submitCommandAsync` consumer: G2_HijackCmd.cpp:73 `g2HijackInternalCallback` -> all G2 page callbacks (G2_Page_Network.cpp:940/950/964, G2_Page_Files.cpp:463), and BLE reply callbacks. The `deferredFn` fast path at HardwareOne.cpp:713-718 has the same property by design — the ESP-NOW jobs correctly compensate with explicit `SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE` / scoped `VFS::systemAuth` (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644/784/842/896, System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:145/205/598), so the pattern is only unhandled on the asyncCallback path.
- **What:** `CommandIdentityScope scope(ctx)` is constructed inside `executeCommand` (System_Utils.cpp:4465) and destroyed when it returns. `commandExecTask` calls `executeCommand` at HardwareOne.cpp:742 and only then invokes `r->asyncCallback(...)` at :796 — by which point cmd_exec_task's TLS slot has been restored to its ambient value, which is the default-constructed ANON `TaskIdentity` (System_AuthIdentity.cpp:42-50, 74-80): the task never calls `initAuthIdentityForCurrentTask()` and nothing else writes the slot. So every `ExecAsyncCallback` body runs unauthenticated.
- **Why it matters:** Concrete instance: `onFilesMutateDone` (G2_Page_Files.cpp:463) calls `fm->forceRescan()` at :481 after a successful `filerename`/`filedelete confirm`. `forceRescan` -> `FileManager::loadDirectory` reads `currentAuthContext()` (System_FileManager.cpp:425) = ANON, so `VFS::openGuarded` fails the `canRead` check (System_VFS.cpp:813), calls `logFsAccessDeny` (:814) writing a spurious [PERM] DENY read line into the audit trail, and `loadDirectory` bails at System_FileManager.cpp:428-432 leaving `loadedAtGen_ = 0` and the cache stale. The user sees the right thing only because `g2ShowFilesMenu()` at G2_Page_Files.cpp:483 installs `G2HijackCtxGuard` and re-scans. The in-code rationale at G2_Page_Files.cpp:459-462 ("Success forces a directory re-scan") is therefore wrong, and any future async callback that touches guarded FS will silently fail closed with no diagnostic beyond a misleading permission-denied audit entry.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: builds with ENABLE_BLUETOOTH && ENABLE_G2_GLASSES (FeatherS3 default)
- **Direction:** Either install the request's identity around the callback in `commandExecTask` (a `CommandIdentityScope` around the `r->asyncCallback(...)` call at HardwareOne.cpp:796, using `r->ctx.auth`), or make each callback that touches the FS install its own guard first — `onFilesMutateDone` should open with `G2HijackCtxGuard` and drop the redundant `forceRescan` since `g2ShowFilesMenu` already re-scans.

### [MEDIUM] UltraSaver's 40 MHz idle floor drops APB below the radio's 80 MHz requirement on the secondary ESP32 target
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2335`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Power.cpp:64-70 (getPowerModeIdleCpuFreq), :28 (the 40 MHz table entry)
- **What:** The idle power-save path calls setCpuFrequencyDrained(getPowerModeIdleCpuFreq(gSettings.powerMode)); for POWER_MODE_ULTRASAVER that resolves to 40 MHz (System_Power.cpp:28, 64-70). Arduino's calculateApb (components/arduino/cores/esp32/esp32-hal-cpu.c:181-190) returns the fixed APB_CLK_FREQ for ESP32-S3 but returns source_freq_mhz/div for CONFIG_IDF_TARGET_ESP32 when the CPU is below 80 MHz. The power-mode table is not board-gated, so the same 40 MHz idle floor is applied on the classic-ESP32 board variants.
- **Why it matters:** On a "Generic ESP32" or "Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2" build (System_BuildConfig.h:790, 957 — sdkconfig.defaults.esp32 exists, so these are real targets), five minutes of idle in UltraSaver drops the APB clock to 40 MHz while WiFi, ESP-NOW and BLE are all still up. The comment immediately above the call (2317-2326) itself names 80 MHz "the 80 MHz Wi-Fi floor" and then sinks below it, justifying the exception purely on UI responsiveness ("40 MHz makes the interactive UI unusably laggy") — the radio axis was never considered. On ESP32-S3 (FeatherS3 primary, XIAO) APB stays pinned at 80 MHz, so this is invisible on the boards that get tested.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: ESP32 classic only (Generic ESP32, Adafruit Feather ESP32 V2). ESP32-S3 boards are unaffected — APB is fixed.
- **Direction:** Board-gate the sub-80 idle floor: on targets with a dynamic APB, clamp getPowerModeIdleCpuFreq to POWER_INTERACTIVE_FLOOR_MHZ whenever any radio is up (wifiRadioOn() || BLE initialised), so UltraSaver's 40 MHz only applies with the radios down.

### [MEDIUM] The main loop runs for the process lifetime under a sticky SYSTEM identity, and the in-tree comment documenting OLED identity says the exact opposite
- **Where:** `main/hardwareone-idf.cpp:45`
- **Also:** `writeSettingsJson()` reached from `wizardMode_onExit` via `cliModeTick` also executes on this task; it happens to use explicit `VFS::systemAuth` (System_Settings.cpp:1018/1020/1061) so it does not depend on the sticky guard — which is what makes the discrepancy easy to miss.
- **What:** `ExecIdentityGuard mainTaskIdentity(systemIdentity("main"));` is constructed in `app_main` before `setup()` and never destructs (`while(true)` at :51-54). `CONFIG_AUTOSTART_ARDUINO is not set` (sdkconfig:603), so the Arduino core's own `app_main`/`loopTask` (components/arduino/cores/esp32/main.cpp:89, :113) are compiled out and this really is the task that runs `hardwareone_setup()`/`hardwareone_loop()`. Every main-loop consumer of `currentAuthContext()` therefore resolves to `FsRole::SYSTEM` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551), which `permsForRole` maps to the rule's `systemPerms` (:1593) — typically PERM_ALL over `/system/*`. Meanwhile OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:711-721 states, as the rationale for its own guard: "The OLED render task runs on the main loop, which has NO sticky identity install — its TLS slot defaults to ANON... The earlier comment claiming 'sticky SYSTEM install in app_main' was stale — that pattern was removed when identity moved to per-task TLS." The code it describes is still there.
- **Why it matters:** Fail-OPEN default on the physically-accessible surface, plus a documented invariant that guarantees future regressions. Today the file browser is the only OLED surface that deliberately downgrades to the session user (OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:732); everything else reached from `oledUpdate()`, `cliModeTick()` and `schedulerTickMinute()` inherits SYSTEM. Concretely, `System_Maps.cpp` passes `currentAuthContext()` straight into guarded VFS at :357, :363, :985, :1057, :1061, :1077, :2303-2319 and :2407-2408 — so the identical map/waypoint file open is SYSTEM when driven from the OLED map mode (main loop), the paired G2 user when driven from `g2MapPageWorker` (G2_Glasses.cpp:17734, guarded), and ANON from an unguarded web handler. Because `displayRequireAuth` off yields the `AuthBypass` sentinel (OLED_Utils.cpp:2980) which `resolveRole` rates as plain USER, any new OLED code that follows the FileBrowser comment will be written expecting ANON/USER and will actually get SYSTEM.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED
- **Direction:** Decide the intent and make code and comment agree. Either drop the `app_main` guard so the main loop really is ANON (and add explicit guards or `VFS::systemAuth` at the handful of main-loop FS sites that need privilege), or keep it and fix the OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:711-721 comment plus audit every main-loop `currentAuthContext()` consumer for the fail-open direction. Do not leave the two in contradiction.

### [LOW] The login line is parsed by three different tokenizers with different quoting and length rules, so the same credential succeeds on one surface and fails on another
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/HardwareOne.cpp:2553`
- **Also:** Bluetooth.cpp:715-740 (second parser, char u[64]/p[128], then re-serialises into `login %s %s bluetooth` for a third parse); System_ESPNow.cpp:5828-5834 (char username[32]/password[32], silent truncation); canonical parser cmd_login System_Utils.cpp:5273 via the quote-aware CommandArgs (System_Command.h:63)
- **What:** Four surfaces authenticate with a username and password and three of them tokenize the line themselves. (a) The serial gate (HardwareOne.cpp:2553-2560) splits on the first space and takes the entire remainder as the password, quotes included. (b) BLE (Bluetooth.cpp:718-734) splits on the first space into fixed buffers char u[64] / char p[128], then snprintf's `login %s %s bluetooth` and hands it to cmd_login, which re-tokenizes with the quote-aware CommandArgs. (c) v4_handle_cmd (System_ESPNow.cpp:5828-5834) splits on colons into char username[32] / char password[32] and silently truncates anything longer. (d) cmd_login itself uses CommandArgs, which strips double quotes and supports embedded spaces. The credential policy that all four are supposed to serve is username 1..64 and password 6..64 (System_User.h:162-164, enforced by isValidPublicUsername/isValidPublicPassword).
- **Why it matters:** A password containing a space is legal (isValidPublicPassword only checks length). Typed bare — `login alice my pass` — it works on the serial console and nowhere else: on BLE and via cmd_login the third token becomes the transport argument and the reply is "Error: Invalid transport". Typed quoted — `login alice "my pass"` — it works everywhere except serial, where the literal quote characters become part of the password and authentication fails. Length is a second axis: a policy-legal 64-character username cannot log in over BLE (u[64] caps at 63), and a policy-legal 32..64-character password or username can never authenticate an ESP-NOW remote command at all (username[32]/password[32] truncate to 31 and the comparison is against the untruncated hash) — the user sees only "Auth failed" with no indication that the credential was mangled in transit. Failures are all fail-closed, so this is a correctness and supportability defect rather than a bypass.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (per-surface: serial always; BLE ENABLE_BLUETOOTH; ESP-NOW ENABLE_ESPNOW)
- **Direction:** Make the serial and BLE gates build a Command line and let cmd_login/CommandArgs do the single tokenization (the BLE path already re-serializes, so it is halfway there). Size the ESP-NOW buffers to kPublicUsernameMaxLen+1 / kPublicPasswordMaxLen+1 and reject rather than truncate on overflow.


### Automation / autostart — 1 findings (1 critical/high)

### [HIGH] Automation `createdBy` is trusted verbatim from automations.json, and the value "system" is a total authorizeCommand bypass
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/System_Automation.cpp:99`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/System_Automation.cpp:884, :1810, :4012 (the three createdBy reads); components/hardwareone/System_Utils.cpp:4373 (the bypass); components/hardwareone/System_Filesystem.cpp:1544 (the FsRole::SYSTEM twin); components/hardwareone/WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:577 (arbitrary /system write via unguarded writeTextAtomic)
- **What:** `authorizeCommand`'s very first rule is an unconditional bypass:

    if (ctx.transport == SOURCE_INTERNAL && ctx.user == "system") return true;   // System_Utils.cpp:4373

`queueAutomationSubCommand` unconditionally sets `uc.ctx.auth.transport = SOURCE_INTERNAL` (System_Automation.cpp:97) and `uc.ctx.auth.user = owner` (:99), where `owner` is the `createdBy` string read out of automations.json by `extractJsonString(obj, "\"createdBy\"", buf, 64)` at :884 (scheduler), :1810 (`automation run`) and :4012 (boot/event dispatch). Nothing between the file read and the bypass test checks that the value is not the reserved sentinel `"system"`.

The same string also drives `resolveRole` in the VFS layer (System_Filesystem.cpp:1544): `if (ctx.transport == SOURCE_INTERNAL && ctx.user == "system") return FsRole::SYSTEM` — the unrestricted filesystem role.

The write path is protected only indirectly. `cmd_automation_add` stamps `createdBy = currentExecUser()` (System_Automation.cpp:1397) and `isValidPublicUsername` blocks "system" at account creation (System_User.cpp:803), so no *account* can be named system. But `/system/automations.json` is a plain file, and `permsForRole` grants `PERM_ALL` to `FsRole::SUPER` while explicitly ignoring the rule table (System_Filesystem.cpp:1587-1592) — the path rule at System_Filesystem.cpp:1348 (`admin = PERM_READ`) does not constrain super at all.
- **Why it matters:** Any context that resolves to `FsRole::SUPER` can write `/system/automations.json` directly (`filewrite`, `/api/files/write`, `/api/files/upload`) with `"createdBy": "system"`. Every command in that automation then executes with the INTERNAL bypass: `commandRequiresSuperAdmin` and `commandRequiresAdmin` are never consulted, and its filesystem access is `FsRole::SYSTEM` (which the rule table grants `PERM_ALL` on `/system/users/`, `/system/certs/`, settings.json — see System_Filesystem.cpp:1343-1367).

This is a privilege-*persistence* and audit-evasion hole rather than a cross-tier escalation, and that is what makes it worth fixing: the super tiers that can reach it are not all permanent.
  * `kBondAdminUser` is super only *for the life of a bond session* (System_User.cpp:365) and is `FsRole::SUPER` on every transport including over the air, by explicit design (System_Filesystem.cpp:1550-1560). A bonded peer that plants `createdBy: "system"` leaves behind a super-privileged execution channel that survives the bond session ending, survives `bondmode off`, and survives reboot.
  * `isSuperAdminUser`'s no-explicit-super fallback (System_User.cpp:418) makes the first users.json entry super. A later `userdemote` of that account does not revoke a planted "system" automation.
  * The unauthenticated migration restore writes arbitrary absolute paths with `writeTextAtomic` (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:577) — which goes through `VFS::open`, NOT `openGuarded`, so no permission check applies at all — and `/system/automations.json` is a legal bundle key (only `path[0] != '/'` is rejected, :521).

In all three cases the resulting identity is invisible to the role model: `automation list` shows `createdBy: system`, but there is no such account, `userdelete system` cannot remove it, and the audit line reads `system@internal`, indistinguishable from a genuine boot-autostart command.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — independently reported by 2 finders; board: all builds with ENABLE_AUTOMATION
- **Direction:** Reject the sentinel where the identity is constructed, not where it is consumed: in `queueAutomationSubCommand`, if `owner` equals "system" (or is empty), refuse to stamp SOURCE_INTERNAL/"system" and either drop the sub-command with a durable log line or downgrade it to an unprivileged identity. The same guard belongs on `AuthContext` construction generally — "system" should only ever be produced by `VFS::systemAuth` and `runUnifiedSystemCommand`, never by copying a string out of a file.


### OLED UI — 8 findings (0 critical/high)

### [MEDIUM] OLED map menu: Maps→Next/Previous Map is outside the guest-mutate set and calls MapCore::loadMapFile, which unloads the live map before its permission check
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1349`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:1387, :1417 (MapCore::loadMapFile call sites); components/hardwareone/System_Maps.cpp:341-361 (unloadMap runs before the existsGuarded check), :795 (unloadMap frees cachePool/slots/tileDir/names and sets valid=false)
- **What:** `executeSubmenuAction()` computes `guestMutate = (submenuType == MENU_CAT_WAYPOINTS || MENU_CAT_TRACKS)` plus `MENU_CAT_GPS`, and only then calls `oledGuestBlocksMutate()`. `MENU_CAT_MAPS` is not in that set, yet its actions 1 and 2 ("Next Map"/"Previous Map") call `MapCore::loadMapFile(path)` directly — not via the `mapload` command, which authorizeCommand would have blocked for a guest. `loadMapFile` takes the map lock, calls `unloadMap()` unconditionally, calls `pollPause()`, and only *then* checks `VFS::existsGuarded(path, currentAuthContext())`. OLED_Mode_Map.cpp installs no `CommandIdentityScope`/`ExecIdentityGuard` (unlike OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:63), so `currentAuthContext()` on the display task is the default ANON identity and `resolveRole` returns `FsRole::ANON` → `permsForRole` returns 0 → the read is denied.
- **Why it matters:** A view-only guest at the OLED opens the Map (OLED_GPS_MAP is guest-allowed), presses the menu, picks Maps → Next Map. `unloadMap()` executes first: it frees the PSRAM tile cache pool, slots, tile directory and name table, sets `_currentMap.valid = false`, and calls `LocationContextManager::invalidate()`. The subsequent load then fails the ANON permission check and returns false. Net effect: the guest destroys the device's loaded map (which is global state shared with the web /maps page, the G2 map lens and every `mapinfo` consumer) and cannot restore it. The unload-before-check ordering also means this is a latent bug for every role using that menu item, not just guests — the map is always dropped and never reloaded from this path.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY + ENABLE_MAPS; prior art: Prior guest audit noted "Map OLED mutate-allowlist can drift" as a LOW; it has now drifted — MENU_CAT_MAPS was never added.
- **Direction:** Add `MENU_CAT_MAPS` to the `guestMutate` set. Separately, move `unloadMap()` in `MapCore::loadMapFile` to after the `existsGuarded`/header-validation checks so a failed load is non-destructive, and consider whether the OLED map path needs an identity scope like the file browser has.

### [MEDIUM] Usernames are interpolated unquoted into `userpromote`/`userdemote` command lines, and three of the six user-creation paths never call isValidPublicUsername
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:287`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/G2_Page_Users.cpp:295-297 (same unquoted build); components/hardwareone/System_ESPNow.cpp:3658 (USER_SYNC, no isValidPublicUsername); components/hardwareone/System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:261 and :891 (first-time setup, no isValidPublicUsername, writes role=superadmin)
- **What:** Both on-device user managers build the role-change command by string concatenation with the target username left unquoted, while every neighbouring command in the same files IS quoted:

    // OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:287
    String cmd = String(gUmRoleSel > cur ? "userpromote " : "userdemote ") + u.name + " " + kUmRoles[gUmRoleSel];
    // G2_Page_Users.cpp:295-297
    snprintf(line, sizeof(line), "%s %s %s", targetRank > curRank ? "userpromote" : "userdemote", r.name, kRoleNames[targetRank]);

(The delete paths in the same files are correct: `userdelete "<name>" confirm` at OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:104 and G2_Page_Users.cpp:307, and useradd quotes both operands at :290 / :264.)

`cmd_user_promote` (System_User.cpp:1881-1882) reads `username = a.arg(0)` and `role = a.arg(1)`. A space in the name therefore shifts the role into arg(0)'s tail and promotes arg(1) to the role slot.

The safety of this depends on usernames never containing a space. Only three of six write paths enforce that. `isValidPublicUsername` (System_User.cpp:786, which rejects spaces at :793-798 and blocks the reserved names "system"/"AuthBypass"/bond-admin at :802-808) has exactly three call sites: `adminCreateUser` (:834), the `userrequest` command (:2712) and web self-registration (WebServer_Server.cpp:3817). The paths that skip it are:
  * ESP-NOW USER_SYNC — `newUser["username"] = targetUser` straight from the peer's JSON (System_ESPNow.cpp:3658), with no validation anywhere in the handler.
  * First-time setup, both variants — `admin["username"] = username` (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:261) and `admin["username"] = u` (:891); the only processing on `u` is `u.trim()` and a non-empty check (:337, :771-772). This account is written `role = "superadmin"`.
- **Why it matters:** Failure scenario: a users.json entry named `bob superadmin` exists (planted via USER_SYNC from a peer, or chosen at first-time setup). An operator opens Config > Users on the OLED, selects that row, and picks the role `user`. The dispatched line is:

    userdemote bob superadmin user

`CommandArgs` yields arg0="bob", arg1="superadmin", arg2="user" (ignored). `cmd_user_demote` reads role=arg(1)="superadmin" — which is not in its allowlist `{admin,user,guest}` (System_User.cpp:1920), so it errors out. On the promote side the allowlist DOES include "superadmin" (:1886), so `userpromote bob superadmin user` passes validation and attempts to grant super-admin when the operator selected `user`.

The blast radius is bounded — `userMutationAllowed` (System_User.cpp:1557, called at :1894) still refuses to grant a rank above the caller's own, so a plain admin cannot mint a super this way. What it does produce reliably is a role-change that does not match what the operator tapped, on a screen whose whole purpose is role administration, with the audit line recording the *shifted* arguments rather than the intent.

The validation gap is independently significant: it is the reason a space-bearing (or reserved-name) account can exist at all, and it contradicts the assertion in docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:132 that reserved usernames are "blocked at creation".
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: OLED site: builds with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY **and** ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM (the extern+call are inside `#if ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM` at OLED_Utils.cpp:3355 and :3381, so the screen is unreachable on the ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM=0 XIAO camera build). G2 site: builds with ENABLE_G2_GLASSES.; prior art: Partially overlaps the 2026-07-20 memory backlog item 1 ("AuthBypass can be a real account"), which noted the FTS and USER_SYNC validation gaps. The unquoted userpromote/userdemote interpolation that 
- **Direction:** Quote the username at both call sites (`userpromote "<name>" <role>`) — `CommandArgs` already handles quoted tokens verbatim and the sibling delete/add lines in the same files show the intended pattern. Separately, route USER_SYNC and both first-time-setup writers through `isValidPublicUsername` so the no-space / no-reserved-name invariant the rest of the code relies on actually holds.

### [LOW] Plaintext passwords are never zeroed after verification, including a static OLED buffer that survives a failed login
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:201`
- **Also:** System_Utils.cpp:5213 (`String password = a.arg(1)` in cmd_login, never cleared); Bluetooth.cpp:735-737 and :755-756 (`char p[128]` and `char loginCmd[256]` on the BTC_TASK stack, never cleared); System_ESPNow.cpp:5830-5836 (`char password[32]` on espnow_task, never cleared); System_User.cpp:703-738 (hashUserPassword clears its salt copy but never the caller's `password`); OLED_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:138-150 (`char originalText[33]` holding the unmasked password, left on the stack)
- **What:** `static String passwordBuffer` (OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:37) is cleared with secureClearString only on the success path (:191-192) and on B-button exit (:246-247). The failure branch at :201-206 sets an error message and returns without clearing, so the plaintext stays in the static String's heap buffer until the user retries, backs out, or reboots. The same non-clearing pattern holds for every other plaintext holder listed above.
- **Why it matters:** secureClearString exists (System_Utils.cpp:146) and is used correctly in OLED_Mode_ChangePassword and OLED_Mode_SetPattern, which shows the intent — but the login path itself is the one that misses it, and the failure branch is exactly the state a device is likely to be left in. On a device where PSRAM and internal RAM are both readable (flash encryption OFF), a resident plaintext credential in a static is a durable exposure rather than a transient one.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: OLED site needs ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY; the others are all boards
- **Direction:** Clear passwordBuffer in the failure branch of OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:201-206. For the CLI/BLE/ESP-NOW sites, wrap the plaintext in a small scope-guard type that volatile-zeroes on destruction, or add explicit clears immediately after isValidUser returns. Note a plain memset before a free/return is an optimization target; secureClearString's volatile loop is the right primitive.

### [LOW] initAsyncMapRenderer latches on a partial allocation: leaks up to 9 KB PSRAM and permanently disables the map renderer with no retry
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:932`
- **Also:** The correct shape is two functions away in the same subsystem: System_Maps.cpp:700-707 frees `pool` when the slot array fails, and OLED_Utils.cpp:2437-2443 hands back whichever half succeeded before publishing.
- **What:** The function allocates sMapBufA (1024 B), sMapBufB (1024 B) and sMapShadeBuf (8192 B) unconditionally (:929-931) and then checks all three at once (:932). On failure it emits one DEBUG_MAPSF line and returns without freeing whichever succeeded and without nulling sMapBufA. The very first line of the function is `if (sMapBufA) return;` (:926).
- **Why it matters:** If sMapBufA succeeds and sMapBufB or sMapShadeBuf fails (plausible: sMapShadeBuf is the 8 KB one and is allocated last), then: (a) sMapBufA and possibly sMapShadeBuf are leaked for the rest of the boot; (b) sRenderSemaphore is never created and sMapFrontBuf/sMapBackBuf stay null; (c) every subsequent call short-circuits at :926, so the async renderer can never recover even after PSRAM frees up. Consumers do guard correctly (`if (mapDirty && sRenderSemaphore)` at :1161, `if (sFrontBufReady)` at :1199) so there is no crash — the map page just silently never renders, with only a debug-flag-gated line to explain it.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all boards with the OLED map mode
- **Direction:** Free the partial allocations and null all three statics on the failure path so a later entry retries, and promote the failure from DEBUG_MAPSF to a WARN so a dead map page is diagnosable.

### [LOW] OLED unified menu reports "Done" for every command including failures
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_UnifiedMenu.cpp:386`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_Mode_Automations.cpp:922 (autoToggleSelected uses fire-and-forget executeOLEDCommand and always toasts "Disabled"/"Enabled")
- **What:** executeMenuItem sets `gPendingCommandStatus` from `success ? "Done" : "Failed"` using executeOLEDCommandWithResult's bool, which is executeCommand's always-true bool. Unlike its siblings — OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:187, OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:92, OLED_Mode_Automations.cpp:256 and :905 all defensively add `|| strncmp(out,"Error",5) == 0` — this site trusts the bool alone.
- **Why it matters:** Any menu-driven command that fails (permission denied on a non-admin OLED session, hardware not present, filesystem full) shows "Done" on the status line while the broadcast of `out` on the next line reads "Error: ...". The remote branch at :378 is worse: it sets "Sent" and arms gPendingRemoteCommand for a command that may have failed locally before ever reaching the radio.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all (ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY)
- **Direction:** Add the same `|| strncmp(out,"Error",5)==0` check the three sibling OLED call sites already use, or better, fix executeCommand's return so none of them need it.

### [LOW] gSettings.localDisplayRequireAuth is registered twice under two different JSON keys and sections, with two independent CLI commands; the OLED copy silently wins the load
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Settings.cpp:17`
- **What:** The same field is claimed by two SettingEntry rows: system.output.auth.displayRequireAuth with cmdKey "displayrequireauth" (System_Settings.cpp:1966) and hardware.sensors.oled.display.oledRequireAuth with cmdKey "oledrequireauth" (OLED_Settings.cpp:17). This was the only duplicate valuePtr in the whole 274-row registry (verified by a mechanical scan of every SettingEntry across the tree).
- **Why it matters:** writeRegisteredSettings emits both keys from the same field, so a firmware-written file always has them equal - but readRegisteredSettings applies modules in registration order and the last write wins. oledSettingsModule is registered at System_Settings.cpp:2158, after outputSettingsModule at :2128, so hardware.sensors.oled.display.oledRequireAuth is authoritative on load. Any file where the two disagree (hand edit, a restore bundle, an ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY=0 build that only maintained the output-module copy and later got flashed with OLED on) resolves to the OLED value with no warning. Secondary effects: notifySettingChanged (System_Settings.cpp:2512-2536) reverse-looks-up by valuePtr and returns the FIRST match, so a change made via `oledrequireauth` is reported under the output module's "Display Require Auth" label; and the web Settings page renders the same underlying toggle in two sections, where flipping one leaves the other visually stale until reload.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all with ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY
- **Direction:** Keep one row as the owner and drop the other (or point it at a distinct field). If both surfaces need to expose it, expose the same cmdKey from both UIs rather than duplicating the registry row.

### [LOW] Cluster of absolute `millis() > deadline` comparisons that invert across the 49.7-day rollover — worst cases leave UI overlays and the voice listen window stuck for ~49.7 days
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_UI.cpp:175`
- **Also:** see the enumerated list in `what`
- **What:** Roughly two dozen sites compare millis() directly against a stored `millis() + N` deadline instead of using the signed-difference idiom. A deadline armed shortly before the wrap becomes a very large number while millis() restarts near 0, so the test flips to the wrong answer for the whole following wrap period. Confirmed instances: OLED_UI.cpp:175 (toast never expires); System_ESPSR.cpp:2280 (voice command-listen window never times out — commandTimeoutMs armed at :2240/:2276/:2456/:2486) and :1283 (audio snip session); System_ESPNow.cpp:3362/:3421/:8325/:12323 (gTopoRequestTimeout, armed at :8306/:12432); OLED_Mode_Auth.cpp:114/:208/:237/:280/:328; OLED_Mode_ChangePassword.cpp:160; OLED_SettingsEditor.cpp:238/:1173; G2_Pet.cpp:470/:490; G2_Glasses.cpp:7998/:8755/:9075 (while-loops collapse to zero wait instead); System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712 (remote sensor lease never expires); System_ESPNow.cpp:3018 (session-prewarm cooldown) and :2961 (oldest-slot eviction by absolute value).
- **Why it matters:** Directional analysis of each: the `millis() < deadline` guards (OLED_Mode_Auth, OLED_SettingsEditor, G2_Pet, G2 while-loops) fail early — an overlay is skipped or a scan wait collapses to zero, recoverable. The `millis() > deadline` timeout tests (OLED_UI.cpp:175, System_ESPSR.cpp:2280/:1283, System_ESPNow_Sensors.cpp:712) fail the dangerous way: after the wrap the condition is false for the full wrap period, so the toast never clears, the voice pipeline stays in listeningForCommand indefinitely, and a remote ESP-NOW sensor lease is never revoked. Trigger window is narrow (the deadline must be armed in the seconds before the wrap) but the stuck state then persists ~49.7 days.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: all; the OLED sites are ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY only, the ESP-SR sites are ENABLE_ESPSR only, the ESP-NOW sites are ENABLE_ESPNOW only
- **Direction:** This repo already has the correct idiom in four places (System_ESPNow.cpp:4641/:13923, System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:923, WebServer_Server.cpp:830, OLED_Mode_UserManager.cpp:140). Consider a tiny shared helper (e.g. `Clock::deadlinePassed(uint32_t)` next to the existing ageMs/ageSeconds) and convert these sites to it, rather than fixing them individually.

### [LOW] shouldBlockForDisplayAuth() returns false for the entire OLED boot window, disabling the display auth gate for ~6+ seconds every boot
- **Where:** `components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:2901`
- **Also:** components/hardwareone/OLED_Utils.cpp:28,3152,4675,4743,4784,4789; components/hardwareone/System_User.cpp:184
- **What:** shouldBlockForDisplayAuth() is `gSettings.localDisplayRequireAuth && !gLocalDisplayAuthed && !oledBootModeActive` (OLED_Utils.cpp:2901, duplicated verbatim at :28). oledBootModeActive is set true when the panel comes up (:4675) and cleared only when BOOT_PHASE_LOGO completes (:4743) — that is the animation phase until bootProgressPercent hits 100 plus 1 s, then a fixed 5 s LOGO_DURATION. Throughout that window requestOLEDMode's auth redirect (:3152-3160) is skipped, and tgRequireAuth's SOURCE_LOCAL_DISPLAY branch (System_User.cpp:184) returns true with ctx.user = gLocalDisplayUser (empty at boot).
- **Why it matters:** For the whole boot window the display gate is off by construction. The modes actually reachable during it are OLED_ANIMATION and OLED_LOGO, which have no action handlers, and the only writer of userOverrodeBootMode is cmd_oledmode (:4105) — itself already authorized under the caller's identity — so I could not construct a concrete escalation. The residual risk is structural: any future boot-phase mode with an input handler, or any code path that lengthens the animation phase (bootProgressPercent stalls below 100 if a slow subsystem never reports), silently extends an unauthenticated display window. Note tgRequireAuth returning true with an EMPTY ctx.user is separately relied upon by authorizeCommand gate 2 to deny — but the OLED command builder substitutes "AuthBypass" (:2980) before that gate ever sees it.
- **Confidence:** PLAUSIBLE — board: all (ENABLE_OLED_DISPLAY)
- **Direction:** Rather than suppressing the gate, let the boot sequence request its modes through a path that does not consult it (an internal setOLEDMode, which already exists at :3139) and drop oledBootModeActive from the predicate. That keeps the gate honest for anything else that runs during boot, and removes the duplicated predicate at :28 and :2901 drifting apart.


### Docs / meta — 2 findings (0 critical/high)

### [MEDIUM] The mechanics doc's ALWAYSINTERNAL "secrets" argument is exactly backwards — it has been blocking the tree's highest-leverage fragmentation fix on a false premise
- **Where:** `docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md:89`
- **Also:** The genuinely-real half of the same paragraph is unaffected and still stands: gSessions is explicitly ps_alloc(PreferPSRAM) at System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:26, so ESP-NOW AEAD session keys are on the PSRAM heap by explicit choice, not by threshold. Also unaffected: heap_caps_malloc_default's final fallback `heap_caps_malloc_base(size, MALLOC_CAP_DEFAULT)` (heap_caps.c:124) can place a small allocation in PSRAM when internal is exhausted — that hazard exists today and lowering the threshold makes it less likely, not more.
- **What:** §4 states: "Lowering ALWAYSINTERNAL silently relocates SHORT allocations to PSRAM, and short allocations are exactly what key material, tokens, and typed passwords look like… Today those are protected by accident — they're small, so ALWAYSINTERNAL keeps them internal." The actual IDF rule is the opposite. esp-idf/components/heap/heap_caps.c:117-121 (heap_caps_malloc_default) and :150-153 (heap_caps_realloc_default) both read `if (size <= (size_t)malloc_alwaysinternal_limit) → MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL; else → MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM`. The Kconfig help says the same: allocations *smaller* than the threshold prefer internal, *larger* ones go external. Lowering 16384 → 4096 moves the 4-16 KB band to PSRAM and leaves everything under 4096 internal — a 32-byte X25519 secret, a 16-32 byte session key, and a typed-password String stay internal at any threshold above ~64 bytes. They become *more* protected, not less, because internal free space grows.
- **Why it matters:** This paragraph is the stated reason the single highest-ranked structural fix in the whole audit tree (§5 item 1: "LOWER CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL (16384 → 4096)… Nothing else has this leverage-to-effort ratio") has not been attempted. It has already propagated into scratchpad/recon_prior.md §2 as a "🔒 Unverified security claim" and into the project memory files, so any future agent re-deriving the decision inherits the error. Acting on the correct semantics is what actually attacks the documented "largest block 9 KB / free 39 KB" state, because it is exactly the 4-16 KB transients (JSON pools, command output buffers, HTML/JSON String bodies, the 12,304 B readTextLimited reserve above) that currently land in and stir the internal free list.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — prior art: KNOWN premise — see docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md §4 and scratchpad/recon_prior.md §2. Reported because the prior doc was wrong.
- **Direction:** Correct §4 and the recon_prior.md entry, then re-rank: the remaining real prerequisites for the change are the DMA-fed-plain-malloc audit (see clean list — I found no first-party DMA-fed heap buffer) and moving gSessions off the PSRAM heap, not a short-allocation relocation risk that does not exist.

### [NOTE] docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md declares the MQTT backlog item resolved and mislabels/misdescribes the ESP-NOW filesystem finding — a maintainer trusting it would close two live bugs
- **Where:** `docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:132`
- **Also:** docs/PRE_1_0_HARDENING_AUDIT.md:134 (§4.1 heading and body); docs/OVERNIGHT_AUDIT_BACKLOG.md:23 and :122 inherit the "FTS AuthBypass" mislabel.
- **What:** Three separable errors. (1) §4 says "**The MQTT backlog item is resolved** — it authenticates per-message via isValidUser and routes through authorizeCommand (System_MQTT.cpp:470, :529)"; :470 is authentication only and :529 is in the local branch that the mesh branch at :486-517 returns before reaching. (2) §4.1 is titled "the FTS AuthBypass", but FTS in this repo means First-Time Setup (System_FeatureRegistry.cpp:662, System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:902) and the actual backlog item by that name is the users.json/username one — two unrelated defects now share a name. (3) §4.1 claims FsRole::SYSTEM is "exempt from the hideAdminPaths rules that constrain even a logged-in admin"; `hideAdminPaths` is only ever passed as `!admin` (System_Filesystem.cpp:556, WebServer_Server.cpp:3923) so it never constrains an admin, and the FS_LIST reply path does not call `buildFilesListing` at all.
- **Why it matters:** The MQTT sentence is the newest written statement about that backlog item and it is the opposite of the code. Anyone triaging the backlog against this doc closes a real privilege-escalation path. The shared name means "FTS AuthBypass fixed" is ambiguous between two different fixes in two different files. The hideAdminPaths sentence overstates the SYSTEM-vs-ADMIN delta and hides the actual one, which is the sensitive-extension exemption plus needing no account at all.
- **Confidence:** CONFIRMED — board: n/a (documentation); prior art: This IS the prior doc.
- **Direction:** Amend §4 to "local path resolved; mesh-routing branch (System_MQTT.cpp:486-517) still bypasses", retitle §4.1 to something like "ESP-NOW remote filesystem served as SYSTEM" with a note that it is NOT the FTS AuthBypass backlog item, and correct the hideAdminPaths sentence to name the sensitive-extension exemption instead.


---

## Verified clean (security-relevant)

Checked and found correct — as requested, this is reported alongside the defects.

- **BLE init handles memory failure at both levels.** `Bluetooth.cpp:883-946`: `checkMemoryAvailable("bluetooth")` gates on ~60 KB up front with a message that even names the known ~10 KB/cycle stop-start leak, the state struct alloc is null-checked, and `BLEDevice::getInitialized()` is re-checked after `init()` with the state struct freed on failure.
- **ESP-NOW init translates internal-DRAM exhaustion into a user-legible cause.** `System_ESPNow.cpp:9768-9800` switches on the `esp_err_t` and maps `ESP_ERR_NO_MEM` to "out of memory — free up DRAM and retry", printing free heap before *and* after plus the WiFi mode, and recording it via `logSystemEvent`. This is the in-tree model for how a P6 failure should surface.
- **Every first-party `MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL` allocation null-checks and cleans up on failure — all eight sites, read individually.** `System_LLM.cpp:265-289` (stack+TCB pair; frees the partial pair and marks retry-later, with a comment explaining the timing choice), `OLED_Utils.cpp:2426-2443` (two-half ring; frees whichever half succeeded, publishes the gate pointer last so readers never see a partial ring), `System_ESPNow.cpp:9810-9822` (RX ring; *fatal* by design — `esp_now_deinit()` and refuse to start rather than come up deaf), `:9860-9871` (broadcast tracker; non-fatal, readers treat null as "no slots"), `System_Automation.cpp:519-531` and `:3717`, `System_LLM_Model.cpp:709-714`, `G2_Page_TestSuite.cpp:1100-1111`. No unchecked internal allocation found.
- **Hard DRAM budget extracted from the checked-in link map** (`build/hardwareone-idf.map`, 2026-07-27, FeatherS3) — the number the prior audits estimated but never measured, since `HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT_MECHANICS.md` explicitly ran no build: `dram0_0_seg` 341,760 B total, minus `.dram0.dummy` 95,232 (IRAM shadow) + `.dram0.data` 25,736 + `.dram0.bss` 54,304 = 175,272 B consumed at link, leaving ~166 KB of that segment for the heap before any runtime allocation. Application (`libhardwareone.a`) share of `.dram0` is 45,472 B across 1,394 symbols. Largest application symbols: `gEventRing` 7,872 · `gRingTxQ` 2,224 · `gOledEspNowState` 2,096 · `gSettings` 1,624 · eight `G2_Health` series 1,172 each · `gCommandModules` 756 · `gSidStats` 576 · `gAnchors` 512 · `gSc` 512.
- **`ESP.getFreeHeap()` semantics verified — every feature-start memory gate is internal-DRAM-correct.** `components/arduino/cores/esp32/Esp.cpp:163-165` defines it as `heap_caps_get_free_size(MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL)`, not the combined heap. So `checkMemoryAvailable()` (`System_MemoryMonitor.cpp:101-143`) and its 13 call sites (gamepad, thermal, imu, tof, fmradio, presence, apds, gps, rtc, espnow, bluetooth, anoencoder) are gating on the pool that actually matters. I specifically checked for the classic error of gating on combined heap; it is not present.
- **`G2_Health`'s 41 KB pixel grid is correctly in PSRAM** (`EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR static uint8_t sGrid[144][288]`, `G2_Health.cpp:79`), verified absent from `.dram0.bss` in the link map. Worth stating explicitly because it is what establishes that the file's author knew the idiom — which is what makes the eight internal-DRAM series buffers an oversight rather than a considered choice.
- **`g2ShowMapPage` (`G2_Glasses.cpp:17897-17907`) gates on largest-free-block, not total free**, with a comment distinguishing what needs contiguity (tile staging) from what does not (the stack, already owned by the session worker). The correct contiguity-aware gate exists in this codebase.
- -fstack-protector is enabled and reaching real code: sdkconfig:733/736 set CONFIG_COMPILER_STACK_CHECK_MODE_NORM=y and CONFIG_COMPILER_STACK_CHECK=y, and I confirmed __stack_chk_guard loads in the actual prologues of captureEspNowFrame (0x42086793) and fsmWorkerTask (0x4206dcff) in the shipped ELF. This is real (partial) mitigation for the canary-only configuration.
- /api/backup is admin-gated (isAdminUser check, WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:326) and /api/restore is triple-gated by first-time-setup state + gAcceptingRestore + on-device OLED/serial confirm (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:634), with a 512 KB body cap and a proper null-checked allocation — no anonymous config import.
- /api/cli/logs is not on the guest GET allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:359-398), so webGuestAccessAllowed (:433-436) blocks guests from polling the web mirror. handleLogs (WebServer_Server.cpp:2890-2896) applies both tgRequireAuth and the guest gate.
- /api/icon (WebServer_Server.cpp:4554) is unauthenticated but touches no filesystem — it serves only compiled-in PNGs looked up by name through findEmbeddedIcon(), returning 404 for any unknown name. There is no path, no traversal surface, and no state change.
- /api/restore is unauthenticated by design but genuinely triple-gated and non-destructive on its own. handleRestore (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:629) requires gFirstTimeSetupState == SETUP_IN_PROGRESS && gAcceptingRestore (:634), the URI handler is only registered during FTS "Import from Backup" (:812) and unregistered afterwards (:830), the body is capped at 512 KB (:658), the HWBACKUP magic and device fingerprint are validated (:706-:718), and the payload is only STAGED — nothing is written to flash until an operator confirms on the OLED or serial console.
- /api/sessions and /api/admin/sessions are admin-gated despite /api/sessions appearing in the guest allowlist — handleSessionsList (WebServer_Server.cpp:2806) and handleAdminSessionsList (:2829) both reject non-isAdminUser before calling buildAllSessionsJson. The allowlist entry is therefore harmless; a guest gets {"success":false,"error":"Admin access required"}, not session IDs.
- /api/sessions appears on the guest API allowlist (WebServer_Utils.cpp:363) which initially read as a session-ID leak to guests, but the handler enforces isAdminUser internally and returns a JSON error otherwise (WebServer_Server.cpp:2809-2812). The allowlist entry is harmless.
- /api/settings does NOT leak secrets. buildSettingsJsonDoc(doc, /*excludePasswords=*/true) (System_Settings.cpp:906-930) walks every registered SettingsModule and removes each entry flagged isSecret — I checked the whole tree and the only two such string settings are bleSecureChannelSecret (Bluetooth.cpp:2111) and mqttPassword (System_MQTT.cpp:267), both correctly flagged. espnowMeshesWriteJson (System_ESPNow.cpp:1385-1409) omits both the mesh passphrase and the PBKDF2-stretched key when excludePasswords is set, and per-network WiFi passwords are omitted entirely (System_Settings.cpp:983).
- /api/settings excludes WiFi passwords for the web API (buildSettingsJsonDoc(...,true), WebServer_Server.cpp:2523), so the guest-allowlisted settings read does not leak WiFi PSKs (it does expose non-secret config such as SSIDs/mesh labels/broker host — minor info disclosure, not credentials).
- /api/user/settings correctly protects the hashes at both ends: GET strips "password" and "gamepad_password" from the response (WebServer_Server.cpp:2651-2652) and POST hard-rejects any patch containing either field with an explicit "prevents storing unhashed passwords" rationale (:2716-2724).
- /api/user/settings does not leak password hashes. handleUserSettingsGet explicitly removes "password", "gamepad_password" and "mustChangePassword" from the response (WebServer_Server.cpp:2650-2653), and handleUserSettingsSet rejects any patch containing those keys with 400 password_not_allowed (:2718-2724). Both verified by reading the handlers end to end.
- /api/user/settings handles the credential fields correctly in both directions: GET strips `password`, `gamepad_password` and `mustChangePassword` from the response (WebServer_Server.cpp:2651-2653), and POST rejects a body containing either password field with a 400 rather than storing an unhashed value (:2718-2723).
- /api/user/settings is correctly self-scoped. GET (:2609) resolves userId from ctx.user only — there is no username or id parameter to tamper with — and strips password / gamepad_password / mustChangePassword from the response (:2657-2659). POST (:2667) resolves the same way, caps the body at 4096, null-checks the allocation, and explicitly rejects any patch containing password or gamepad_password (:2698-2703).
- /api/user/settings strips password, gamepad_password and mustChangePassword from the response before serializing (WebServer_Server.cpp:2651-2653).
- /login/setsession is dead code, not a session-minting bypass: it reads global gSessUser which is never assigned a username anywhere (only declared and cleared); handleLogin mints sessions via setSession(req, ctx.user) directly after isValidUser. The handler always redirects to /login.
- /system/espnow/identity.json is the best-behaved state writer in the tree: version-checked on read, the Ed25519 secret is hex-encoded before encryption so NUL bytes survive, sec+32 is cross-checked against pub, and a corrupt file makes the subsystem REFUSE to start rather than regenerate over it (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:133-205). Its decrypt also happens well after selectDeviceKeyEpoch has run.
- /system/sys_logs/ is admin-read-only in the VFS path rules (System_Filesystem.cpp:1367, userPerms=0), so command-audit.log, events.log and errors.log are not reachable by a regular user through fileread or the web file browser. They ARE reachable via physical flash access (encryption off) and via a bond-admin espnowfetch.
- /system/users/users.json is NOT reachable by a non-admin through any local file surface. lookupRule() falls through to the {"/system/", userPerms=0, adminPerms=PERM_READ} row (System_Filesystem.cpp:1382), so checkPerm denies FsRole::USER and FsRole::GUEST. handleFileRead additionally short-circuits with a 403 because isAdminOnlyPath() is true for that rule (WebServer_Server.cpp:1692). All six file CLI commands (files/fileview/fileread/filewrite/filedelete/filerename) are requiresAdmin=true (System_Filesystem.cpp:1264-1282). Verified by tracing lookupRule row-by-row for that exact path.
- A bonded peer's `automations.json` is received into `/espnow/received/<mac>/automations.json` and only rendered as a summary (System_ESPNow.cpp:4467, :6790-6791) — it is never installed over `/system/automations.json`, so the bond file-transfer channel is not an automation-injection vector.
- A low-privilege user has no path to create or modify an automation at all. `automation`, `automationadd`, `automationrun` and `automationtrigger` are all requiresAdmin=true (System_Automation.cpp:4145-4152), the edit/enable/disable/delete paths additionally re-check `createdBy` against `currentExecUser()` (System_Automation.cpp:1173, 1475, 1558, 1814), and `/system/automations.json` grants userPerms=0, adminPerms=PERM_READ (System_Filesystem.cpp:1350) — so there is no filesystem route either. `cmd_conditional` and `cmd_downloadautomation` (HardwareOne.cpp:1220-1227) are never registered in any command table, so the `IF ... THEN <cmd>` engine has no CLI entry point.
- A reboot does NOT enable replay, and no persisted counter is needed. Sessions and their keys are RAM-only and vanish together; every re-handshake generates fresh X25519 ephemerals (Handlers_Crypto.cpp:1072, 835) so post-reboot keys are unrelated to pre-reboot keys and a captured SESSION_FRAME fails AEAD open. Explicitly checked for the classic embedded footgun of a persisted key plus a reset counter — it is absent by construction.
- A reboot does NOT enable session replay even though rxSeqHighWater is RAM-only and never persisted. Sessions die with the reboot, the re-handshake generates fresh ephemeral X25519 keys, and captured frames cannot decrypt under the new AEAD keys. Not persisting the counter is the correct design here, and the header comment at System_ESPNow_Sessions.h:10-13 states the reasoning accurately.
- A single flash erase does not drop ESP-NOW frames. The RX callback fills an 8-slot ring (System_ESPNow.cpp:393) drained by espnow_task every 10 ms, and behind it sit 16 static + 32 dynamic WiFi RX buffers (sdkconfig:1836-1837). During a ~40 ms erase the WiFi task is parked too, so nothing is added to the ring; the hardware buffers absorb the burst at the measured ~37 frames/s. Only the multi-second rotation storm (finding 3) exhausts them.
- A user account literally named `system` does NOT get FsRole::SYSTEM. resolveRole (System_Filesystem.cpp:1551) requires `ctx.transport == SOURCE_INTERNAL && ctx.user == "system"`, and authorizeCommand's internal bypass (System_Utils.cpp:4303) uses the identical conjunction. An external transport can never set SOURCE_INTERNAL, so planting that name buys nothing on the filesystem or the command gate.
- A8's core claim about the ESP-NOW nonce counter holds up on re-reading: sessionWrapFrame's `++s->txSeqNext` and the AEAD seal are genuinely inside EspNowTxGuard, and the rollback on seal failure (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:320) is inside the same section. The residual problem is the slot's lifetime, not the counter — filed separately so the correct part of A8's assessment is not discarded with it.
- AAD/HMAC coverage of the header is complete and correct on both paths: the first 30 bytes (type, flags, msgId, origin, ttl, fragIndex, fragCount, meshFingerprint, sessionId, frameSeq) are bound for SESSION_FRAME (Sessions.cpp:310-318) and the identical 30 bytes are the first HMAC chunk for BROADCAST_AUTH (System_ESPNow.cpp:1673-1677). In-flight header tampering, including a forged origin or a modified ttl, is detected.
- ADMIN GATES VERIFIED AS REAL GATES, NOT INCIDENTAL FIELD READS (my first automated scan produced false positives here, so each was opened): /api/sessions (:2809), /api/admin/sessions (:2832), /api/debug/flags (:3069), /api/admin/pending (:4708), /api/admin/approve (:4755), /api/admin/reject (:4797) and /api/backup (WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:326) all perform `if (!isAdminUser(ctx.user)) { 403; return; }` BEFORE any side effect or data assembly.
- AEAD nonce construction is sound and race-free. buildNonce (Sessions.cpp:181-197) is sessionId(2) || direction(1) || frameSeq(4) || zeros, and the direction byte differs per side, so A's and B's streams cannot collide on the same key. sessionWrapFrame serializes the ++txSeqNext and the seal under EspNowTxGuard (:295) against the multiple sender tasks, refuses to wrap at txSeqNext == 0xFFFFFFFF, and rolls the counter back if the seal fails. sessionApplyRekeyedKeys takes the same lock for the key/counter swap.
- ALLOCATION-FAILURE HANDLING - I opened and read every first-party PSRAM allocation site in the tree and every single one null-checks before first use, with correct partial-rollback where multiple allocations are chained.
