Specifically verified: System_Maps.cpp:487/519/636 (halving retry loop)/700/2265, System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:392-448 (frees the partially-allocated stream half-buffers, then the dataBuffer when chunkMap fails), System_ESPNow.cpp:9608/9623/9632 (three-tier fallback ending in a documented disabled-ring state), System_ESPSR.cpp:1212/1251/1968-2003 (internal fallback per buffer, then a combined check that frees all four and vTaskDeletes), G2_Glasses.cpp:1686-1714/8618/10629-10645 (rolls back partial strdups)/14677, OLED_RemoteSettings.cpp:182-189, OLED_Mode_UnifiedMenu.cpp:278/335, OLED_Mode_Map.cpp:929-935, OLED_Utils.cpp:2427-2441 (hands back whichever half succeeded, publishes the gate pointer last), System_MQTT.cpp:108-113, System_Debug.cpp:438-450/603-610/624-631, System_Automation.cpp:521/3716, WebPage_Maps.cpp:594, WebPage_Sensors.cpp:970, WebServer_Server.cpp:4427, WebServer_MigrationTool.cpp:667, System_Microphone.cpp:1127, System_LLM.cpp:2526, System_EdgeImpulse.cpp:526/595, G2_Page_Files.cpp:194 (all six ensureFm() consumers at :234/:439/:480/:728/:800/:989 null-check the return). I found ZERO cases of a PSRAM pointer being dereferenced without a check.
- AUTOMATION SUB-COMMANDS CANNOT ESCALATE VIA THE SOURCE_INTERNAL FAST PATH. `queueAutomationSubCommand` sets `transport = SOURCE_INTERNAL` with the automation's `createdBy` as the user (System_Automation.cpp:97-99), but `authorizeCommand`'s internal bypass additionally requires `ctx.user == "system"` (System_Utils.cpp:4303) and "system" is in the reserved-name table that `isValidPublicUsername` rejects (System_User.cpp:803). An empty owner still fails every admin/super gate and resolves to `FsRole::ANON` at the FS layer.
- AUTOMATIONS CANNOT BE CREATED OR EDITED FROM THE WEB WITHOUT PASSING THE CLI GATE. The only registered automation HTTP endpoints are `/automations` (GET page), `/api/automations` (GET) and `/api/automations/export` (GET) — WebServer_Server.cpp:5361, :5368, :5369. Creation and import both go through `postCLI('automation add …')` (WebPage_Automations.h:1346, :1614), i.e. `/api/cli` -> `authorizeCommand`, and `automationadd` is registered `requiresAdmin=true` (System_Automation.cpp:4151). Edit/enable/disable/delete/run each re-check `createdBy` against `currentExecUser()` unless the caller is admin (:1174, :1475, :1558, :1814).
- Account approval cannot mint privilege. `approvePendingUserInternal` hard-codes `newUser["role"] = "user"` (System_User.cpp:1412) — the pending record carries no role field at all — so neither the CLI `userapprove` nor the web POST handler (WebServer_Server.cpp:4752) can turn a self-registration into an admin, regardless of what the requester submitted.
- All 31 `strcpy()` sites take compile-time string literals into buffers of known-larger size (menu row labels, "<- Back", "--", MAC placeholder, error text). None takes a runtime-derived source.
- All 5 remaining raw `sprintf()` sites are provably bounded by fixed-size hex formatting, not by input length: System_ESPNow.cpp:856 (`formatSessionToken`, 16x2 hex into char[33], exact fit) and System_Settings.cpp:415-421 (AES encode, buffer sized `4 + 32 + 1 + paddedLen*2 + 1` and written exactly that).
- All FS_LIST/STAT/GET wire-struct receivers validate `payloadLen >= sizeof(struct)` before memcpy, and the reply's `expectedLen = sizeof(hdr) + entryCount * 76` cannot overflow uint16 (entryCount is uint8, max 19520).
- All POST endpoints are unreachable for guests: `webGuestAccessAllowed` (WebServer_Utils.cpp:415) 403s any method that is not GET/HEAD before path matching. Verified specifically for `/api/user/settings`, `/api/waypoints`, `/api/cli`, `/api/cli/batch`, `/api/files/write`, `/api/files/upload`, `/api/files/create`, `/api/llm/*`, `/api/bond/*` and `/api/admin/*`.
- All nine filesystem CLI verbs are admin-gated (files, filecreate, fileview, fileread, filewrite, filedelete, filerename and the mkdir/rmdir pair, System_Filesystem.cpp:1264-1282, every entry registered with adminOnly=true), so a User-tier session obtained over BLE or web cannot read the filesystem through the CLI.
- All nine sensor OLED X-toggles plus the RTC `ntpsync` Y-action are command-routed (`openimu`/`closeimu`, `openthermal`, `opengps`, `openrtc`, `opentof`, `openapds`, `openpresence`, `openinput`, `ntpsync`). They carry no local guard, but they fail safe at `authorizeCommand` gate 3. Re-verified in all eight i2csensor_*_oled.h files.
- All other session-id log sites truncate correctly: WebServer_Server.cpp:419, 1231 and 1232 print `%.8s...`. Only :322 and :376 print the whole token.
- All three AES helpers zero their plaintext buffers before free: encryptString (System_Settings.cpp:397), decryptString (:543), and the boot-time epoch prober aesBlobDecryptsWith (:658). decryptString also does strict full PKCS#7 validation (:523-529) rather than the single-byte range check, which is what stops a wrong-key decrypt from masquerading as a recovered secret.
- AllocPref::PreferInternal MISUSE - none. The trap is real (PreferInternal degrades to plain malloc(), which lands in PSRAM above CONFIG_SPIRAM_MALLOC_ALWAYSINTERNAL=16384) and is documented in-tree at System_Automation.cpp:3711-3714. All three live call sites request far less than 16 KB: System_ESPNow.cpp:9632 (~4.7 KB fallback stream ring), System_Debug.cpp:623 (only taken when hasPsram is false), OLED_Mode_FileBrowser.cpp:580 (one small job struct). The two places where internal residency is a hard requirement bypass the helper and spell out MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL directly with the reasoning written down: System_Automation.cpp:3716 (event drain buffer) and OLED_Utils.cpp:2427-2429 (CLI history ring, which holds echoed credentials).
- Allocation-failure handling: I read every explicit PSRAM allocation site in the tree (~60 heap_caps_*/ps_* calls plus 10 PSRAM_STATIC_BUF) and ran a scripted null-check sweep over all of them. Every single one checks the result before use. Standouts: System_ESPSR.cpp:1968-2003 (4 buffers, internal-heap fallback each, full rollback + task self-delete), System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:390-450 (partial stream-buffer rollback), System_Automation.cpp:3710-3759 (throttled warn, skips the tick rather than dereferencing null, and explains why the cursor stays sane), and the ~30 `if (!jbuf) jbuf = ps_alloc(...); if (!jbuf) return "{\"error\":\"oom\"}"` command buffers.
- An unprovisioned device fails closed, not open. detectFirstTimeSetupState gates on users.json existing (System_FirstTimeSetup.cpp:86) and firstTimeSetupIfNeeded() blocks at HardwareOne.cpp:1668 — before WiFi init at :1695 — so the device never reaches the network unprovisioned. If users.json exists but is unparseable, getUserIdByUsername returns false for everyone (System_User.cpp:1181-1185, which also posts SYSEVT_AUTH_DB_FAULT), isAdminUser/isSuperAdminUser both bail on an unparseable owner (:316, :388), and serialRequireAuth defaults true (System_Settings.h:359) — bricked, but not open. This confirms the prior refutation in docs/SETTINGS_LIFECYCLE_AUDIT.md.
- Anti-key-substitution is real. Both v4hKeyExHello (:259-289) and v4hKeyExReply (:363-394) refuse to overwrite a stored peer pubkey, log a rate-limited SECURITY event, and reply status=2. The only override is espnowConsumePairingWindow (:13919-13928): per-MAC, time-bounded, one-shot, and armed only by a local `espnowpairsecure`.
- Anti-key-substitution on pairing is real. A peer presenting a different Ed25519 pubkey for a MAC already on file is refused in both KEY_EX_HELLO and KEY_EX_REPLY (Handlers_Crypto.cpp:257-291 and :360-395), the refusal is symmetric (both send CONFIRM status=2), and it is only overridable by a deliberate local espnowpairsecure re-key window. The refusal is written to the system event log with a 60 s throttle so it cannot be flooded away.
- Arduino String mechanics re-derived from the vendored source and confirmed exactly as the brief states: SSOSIZE is 15 (WString.h:326), changeBuffer takes the SSO path only for maxStrLen < 14 (WString.cpp:187), so length ≥ 14 heap-allocates; growth is `(maxStrLen + 16) & ~0xf` (WString.cpp:206), i.e. 16-byte-granular exact-fit, so `+=` in a loop reallocs every 16 bytes.
- At-rest wrapping of the ESP-NOW key material is real and carefully done. The 64-byte Ed25519 secret is hex-encoded, AES-wrapped via encryptString, and the plaintext hex scratch buffer is memset immediately after use (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:82-87). Per-peer 16-byte LMKs and even the peer MAC addresses are AES-wrapped in devices.json (System_ESPNow.cpp:551-582). Mesh passphrases and the PBKDF2-stretched mesh key are both wrapped and both excluded from the sync and web paths, with a comment at System_ESPNow.cpp:1393-1399 correctly explaining that the stretched key is key material and not a harmless digest.
- Audit-log redaction works for the credential commands it covers — `redactCmdForAudit` (System_Utils.cpp:1179-1209) is quote-aware via `nthTokenBounds` and `kRules` (:1114-1140) correctly masks `login`, `wifiadd`, `useradd`, `blesecret`, `mqttpassword`, `userchangepassword`, `userresetpassword`, `usersync`, `espnowremote`, `espnowbrowse`, `espnowfetch` and both passphrase setters. Every `logCommandExecution` write (:923) and console echo (:953) goes through it.
- Auth-toggle defaults are fail-closed: serialRequireAuth, localDisplayRequireAuth and bleRequireAuth all default true (System_Settings.h:359, :365 and the settings table at System_Settings.cpp:1965-1966), and bleAutoStart defaults false (:364) so the BLE server is opt-in. The BLE chain therefore requires an operator who deliberately enabled BLE.
- Automations fail closed for guests. Creation is admin-gated (`automationadd`, requiresAdmin=true), and `queueAutomationSubCommand` stamps `auth.user = createdBy` with SOURCE_INTERNAL but *not* user "system" — so gate 3 still applies and an owner who is later demoted to guest simply has their automations stop firing rather than executing with stale privilege.
- BLE INGRESS SANITISES CONTROL CHARACTERS. `processBleCommandLine` (Bluetooth.cpp:677-688) rebuilds the line dropping NUL, mapping CR/LF/TAB to a space, and discarding everything outside printable ASCII. This is the one transport immune to the audit-log injection in finding 1, and it is the model for the fix.
- BLE Secure Channel header claims verified against the implementation. System_BleSecureChannel.h:33-38 specifies PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with salt "HW1-SC-v1" at 100000 iterations, HKDF info "HW1-SC-v1", and a "per-direction ctr strictly increasing (CONFIRM=0, DATA=1,2,3,...) -> replay-safe" property. All four check out in System_BleSecureChannel.cpp: SC_PSK_SALT/SC_INFO at :46-47, SC_PBKDF2_ITERS = 100000 at :48, and the replay enforcement is real — `if (ctr <= c->rxCtr) return BLE_SC_ERROR;` at :371, with rxCtr advanced only after a successful AEAD open (:379). This is an auth-subsystem comment that says exactly what the code does.
- BLE command path funnels correctly. `processBleCommandLine` (Bluetooth.cpp:673) intercepts only `login` (which it forwards as a real `login` command), `logout` (self-scoped `bleLogout`) and `whoami` (read-only); everything else goes to `submitCommandAsync` → `executeCommand`. The secure-channel gate for file-browser commands is applied on top, not instead of, the auth gate.
- BLE default posture is correct: bleRequireAuth defaults true (System_Settings.h:365) and blerequireauth/blesecret are both registered requiresSuperAdmin=true. The file-browser verbs additionally require an established secure channel regardless of the global plaintext setting (Bluetooth.cpp:811), and the plaintext path is refused outright when bleScRequired() (:871).
- BLE file-browser commands (`fileread`/`filewrite`/`filedelete`/`filerename`/`mkdir`/`rmdir`) are gated behind an established secure channel before they are allowed to submit (Bluetooth.cpp:661, :811), on top of the normal `bleRequireAuth` session check.
- BLE inbound framing `processIncomingBLECommand` (Bluetooth.cpp:859-877) gates the memcpy with `len <= sizeof(BleScDeferred::buf)` before copying, and `processBleCommandLine` (:673-687) filters to printable ASCII into a 512-byte stack buffer with an `outIdx < sizeof(cmdBuf)-1` loop bound. The `login` sub-parser clamps both the username (64) and password (128) copies.
- BLE secure-channel frame parser `bleScHandleInbound` (System_BleSecureChannel.cpp:301-383) checks exact lengths for HELLO (49) and CONFIRM (19), enforces `len >= 25` for DATA, derives `ctLen = len - 25`, and rejects `ctLen + 1 > outCap` BEFORE the AEAD decrypt writes into the caller's buffer. Counter replay is rejected (`ctr <= c->rxCtr`). No path reaches plaintext without a successful Poly1305 verify.
- BLE server is opt-in (bleAutoStart=false, System_Settings.h:363) and bleRequireAuth defaults true, so an unauthenticated BLE peer can only run 'login' (the residual is the missing BLE login throttle, PRE_1_0 3.4). The Secure Channel ships inert (secret empty) but that only means plaintext transport, not an auth bypass.
- BROADCAST_AUTH really is auth-only, exactly as System_ESPNow_Wire.h:42-48 documents. The payload is copied verbatim and a 32-byte HMAC-SHA256 is appended (System_ESPNow.cpp:1670-1684); on RX the tag is verified with sodium_memcmp and stripped, and only wasAuthenticated is set — wasSessionEncrypted is deliberately left false (:5216), with the distinction correctly plumbed through V4RxCtx. No confidentiality is claimed or implied anywhere in the code.
- BROADCAST_AUTH verification fails closed when mesh keys are missing: System_ESPNow.cpp:5189-5196 silently drops the frame if meshKeysFindByFingerprint returns null or invalid, and the per-opcode handler table enforces REQ_AUTHENTICATED / REQ_SESSION_ENC (:2799-2811) for state-mutating opcodes. meshKeysGet/meshKeysDerive return nullptr/false rather than a zero key when passphraseStretchedKeyValid is false (System_ESPNow_MeshKeys.cpp:88-93, 133-138).
- Board-specific pin macros that leak defaults into shared files are all inert where the hardware is absent: HAL_Audio.cpp:27-31 and System_ESPSR.cpp:63-67 default MIC_CLK_PIN/MIC_DATA_PIN to 42/41 but sit inside #if ENABLE_MICROPHONE_SENSOR and #if ENABLE_ESP_SR respectively (both 0 on FeatherS3; ENABLE_ESP_SR is 0 project-wide at System_BuildConfig.h:293); System_ImageManager.cpp:27-28 defines SD_CS_PIN 21 as a fallback but never uses the macro; every SD path in System_VFS.cpp is wrapped in #if defined(SD_CS_PIN).
- Bond mode cannot be enabled over the air by a peer. Settings arriving as _settings_out.json are only cached to LittleFS (processBondSettings :7184-7248) and never merged into gSettings, and v4FileBondMagicAllowed (:4221-4233) restricts the three magic filenames to the configured bond peer while bond mode is already on and the peer is online. Every bond-gated opcode carries REQ_PAIRED|REQ_BOND_MODE|REQ_SESSION_ENC (:5014-5023).
- Bond mode cannot be entered over the radio. gSettings.bondModeEnabled has no inbound-frame writer anywhere in the ESP-NOW RX path; every RX-side use is a read. Bond-magic filenames on FILE_END are additionally pinned to the configured bond peer while it is online by v4FileBondMagicAllowed (System_ESPNow.cpp:4221-4234), so another paired peer with a valid session cannot poison the bond settings cache.
- CLI tokenizer (CommandArgs::parse, System_Command.cpp:388): token count bounded by MAX_ARGS=10 matching the args_/offsets_/quoted_ array sizes; all slicing via bounds-checked Arduino String::substring.
- CONFIG_ESPTOOLPY_FLASHMODE="dio" sitting next to CONFIG_ESPTOOLPY_FLASHMODE_QIO=y is NOT a misconfiguration. This is deliberate upstream IDF behaviour — the string symbol is forced to "dio" for QIO/QOUT/DIO so the bootloader is flashed in DIO mode for compatibility, and the app switches to QIO after boot. flasher_args.json correctly shows flash_mode dio, flash_freq 80m, flash_size 16MB. Checked so it would not be filed as a bug.
- CONFIG_FREERTOS_THREAD_LOCAL_STORAGE_POINTERS=4 is set and matches what System_AuthIdentity needs. Verified live at sdkconfig:1978 and pinned in sdkconfig.defaults with the full rationale (slot 0 owned by IDF pthread, slot 1 for TaskIdentity). No contradiction between the per-task-TLS auth model and the build config.
- CROWN JEWEL, ANSWERED: automations run as their CREATOR, never as "system", and fail closed. `queueAutomationSubCommand` stamps `ctx.auth.transport = SOURCE_INTERNAL` with `ctx.auth.user = createdBy` (System_Automation.cpp:97-99). The authorizeCommand internal bypass requires `ctx.user == "system"` exactly (System_Utils.cpp:4303), and "system" is a reserved username that `isValidPublicUsername` rejects (System_User.cpp:803), so no account can hold it and `automationadd`'s `currentExecUser()` stamp (System_Automation.cpp:1397) can never produce it. Privilege is re-resolved from users.json on every sub-command, so demoting or deleting the creator immediately downgrades the automation. A missing `createdBy` yields "" (extractJsonString zeroes the buffer first, System_Automation.cpp:233), and `isAdminUser("")`/`isSuperAdminUser("")` both return false on their length-0 guards (System_User.cpp:283, :361) — User tier, not admin.
- Channel selection above 11 is unreachable from the two on-device UIs: both the OLED (OLED_ESPNow.cpp:2085-2091) and the G2 lens (G2_Page_ESPNow.cpp:966-978) cycle only Auto → 1 → 6 → 11, the non-overlapping set. The 12/13 exposure is confined to the CLI, the web settings page and the settings registry.
- Command-layer chokepoint intact. `authorizeCommand` (System_Utils.cpp:4300) still runs gate 3 (`isGuestUser(ctx.user) && !commandAllowedForGuest(line)`) at System_Utils.cpp:4392, before the super and admin gates, on every line that reaches `executeCommand`. `commandAllowedForGuest` (:4356) still permits only `login` and `logout` — no command has been quietly added to the allowlist.
- Command-line truncation happens before authorization, not after: submitAndExecuteSync strncpy's into ExecReq::line (System_Utils.cpp:4810) and executeCommand then authorizes r->line, so a truncated line cannot be authorized in one form and executed in another. I checked this specifically because it is a classic ordering bug.
- Core assignment matches the documented policy at every site checked: all nine I2C sensor pollers plus sensor_queue_task and cam_pwr use I2C_SENSOR_CORE; compute/render (mapRender, sr_task, ei_continuous, mic_viz, llm_gen, g2_img_probe) is on APP_CORE; cmd_exec_task, debug_out, espnow_tx and sr_snip_wr are on PRO_CORE.
- Correcting docs/HOT_PATH_HEAP_AUDIT.md's unverified orphan at WebServer_Server.cpp:4811 (now :5096): handleCliBatch does push the UNREDACTED `out` into the results array while only the broadcast copy gets redactOutputForLog (:5094-5096), whereas the single /api/cli returns the redacted copy (:3296). But the batch response goes only to the authenticated client that issued the command, i.e. someone already authorised to run it — so the prior doc's "possible credential disclosure" framing does not hold. It is an inconsistency between two endpoints, not a cross-user leak.
- Credential compares in the handshake are full-length and constant-time: ctMemcmp32 over the whole 32-byte KEY_EX HMAC (Handlers_Crypto.cpp:126-130, used at :160) and sodium_memcmp over the whole 32-byte BROADCAST_AUTH tag (System_ESPNow.cpp:5207). No truncated-tag comparison anywhere.
- Credential verification fails closed on an unparseable auth DB. getUserIdByUsername (System_User.cpp:1167-1201) posts SYSEVT_AUTH_DB_FAULT and returns false on any DeserializationError, so isValidUser (:1137) cannot resolve anyone — login becomes impossible, not universal. Passwords live in per-user settings files and are PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256; verifyUserPassword rejects any stored hash that is empty or lacks the PBKDF2: prefix (:740-751).
- Crypto placement that is CORRECT: gIdentity (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:28), gMeshDerivedKeys (System_ESPNow_MeshKeys.cpp:18) and gSc (System_BleSecureChannel.cpp:71) are plain statics in internal DRAM. gPeerIdentities is EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR (System_ESPNow_Identity.cpp:298) but PeerIdentity holds only the peer's Ed25519 PUBLIC key (System_ESPNow_Identity.h:107) — not a secret, correctly PSRAM. gSessionPrewarm (System_ESPNow.cpp:2954) is EXT_RAM_BSS_ATTR and holds no key material. This confirms the 'gPeerIdentities PSRAM/DRAM contradiction does not exist' entry in the prior-audit index.
- DEFAULT IDENTITY IS FAIL-CLOSED, everywhere. A task that never installed a guard sees the zero-initialized `TaskIdentity` (System_AuthIdentity.cpp:42-55, 74-80): user="", transport=0. That resolves to `FsRole::ANON` (System_Filesystem.cpp:1550) which `permsForRole` maps to 0 for every rule (:1584), `authorizeCommand` gate 2 rejects it for every command except `login` (System_Utils.cpp:4312-4322), and `isAdminUser("")` returns false before touching users.json (System_User.cpp:283). I found no path where an uninitialized TLS slot yields a permissive identity.
- Deferring the ESP-NOW channel work off the tiny arduino_events stack is done correctly. The WiFi event handler only sets a volatile flag (espnowNoteWifiChannelMayHaveChanged, System_ESPNow.cpp:994-996); the actual esp_wifi_get_config / esp_wifi_set_channel work runs on espnow_task in processMeshHeartbeats (8433-8483). The disconnect logger uses the same defer-and-drain pattern (System_WiFi.cpp:1647-1692). No radio API with a deep stack frame is called from the event task.
- Deliberate internal-DRAM choices verified sound and well-documented: the ESP-NOW inbound RX ring and broadcast trackers (System_ESPNow.cpp:9811/9861, MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL, fatal-on-failure for the ring), the automation event drain buffer (System_Automation.cpp:3716, with an explicit note at :3711-3715 on why ps_alloc(PreferInternal) is not good enough), gAutomationsCache (System_Automation.cpp:521), the OLED console ring (OLED_Utils.cpp:2427, internal because it holds echoed CLI credentials), and gLLM.stateHotData (System_LLM_Model.cpp:709, internal first with PSRAM fallback). The streamQueue two-tier PSRAM/internal fallback (System_ESPNow.cpp:9620-9648) degrades correctly and disables the ring cleanly when both fail.
- Demote/delete correctly tears down the G2 stamp. `revokeUserSessions` (System_User.cpp:565) clears `pairedByUser` when the target matches (:609), and the ban path repeats it (:1070). So an admin who pairs the lens and is later demoted to guest does not leave a stale privileged stamp behind.
- Direct role-mutation commands are correctly double-gated. `useradd`, `userpromote`, `userdemote`, `userdelete`, `userresetpassword` are all registered requiresAdmin=true (System_User.cpp:3387-3399) AND independently rank-checked by `userMutationAllowed()` (System_User.cpp:1557-1570), which resolves the caller from the per-task TLS identity (`currentExecUser()`) and refuses both "act on a target above your rank" and "grant a role above your own". A Guest or User cannot reach them at all; an Admin cannot mint or seize a Super Admin. `setUserRoleInternal` additionally hard-protects the founder (id==1) at System_User.cpp:1632-1634.
- Discover-then-confirm pairing really does require a human accept. v4h_pair_request (System_ESPNow.cpp:4866) drops unless the target's own discovery window is open (:4871) and only records a pending request for `espnowaccept`/`espnowreject`; the registry write happens in cmd_espnow_pairsecure on both sides. No unsolicited auto-pair path was found.
- Docs' BROADCAST_AUTH claim is VERIFIED as written: it is authentication only. System_ESPNow.cpp:1661-1685 appends an HMAC-SHA256 tag over (header[0..29] || payload) keyed by the mesh group key to an otherwise plaintext payload, and the RX path at :5175-5215 sets wasAuthenticated=true while leaving wasSessionEncrypted=false. Confidentiality genuinely comes only from SESSION_FRAME AEAD.
- Duplicate command registrations carry IDENTICAL auth flags — no privilege divergence from the first-wins duplicate. `python3 tools/command_registry.py audit` reports exactly three unexpected duplicates (espnowenabled, pendinglist, serialrequireauth) and all three pairs match on admin=/super=. Read the espnowenabled pair directly (System_Settings.cpp:224 and System_ESPNow.cpp:15981): same handler symbol `cmd_espnowenabled`, both requiresAdmin=true. `findCommand` (System_Command.cpp:110-143) prefers longer names with a strict `entryLen > bestLen`, so ties resolve to the first registration deterministically — the dead slot cannot silently carry a weaker gate.
- ENUMERATION COMPLETE: 124 httpd_uri_t registrations across 15 files were enumerated programmatically and every handler body was then opened and read. Registration sites: WebServer_Server.cpp:5324-5397 (core), plus registerSensorHandlers/registerBluetoothHandlers/registerSpeechPageHandlers/registerEspNowHandlers/registerBondHandlers/registerMqttHandlers/registerBatteryHandlers/registerR1HealthHandlers/registerGamesHandlers/registerDarkRoomHandlers/registerLLMHandlers/registerMapsHandlers/registerEdgeImpulseHandlers/registerESPSRHandlers/registerMigrationBackupHandler. No handler is board-gated; all gating is by feature macro (ENABLE_HTTP_SERVER, ENABLE_WEB_*, ENABLE_AUTOMATION, ENABLE_ONDEVICE_LLM, ENABLE_MIGRATION_TOOL).
- ESP-NOW AEAD unwrap (sessionUnwrapFrame, System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:329): checks payloadLen >= tag len, derives cipherLen, writes into a MAX_PAYLOAD-sized plainBuf; replay-checked; plaintext wiped on replay reject.
- ESP-NOW CMD opcode (the only RF path that can run arbitrary commands) is NOT unauthenticated even for a mesh-key holder: v4h_cmd rejects any non-session-encrypted frame (System_ESPNow.cpp:5739) and then requires EITHER a valid @BOND session token (validateBondSessionToken) OR isValidUser(user,pass) before submitCommandAsync -> executeCommand -> authorizeCommand. No account/bond token => no command execution.
- ESP-NOW CMD/TEXT/STREAM/metadata handlers: all copy min(payloadLen, sizeof(dest)-1) into fixed buffers (deferredCmdPayload[256], text/stream slot content, deferredMetadataPayload[216] vs 202-byte struct) with explicit NUL termination.
- ESP-NOW CMD_RESP deferred copy (System_ESPNow.cpp:2919-2930): resultLen clamped to V4_REASM_MAX_MSG against a V4_REASM_MAX_MSG+1 PSRAM allocation (System_ESPNow.cpp:9608); NUL write in bounds.
- ESP-NOW DEFERRED JOBS DO NOT RELY ON AMBIENT TLS. The inbound file writer uses explicit scope-confined `VFS::systemAuth(VFS::Scopes::ESPNOW_RECEIVED, ...)` at System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:145, 205 and 598-600, and the FS_LIST/STAT/GET responders install `SYSTEM_IDENTITY_SCOPE` inside each op (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:644, 784, 842, 896). These are exactly the jobs that run on cmd_exec_task at ANON, and each one handles it explicitly rather than inheriting.
- ESP-NOW FILE_* and SENSOR_* opcodes carry session-encryption requirements — System_ESPNow.cpp:4993-4996 registers FILE_START/DATA/END/CANCEL with `REQ_PAIRED | REQ_SESSION_ENC`, and SENSOR_REQ/SENSOR_ENVELOPE likewise at :4978-4979. A spoofed paired MAC cannot inject plaintext transfers or forged cancels. TIME_SYNC and the PAIR_* opcodes carry REQ_AUTHENTICATED. Only the FS_* rows are under-gated.
- ESP-NOW FS-list request timeouts use `(int32_t)(now - sPending[i].deadlineMs) < 0` (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:923) — wrap-correct.
- ESP-NOW FS_LIST reply builder (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:672-738): emit index bounded by cap = min(maxEntries, FS_LIST_ENTRIES_PER_REPLY) against a 32-entry allocation; request path fields NUL-terminated in captureDeferred.
- ESP-NOW FS_LIST reply parser (System_ESPNow_FsList.cpp:292): validates payloadLen >= header, recomputes expectedLen = header + entryCount*entrySize with the length check, and clamps entryCount to FS_LIST_ENTRIES_PER_REPLY before indexing.
- ESP-NOW INBOUND FILE WRITES ARE SCOPE-CONFINED, NOT JUST NORMALIZED. Both the RAM path (System_ESPNow.cpp:4426 `VFS::systemAuth(VFS::Scopes::ESPNOW_RECEIVED, ...)`) and the streaming path (:4185) use the two-argument `systemAuth` that populates `ctx.scope` (System_VFS.cpp:770-774). `checkPerm` evaluates `pathWithinScope` BEFORE any role logic (System_Filesystem.cpp:1620), and `pathWithinScope` (:1601) is boundary-aware (`/sd/VIDEOS` does not match `/sd/VIDEOS_secret`). So even a peer-controlled `filename` containing slashes or an absolute path cannot direct a write outside `/espnow/received`. The `.part` staging names carry only a MAC token, a CRC32 and a slot index — no peer-controlled bytes.
- ESP-NOW KEY_EX / SESSION_OPEN / SESSION_CONFIRM / SESSION_REKEY handlers: each requires exact-size payload (`payloadLen != sizeof(...)`) before casting; deferred copies are fixed-size struct memcpys.
- ESP-NOW RADIO PATH - the two structures the WiFi-task callbacks touch are explicitly internal and say why: gEspNowRxRing (System_ESPNow.cpp:9811, MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL|MALLOC_CAP_8BIT, comment at :9803-9809) and gBroadcastTrackers (:9861, same caps). gEspNow itself IS PSRAM (:9572 ps_alloc PreferPSRAM) and onEspNowDataSent writes gEspNow->routerMetrics through it - but that callback runs on the WiFi task, not an ISR, so PSRAM is legal there. (Note the misleading doxygen at System_ESPNow.cpp:6098 that claims interrupt context; recon_isr.md already flags it.)
- ESP-NOW RX callback `onEspNowDataReceived` (System_ESPNow.cpp:1092-1111) is correct: null/len guards, `it.len` clamped to [0,250] before the memcpy into the 250-byte ring slot, head/tail advanced with a modulo of a named constant (with an explicit comment about why sizeof/sizeof would be a divide-by-zero). No allocation, no logging, no blocking on the WiFi task.
- ESP-NOW RX front end: onEspNowDataReceived clamps `it.len` to 250 into `data[250]` (System_ESPNow.cpp:1105/:388); v4_try_handle_incoming derives `payloadLen = len - 32 <= 218 == ESPNOW_V4_MAX_PAYLOAD == sizeof(plainBuf)`; sessionUnwrapFrame writes `cipherLen = payloadLen - 16 <= 202`. The AEAD unwrap cannot overflow plainBuf.
- ESP-NOW SD traffic capture stores wire bytes, not plaintext. captureEspNowFrame (System_ESPNow.cpp:6015-6068) base64s the frame as handed to/from the radio, and remote commands are rejected unless they arrived AEAD-wrapped (:5739-5753), so captured CMD frames are ciphertext.
- ESP-NOW SendStatus ring access from the web is bounded and safe. handleEspNowMessages (WebPage_ESPNow.cpp:344-362) iterates via sendStatusAt(i), which range-checks i against kSendStatusSlots and returns nullptr for a null table (System_ESPNow_Sessions.cpp:750-751); the handler null-checks and skips !inUse entries. The slots are a fixed-capacity ps_alloc'd table that is never freed or compacted, so unlike gEspNow->devices[] there is no pointer-invalidation hazard — a torn read yields a stale delivery state, nothing worse.
- ESP-NOW USER_SYNC account creation is gated correctly despite the flag-0 opcode row: doUserSyncWork requires session encryption (:3549), the espnowUserSyncEnabled opt-in (:3557), valid credentials for an account on the *receiving* device (:3596) and that account being admin (:3603) — and then force-writes role="user" (:3664), so a source-device admin is never propagated across the mesh. (The pre-check allocation is a separate DoS finding; the authorization itself is right.)
- ESP-NOW USER_SYNC cannot propagate privilege: newUser["role"] is hard-coded to "user" at System_ESPNow.cpp:3663 regardless of the sender's claimed role, with a SECURITY comment stating the intent. It also refuses to overwrite an existing account (:3609). Only the username validation is missing (filed separately).
- ESP-NOW USER_SYNC does NOT propagate admin privilege. System_ESPNow.cpp:3661 forces `newUser["role"] = "user"` with a SECURITY comment, and the source role is read only to log a downgrade notice (:3707-3714). A user who is admin on the sending device lands as a plain user on the receiver. (The username field is the part that is unvalidated — filed separately.)
- ESP-NOW USER_SYNC does not propagate privilege: synced accounts are hardcoded to role "user" regardless of the source device's role, with the source role read only to log a downgrade notice (System_ESPNow.cpp:3659-3663). It also requires session encryption (:3549), the espnowUserSyncEnabled toggle (:3557), valid local admin credentials (:3594) and local admin role (:3601) before writing anything.
- ESP-NOW USER_SYNC never propagates privilege — System_ESPNow.cpp:3663 hard-codes `newUser["role"] = "user"` with an explicit SECURITY comment, and the source role is read only to log a downgrade notice (:3703). The handler additionally requires `w->isSessionEncrypted` (:3549), the `espnowUserSyncEnabled` setting (:3557), valid local credentials (:3594) AND local admin (:3601), and it refuses to overwrite an existing account (:3609).
- ESP-NOW V4 fragment reassembly is correctly bounds-checked: `offset + payloadLen > e->bufferSize` rejects and resets before the memcpy (System_ESPNow.cpp:5364-5371); `fragCount` is clamped to V4_FRAG_MAX at allocation; `uint16_t offset = fragIndex * 200` cannot wrap (255*200 = 51000).
- ESP-NOW V4 frame parser (v4_try_handle_incoming, System_ESPNow.cpp:5123): payloadLen is derived as radio-len minus fixed header, guarded by `len >= sizeof(EspNowV4Header)`, magic/ver/headerLen validated; no length field is trusted from the wire.
- ESP-NOW `USER_SYNC` cannot propagate privilege: it hard-codes `newUser["role"] = "user"` (System_ESPNow.cpp:3665) with an explicit SECURITY comment, requires the frame to have arrived AEAD session-encrypted (:3549), requires `espnowUserSyncEnabled` (:3557, itself a requiresSuperAdmin setting), and validates the supplied credentials against the LOCAL roster with both `isValidUser` (:3596) and `isAdminUser` (:3603). A remote admin gets a plain user on this device, never an admin.
- ESP-NOW file receive is clean end-to-end: fileSlotsAllocate hashes the wire filename with strnlen bounded to sizeof(filename)-1 (System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:271), fileSlotsWriteChunk enforces `chunkIdx < totalChunks` and `offset + dataLen <= dataBufferLen` (:503-508), and the publish paths' `filepath[160]` cannot truncate (17-char prefix + 12-char MAC token + '/' + 63-char name = 93).
- ESP-NOW file receive path builds paths without truncation: `filepath[160]` receives at most a 30-byte deviceDir + '/' + 63-byte filename (System_ESPNow.cpp:4184, 4408); the `.part` staging name fits 80 bytes with a 12-char MAC token and two 8-hex fields.
- ESP-NOW file transfer chunk writes are bounds-checked on both paths: `fileSlotsWriteChunk` validates `offset + dataLen > s.dataBufferLen` (System_ESPNow_Files.cpp:504) and `fileSlotsStreamAppend` validates `dataLen > streamBufCap` and `streamFill + dataLen > streamBufCap` before every memcpy (:653, :661).
- ESP-NOW file-transfer stitching (System_ESPNow_Files.cpp): fileSlotsWriteChunk validates chunkIdx < totalChunks and offset+dataLen <= dataBufferLen (line 504); streaming fileSlotsStreamAppend rejects gaps/dups/oversized chunks and dataLen > streamBufCap; chunkMap byteIndex bounded by chunkMapBytes.
- ESP-NOW fragment reassembly memcpy is correctly bounded: fragCount is clamped to V4_FRAG_MAX in v4_reasm_find_or_alloc (:203), bufferSize is derived from the clamp, and `offset + payloadLen > e->bufferSize` rejects before the memcpy at :5371. `offset = fragIndex * 200` cannot exceed uint16 (255*200 = 51000).
